# Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy

אבן עזרא על דברים

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy)

פירוש פשט המשלב ביאורים המבוססים על דקדוק ובלשנות; חובר במאה ה-12.

12th-century commentary focusing on the simple meaning of the text and incorporating grammar and linguistics.

Sections: Chapter-Verse-Comment

Location: Tanakh/Rishonim on Tanakh/Ibn Ezra/Torah

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): On Your Way (Segments: 1201, License: Public Domain)

Version (En): Ibn Ezra on the Pentateuch; trans. by Jay F. Shachter (Segments: 1201)


## Chapter 1


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:1:1)


**יש** אומרים כי טעם אחד עשר יום מחורב דרך הר שעיר והנה הם הלכו במדבר ארבעים שנה וזה פי' ויהי בארבעים שנה. ואלה טעו כי עד קדש ברנע באו בשנה השנית רק ויהי בארבעים שנה דבק עם דבר משה והישר בעיני שפירושו אלה הדברים שהם דברי המצות הכתובים בפרשת ראה אנכי ושופטים וכי תצא והיה כי תבא כבר אמר אותם כאשר היו במדבר ויהי' דבר משה מושך עצמו ואחר עמו וכן הוא דבר משה במדבר בערבה מול סוף מיום שנסעו מסיני:


In the name of the one who speaks righteousness and declares things that are right¹ I will explain the Torah portion which begins with *These Are The Words*. Some say that the meaning of *eleven days’ journey from Horeb…by the way of mount Seir* (v. 2) is: Look. They traveled for forty years in the wilderness.² (v. 2) is, that it took them 40 years to make a journey that normally takes eleven days. This is the meaning of *And it came to pass in the fortieth year* (v. 3).³ However, they err, for Israel came up to Kadesh-barnea in the second year.⁴ Actually, *And it came to pass in the fortieth year* is connected to *that Moses spoke*⁵ (v. 3). I believe that *These are the words* (v. 1) refers to the precepts recorded in the Torah portions *Re’ah Anokhi*,⁶ *Shoftim*,⁷ *Ki Tetze*,⁸ and *Ve-hayah Ki Tavo*,⁹ which were already spoken by Moses when they were in the wilderness. *Moses spoke* (v. 1) should be read as if written twice.¹⁰ This means Moses spoke,¹¹ in the wilderness, in the Arabah, and in Suph from the day that they journeyed from Sinai.¹²

footnotes:
¹ Is. 45:19.
² In other words, the meaning of eleven days’ journey from Horeb unto Kadesh-barnea…by the way of mount Seir
³ They came to Kadesh-barnea in the fortieth year. Verse 3 explains verse 2.
⁴ Moses sent out the spies in the second year following the Exodus. He sent out the spies from Kadesh-barnea. See Num. 32:8. It should be noted that Num. 13:3 identifies the place from which the spies departed as the wilderness of Paran. The commentaries explain that Kadesh-barnea is at the edge of the wilderness of Paran. See Weiser.
⁵ Not to Kadesh-barnea.
⁶ Deut. 11:26–16:17.
⁷ Deut. 16:18–21:9.
⁸ Deut. 21:10–25:19.
⁹ Deut. 26:1–29:8.
¹⁰ Verse 1 is to be interpreted as if written: These are the words which Moses spoke unto all Israel beyond the Jordan. He spoke these words in the wilderness, in the Arabah, etc. He spoke them on the eleven days’ journey from Horeb unto Kadesh-barnea.
¹¹ These precepts.
¹² I.E. interprets verse 1-3 as follows: These precepts, which Moses recorded in the Book of Deuteronomy beyond the Jordan, were first delivered by Moses in the wilderness between Horeb and Kadesh-barnea.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:1:2)


**בין פארן ובין תופל ולבן וחצרות ודי זהב.** מקומות לא נזכרו בפ' אלה מסעי או נזכרו בשמות אחרים כי הנה שניר יש לו ג' שמות ורבים כן:


BETWEEN PARAN AND TOPHEL, AND LABAN, AND HAZEROTH, AND DI-ZAHAB. Places not mentioned in the Torah portion *These Are the Stages*¹³ (Num. 33:1). On the other hand, they might have been mentioned¹⁴ under other names, for, observe, Senir¹⁵ has three names.¹⁶ There are many other such instances.

footnotes:
¹³ Wherein Moses records the journeys of the children of Israel in the wilderness.
¹⁴ There.
¹⁵ A mountain in northern Israel. See Deut. 3:9
¹⁶ It is also known as Hermon and Sirion. See Deut. 3:89.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:2:1)


**אחד עשר יום.** הטעם שדבר אלה הדברים באותם י״‎א יום **מחורב.** שהלכו מחורב עד קדש ברנע וכבר הגדתי לך כי מקדש ברנע הלכו המרגלי' ואחר שנשבע השם שלא יכנסו לארץ לא התחדש' מצו' רק בשנת הארבעים כי כן מצאנו והעד שאמר באר את התורה הזאת הנה באר התורה שאמר בין פארן ובין תפל כי רוב המצות הם בספר הזה וכן פי' ככל אשר צוה ה' אותו אליהם בעבר הירדן במדבר בערבה ואם תבין סוד (השרים) [צ״‎ל השנים] עשר גם ויכתוב משה והכנעני אז בארץ בהר ה' יראה גם והנה ערשו ערש ברזל תכיר האמת:


IT IS ELEVEN DAYS’ JOURNEY. This means that Moses spoke these things¹⁷ in those eleven days. FROM HOREB. That they went from Horeb to Kadeshbarnea. I have already told¹⁸ you that the spies went out from Kadesh-barnea. After God swore that they would not enter the land,¹⁹ no new commandment was given to them.²⁰ No new commandment was revealed until the fortieth year. We find the aforementioned to be so.²¹ *Took Moses upon him to expound this law* (v. 5) is proof of this. Moses expounded the laws²² refers to. which he had stated between Paran and Tophel, for most of the commandments are in this book.²³ *According unto all that the Lord had given him in commandment unto them* (v. 3), refers to the precepts given *beyond the Jordan; in the wilderness, in the Arabah* (v. 1).²⁴ (v. 3) is to be interpreted as: Moses spoke… according unto all that the Lord had given him in commandment unto them *beyond the Jordan; in the wilderness, in the Arabah*, and in all the other places mentioned earlier in the eleven days’ journey from Horeb to Kadesh-barnea. If you understand the secret of the twelve²⁵ and also that of *So Moses wrote* (Deut. 31:22);²⁶ *And the Canaanite was then in the Land* (Gen. 12:6);²⁷ *In the mount where the Lord is seen* (Gen. 22:14);²⁸. Hence this verse was inserted in the Torah at a later date. and *behold, his bedstead was a bedstead of iron* (Deut. 3:11);²⁹ then you will recognize the truth.³⁰

footnotes:
¹⁷ These words of the Torah repeated by Moses.
¹⁸ I.E. is referring to his comments above.
¹⁹ See Num. 14:26-38.
²⁰ See I.E. on Num. 20:1 (Vol. 4, p. 157).
²¹ The Torah does not record any commandments or prophetic utterances during this period. See I.E. on Num. 20:1 (Vol. 4, p. 157).
²² For that is what this law
²³ The Book of Deuteronomy.
²⁴ According to I.E., Moses spoke…
²⁵ The last twelve verses of the Torah. According to I.E. the last twelve verses of the Torah were not written by Moses, for they record the death of Moses.
²⁶ I.E. believes that Moses did not write this verse.
²⁷ I.E. believes that Moses did not write this verse, for Scripture implies that the Canaanite was then in the land but not at the time that this verse was written. See I.E. on Gen.12:6 and the notes thereto (Vol. 1, p. 151).
²⁸ The reference is to Mount Moriah. However, in the days of Moses the temple had not yet been built. Thus Moses would not have said, In the mount where the Lord is seen
²⁹ The reference is to the bed of Og, king of Bashan, slain by Moses. I.E. believes that Moses would not have had to prove the height of the king by pointing to his bed, for all of Israel then knew of his height. Hence the verse is post-Mosaic.
³⁰ That the first five verses of this book were not composed by Moses but at a later period. I.E. hints at the above because he did not want people to generalize and assume that there are other post-Mosaic additions in the Pentateuch. See I.E. on Gen. 36:31, “…Yitzchaki claims in his book that this chapter was composed during the reign of King Jehosaphat…Far be it for one to believe that our chapter was written in the days of Jehosaphat as Yitzchaki maintains. Indeed his book is fit to be burned” (Vol. 1, p. 341).



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:3:1)


י״‎א כי באחד [באדר] (ס״‎א בשבט) מת משה ואמר כי התאבלו שלשים יום וישבו שם (שתי) [צ״‎ל שש] שבתות והראי' שלהם ויהי בעצם היום הזה כי ביום אחד קרא זה הספר אל כל ישראל ובו מת וזאת איננה ראיה כי בעצם היום הזה הוא על השיר' והנה כתוב אתה עובר היום את הירדן:


Some say that Moses died on the first day of Shevat.³¹ Scripture states that Israel mourned thirty days (Deut. 34:8). They spent six Sabbaths there.³² refers to the day that Moses finished reviewing the Torah. If this was the case, then two Sabbaths remained from the end of the period of Moses’ mourning and the tenth of Nisan. Both of these opinions take issue with Rabbinic tradition, which believes that Moses died on the seventh of Adar. According to Weiser the opinions quoted and rejected by I.E. are those of the Karaites. It is worth noting that I.E. does not quote the Rabbinic opinion. They offer proof from *that selfsame day* (Deut. 32:48), i.e., that Moses read this book before all of Israel in one day and then died on that very day.³³ refers to the day that Moses repeated the Torah to all of Israel. However, this is no proof, for *that selfsame day* refers to the song.³⁴ (give ear) (Deut. 32:1-43). Look! Scripture states, *thou art to pass over the Jordan this day* (Deut. 9:1).³⁵.

footnotes:
³¹ This opinion holds that Moses died on the very day that he explained the Torah to Israel, namely, the first day of Shevat (v. 3).
³² In the land of Moab following the death of Moses. Israel mourned Moses for 30 days (Deut. 34:8). The period of mourning concluded on the first of Adar. Israel crossed the Jordan on the tenth day of Nisan (Josh. 4:19). There are six Sabbaths between the first of Adar and the tenth day of Nisan. It should be noted that some manuscripts read: “Some say that Moses died on the first day of Adar. Scripture states that Israel mourned thirty days (Num. 34:8). They spent two Sabbaths there.” According to this opinion Moses reviewed the Torah for thirty days, and that selfsame day
³³ They believe that that selfsame day
³⁴ The day on which Moses recited the song that opens with the words ha’azinu
³⁵ Israel did not cross the Jordan on the day that Moses uttered Deut. 9:1, for Scripture tells us that Israel crossed into Canaan after the death of Moses. We thus see that the term “that day” is not to be taken literally, but means at that time. The same applies to that selfsame day


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:3:2)


**בעשתי.** פירשתיו בפרשת נשא:


IN THE ELEVENTH MONTH. I have explained the meaning of *ashte asar* (eleven) in my comments on the Torah portion *Nasso*.³⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ See I.E. on Num. 7:72 (Vol. 4, p. 54).



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:5:1)


**הואיל משה באר.** שנים פעלים עוברים דבקים בלא וי״‎ו וכן חמק עבר והנה משה החל לפרש לבנים שנולדו במדבר מה שאירע לאבותיהם ואמר להם כל המצות גם עשרת הדברים ששמעו אבותיהם מפי השם ישמעום גם הם מפי ציר נאמן:


TOOK MOSES UPON HIM TO EXPLAIN. *Ho’il* (took upon himself) and *be’er* (to explain) are two verbs in the perfect attached to each other without a connective *vav*.³⁷. Hence I.E.’s comments. According to I.E., *ho’il moshe be’er* (took Moses upon himself to expound) is to be explained as if written, *ho’il moshe u-ve’er* (Moses began and explained). We find the same in *chamak avar* (had turned away, and was gone) (Cant. 5:6). Look! Moses began to explain to the children who were born in the wilderness what happened to their fathers. He related to them all the commandments. They would also hear from the mouth of the faithful messenger the Ten Statements which their fathers heard from God.

footnotes:
³⁷ Two verbs usually are connected with a vav



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:6:1)


**בחורב.** הוא סיני כאשר אמרתי:


HOREB. This is Sinai,³⁸ as I have previously noted.

footnotes:
³⁸ See I.E.’s short commentary on Ex. 3:1.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:7:1)


**פנו.** עזבו זה המקום **וסעו לכם.** דרך הלשון כמו לך לך:


TURN YOU. Leave this place. AND TAKE YOUR JOURNEY. It is the style of Scripture to say, *u-se’u lakhem*³⁹ literally means, and journey for yourselves. Hence I.E.’s comment. (and take your journey). Compare, *lekh lekha*⁴⁰ (get thee out) (Gen.2:1).

footnotes:
³⁹ U-se’u lakhem
⁴⁰ Literally, go to you.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:9:1)


**לא אוכל לבדי.** כטעם לא תוכל עשוהו לבדך:


I AM NOT ABLE TO BEAR YOU MYSELF ALONE. Its meaning is similar to *thou art not able to perform it alone* (Ex. 18:18).⁴¹

footnotes:
⁴¹ In other words, the meaning of our verse is, I am not able to do it alone.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:10:1)


**הרבה אתכם.** במצרים ונתקיימה הברכה שברך אביכם:


HATH MULTIPLIED YOU. In Egypt, and the blessing which your father blessed you was fulfilled.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:10:2)


וטעם **ככוכבי השמים.** דרך משל:


[AS THE STARS OF HEAVEN.] This is a metaphor.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:11:1)


**פעמים** עם אלף וכן מאה הפך לשון קדר:


A THOUSAND TIMES. The word *pe’amim* (times) comes with *elef* (a thousand)⁴² precedes the word *pe’amim*. and with *me’ah* (a hundred).⁴³ (a hundred times). The reverse is the case in Arabic.⁴⁴

footnotes:
⁴² The word elef
⁴³ We also come across the phrase me’ah pe’amim
⁴⁴ The Arabic word for times comes before the number.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:12:1)


**טרחכם.** מגזרת היו עלי לטורח ופי' טרחכם להבין אנשים פתאים המצות:


YOUR CUMBRANCE. *Torchakhem* (your cumbrance) is related to the word *torach* (burden) in *They are a burden unto me* (Is. 1:14).⁴⁵ *Your cumbrance* refers to teaching the commandments to the simple.

footnotes:
⁴⁵ They come from the same root and have a similar meaning.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:12:2)


**ומשאכם.** שבקשו לחם ומים ובשר כמו לשום את משא כל העם הזה:


AND YOUR BURDEN. They demanded bread, water, and meat. Compare, *that Thou layest the burden of all this people upon me* (Num. 11:11).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:12:3)


**וריבכם.** זה עם זה:


AND YOUR STRIFE. One with another.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:13:1)


**הבו.** מלה זרה והיה הה״‎א ראוי להתנועע בשו״‎א ופת״‎ח:


GET YOU. The word *havu* (get) is irregular. Its *heh*⁴⁶. should have been vocalized with a *sheva na* and a *pattach*.⁴⁷. See I.E. on Gen. 11:4; 47:16, and the notes thereto (Vol. 1, pp. 139,140, 408).

footnotes:
⁴⁶ Which is vocalized with a kamatz
⁴⁷ Beneath the heh


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:13:2)


**וידועים.** שהם היו ידועים שיכירום הכל:


AND FULL OF KNOWLEDGE.⁴⁸ (literally, and known). Hence I.E.’s comment. Men who were known, men whom all recognized.

footnotes:
⁴⁸ Hebrew, vi-yedu’im



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:15:1)


**שרי אלפים.** פירשתיו:


CAPTAINS OF THOUSANDS. I have already explained this.⁴⁹

footnotes:
⁴⁹ See I.E. on Ex. 18:21 (Vol. 2, p. 365).



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:16:1)


**שמוע.** שם הפועל כמו זכור את יום השבת והוא שמוע שמעו או שמעו שמוע:


HEAR. *Shamo’a* (hear) is an infinitive. It is like the word *zakhor* (remember) in *Remember the sabbath day* (Ex. 20:8).⁵⁰ is an infinitive. *Shamo’a* is short for *shamo’a shimu* (you shall surely hear) or *shimu shamo’a* (you shall surely hear).⁵¹ literally means to hear. This makes no sense here. Thus I.E. believes that it is short for *shamo’a shimu* or *shimu shamo’a* (you shall hear), for it is good Hebrew style to combine a conjugated verb with an infinitive for purposes of emphasis.

footnotes:
⁵⁰ Zakhor
⁵¹ The word shamo’a


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:16:2)


**גרו.** כבר פירשתיו:


THE STRANGER THAT IS WITH HIM. I have already explained the meaning of the term *ger* (stranger).⁵²

⁵² See I.E. on Gen. 15:13 (Vol. 1, p. 173).



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:17:1)


**תכירו.** להכריח הדין למי שיכיר:


RESPECT PERSONS IN JUDGMENT.⁵³ To force judgment in favor of the person whom he [the judge] recognizes.

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:18:1)


**אשר תעשון.** הם החוקים והמשפטי' שהורום:


WHICH YE SHOULD DO. The reference is to the statutes and laws which he [Moses] taught them.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:19:1)


**את כל המדבר.** זה אחד עשר יום:


ALL THAT GREAT AND DREADFUL WILDERNESS. This is what the *eleven day’s journey* (v. 2) refers to.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:19:2)


וטעם **הר האמורי.** כי כן כתיב **בשעיר עד חרמה.** והטעם שנצא להר האמורי:


[THE HILL-COUNTRY OF THE AMORITES.⁵⁴] For Scripture states, [*And the Amorites, that dwell in that hill-country, came out against you…*] *and beat you down in Seir, even unto Hormah* (Deut. 1:44).⁵⁵ This⁵⁶. means that we should go out toward the hill country of the Amorites.⁵⁷ refers to the charge in verse 7 to march towards the Promised Land, namely, *turn you, and take your journey, and go to the hill-country of the Amorites…*

footnotes:
⁵⁴ Literally, the mountain of the Amorites.
⁵⁵ Deut. 1:2 notes that Kadesh-barnea is eleven days’ journey from Horeb via Mount Seir. However, our verse (according to I.E.’s interpretation) tells us that it is eleven days’ journey from Horeb to Kadesh-barnea via the mountain of the Amorites. Therefore I.E. points out that Deut. 1:44 indicates that mount Seir and the mountain of the Amorites are next to each other.
⁵⁶ The clause as the Lord our God commanded us
⁵⁷ As the Lord our God commanded us



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:21:1)


**רש.** במשקל שב מגזרת ירושה:


TAKE POSSESSION. *Resh* (take possession) is vocalized like the word *shev* (sit).⁵⁸. *Resh* comes from the root *yod, resh, shin. Shev* comes from the root *yod, shin, bet*. *Resh* is related to the word *yerushah* (possession).

footnotes:
⁵⁸ Both words are peh yods


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:21:2)


**תחת.** כמו ידל כבוד יעקב מפעלי הכפל:


DISMAYED. *Techat* (dismayed) is similar⁵⁹ to the word *yiddal* (shall be made thin) in *That the glory of Jacob shall be made thin* (Is. 17:4).⁶⁰ the *chirik* changes to *tzereh* because of the *chet*. *Techat* comes from a double root.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ In vocalization.
⁶⁰ Both words come from a double root and are similarly vocalized, except that in techat



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:22:1)


**ויחפרו.** כמו לחפור את כל הארץ כי החופר יחפש מה שיש בסתר:


THAT THEY MAY SEARCH THE LAND FOR US. *Veyachperu* (that they may search) is similar⁶¹ to *la-chepo*r (to search out) in *to search out all the land* (Josh. 2:3). One who digs (*chofer*) searches for that which is hidden.⁶² (that they may search) and *la-chepor* (to search out) come from the root *chet, peh, resh*, which means to dig. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁶¹ In meaning.
⁶² Ve-yachperu


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:22:2)


**אשר נבוא אליהן.** בתחלה:


BY WHICH WE MUST GO UP. At first.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶³ For strategic purposes (Weiser).



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:23:1)


ו**ייטב בעיני הדבר** בעבור שכלכם הסכמתם על זה:


AND THE THING PLEASED ME WELL. Because you all assented to this.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:25:1)


וי**ורידו** בעבור גובה העץ והאמת בעבור היותם בנגב:


AND BROUGHT IT DOWN. Moses said this because of the height of the tree.⁶⁴ is, and they brought the fruit down from tree and delivered it to us. Actually, however, he said this because they⁶⁵ were in the south.⁶⁶ means: and brought the fruit to us from the north.

footnotes:
⁶⁴ In other words, the meaning of and brought it down unto us
⁶⁵ Israel.
⁶⁶ South of the Land of Israel (See I.E. on Num. 13:17, Vol. 4, p. 102). In other words, and brought it down unto us



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:27:1)


**ותרגנו.** כמו ורוגנים ילמדו לקח והוא מבנין נפעל כמו אל תעצבו:


AND YE MURMURED. *Va-teragenu* (and ye murmured) is similar to *rogenim* (murmur) in *And they that murmur shall learn instruction* (Is. 29:24). *Teragenu* (murmured) is in the *nifal*. Compare, *al te’atzevu*⁶⁷. (be not grieved).

footnotes:
⁶⁷ Which is in the nifal



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:29:1)


**לא תערצון.** כמו לא תפחדון והוא מגזרת והוא מעריצכם והטעם שבר הלב בפחד:


DREAD NOT. *Lo ta’artzun* means dread not. *Ta’artzun* is related to the word *ma’aritzekhem* (your dread) in *and let him be your dread* (Is. 8:13). It means the breaking of the heart by fear.⁶⁸ comes from the root *ayin, resh, tzadi*, which means to break. Thus *lo ta’artzun* literally means do not be broken. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁶⁸ Ta’artzun



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:30:1)


**ילחם לכם.** בשבילכם:


HE SHALL FIGHT FOR YOU. *Lakhem* means for you.⁶⁹ literally means to you. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁶⁹ Lakhem



### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:33:1)


**לחנותכם.** איננו פעל יוצא כי לא מצאנו מזה הבנין כן:


TO PITCH YOUR TENTS. *La-chanotekhem* (to pitch your tents) is not a transitive verb,⁷⁰ for we do not find this form to be transitive.

footnotes:
⁷⁰ It does not mean to cause you to pitch your tents but to pitch your tents. Thus our verse should be rendered: to seek you out a place to pitch your tents, not to seek you out a place to cause you to pitch your tents [there].


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:33:2)


**לראותכם.** נפתח הלמ״‎ד ללמד על האות שהוא ה״‎א ה״‎א הדעת הנעדרת:


TO SHOW YOU. *La-re’otekhem* (to show you) is vocalized with a *pattach* to show that the letter *heh*, which is placed before the direct object⁷¹ is the *heh* of the *hifil*. (*le-hare’otekhem*) is missing.

footnotes:
⁷¹ This is probably a scribal error for the missing heh



### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:36:1)


**זולתי.** היו״‎ד נוסף כי נמצא בלא י':


SAVE. The *yod* in *zulati* (save) is superfluous, for the word is found without the *yod*.⁷². See II Kings 24:14.

footnotes:
⁷² Zulat


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:36:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:36:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:36:2)


**ולו אתן את הארץ אשר דרך בה.** היא חברון:


AND TO HIM WILL I GIVE THE LAND THAT HE HATH TRODDEN UPON. The reference is to Hebron.⁷³

footnotes:
⁷³ See I. E. on Num. 13:22 (Vol. 4, p. 103).



### Verse 37

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:37:1)


כבר ביארתי כי אפו הנו״‎ן מובלע בפ״‎א והיא בל' ארמי' נראית גם הוא מגזרת כי אנפת בי גם בי התאנף ה':


[ALSO THE LORD WAS ANGRY WITH ME.] I have already shown you⁷⁴ that the *nun* of the word *appo* (his anger) (Gen. 39:19) is swallowed by the *peh*.⁷⁵ is *alef, nun, peh*. In *appo* the *nun* drops out and a *dagesh* is placed in the *peh*. However, it is present in the Aramaic.⁷⁶ (Krinsky). Furthermore, *appo* is also related to the word *anafta*⁷⁷. (thou was angry) in *For though Thou was angry with me* (Is. 12:1) and *hitannaf* (was angry)⁷⁸. in *Also the Lord was angry with me*. Moses mentions Caleb first, then Joshua.⁷⁹ I have already explained why.⁸⁰

footnotes:
⁷⁴ See I.E.’s comments on Ex. 2:2 (Vol. 2, p. 32).
⁷⁵ The root of appo
⁷⁶ Cf. anpa, anpin
⁷⁷ From the root alef, nun, peh
⁷⁸ From the root alef, nun, peh
⁷⁹ Moses mentions Caleb before Joshua.
⁸⁰ Caleb is mentioned first because he quieted the people. See I.E. on Num. 14:24,30 (Vol. 4, pp. 111,113).



### Verse 38

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:38:1)


הזכיר כלב בתחלה כאשר פירשתי ואח״‎כ יהושע **העומד לפניך.** הוא משרתו:


WHO STANDETH BEFORE THEE. This is *his minister* (Ex. 24:13; Num. 11:28).⁸¹ one *who standeth before thee* means the one who stands before you to serve you (Krinsky).

footnotes:
⁸¹ The



### Verse 39

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:39:1)


**אשר לא ידעו היום.** באותו היום:


THAT THIS DAY HAVE NO KNOWLEDGE. That day.⁸² refers to the day that God meted out the punishment to the generation that exited Egypt, not to the day that Moses reviewed the Torah.

footnotes:
⁸² This day


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:39:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:39:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:39:2)


**טוב ורע.** על רובם דבר הכתוב:


OF GOOD OR EVIL. Scripture speaks of the majority.⁸³

footnotes:
⁸³ Of their children. According to I.E. a person comes to the knowledge of good and evil at the age of puberty. See I.E. on Gen. 3:6 (Vol. 1, p. 68).



### Verse 41

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:41:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:41:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:41:1)


**ותהינו.** ותאמרו הן ואל תתמה איך יבא פעל נגזר ממלה מדבקת כי כן מלת על גם פן שמצאנו אפונה כאשר פירשתיו וי״‎א שהוא מגזרת הין והטעם מדה והוא רחוק:


AND DEEMED IT A LIGHT THING. The meaning of *va-tahinu* (and deemed it a light thing) is, and you said behold (*hen*).⁸⁴), and will go up unto the place which the Lord hath promised (Num. 14:40). Do not ask how a verb⁸⁵. can be derived from a word that is connected to another word,⁸⁶ for we find the same with *al*.⁸⁷ (went up) is derived from *al* (on). The same is true, as I have explained,⁸⁸ of the word *pen* (lest), for we find the word *afunah* (I am distracted) (Ps. 88:16).⁸⁹ is derived from the word *pen*. Others say that *va-tahinu* is related to the word *hin*.⁹⁰ Its meaning is a measure.⁹¹ is, they measured; i.e., they were ready to measure the mountain, that is, to take the mountain (Sarim). However, this is far-fetched.

footnotes:
⁸⁴ That is, Behold, we are here (hinnennu
⁸⁵ The word va-tahinu
⁸⁶ That is, to an adverb or preposition. See Weiser.
⁸⁷ The word alah
⁸⁸ In his commentary on Ps. 88:16.
⁸⁹ Afunah
⁹⁰ A liquid measure. See Num. 28:5.
⁹¹ According to this interpretation the meaning of va-tahinu

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:44:1)


**ויצא האמורי.** הוא הכנעני כאשר פירשתי:


AND THE AMORITES…CAME OUT. The reference, as I have explained, is to the Canaanites.⁹². Hence I.E.’s comment. The Amorites were a branch of the Canaanite people. See I.E. on Gen. 15:16 (Vol. 1, p. 175).

**כאשר תעשינה הדבורים.** כי מי שיגע אל ביתם מיד ירדפו אחריו וינשכוהו:


AS BEES DO. For they immediately chase after and bite whoever touches their abode.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:44:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:44:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:44:3)


**ויכתו.** פירשתיו מגזרת וכתותי:


AND BEAT YOU DOWN. I have explained the meaning of *va-yakketu* (and beat you down).⁹³ It is related to the word *ve-khatoti* (and I will beat to pieces) (Ps. 99:24).⁹⁴.

⁹³ See I.E. on Num. 14:45 (Vol. 4, p. 115).

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:44:4

**עד חרמה.** מקום או שם הפעל:


UNTO HORMAH. Hormah is either the name of a place or an infinitive.⁹⁵

### Verse 46

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:46:1)


**ותשבו בקדש.** ברנע:


SO YE ABODE IN KADESH. Kadesh-barnea.⁹⁶

⁹⁶ There was a city in the land of Moab called Kadesh. Hence I.E.’s comment.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 1:46:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:46:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/1:46:2)


**ימים רבים כימים אשר ישבתם.** ברגול הארץ ואם טען טוען המרגלים ישבו לבדם הנה כן כמספר הימים אשר תרתם את הארץ:


MANY DAYS, ACCORDING UNTO THE DAYS THAT YE ABODE THERE. In spying out the land. If one should argue, the spies alone abode there.⁹⁷ [The answer is:] Scripture similarly reads, *After the number of days in which ye spied out the land* (Num. 14:34).⁹⁸

footnotes:
⁹⁷ That is, in Canaan. How then can Scripture say that Israel spent 40 days in Canaan?

## Chapter 2


### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:5:1)


**אל תתגרו בם.** כמו יגרה מדון כענין תנוע' ושבוש:


CONTEND NOT WITH THEM. The word *titgaru* (contend) is similar to the word *yigareh* (strife) in *stirreth up strife* (Prov. 28:25). It means to disturb and err.¹

footnotes:
¹ Strife is caused by disturbance and distance from that which is normal.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:6:1)


**אכל תשברו מאתם.** אם ירצו למכור וי״‎א כי המלה בתימ' והטעם כי אין להם צורך למאכל ולמשתה אלה השנים שעברו והעד על כן כי ה' אלהיך ברכך:


YE SHALL PURCHASE FOOD OF THEM. If they want to sell.² Some say that our clause is to be read as a question. It means that Israel had no need for food and drink in these past years. *For the Lord thy God hath blessed thee* (v. 7) is proof.

footnotes:
² We will not demand that they sell food to us.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:15:1

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:16:1)


**ויהי כאשר תמו.** דבק עם וידבר ה' אלי כמו ויהי ככלות משה לכתוב גם ויהי ביום דבר ה' ורבים כן והטעם ויהי כאשר תמו הם אמר השם שנעבור את גבול ער:


SO IT CAME TO PASS, WHEN ALL THE MEN OF WAR WERE CONSUMED. Our verse is connected to *that the Lord spoke unto me saying* (v. 17).⁹, they are in two sections. It is similar to *And it came to pass, when Moses had made an end of writing* (Deut. 31:24)¹⁰ and *And it came to pass on the day when the Lord spoke* (Ex. 6:28).¹¹, they do not form one section. See I.E. on Ex. 6:28 (Vol. 2, p. 141). There are many other such cases. It means,¹² And it came to pass when they¹³ were consumed, God said that we are about to pass over the border of Ar.

footnotes:
⁹ Even though, according to the massorah
¹⁰ This verse is connected to the verse which follows even though the next verse appears to belong in a new paragraph (Weiser).
¹¹ This verse is connected to the verse which follows even though, according to the massorah
¹² The meaning of verses 16 and 17.
¹³ All the men of war, those of the age of going to war among the generation that left Egypt.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:19:1)


**וקרבת מול בני עמון.** פירוש ובקרבך אליהם:


AND WHEN THOU COMEST NIGH OVER AGAINST THE CHILDREN OF AMMON. The meaning of *ve-karavta* is, when you come close to them.¹⁴ here has the meaning of when, not that of and.

¹⁴ In other words, the vav


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:19:2

**אל תצרם.** מפעלי הכפל:


HARASS THEM NOT. *Tetzurem* (harass) comes from a double root.¹⁵.

¹⁵ Tzadi, resh, resh


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:19:3)


**ואל תתגר בם.** מבנין התפעל מנחי הלמ״‎ד ה״‎א:


NOR CONTEND WITH THEM. *Titgar* (contend) is in the *hitpa’el*. Its root ends in a *heh*.¹⁶.

¹⁶ Its root is gimel, resh, heh



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:20:1

**זמזמים.** מלה מתוקנת על משפט לשון הקדש כמו האחשדרפנים:


ZAMZUMMIM. A foreign term which was Hebraized.¹⁷) is Hebrew. However, the basic word is not Hebrew. Compare, *ha-achashdarpenim* (the satraps) (Esth. 8:9).¹⁸

¹⁷ The ending (im

### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:21:1)


**וישמידם ה'.** ספר הכתוב כי השם השמיד אחרים מפני המואבים ונתן להם הארץ לירושה כהר שעיר לעשו:


BUT THE LORD DESTROYED THEM. Scripture relates that God destroyed a people on behalf of the Moabites¹⁹ means on behalf of the Moabites. The previous verse mentions the Amonites. Hence I.E. points out that *them* in our verse refers to the Moabites. and gave them the land as an inheritance, as he did with Mount Seir on behalf of Esau.

footnotes:
¹⁹ In other words, before them



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:23:1)


וטעם **עד עזה.** כי היא היתה לפלשתים וכבר היתה בין אברהם ובין אבימלך ברית אם תשקור לי ולניני ולנכדי והכתוב ספר כי העוים לקחו עזה:


[AS FAR AS GAZA.] Scripture reads *as far as Gaza* because Gaza belonged to the Philistines and there already was a covenant between Abraham and Abimelech, namely, *that thou wilt not deal falsely with me, nor with my son, nor with my son’s son* (Gen. 21:23).²⁰ Scripture relates that the Avvim took Gaza.²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ Hence Israel would not be permitted to take Gaza.
²¹ From the original inhabitants. It was with the Avvim (a Philistine tribe) that Abraham made the treaty recorded in Gen. 21:23. However, the Kaftorim (a different Philistine tribe) displaced the Avvim. The treaty which Abraham made did not apply to this tribe. See Rashi. Israel was henceforth permitted to take Gaza.

**החל רש.** מירוש':


BEGIN TO POSSESS IT. The word *rash* (possess) comes from the word *yerushah* (possession).²². There is no *yod* in *rash*. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
²² Its root is yod, resh, shin

OUT OF THE WILDERNESS OF KEDEMOTH. I believe that *the wilderness of Kedemoth* is the same as the place called *the wilderness to Mattanah* (Num. 21:18).

THOU SHALT SELL ME FOOD FOR MONEY, THAT I MAY EAT. If I need to.²⁴

### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/2:29:1)


**כאשר עשו לי בני עשו.** יש מפרשים כי פירושו על אוכל בכסף תשבירני א״‎כ הוא מה יעשו עם יושבי ער שהם מואבים והכתוב אמר על דבר אשר לא קדמו אתכם והוצרכו לפירוש זה בעבור שאמר מלך אדום לא תעבור בי ולפי דעתי שפירושו על טעם בדרך בדרך נלך כאשר עשו לי בני עשו שסבבו הר שעיר בדרך בדרך וכן כתוב אתם עוברים בגבול אחיכם בני עשו היושבים בשעיר רק המלך שהוא מלך אדום לא עזבם שיעברו דרך מדינתו כי משם היה מקום ארץ כנען קרוב ולכן עברו על ער כי כן כתוב ורבים אמרו הם לא קדמו רק ישראל קנו מהם:


AS THE CHILDREN OF ESAU…AND THE MOABITES THAT DWELL IN AR DID UNTO ME. Some say that *did unto me* refers to *thou shalt sell me food for money*.²⁵ If this is so, then what will they do with the inhabitants of Ar who were Moabites,²⁶ about whom Scripture writes, *because they met you not with bread and with water in the way* (Deut. 23:5)?²⁷ They were compelled to offer this interpretation²⁸ refers to *thou shalt sell me food for money*. because the king of the Edomites said, *Thou shalt not pass through me* (Ex. 20:18). I believe that Israel’s request refers to, *I will go along by the highway* (v. 27)²⁹ as the children of Esau did, for Israel went around Mount Seir by the highway. Indeed, Scripture reads, *Ye are to pass through the border of your brethren the children of Esau, that dwell in Seir* (v. 4). However, the king, that is, the king of Edom, did not allow them to pass through his country, which was near Canaan. Israel therefore passed by Ar, as Scripture records (v. 18). Others say that they³⁰ did not meet the Israelites³¹ but the Israelites bought³² from them.

footnotes:
²⁵ For the children of Esau did not permit the Israelites to pass through their lands.
²⁶ Verse 18.
²⁷ The meaning of which is, they did not sell you bread or water.
²⁸ That did unto me
²⁹ Israel asked Sihon to permit them to go on the highway.
³⁰ The Ammonites and Moabites.
³¹ With bread and water.
³² Bread and water.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 2:31:1

I HAVE BEGUN. The *heh* of *hachilloti* (I have begun), which is the *heh* of the form,³³ form. is vocalized with a *pattach*³⁴. because of the guttural.³⁵. ### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:3:1)


**השאיר לו.** כל אחד מישראל והוא פעל עבר:


NONE WAS LEFT TO HIM.¹ (he [Israel] left him). Each of the Israelites [left none to him].² (none was left) is in the singular. Thus our verse literally reads, and we smote him until he left him with no one remaining. I.E. renders this, and we smote him until each one of the Israelites left him no one standing. *Hishir* is in the perfect.

footnotes:
¹ Hebrew, hishir
² Hishir



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:4:1)


**חבל ארגוב.** דברי המתרגם ארמית ידועים וי״‎א שהוא שם אדם היה בחלקו מירושת אביו זה החבל וי״‎א כי האל״‎ף נוסף והוא מגזרת ורגבים ידובקו בדרך רחוקה:


THE REGION OF ARGOB. The words of the Aramaic translator³ are known.⁴ as *tarchona*, i.e., Trachonites. Trachonites is a district on the Golan heights. Some say that Argob is the name of a person who possessed this region as an inheritance from his father. Some go far afield and say that the *alef* of Argob is superfluous and that Argob is related to the word *regavim* (clods) in *And the clods cleave fast together* (Job 38:38).⁵ Argob as the district of clods.

footnotes:
³ Onkelos.
⁴ Onkelos renders chevel
⁵ They render chevel



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:11:1)


**באמת איש.** באמת כל אדם והנה הוא כפול ולא יתכן להיות באמתו כי מה בא הכתוב ללמד ועוד שלא יהיה בצלם אדם כלל:


AFTER THE CUBIT OF A MAN. After the cubit of a normal person. Observe, Og was double in size.⁶ It is unlikely that the Torah is speaking of Og’s cubit,⁷ for if this were so, then what is Scripture trying to teach?⁸ Furthermore, he would not have the image of a person at all.⁹

footnotes:
⁶ Og was double the size of a normal person. I Sam. 17:4 tells us that Goliath’s height was six cubits and a span. This is about twice the height of a normal person. Goliath was of the Rephaim (I Chron. 20: 4-8), as was Og. Hence I.E.’s reasoning (Weiser).
⁷ According to Rashi, Scripture speaks of Og’s cubit when it describes his bed.
⁸ Since we do not know Og’s span, Scripture’s statement that Og’s bed was nine cubits long and four cubits wide is meaningless.
⁹ If we assume that Og’s span was twice that of a normal person, then his bed indicates that Og’s dimensions were way beyond that of a human being. I.E. did not believe that such giants existed.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:16:1

THE MIDDLE OF THE VALLEY FOR A BORDER.¹² The word *u-gevul* (for a border)¹³ is be rendered, and its border.¹⁴ So too *u-gevul* (the border thereof) in *the Jordan being the border thereof* (v. 17).¹⁵

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:18:1)


**ואצו אתכם.** עם בני ראובן וגד ידבר בעבור שהיו בכלל ישראל אמר אתכם ולא אותם והפך זה ויצו משה את הלוים לקוח את ספר התורה כי אנכי ידעתי את מריך איננו מדבר עם הלוים רק עם כל ישראל ובעבור היות הלוים בכלל דבר אליהם:


AND I COMMANDED YOU. Moses speaks to the children of Gad and Reuben.¹⁶ He uses the term you, not them,¹⁷ because they are included among the Israelites. The reverse is true with, *that Moses commanded the Levites…Take this book of the law…For I know thy rebellion* (Deut. 31:25-27).¹⁸ Moses did not address the Levites.¹⁹ On the contrary, he spoke to all of Israel.²⁰ He addressed the Levites²¹ because they are included in all of Israel.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Even though he was addressing all the Israelites.
¹⁷ Since Moses spoke to Israel, he should have used the term them when referring to Reuben and Gad.
¹⁸ Here Moses is addressing the Levites, but part of what he says is directed to Israel.
¹⁹ When he said, For I know thy rebellion…
²⁰ When he said, For I know thy rebellion…
²¹ The point is that some of what Moses said when he spoke to the Levites was directed specifically at the Levites, but the rest was directed to all of Israel. Moses could address Israel while speaking to the Levites because the Levites are part of Israel.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:23:1)


**ואתחנן.** הטעם וכבר התחננתי כי כאשר הזכיר יהושע ואמר צויתיו בעבור כי אינני עובר עמכם כי התחננתי לשם לעבור עמכם בעבור כי לגדולת השם אין חקר וחדשים לבקרים רבה אמונתך:


The meaning of *va-etchannan* (and I besought) is, *and I had already besought*.²² When Moses mentioned Joshua and said, *And I commanded Joshua*, etc. (v. 21) because I am not passing over with you, he had already asked the Lord to be allowed to pass over with the Israelites; *for God’s greatness is unsearchable* (Ps. 145:3) and *They are new every morning; Great is Thy faithfulness* (Lam. 3:23).²³

footnotes:
²² Before charging Joshua. He would not beseech God to pass over the Jordan after telling Joshua that he would lead Israel into Canaan.
²³ Hence Moses thought that God might relent and permit him to enter Canaan.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:1)


**אתה החלות.** אמר חכם מהחכמים אתה החלות להראות את גדלך בשמים ובארץ במעשי' שנוהג העולם בם:


THOU HAST BEGUN. The wisest of the wise²⁴ said: *Thou hast begun to show Thy servant Thy greatness*, in heaven and on earth,²⁵ i.e., the manner in which You conduct the world.

footnotes:
²⁴ Moses.
²⁵ I.E.’s paraphrase of the second part of our verse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:2)


**ואת ידך החזקה.** באותות שנעשו על ידו עד שנודע בעולם כי אין אל כי אם הוא לבדו והטעם תקיף **בשמים** ששם המלאכים והצבא **ובארץ** ששם החיות ובני אדם:


AND THY STRONG HAND. Via the signs performed through Moses so that the world learned that there is no God but the Lord. The meaning of *El*²⁶ (God) is mighty. IN HEAVEN. Wherein are the angels and the hosts.²⁷ OR ON EARTH. Wherein are the beasts and human beings.

footnotes:
²⁶ The term for God in our verse.
²⁷ The stars.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:3)


**כמעשיך.** שהם כולם בחכמה:


ACCORDING TO THY WORKS. All of which are governed by wisdom.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:24:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:24:4)


**וכגבורתך.** ביכולת וטעם זו הפרשה לחבב את ארץ ישראל ואם הארץ תהיה חביבה ישמרו מצות השם שלא יגלו ממנה:


MIGHTY. In ability.²⁸ This section was written to instill a love of the Land of Israel. If they love the land, they will observe God’s commandments so that they will not be exiled from the land.

footnotes:
²⁸ God has the might to execute His will.



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:27:1)


**עלה ראש הפסגה.** הוא הגיא אשר בשדה מואב ראש הפסגה:


GET THEE UP INTO THE TOP OF PISGAH. The reference is to the valley that is in the field of Moab, the top of Pisgah.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁹ See I.E. on Num. 21:18 (Vol. 4, p. 172).



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 3:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/3:29:1)


**ונשב בגיא.** טעמו וכבר ישבנו בגיא הזה שהוא ראש הפסגה כי משם נסעו אל ערבות מואב והגיא הוא בהר העברים כי כן מפורש ועתה החל לדבר על המצות בעבור שהיו מול בית פעור החל לדבר בעבודת כוכבים:


SO WE ABODE IN THE VALLEY. The meaning is, and we had already stayed in this valley, which is the top of Pisgah,³⁰ for from there³¹ they journeyed to the plains of Moab.³² The valley is in the mountains of Abarim, for the latter is clearly stated.³³ (Num. 33:48). Moses now commenced to speak about the commandments. He began with idol worship because they were near Bethpeor.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁰ Before coming to the plains of Maob, where they presently were.
³¹ Mount Abiram.
³² Where they now were.
³³ And they journeyed from the mountains of Abarim, and pitched in the plains of Moab
³⁴ Where Israel worshipped Baal of Peor. See Num. 25:3.



## Chapter 4


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:1:1)


**מלמד אתכם לעשות.** כי עיקר הלמוד לעשות המצות:


WHICH I TEACH YOU, TO DO THEM. The main purpose of study is the observance of the commandments.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:1:2)


**למעז תחיון.** כי עובדי פעור נשמדו:


THAT YE MAY LIVE. For those who worshipped *pe’or* were destroyed.¹

footnotes:
¹ Verse 3. For the incident of Baal-peor, see Num. 25:1-9.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:1:3)


**וירשתם.** בעבור שנח ונעלם היו״‎ד בשבר הוי״‎ו נשבר עי״‎ן הפעל ואפס על משקלו:


AND POSSESS. The middle root letter of the verb *vi-yerishtem* (and possess)². The root of *vi-yerishtem* is *yod, resh, shin*. is vocalized with a *chirik* because the *yod* is silenced and made invisible by the *chirik* beneath the *vav*.³. Hence I.E.’s comment. There is no other word that is similarly vocalized.

footnotes:
² The resh
³ According to the rules of Hebrew grammar the word should be vocalized vi-yerashtem



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:2:1)


**לא תוספו.** מדעתכם ותחשבו כי היא עבודה לשם ג״‎כ לא תגרעו ואשר אמרתי למען תחיו אין צורך לעדים כי עיניכם הרואות:


YE SHALL NOT ADD. On your own⁴ and think that it is in the service of God. You shall similarly not diminish.⁵ Now, my⁶ statement *that ye may live* does not require witnesses, for *Your eyes have seen…* (v. 3).⁷

footnotes:
⁴ According to Rabbi Judah Ha-Levi, the Chief Bet Din may add laws to the Judaic code. The individual may not. I.E. seems to take the same position.
⁵ From God’s commandments.
⁶ Moses.
⁷ That God destroyed those who worshipped Baal-peor, but those who remained faithful to the Lord are alive today (v. 4).



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:3:1)


**בבעל פעור.** בעבור בעל פעור ורבים כן ומי שלא עבד בעל פעור הנו חי:


IN BAAL-PEOR. *Be-va’al pe’or* (in Baal-peor) means because of Baal-peor.⁸ here has the meaning of because rather than in. I.E. renders our clause as: your eyes have seen what the Lord did because of Baal-peor. There are many such instances.⁹ here has the meaning of because. Whoever did not worship Baal-peor is alive.

footnotes:
⁸ The bet
⁹ Where the bet



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:5:1)


וטעם **למדתי אתכם.** אחר שאמר אשר אנכי מלמד אתכם להוסיף כאשר צוני ה' כי המצות הם מצותיו שתעשו בארץ שיתן לכם השם לירושה ועוד כי יש לכם תפארת במצותיו על כל הגוים והטעם כי עיקר כל המצות יבינם המשכיל וידע למה נתנו:


[I HAVE TAUGHT YOU.] Moses states, *I have taught you*, after saying *which I teach you* (v. 1), because he wants to add, *even as the Lord my God commanded me*. The meaning of our verse is, the precepts which I taught you are God’s commandments, which you should observe in the land that God will give you for a possession. Furthermore, the commandments are your glory over the nations. 6. YOUR WISDOM AND YOUR UNDERSTANDING IN THE SIGHT OF THE PEOPLES. Because an intelligent person can understand the basis of all the commandments. An intelligent person can ascertain the reason why they were given.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:7:1)


**כי מי גוי גדול.** שיש לו אלהים שיהיה קרוב אליו שיענהו תמיד בכל אשר יבקש ממנו בחכמה גם אין גוי גדול אשר לו חקים ומשפטים צדיקים בשקול הדעת כמו ישראל:


FOR WHAT GREAT NATION IS THERE. That has God so near in that He always answers him in all that he intelligently¹⁰. I.E. adds *be-chokhmah* to indicate that God responds only to intelligent requests. The word *be-chokhmah* is not found in *Vat. Ebr.* 28. requests of Him. Furthermore, unlike Israel there is no great nation that has statutes and ordinances that reason tells us are righteous.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Hebrew, be-chokhmah


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:7:2)


וטעם **גוי גדול.** בעבור חכמי לב יראי ה':


A great nation means, great in intelligent people who fear the Lord.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:9:1)


**רק השמר לך.** הטעם אם תשכח כל דבר אל תשכח יום אשר עמדת בסיני:


ONLY TAKE HEED TO THYSELF. Its meaning is, should you forget everything, do not forget the day that you stood at Sinai.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:9:2)


**אשר ראו עיניך.** זהו וכל העם רואים את הקולות:


WHICH THINE EYES SAW. The reference is to *And all the people perceived the thunderings, and the lightnings* (Ex. 20:15).



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:10:1)


וטעם **כל הימים אשר הם חיים על האדמה.** כי השם ידע שלא יוכלו לעשות מצותיו כראוי והם בארצות המושלים בהם:


[ALL THE DAYS THAT THEY LIVE UPON THE EARTH.] Scripture says this because God knows that they can not properly observe His commandments when they are in countries where others rule over them.¹¹) as referring to the Land of Israel, as in Deut. 11:21.
