¹¹ I.E. explains earth (adamah



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:11:1)


**לב השמים.** באמצע כנגד הרואים וכן בלב ים:


UNTO THE HEART OF HEAVEN. The middle of heaven.¹² Scripture speaks of those who look up. *The heart of the sea* (Ex. 15:8) is similar.¹³

footnotes:
¹² The heart lies in the middle of the body. Hence heart is used in the sense of middle. See I.E. on Ex. 15:8 (Vol. 2, p. 299). When a person looks straight up, it appears to him that he is staring at the middle of the sky.
¹³ It means the middle of the sea. See above note.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:12:1)


**זולתי קול.** ששמעתם. כי הקול איננו נראה בעין וכבר פירשתי וכל העם רואים את הקולות:


ONLY A VOICE. Which you heard,¹⁴ is short for you only heard a voice. for the voice is not visible to the eye.¹⁵ I have already explained the meaning of *And all the people saw the sounds*¹⁶ (Ex. 20:15).¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁴ Only a voice
¹⁵ Hence this interpretation.
¹⁶ Translated literally.
¹⁷ Scripture interchanges the way the senses are referred to because they are all perceived in one central place. See I.E. on Gen. 42:1 (Vol. 1, p. 386). Hence Scripture uses the term saw for heard.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:14:1)


וטעם **ואותי צוה ה'.** כי בסיני צוה השם למשה את כל החקים והמשפטים רק הגידם לישראל בשנה השנית. ופעם אחרת ובשנה הארבעים. ואחר שלא ראיתם כל תמונ' השמרו לכם פן תשחיתון מה שראיתם והטעם האמת או דרכיכם:


[COMMANDED ME AT THAT TIME.] For God told Moses all the statutes and ordinances at Sinai. However, Moses related them to Israel in the second year¹⁸ and again in the fortieth year.¹⁹ [Moses tells the Israelites:] Since *you saw no form* (v. 12) *take ye therefore good heed unto yourselves* (v. 15) lest you corrapt what you saw (v. 16), that is, lest you corrupt the truth²⁰ or your ways.²¹

footnotes:
¹⁸ That is, from the time of the revelation at Sinai until they sent out the spies to Canaan in the second year.
¹⁹ Deut. 1:5.
²⁰ To claim that you saw an image.
²¹ You make an image.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:16:1)


**פסל.** דבר נקצב כמו ויפסלו בוני שלמה:


A GRAVEN IMAGE. The word *pesel* (graven image) refers to an object that has been fashioned.²² (literally, cut). Compare, *va-yifselu* (did fashion) in *And Solomon’s builders…did fashion them* (I Kings 5:32).

footnotes:
²² Hebrew, niktzav


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:16:2)


**זכר או נקבה.** בגוים ידועים. גם יש גוים סוגדים ועובדים כל בהמה שיפגעו בתחל' יומו וי״‎א להוריד כח עליונים כצורת עקרב לנשוך והצורות הארבעים ושבעה הנשארות:


MALE OR FEMALE. Worshipped by some well-known nations. There are also nations that bow down and worship any beast that they meet at the beginning of their day in order to bring down a power from on high; for example, they worship the scorpion²³ if they want someone bitten. The same applies to the remaining forty-seven forms.²⁴

footnotes:
²³ Which has the form of the constellation Scorpio.
²⁴ There are 48 forms in heaven. Since I.E. already mentioned Scorpio, there are 47 remaining forms.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:19:1)


**השמש והירח.** שהם המאורות הגדולים ואחר שאמר ואת הככבים באר בשם כלל כל צבא השמים ככבי לכת שהם ככבי אור וכל המזלות והצורות:


THE SUN AND THE MOON. Which are the great lights.²⁵ After mentioning the stars, Scripture goes on to use a general term, namely, *even all the host of heaven. All the hosts of heaven* refers to the planets,²⁶ which are light-giving stars, and all the constellations and forms.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁵ Gen. 1:16.
²⁶ Literally, moving stars.
²⁷ Which appear in heaven (Weiser).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:19:2)


**ונדחת.** כאדם שידחה ויפול:


THOU BE DRAWN AWAY.²⁸ (literally, and you will slip). Hence I.E.’s comment. You will, as it were, slip and fall.

footnotes:
²⁸ Hebrew, ve-nidachta


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:19:3)


**אשר חלק.** דבר מנוסה הוא כי יש לכל עם ועם ככב ידוע ומזל וכן יש מזל לכל עיר ועיר והשם שם לישראל מעל' גדול' להיות השם יועצם ולא ככב להם והנה ישראל נחלת השם:


WHICH THE LORD THY GOD HATH ALLOTTED. It has been empirically established that each and every nation has a specific star and constellation. Every city similarly has its own constellation. God raised Israel to a very high level in that God, and not any star, is Israel’s guide.²⁹ Israel is thus God’s portion.

footnotes:
²⁹ Literally, advisor.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:21:1)


וטעם **וה' התאנף בי.** בעבור שאמר ויוצא אתכם להיות לו לעם נחלה בארץ שתעבדוהו רק אני לא אראה בטובה הזאת:


[NOW THE LORD WAS ANGERED WITH ME.] Moses says this because he had stated earlier, *But you hath the Lord taken and brought forth…to be unto Him a people of inheritance* (v. 20) in the land, so that you will serve Him. However, I will not share in this good.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:21:2)


**על דבריכם.** אתם סבבתם שלא אכנס לארץ כאשר פירשתי:


FOR YOUR SAKES. You were the cause of my not entering the land. I have already explained this.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ How Israel was the cause of Moses’ not entering the Land of Israel. See I.E. on Num. 20:8 (Vol. 4, p. 159).



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:23:1)


**השמרו לכם ועשיתם פסל.** וכבר אמר כזה והטעם להוסיף אש אוכלה על כן אנכי מצוך:


TAKE HEED UNTO YOURSELVES…AND MAKE YOU A GRAVEN IMAGE. Moses had previously said something to this effect.³¹ He repeated himself because he wanted to add, *For the Lord thy God is a devouring fire* (v. 24). Hence³² I command you to [*take heed unto yourselves*…].

footnotes:
³¹ Verses 14 and 15.
³² Moses repeats himself because he wants to add: Since the Lord Your God is a devouring fire, take heed to yourself and do not worship idols.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:23:2)


**תמונת כל.** כל דבר:


EVEN THE LIKENESS OF ANY THING. *Temunat kol*³³ is to be rendered as the likeness of anything.³⁴ is short for *kol davar* (all things, i.e., any thing).

footnotes:
³³ Literally, the likeness of all.
³⁴ In other words, kol


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:23:3)


**אשר צוך.** כאשר צוך או מלת צוך שלא לעשות כמו ועל העבים אצוה ויש אומר על צורת הככבים וזה רחוק בעיני:


WHICH THE LORD THY GOD HATH FORBIDDEN THEE. *Asher tzivvekha* (hath forbidden thee) is to be rendered as He commanded thee.³⁵ (hath forbidden thee) literally reads, which He has commanded you. However, it cannot have this meaning here for then our verse would read: and make you a graven image, even the likeness of anything which the Lord your God has commanded you. Hence I.E. comments that *asher tzivvekha* is to be read, *ka’asher tzivvekha*. In other words, it is to be interpreted as, which the Lord your God had commanded. Or *tzivvekha* may be interpreted: which He commanded you not to make.³⁶ has the meaning of prohibited. Compare, *I will also command* (atzavveh) *the clouds* (from raining rain upon it)³⁷ (Is. 5:6).³⁸ Some say that the reference³⁹ (likeness of anything) refers to the making of an image of the stars. is to the likeness of the stars.⁴⁰ (even any manner of likeness, of anything that is in heaven above). However, this appears to me to be far-fetched.

footnotes:
³⁵ Asher tzivvekha
³⁶ In other words, commanded
³⁷ Translated literally.
³⁸ Here too the word command has the meaning of prohibit.
³⁹ That is, temunat kol
⁴⁰ For Deut. 5:8 speaks of kol temunah asher ba-shamayim mi-ma’al



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:24:1)


**אש אוכלה הוא.** בלי כ״‎ף הדמות והטעם כאש אוכלה הוא:


A DEVOURING FIRE. There is no *kaf* with the meaning of like prefixed to the word *esh* (fire). The meaning of *esh okhlah hu* (a devouring fire) is, like a devouring fire.⁴¹ (a devouring fire) should be interpreted as if written, *ke-esh okhlah hu* (like a devouring fire). I.E. notes this lest anyone take our text to imply any corporeality in God.

footnotes:
⁴¹ In other words, esh okhlah hu


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:24:2)


**אל קנא.** לעבודת כוכבים וקנאתו תאכל כאש שלא תעזוב שורש וענף:


A JEALOUS GOD. With regard to idol worship. His jealousy devours like fire, which does not leave root or branch.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:25:1)


**כי תוליד בנים.** אמר אל קנא ואחר כן באר מה יקרה להם אם יעשו פסל:


WHEN THOU SHALT BEGET CHILDREN. Moses first tells the Israelites that the Lord God is a jealous God. He then tells them what will happen to them if they make an image.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:25:2)


**ועשיתם הרע.** בשאר המצות כמו לרצוח ולנאוף:


AND YOU SHALL DEAL CORRUPTLY. With regard to the rest of the commandments such as to murder or to commit adultery.



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:26:1)


**העידותי בכם היום.** י״‎א כי טעם עדות שמים וארץ המלאכים ובני אדם ואחרים אמרו על הגשם כטעם את שמיכם כברזל ואת ארצכם כנחושה והנכון בעיני להיותם עדים שיראום תמיד הם ובניהם כי הם עומדים כטעם כימי השמים על הארץ וכן שמעו הרים את ריב ה'. וכן האבן הזאת תהיה בנו לעדה ושמא יטעון טוען כי שמים כעשן נמלחו וחביריו הטוען ער ולבו ישן:


I CALL HEAVEN AND EARTH TO WITNESS AGAINST YOU THIS DAY. Some say the witness of heaven and earth refers to the angels and the human beings.⁴² means I call the angels in heaven and the inhabitants of earth to witness against you. Others say that the reference is to the rain, as in *and I will make your heaven as iron, and your earth as brass*⁴³ (Lev. 26:19). However, it appears to me that heaven and earth are witnesses, and the Israelites and their children will always see them, for they are everlasting, as in *as the days of the heavens above the earth*⁴⁴ means eternally. (Deut. 11:21). *Hear, O ye mountains, the Lord’s controversy* (Micah 6:2); and *Behold, this stone shall be a witness against us* (Josh. 24:27) are similar.⁴⁵ Should one dispute this on the basis of *For the heavens shall vanish like smoke*⁴⁶ (Is. 51:6) and the like,⁴⁷ [then I respond] the one who makes this argument is awake but his heart sleeps.⁴⁸

footnotes:
⁴² In other words, I call heaven and earth to witness against you
⁴³ As a result of lack of rain.
⁴⁴ As the days of the heavens above the earth
⁴⁵ To heaven and earth bearing witness.
⁴⁶ Which implies that the heavens are not eternal.
⁴⁷ Other similar verses.
⁴⁸ He does not understand the true meaning of the verse, for it is not to be taken literally.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:26:2)


**תאבדון.** כמו תלמדון בעבור שאיננו סמוך:


YE SHALL SOON UTTERLY PERISH. The word *tovedun* (ye shall utterly perish)⁴⁹. Hence I.E.’s comment. is similar to *tilmedun*⁵⁰ (v. 10). *Tilmedun* is not found in Scripture. See Weiser. (that you may teach).⁵¹. The word⁵². is so vocalized⁵³ rather than a *sheva* beneath the *bet* as in *yilmedun*. because it is not in the construct.⁵⁴. Compare *te’abbedun* (ye shall surely destroy) (Deut. 12:2) (Krinsky).

footnotes:
⁴⁹ The usual form of the word is tovedu
⁵⁰ The correct reading is yilmedun
⁵¹ The usual form of the word is yilmedu
⁵² Tovedun
⁵³ With a tzere
⁵⁴ If the word was closely connected to that which follows then it would have been vocalized with a sheva



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:27:1)


**מתי מספר.** פירשתיו:


FEW IN NUMBER. I have already explained the meaning of *mete* (few).⁵⁵ (few) means men of. Anything that can be numbered is deemed a few. See I.E. on Gen. 34:30 (Vol. 1, p. 330).

footnotes:
⁵⁵ Mete



### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:28:1)


**יראון וישמעון.** הזכיר הארבעה הרגשו' ואין צורך להזכיר החמישי' כי היא עבה מכולם ועוד כי היא שורש חיי הגוף ואל תתמה על מלת ובקשתם ומצאת כי ידבר לרבים ולגוי אחד או לאדם אחד:


WHICH NEITHER SEE, NOR HEAR. Scripture mentions the four senses.⁵⁶ It is unnecessary to mention the fifth⁵⁷ because it is thicker⁵⁸ than the others.⁵⁹ Furthermore, it is the root of the life of the body.⁶⁰ Do not be amazed at the terms *u-vikashtem* (but…ye will seek) and *u-matzata* (and thou shalt find)⁶¹ (but…ye will seek) is plural. *U-matzata* (and thou shalt find) is singular. We would expect Scripture to be consistent. for Scripture speaks to many, to an entire nation, or to one person.⁶²

footnotes:
⁵⁶ Seeing, hearing, tasting, and smelling.
⁵⁷ The sense of touch.
⁵⁸ Coarser.
⁵⁹ The other senses are of a higher order. In other words, Scripture speaks only of the specialized senses.
⁶⁰ It is spread all over the body. All parts of the body are sensitive to touch.
⁶¹ U-vikashtem
⁶² Hence it employs both the singular and the plural.



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:29:1)


**בכל לבבך.** עוד אפרשנו:


WITH ALL THY HEART. I will explain this later.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶³ The observance of the commandments is contingent on the perfection of the heart. See I.E. on Deut. 30:10.



### Verse 31

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:31:1)


**ירפך.** הפך יחזקך:


HE WILL NOT FAIL THEE. *Yarpekha* (fail thee) is the opposite of strengthen thee.⁶⁴ (fail thee) means will cause you to weaken.

footnotes:
⁶⁴ Thus yarpekha



### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:1)


**כי שאל נא.** **לימים.** שאל עתה:


FOR ASK NOW OF THE DAYS PAST. *She’al na* means ask now.⁶⁵ means now. See I.E. on Gen. 12:11 (Vol 1, p. 154).

footnotes:
⁶⁵ In other words, na


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:2)


**למן היום.** הלמ״‎ד נוסף וכן רבים או היא דרך צחות:


SINCE THE DAY. The *lamed* of *le-min* (since) is superfluous.⁶⁶ literally means to-from. Hence I.E.’s comment that *le-min* is to be interpreted as if written from (or since). There are many such examples.⁶⁷ On the other hand, it may be there for literary purposes.⁶⁸, and other *lameds* in our verse.

footnotes:
⁶⁶ Le-min
⁶⁷ Of Scripture’s use of superfluous letters. See Vol. 1, p. XVII.
⁶⁸ Perhaps to rhyme with le-yamim, le-miktzeh


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:32:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:3)


**ולמקצה השמים.** רמז לאויר כאשר פירשתי שיש לו קצוות:


AND FROM THE ONE END OF HEAVEN. Heaven, as I have explained, refers to the air,⁶⁹ which has ends.⁷⁰ rather than *ketzot ha-shemesh*.

footnotes:
⁶⁹ The firmament.
⁷⁰ At the point where the firmament appears to merge with the sea. See I.E. on Gen. 1:6 and the notes thereto (Vol. 1, p. 34). From this it appears that the correct reading in I.E. on Gen. 1:6, is, as was stated, ketzot ha-shamayim


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:32:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:4)


**הנהיה.** בימיו:


WHETHER THERE HATH BEEN. In his days.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:32:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:32:5)


**או הנשמע כמוהו.** ומה הוא ששמע עם:


OR HATH BEEN HEARD LIKE IT. The reference being to *Did ever a people hear…*(v. 33).



### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:1)


**או הנסה אלהים.** י״‎א שהוא לשון חול וחלילה חלילה רק לשון קדושה:


OR HATH GOD ASSAYED. Some say that *Elohim* (God) does not refer to the Lord.⁷¹ Heaven forbid. It most certainly refers to God.

footnotes:
⁷¹ It refers to any god.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:2)


**הנסה.** כדרך בני אדם כדי שיבינו השומעים:


ASSAYED. Scripture employs human language so that those who hear will understand.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:3)


**במסות.** שם כלל או טעמו במסות שהיו באותות כמו ויעש האותות לעיני העם:


BY TRIALS. This is a general term.⁷² Or it refers to trials done via signs.⁷³ Compare, *and did the signs in the sight of the people* (Ex. 4:30).

footnotes:
⁷² It refers to all sorts of trials.
⁷³ In other words, it refers to signs which were brought by God in bringing the children of Israel out of Egypt.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:4)


**ובמופתים.** עשר מכות וכתיב ומשה ואהרן עשו:


AND BY WONDERS. The ten plagues. Scripture states, *and Moses and Aaron did all these wonders* (Ex. 11:10).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:5)


**ובמלחמה.** יתכן על הריגת הבכורים ושפטים באלהי מצרים:


AND BY WAR. The reference is most probably to the killing of the first-born and to the punishments meted out to the gods of Egypt.⁷⁴

footnotes:
⁷⁴ See I.E. on Ex. 12:12.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:6)


**וביד חזקה.** שיצאו ישראל ביד רמה ובזרוע נטויה על טעם בעמוד אש וענן:


AND BY A MIGHTY HAND. The reference is to Israel’s departure by high hand.⁷⁵ The outstretched arm refers to the pillar of fire and the pillar of cloud.

footnotes:
⁷⁵ Confidently and fearlessly. See Ex. 14:8.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:7)


**ובמוראים גדולים.** טביעת פרעה וחילו עם בקיעת הים לישראל:


AND BY GREAT TERRORS. The drowning of Pharaoh and his army along with the splitting of the sea on behalf of Israel.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:34:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:34:8)


**במצרים.** באנשי מצרים:


IN EGYPT. To the people of Egypt.



### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:35:1)


**הראת.** מבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו והטעם כי כל אלה הראך האלהים עד אשר תדע כי הוא האלהים לבדו:


IT WAS SHOWN. *Horeta* (it was shown) is a *hofal*. The meaning of our clause is, God showed you all this so that you will know that He alone is God.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:35:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:35:2)


וטעם **אין עוד.** כאשר רמזתי בפירוש קהלת בפסוק וגבוהים עליהם על כן מלת אין עוד:


[THERE IS NONE ELSE.] This is to be understood according to the hint which I gave in my commentary on Koheleth on the verse that reads, *for one higher than the high* (Eccles. 5:7).⁷⁶ Hence Scripture reads, *There is none else*.⁷⁷

footnotes:
⁷⁶ I.E. there explains that the verse alludes to a hierarchy of 55 powers in heaven.
⁷⁷ That is, God is the ultimate power.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:35:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:35:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:35:3)


ומלת אלהים פירשתיה בפרשת וישמע יתרו כתוב וידבר אלהים והאלהים יעננו בקול בא האלהים אשר שם האלהים כי המדבר הוא אלהים כי יש מי שלא יבין דרך קצרה והוא סוד עמוק:


[GOD.] I have explained the meaning of *Elohim* (God) in the Torah portion *And Jethro Heard*.⁷⁸ Scripture states, *And God* (Elohim) *spoke* (Ex. 20:1); and *God* (Elohim) *answered him by a voice* (Ex. 19:19); *God* (Elohim) *is come* (Ex. 20:17); *where God* (Elohim) *was* (Ex. 20:18); for *Elohim* (God) was the speaker.⁷⁹ [I quoted these verses] because there are some who do not understand when one is being brief.⁸⁰ It is a deep secret.⁸¹. I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 26) explains that *Elohim* means angels and that God is referred to as *Elohim* because God works through the angels. I.E. seems to imply that God revealed himself on Sinai through angels rather than through His own essence.

footnotes:
⁷⁸ See I.E. on Ex. 18:19 (Vol. 2, p. 361).
⁷⁹ Hence Scripture describes God as speaking.
⁸⁰ Hence one quote would be insufficient.
⁸¹ Why Scripture refers to God by the term Elohim



### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:36:1)


**ליסרך.** על כן השמיעך את קולו שתירא ממנו כאשר אמרו ואל ידבר עמנו אלהים פן נמות והנה פחדת בעבור העינים והאזנים ועוד לדבר אחר השמיעך את קולו בעבור כי אהב את אבותיך הם השלשה:


THAT HE MIGHT INSTRUCT⁸² (literally, to discipline you). THEE. He made thee to hear Him so that you would fear Him.⁸³ as to put God’s fear in you. Hence his interpretation. This is borne out when Israel said, *but let not God speak with us, lest we die* (Ex. 20:16). Look, you feared because of what your eyes saw and your ears heard. There was another reason why God made you hear His voice. He did so because He loved your fathers (v. 37), your fathers being three.⁸⁴ (v. 37) refers to Abraham, Isaac and Jacob.

footnotes:
⁸² Hebrew, le-yassereka
⁸³ I.E. interprets le-yassereka
⁸⁴ In other words, thy fathers



### Verse 37

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:37:1)


**ויבחר בזרעו.** רמז ליעקב כי אם אמר בזרעם יכנסו עמנו שמנה גוים:


AND CHOSE THEIR SEED. *Be-zaro* (their seed)⁸⁵ alludes to Jacob. If Scripture had read *be-zaram* (their seed), then the eight nations⁸⁶ would have been included with us.

footnotes:
⁸⁵ Literally, his seed.
⁸⁶ The six nations descended from Keturah the concubine of Abraham (Gen. 25:2), the descendants of Ishmael, and the descendants of Esau.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:37:2)


ו**יוציאך בפניו.** בכעס שכעס על מצרים וי״‎א כמו ומלאך פניו הושיעם ולפי דעתי שהוא כמו ופניך הולכי' בקרב:


AND BROUGHT THEE OUT WITH HIS PRESENCE. *Befanav* (with His presence)⁸⁷ means with His anger.⁸⁸ He was angry at Egypt. Others say that *panav* (presence) here is similar to *panav* (presence) in *And the angel of His presence saved them* (Is. 63:9).⁸⁹ However, I believe that *panav* (presence) here is similar to *panekha* (thine own person) in *and thou go to battle in thy own person* (Josh. 17:11).⁹⁰ is to be rendered presence. See I.E. on Ex. 33:14 (Vol. 2, p. 688).

footnotes:
⁸⁷ Literally, with His face.
⁸⁸ See I.E. on Lev. 26:17.
⁸⁹ I.E. explains this verse as follows: And the angel which He sent with His mouth (the mouth is in the face) saved them; that is, God commanded an angel to save them.
⁹⁰ In other words, panav



### Verse 38

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 4:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/4:38:1)


**כיום הזה.** קרוב מהיום הזה והאמת שהוא רמז על אשר נחלו ארץ ב' מלכי האמורי כי ראובן וגד וחצי שבט מנשה ישראל יקראו וכן כתוב כאשר עשה ישראל לארץ ירושתו ואינו דבר עתיד כאשר פירשוהו רבים והעד למכה מלכים גדולים במזמור הודו לה' כי טוב ולא הזכיר רק סיחון ועוג ואמר נחלה לישראל עבדו ואחר שאמר כי ה' הוא האלהים אין עוד מלבדו אמר טעם נכבד כי הראשון הראי' ממרא' העין ובשקול הדעת ובחכמת הלב תדע כי השם הוא לבדו אלהים בשמים ממעל שהם ממעל בן אדם ועל הארץ שהיא תחתיו והעד כי כן פי' והיו שמיך אשר על ראשך נחשת ואחר שראית הנוראות והשבות אל לבבך אתה חייב לשמור מצותיו גם הוא טוב לך ולבניך אחריך:


AS IT IS THIS DAY. Close to this day.⁹¹ However, it really alludes to their possession of the land of the two Amorite kings, for Reuben, Gad, and the half tribe of Manasseh are called Israel.⁹² Scripture similarly reads, *as Israel did unto the land of his possession* (Deut. 2:12). The reference is not to the future, as many explain.⁹³ Proof of the latter is the fact that the psalm which opens with the words, *O give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good* (Ps. 136) reads, *To Him that smote great kings* (Ps. 136:17). Now the psalm only mentions Sihon⁹⁴ and Og⁹⁵ (Ps. 136:19,20). The psalm says, *And gave their land for a heritage…Even a heritage unto Israel*⁹⁶ (Ps. 136:21,22). After stating that the Lord, *He is God; there is none else beside Him* (v. 35), Moses explains why by giving a very valuable reason. He begins with what the eye sees⁹⁷ (v. 35). and goes on to logic and the wisdom of the heart.⁹⁸. I.E.’s point is that *Unto thee it was shown, that thou mightest know* means what you saw with your eyes convinced your mind that the Lord alone is God. He says, *Know that the Lord alone is God in the heavens above*, that is, in the heavens which are above human beings⁹⁹ and upon the earth which is under people. *And thy heaven that is over thy head shall be brass* [*and the earth that is under thee shall be iron*] (Deut. 28:23) is proof that this interpretation¹⁰⁰ is correct. Now that you have seen the terrors, you shall place in your heart the conviction that you are obligated to observe God’s commandments.¹⁰¹ The observance of God’s precepts will also go well with you and with your children after you.¹⁰²

footnotes:

⁹³ But rather to the land possessed by Reuben, Gad and half of the tribe of Manasseh.
⁹⁴ King of the Amorites who dwelled in Transjordan
⁹⁵ King of Bashan.
⁹⁶ Even though their land was given to the above-mentioned two and a half tribes.
⁹⁷ Unto thee it was shown
⁹⁸ That thou mightest know
⁹⁹ The layer of air. I.E. distinguished between the Heaven of Heavens (the place of the stars and planets) and the firmament. See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 21).
¹⁰⁰ That heaven refers to the firmament and not to the Heaven of Heavens.
¹⁰¹ Verse 40.
¹⁰² Verse 40.

### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:3:1)


**לא את אבותינו.** לבדם:


THE LORD MADE NOT THIS COVENANT WITH OUR FATHERS. Alone.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ But also with us.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:3:2)


**כי אתנו אנחנו.** והטעם כי גם אתנו כמו לא יקרא שמך עוד יעקב. או טעמו לא כרת עם אבותינו שהיו במצרים רק אתנו כרת הברית כי רבים יש במחנ' ששמעו הברית מפי השם על כן אמר אנחנו אלה שהם פה והם חיים והעד על יושר זה הפירוש שאמר עמכם וביניכם:


BUT WITH US, EVEN US. The meaning of *ittanu*⁵ (even us) is, but also with us.⁶). I.E. believes that this should be interpreted: The Lord made not this covenant with our fathers alone, but also with us. In other words, *ittanu* (us) should be interpreted as if written *gam* (also) *ittanu*. It is similar to *thy name shall not be called any more Jacob* (Gen. 35:10).⁷ should be read as if written, Thy name shall not be called any more only Jacob. See I.E. on Gen. 35:10 (Vol. 1, p. 332). Or it means the Lord did not make a covenant with our fathers who were in Egypt but only with us,⁸ for there were many in the camp who heard the covenant from the mouth of God.⁹ Moses therefore said even us, those who are here, and are alive. Moses’ words, *with you* (v. 4) and [*I stood*] *between* [*the Lord*] *and you* (v. 5) is proof of the correctness of this interpretation.

footnotes:
⁵ Literally, with us.
⁶ Our verse literally reads: The Lord made not this covenant with our fathers, but with us (ittanu
⁷ Thy name shall not be called any more Jacob
⁸ In this case, no words have to be added to our verse and the clause is to be taken literally; i.e., the Lord made not this covenant with our fathers, but with us.
⁹ The tribe of Levi and those who were under twenty when Moses sent out the spies to Canaan.

FACE TO FACE. Without an intermediary.¹⁰ This means I spoke to you, although you did not see Me. The metaphor used in our verse is that of a person who speaks to another person face to face even though the person being spoken to does not see that speaker.¹¹

**כי יראתם מפני האש.** ואם לא עליתם בהר:


FOR YE WERE AFRAID BECAUSE OF THE FIRE. Even though you did not go up the mountain.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:5:3)


ומלת **לאמר.** דבקה עם דבר ה' עמכם בהר מתוך האש לאמר ויתכן להיות פירוש אנכי עומד בין ה' אחר מעמד הר סיני והטעם שהוא דבר עמכם פנים בפנים ומאותו היום הייתי אני עומד בין ה' וביניכם כי יראתם כאשר יפרש:


[SAYING.] The word *saying* is connected to the previous verse. Its meaning is, *The Lord spoke with you face to face…out of the midst of the fire* (v. 4) *saying*.¹³, etc. It is possible that *I stood between the Lord and you* means I stood between the Lord and you after the revelation at Sinai. Its meaning is, God spoke with you face to face [at Sinai] and from that day on I stood between the Lord and you because you were afraid,¹⁴ as Moses goes on to explain.

footnotes:
¹³ I am the Lord thy God
¹⁴ Of the fire.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:5:4)


**ולא עליתם בהר.** כאשר עליתי אני ומשה נגש אל הערפל ואל תשים אל לבך בעבור היות מלת לאמר רחוקה כי כן רבים והמדבר לא הפסיק הפסוקי' ולא הפרשיות גם אל תשית לב אל המלות כי הם כגופות והטעמים כרוחות והכורת בשני כלים זה כמו זה במעשה מעשה אחד הוא על כן שוא ושקר כי הם אחים וכל שקר שוא וכבר פירשתי זכור שהוא כמו שמור. והוסיף כאשר צוך ה' אלהיך והטעם במעמד הר סיני:


AND WENT NOT UP INTO THE MOUNT. When I went up. Compare, *but Moses drew near unto the thick darkness* (Ex. 20:18). Do not pay attention to the fact that the word *saying* is far removed [from the end of verse 4],¹⁵ for there are many such instances.¹⁶ The speaker¹⁷ did not separate the verses and sections.¹⁸ Also do not pay attention to the words,¹⁹ for they are like bodies and their meanings are like souls.²⁰ One who cuts with two similar tools produces the same result. Scripture therefore interchanges *shav* (false)²¹ (v. 17) and *sheker* (false) (Ex. 20:13),²² (ed shaker). Deut. 5:17 reads: *Neither shalt thou bear* false/vain *witness* (ed shav). as these terms are brothers.²³ All lies are vanity. I have already explained that the word *zakhor* (remember) (v. 8) means the same as *shamor* (keep).²⁴ (zakhor) *the sabbath day to keep it holy*. Deut. 5:12 reads: *Remember* (shamor) *the sabbath day to keep it holy*. Hence I.E.’s comment. Moses adds,²⁵ is not found in Exodus. Hence I.E.’s comment. *as the Lord thy God commanded thee* (v. 12). It means, when you stood around Mount Sinai.²⁶ is keep the Sabbath as God commanded you at Sinai when he revealed the Decalogue.

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:14:1)


**ושורך וחמורך וכל בהמתך.** פרט ואחר כך כלל ובתחלה כלל זה וזה שוה:


NOR THINE OX, NOR THINE ASS, NOR ANY OF THY CATTLE. A particular followed by a general term.²⁷ At first²⁸. Ox and ass are not mentioned. only the general term is used. Both say the same.²⁹

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:14:2)


**למען ינוח.** הוסיף לבאר טעם עבדך ואמתך וזכרת כי עבד היית והניח עבדך על כן צוך ה' אלהיך לעשות כן ביום השבת והטעם למנוח' העבד וי״‎א כי העבד לא ינוח והנה השם הוציאך מעבדות וצוך שתנוח למען תזכור כי היית עבד ובצווי כבד כאשר צוך כאשר פירשתי:


THAT…MAY REST. Scripture here adds the explanation of the law regarding the man-servant and the maid-servant,³⁰ namely, *And thou shalt remember that thou was a servant*.³¹ Now God gave rest to your servant. *Therefore the Lord your God commanded* [thee] to do this on the Sabbath day refers to giving rest to one’s servant.³². Some say³³. that a servant does not rest.³⁴ Now God took you out of slavery and commanded you to rest so that you will remember that you were a slave.³⁵ In the commandment that begins with the word *Honor* (v. 16),³⁶. Scripture goes on to say, *as the Lord thy God commanded thee*. This is to be understood in line with my interpretation.³⁷ (v. 12) means as the Lord thy God commanded thee when you stood around Mount Sinai. Similarly, here.

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:16:1)


**ולמען ייטב לך.** י״‎א רמז לעוה״‎ב וסוד העוה״‎ב ארמזנו בפסוק אני אמית ואחיה:


AND THAT IT MAY GO WELL WITH THEE. Some say that this alludes to the world to come. I will allude to the secret of the world to come in my comments on *I kill, and I make alive* (Deut. 32:39).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:16:2)


**ימיך.** באר שיהיו טובים. דע כי דעת כל הקדמונים כי הדבור הראשון הוא אנכי ואם יש לשאול למה הפסיק המפסיק לא יהיה לך וכבר פיר' עשרת הדברים כי סמכתי על דעתם רק הישר בעיני שמלת אנכי איננ' מהעשר' כי דבור אנכי הוא המצוה ועיקר המצות כאשר פירשתי כבר:


THY DAYS. Scripture goes on to explain that they will be good days.³⁸ elaborates on *that thy days may be long*; i.e., your long days will be good days. Know that all the early sages are of the opinion that *I am* (v. 6) is the first statement³⁹ even though there is room to ask why the one who separated the statements did not separate *Thou shalt have no other gods* (v. 7).⁴⁰. The Decalogue is divided into ten sections in the massoretic edition of the Torah. Each of these sections is devoted to a particular statement. Now, if *I am* is an independent statement, it should form an independent section. However, it does not. Hence *I am* seems to be an introduction to that which follows. We have followed *Vat. Ebr*. 38 which reads *lo hifsik*. I have already explained the Ten Statements,⁴¹ is the first of the Ten Statements. See I.E.’s comments on Ex. 20:1 in both the long and the short commentary. See Vol. 2, pp. 401-404. relying on their opinion.⁴² However, I believe that the statement *I am* is not part of the Ten Statements, for the meaning of *I am* is, I am the one who commands. *I am* is the basis of the commandments, as I have explained previously.⁴³ includes all precepts directed at the heart, tongue, and human deeds. For one who does not believe in his heart that God exists has no reason to be obligated to observe any commandments” (I.E., Vol. 2, p. 415).

³⁸ And that it may be well with thee
³⁹ Contra I.E. who believes that the Decalogue begins with v. 7.
⁴⁰ From I am
⁴¹ And noted that I am
⁴² On the opinion of the early sages.
⁴³ “I am



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/5:18:1)


ומלת **חמד** בלשון הקדש מתפרשת לשני טעמים האחד גזל ועושק וקחת של אחרים בחזקה ובאונס וכך ולא יחמוד איש את ארצך כי אם אין פירושו כן הנה תהיה הארץ רעה ולא בא הכתוב אלא לשבח והטעם השני לשון תאוה בלב ולא תצא לפועל. והנה לא תחמוד בית רעך. הדבור התשיעי. ולא תחמוד אשת רעך הדבור העשירי. ויהיה בית רעך ואשת רעך נדבקים זה עם זה או בית רעך כלל והעד שאמר משה בספר הזה שבאר את התורה תחת לא תחמוד אמר לא תתאוה. ורבים אמרו כי אין עון במחשבת הלב ואין עליהם שכר ועונש ויש ראיות רבות להשיב עליהן ולא אאריך רק אראה להם לב חורש מחשבות און הטיבות כי היה עם לבבך ולישרים בלבותם ומשה אמר בסוף בפיך ובלבבך לעשותו. ועיקר כל המצות ליישר הלב, ורובם זכר והמזיד והשוגג יוכיחו:


The word *chamad* (coveted) has two meanings in Hebrew.⁴⁴ (neither shalt thou covet). One is to rob, to extort, to take someone’s property by force and compulsion. This is the meaning of the word in *neither shall any man covet* (yachmod) *thy land*⁴⁵ (yachmod) *thy land* means no one shall desire your land, then the Land of Israel would be an undesirable land. Hence the meaning of *neither shall any man covet* (yachmod) *thy land* must be no one will take your land. (Ex. 34:24). For if this is not its meaning, then the land is bad. But Scripture only comes to praise the land. The second meeting is to desire in the heart without acting.⁴⁶ Now, *Neither shalt thou covet thy neighbor’s house* (Ex. 20:14) is the ninth statement, and *thou shalt not covet thy neighbor’s wife* (*Ibid.*) is the tenth statement.⁴⁷ as the first statement of the Decalogue. Therefore, it counts *Neither shalt thou covet thy neighbor’s house* and *thou shalt not covet thy neighbor’s wife* as two separate statements. According to this interpretation, *thy neighbor’s house* and *thy neighbor’s wife* are connected to each other.⁴⁸ precedes *thou shalt not covet thy neighbor’s wife*, while the reverse is true in Deuteronomy (Weiser). On the other hand, *thy neighbor’s house* might be a general rule.⁴⁹ is the tenth statement. Scripture first states the general rule and then goes into detail; that is, *Neither…thy neighbor’s house* includes your neighbor’s wife, servant, ox, ass, and all that he owns. According to this interpretation, *I am* is the first statement and *Neither shalt thou covet thy neighbor’s house* is the tenth. The fact that Moses in this book [Deuteronomy] in which he explains the Torah, employs *ve-lo*⁵⁰. *titavveh* (neither shalt thou covet) (v. 18) in place of *lo tachmod* (thou shalt not covet) (Ex. 20:14) is proof of this.⁵¹ is a general statement, for Moses explains it by using a different term in Deuteronomy. Many say that one cannot sin with one’s thoughts and there is no reward and punishment for what one thinks. However, one can refute them by quoting many verses. I will not prolong my argument. I will merely point to the following: *A heart that deviseth wicked thoughts* (Prov. 6:18); *thou didst well that it was in thy heart* (II Chron. 6:8); *And to them that are upright in their hearts* (Ps. 125:4). Moses at the end⁵² said, *in thy mouth, and in thy heart, that thou mayest do it* (Deut. 30:14). The main purpose of the *mitzvot* is to perfect the heart. Most of them serve as a memorial.⁵³, translated by H. Norman Strickman, Jason Aaronson, 1995, p. 78: “Some commandments serve as reminders of the fundamental precepts. For example, the Sabbath commemorates creation. Likewise, not permitting one’s slave to work on the Sabbath recalls the Exodus from Egypt. Similarly, the Pascal lamb, the *matzot*, the bitter herbs, the *sukkah*, the *mezuzah*, and the *tefillin* worn on the hand and head. Likewise, the *tzitzit.”* The distinction made between a sin committed deliberately and one committed inadvertently should convince them.⁵⁴

### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 5:26:1

**מי יתן.** אין הכתוב או' מי יתן לי רק מי יתן להם ע״‎כ נכתב להם אחר והיה לבבם זה ודע כי שורש כל המעשים והתנועות גזרות השם וכל הנמצאים תחת השמים כחם ותולדותם כפי המערכ' העליונה עם השרשים למטה וכן עם המורכבים וכן כפי תנועתם יום יום ועת ועת כי לעולם יהיה שנוי כאשר פירש בעל ספר יצירה והחלקים יקבלו מהכללים כפי תולדותם ובעבור כח הכללים יוכלו לשנות מעט בתולדת וזה טעם ויחזק ה' את לב פרעה ובמקום אחר ויכבד לבו הוא ועבדיו והכל אמת על כן אומר ידעתי ה' כי לא לאדם דרכו ואומר למה תתענו ה' מדרכך ומשה אמר ובחרת בחיים ואחר שהשם לא ימנע טוב הנה הוא אוהב להיטיב ואמר הכתוב כלשון בני אדם מי יתן:


OH THAT. Scripture does not say *mi yitten li* (oh that I) but rather *mi yitten la-hem* (oh that they).⁶⁵ is Israel not God. The word *la-hem* (they) thus follows *ve-hayah levavam zeh* (such a heart as this).⁶⁶ (oh that they). This literally reads, Oh that such a heart as this to them. I.E. believes that this should be read as if written, *mi yitten la-hem ve-hayah levavam zeh lahem* (oh that they had such a heart as this). Note, God’s decrees are the source of all actions and movements.⁶⁷ The power and nature of all that is found beneath the heavens is determined by the arrangement of the heavenly bodies. These are connected to the roots below.⁶⁸ The same applies to the compounds.⁶⁹ All things similarly depend on the movements of the heavenly bodies day by day and time by time, for there is always a change,⁷⁰, p. 173). as the author of the *Sefer Yetizrah* explained.⁷¹ is a mystical work composed between the third and sixth centuries either in the Land of Israel or in Babylonia. The parts⁷² receive⁷³ from the categories⁷⁴ according to their nature.⁷⁵ It is due to the power of the “categories” that they⁷⁶ can make some small changes in nature.⁷⁷ This is the meaning of *and the Lord hardened Pharaoh’s heart* (Ex. 11:10).⁷⁸ In another place, Scripture states, *and hardened his heart, he and his servants* (Ex. 9:34).⁷⁹ It is all true. Scripture therefore states, *O Lord, I know that man’s way is not his own* (Jer. 10:23). It similarly states, *O Lord, why dost Thou make us to err from Thy ways* (Jer. 63:17). Moses said, *therefore choose life* (Deut. 30:19).⁸⁰ Now, since God does not withhold good,⁸¹ He loves to do good. Scripture employs human language when it says, *Oh that*.⁸²

A LAND FLOWING WITH MILK AND HONEY. This is connected to *to possess it* (v. I).¹ is connected to the end of the first verse. ### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:4:1)


ומה שהעתיקו קדמונינו ז״‎ל על ק״‎ש הוא האמת ואין צורך לחפש דע כי זה השם הנכבד הוא שם העצם ואם כן מה טעם לאמרו פעם שנית והתשובה כי אדם שם עצם ושם מקרה שאיננו כן ואם הוא נקרא מעצם האדמה וכן זה והעד השם שהוא סמוך אל צבאות או העיקר הוא אלהינו ונכפל כמו כן לומר אחד והטעם לבדו וראיות עד אין חקר יש כי השם אחד והפסוק שאמר ביום ההוא יהיה ה' אחד על מחשבות בני אדם והנכון בעיני שהוא דבק עם מלך כי כן כתוב והיה ה' למלך על כל הארץ ביום ההוא יהיה ה' מלך אחד ושמו אחד כטעם כי אז אהפוך אל עמים שפה ברורה והנה כל העולם ידברו שפה אחת על כן יהיה שם ה' אחד:


HEAR O ISRAEL: THE LORD (*YHVH*) OUR GOD, THE LORD (*YHVH*) IS ONE.] That which the ancients, of blessed memory, transmitted regarding the reading of the *shema* is true,⁵ (shema), *O Israel*, morning and evening. and there is no need for further investigation. Note that the glorious name (*YHVH*) is a noun.⁶ This being the case, why is it said a second time?⁷) our God is one. It should be noted that I.E. comments elsewhere that it is biblical style to repeat nouns in the same verse. See I.E. on Gen. 19:24, (Vol. 1, p. 208) and on Ps. 1:2. The answer is that Adam is a noun. Adam is also an adjective, and not a noun,⁸ even though⁹). he was called after the very earth. The same is true of the name *YHVH*.¹⁰ The fact that *YHVH* is connected to *tzeva’ot* (hosts)¹¹ (the Lord of hosts) (Is. 1:9). is proof.¹² is in the construct with *tzeva’ot*, whereas a proper name cannot come in the construct. See I.E. on Ex. 3:15 (Vol. 2, p. 65). On the other hand, the term *Elohenu* (our God) might be the object.¹³ is our God. The name *YHVH* was repeated in order to state *YHVH echad* (the Lord is one) the meaning of which is, only *YHVH*.¹⁴ is our God, it goes on to say that only *YHVH* is our God. There are many proofs that *YHVH* alone is God.¹⁵ The verse that states, *In that day shall the Lord be One* (echad)¹⁶ (Zech. 14:9), speaks of men’s thoughts.¹⁷ In my opinion, the word one¹⁸ is connected to the word king, for Scripture writes: *And the Lord shall be King over all the earth; In that day shall the Lord* alone be King *and His name one* (Zech. 14:9).¹⁹. I.E.’s interpretation eliminates the implication that today there are more gods than *YHVH*, for it speaks of all people recognizing one king, i.e., the Lord. The latter is similar in meaning to *For then will I turn to the peoples, A pure language, That they may all call upon the name of the Lord* (Zeph. 3:9}. Behold, all the nations will speak one language. The name of the Lord will therefore be one.

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:1)


**ואהבת.** ואחר שאין לנו אלוה אחר רק הוא לבדו חייב אתה שתאהבנו כי אין לנו אלוה אחר:


AND THOU SHALT LOVE. Since we have no other god aside from the Lord, you are obligated to love Him, for we have no other god.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ I.E. repeats himself for purposes of emphasis. It is also possible that we have a conflation of two different manuscripts.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:2)


**בכל לבבך ובכל נפשך.** הלב הוא הדעת והוא כנוי לרוח המשכלת כי היא המרכב' הראשונה וכן חכם לב קונה לב:


WITH ALL THY HEART, AND WITH ALL THY SOUL. The heart refers to knowledge. It is another term for the spirit of intelligence,²¹ for the heart is its first residence.²² (lip) because it is seen to come from the lips. Similarly, man’s highest soul is called heart (*lev*)…because the heart is its first resting place.” The same applies to *wise-hearted* (Ex. 36:1) and to *He that getteth a heart* (Prov. 19:8).²³

footnotes:
²¹ The rational soul.
²² See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 25): “Speech is called safah
²³ Translated literally.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:3)


**נפשך.** היא הרוח שבגוף והיא המתאו' וכחה נראה בכבד:


THY SOUL.²⁴. The reference is to the spirit, which is in the body.²⁵), animal (*ru’ach*), and rational (*neshamah*). See Vol. 1, p. xiii; also *The Secret of the Torah*, p. 96. It is the spirit which lusts.²⁶. Its power is manifest in the liver.

footnotes:
²⁴ Hebrew, nafshekha
²⁵ I.E. believed that man has three souls; vegetative (nefesh
²⁶ The nefesh


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:5:4)


**מאדך.** מטעם מאד מאד והטעם לרוב אהוב אותו בכל מה שתוכל ותהיה אהבה גמורה בלב:


THY MIGHT. The meaning of *me’odekha* (thy might) is very very much.²⁷ means much. Thus *me’odekha* (your might) literally means your much. Hence I.E.’s comment. It means that you should love Him to the utmost in any way that you are able. Your heart should be totally committed to the love of God.

footnotes:
²⁷ The word me’od



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:6:1)


**והיו הדברים האלה.** אמרו המכחשים על עשרת הדברות ועליהם נאמר וכתבתם על מזוזות ביתך והאמת כל המצות:


AND THESE WORDS…SHALL BE. Those who deny the Rabbinic tradition²⁸ say that the reference is to the Decalogue, and that it is in reference to the Decalogue that Scripture states, *And thou shalt write them upon the door-posts* (v. 9). However, the truth is that *and these words…shall be* refers to all of the commandments.

footnotes:
²⁸ The Karaites.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:7:1)


**ושננתם.** מגזרת חץ שנון. וידוע איך ישונן החץ הנה נרא' כי עיקר כל האדם עבודת השם ועבודתו להכיר פעליו והמעתיקי' העתיקו שעת ק״‎ש וכלנו נסמוך עליהם:


AND THOU SHALT TEACH THEM. *Ve-shinantam* (and thou shalt teach them) is related to the word *shanun* (sharp) in *and a sharp arrow* (Prov. 25:18). The method of sharpening an arrow is well known.²⁹ Observe. Our verse teaches that the main purpose of man is to serve God.³⁰ God’s service consists in recognizing God’s works.³¹, the reference is to the study of the Torah (see Weiser). However, it should be noted that in I.E., study of God’s works usually refers to the study of the sciences. Thus I.E. writes: “When one masters astronomy and the circuits of the [sun and. moon], he comes to know the works of God the glorious” (*The Secret of the Torah*, p. 21). Perhaps I.E. is alluding here to his belief that the Torah cannot be fully understood unless it is interpreted in light of the studies of science (see *The Secret of the Torah*, pp. 33,34). However, the transmitters of tradition taught that [our verse] refers to the time of reading the *shema*.³² means that we should read the *shema* at night, and *when thou risest up* refers to the reading of the *shema* in the morning. See chapter 1 of *Mishnah Berakhot*. We all rely on them.

footnotes:
²⁹ It is sharpened by consistent filing. Similarly, the words of the Torah must consistently be drilled into a child (Weiser).
³⁰ By studying the Torah at all times. See next note.
³¹ Prima facie
³² According to the sages when thou liest down


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:7:2)


**בשבתך.** דבק עם ודברת בם:


WHEN THOU SITTEST. This is connected to *and shalt talk of them*.³³

footnotes:
³³ That is, our verse should be read, And thou shalt teach them diligently unto thy children, and shalt talk of them when thou sittest in thy house, not And thou shalt teach them diligently unto thy children, and shalt talk of them.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:7:3)


**ובשכבך.** לישן כי אין מצוה על הישן והנה ודברת בם בפה ובלב:


AND WHEN THOU LIEST DOWN. To sleep, for one is not obligated to observe the commandments when one sleeps.³⁴ can’t be taken to mean when you sleep. Behold, the meaning of our verse is, *And thou shalt talk of them* verbally and in your heart.

footnotes:
³⁴ Thus and when thou liest down



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:8:1)


**וקשרתם לאות על ידך.** מפורש:


AND THOU SHALT BIND THEM FOR A SIGN UPON THY HAND. This has been explained.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁵ See I.E. on Ex. 13:9, (Vol. 2, pp. 261,262).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:8:2)


**לטטפת.** אין למלה הזאת ריע במקרא והמכחשים אמרו שהוא מגזרת והטף אל דרום וזה לא יתכן כי שרש הטף נטף וזה חסר נו״‎ן וכפול הטי״‎ת:


AND THEY SHALL BE FOR FRONTLETS. The word *totafot* (frontlets) is not found elsewhere in Scripture. Those who dispute the Rabbinic tradition³⁶ is related to the word *ve-hatef* (and preach). Actually this opinion was quoted by Rashi in the name of Menachem ben Saruk, who, far from being a heretic, was an accepted tenth-century grammarian and Bible commentator. See I.E. on Ex. 13:16 (Vol. 2, p. 266). say that *totafot* is related to the word *ve-hatef* (and preach) in *and preach toward the South* (Ezek. 21:2). This is impossible, for the root of *hattef* is *nun, tet, peh*. However, there are two *tets* ands no *nun* in *totafot*.

footnotes:
³⁶ The Karaites. However, in his commentary on Exodus, I.E. does not identify this opinion as being heretical. He merely notes that “others say” that totafot

ALL GOOD THINGS. The word *tuv* (good) is vocalized with a *shuruk* in place of a *cholam*,⁴⁰. for these vowels interchange and *tuv* is not in the construct.⁴¹ is the normal vocalization for the word in the construct.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:13:1

**תירא.** שלא תעשו מצות לא תעשה:


THOU SHALT FEAR. You shall not violate any of the negative commandments.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:13:2)


**תעבוד.** במצות עשה:


SHALT THOU SERVE. By observing the positive commandments.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:13:3)


**ובשמו תשבע.** לא בשם אלהים אחרים להקים דבר ולכרות ברית וכן תשבע כל לשון רק באמת והטעם שלא יהיה לך אלוה שתשבע בשמו רק שם השם לבדו. ע״‎כ כתוב אחריו לא תלכון אחרי אלהים אחרי' ולא תנסו:


AND BY HIS NAME SHALT THOU SWEAR. And not by the name of another god when you affirm anything or make a covenant. Similarly, *Every tongue shall swear*⁴² (Is. 45:23). You shall swear only the truth. The meaning of our verse is, you shall have no other god to swear by his name. You shall only swear by the name of the Lord. Scripture therefore afterwards reads, *Ye shall not go after other gods* (v. 14), and *ye shall not try the Lord your God* (v. 16).

footnotes:
⁴² When affirming anything or making a covenant.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:16:1)


**במסה.** שם מקום הנזכר ויקרא שם המקום מסה ונפתח הבי״‎ת כמו וזבח וצלמונע בקרקר ויפקדם בטלאים ושם פירשתיו והטעם שאמרו היש השם בקרבנו ואם הוא נעבדנו רק אין לך צורך לבד שתשמור מצותיו ואם אתה תעשה הטוב בעיניו יעשה לך כל טוב ויהדוף את כל אויביך:


IN MASSAH. Massah is the name of a place mentioned earlier, *And the name of the place was called Massah* (Ex. 17:7). The *bet* which is prefixed to Massah is vocalized with a *pattach*⁴³ (in Massah). The usual way to say in Massah is *be-massah*. like the *bet* prefixed to *karkor* in *Now Zebah and Zalmona were in Karkor*⁴⁴. (Jud. 8:10), and the *bet* placed before Telaim in *And numbered them in Telaim*⁴⁵, *ba-talaim*. (I Sam. 15:4). I explained it⁴⁶ there. Massah was so called because Israel said, *Is the Lord among us* (Ex. 17:7).⁴⁷. The latter means to test. If He is, then we will serve Him. [Moses told Israel:] you have no need to test God. All you have to do is observe His commandments. If you do what is good in His sight, the Lord will do to you all that is good for you and will thrust out all of your enemies.

footnotes:
⁴³ Our verse reads ba-massah
⁴⁴ Hebrew, ba-karkor
⁴⁵ Hebrew
⁴⁶ The reason for the irregular vocalization. We do not know how I.E. explained the latter, for his commentary on Judges has been lost.
⁴⁷ Massah comes from the root nun, sin, heh



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:19:1)


**להדוף.** ה״‎א להדוף שורש והעד הדפו:


TO THRUST OUT. The *heh* in *la-hadof* (to thrust out) is a root letter, as the word *hadofo* (cast it upon him) (Num. 35:22) proves.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:20:1)


**מה העדות.** הטעם למה זה העול עלינו בינות בני אדם ואמרת כי השם פדנו מבית עבדים והנה עשה לנו זאת הטובה והנה חייבים אנחנו ליראה את שמו כי כבר הכרנו כי הוא הטיב לנו ועוד ייטיב לנו להחיותנו כי מצותיו חיים הם למוצאיהם:


WHAT MEAN THE TESTIMONIES. It means, Why, out of all people, is this burden placed upon us?⁴⁸ [21.] THEN THOU SHALT SAY. For God redeemed us from the house of bondage. Now, God did us this good. We are obligated to fear His name⁴⁹ because we have recognized that He did good to us in the past. He will do good for us in the future to keep us alive, for His commandments *are life unto those that find them* (Prov. 4:22).

footnotes:
⁴⁸ That is, the import of our verse is, Why out of all people is the burden of observing these testimonies placed upon us?
⁴⁹ In the present.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 6:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/6:25:1)


**וצדקה תהיה לנו.** י״‎א מכאן רמז לשכר המצות לעוה״‎ב ולפי הפשט כי מדרך הצדקה חייבים אנחנו לשמור את רצונו כי הוא אדוננו והנכון בעיני כי כל הגוים יראו שאנחנו צדיקים בשמרנו מצותיו וחקיו שהם צדיקים כי כן כתוב:


AND IT SHALL BE RIGHTEOUSNESS UNTO US. Some say that this verse hints at the reward in the world to come for the observance of the commandments.⁵⁰. However, according to the plain meaning of the text, our verse teaches that following God’s will is the righteous thing to do, for He is our Master. I believe that the correct meaning is, all the nations shall see that we are righteous when we keep God’s commandments and statutes, which Scripture clearly states are righteous.⁵¹

footnotes:
⁵⁰ See Targum Jonathan: And merit will be kept for us for the world to come
⁵¹ See Deut. 4:8.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:1:1)

AND SHALL CAST OUT. The *nun* in *ve-nashal* (and shall cast out) is a root letter. It is related to the word *yishal* (shall drop off)¹. in for *thine olives shall drop off* (Deut. 28:40).

FOR HE WILL TURN AWAY THY SON. The reference². is to “his son” mentioned earlier.³ That is, do not give your daughter to his son⁴ or do not take the daughter⁵ mentioned.⁶

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:5:1)


**כי אם.** רק כה עשו להם כטעם כי עם קדוש אתה ולא תתגאל בדברי הכותיו' המצויירו' במצבות ואשרים:


BUT THUS. *Ki im ko ta’asu la-ham* means but thus⁷, means but thus. *Ki* literally means but, for, when; *im* means if. Thus *ki im* literally means but if. Hence I.E.’s comment that *ki im* should be rendered but thus. shall ye deal with them⁸ because *thou art a holy people* (v. 6) and should not defile yourself with pagan images drawn on pillars, or with *asherim*.⁹

⁸ You shall destroy their images.
⁹ Sacred trees (Rashi).

HIS OWN TREASURE. I have already explained the meaning of the word *segullah* (treasure).¹⁰ It means a desirable thing to which nothing comparable is to be found any-where. *Segullat* (treasure)¹¹ in *and treasure such as kings…have as their own* (Eccles. 2:8) is similar.

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:1)


**הוא האלהים.** הטעם זה הוא האלהים באמת שיש לו כח והוא נאמן והטעם בעבור השבוע' כי יש נאמן ואין בו כח למלאו' שבועתו ויש בעל כח שאיננו נאמן:


HE IS GOD. This means this is truly the God who has the power and is faithful. *And is faithful* refers to the oath which God took. Scripture says that God is powerful and faithful, because there are others who are faithful but lack the power to keep their oaths and others who are powerful but are unfaithful.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:2)


**שומר הברית והחסד לאוהביו.** אין למעלה מהם:


WHO KEEPETH COVENANT AND MERCY WITH THEM THAT LOVE HIM. There are none higher then they.¹²

footnotes:
¹² That love God.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:3)


**ולשומרי מצותיו.** אחריהם. או פירושו אוהביו בלב הם שומרי מצותיו בפה ובפעל:


AND KEEP HIS COMMANDMENTS. After them.¹³ Or its meaning is, them that love Him in their hearts, they are the ones who keep His commandments verbally and in practice.

footnotes:
¹³ The future generations. God’s oath is conditional upon their keeping of His commandments.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:9:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:9:4)


**לאלף דור.** טעה האומר כי בשלשים אלף וששת אלפים ישוב גלגל המזלות כי הנה נמצא' המעל' בשבעים שנה וטעם אלף עד אין קץ:


TO A THOUSAND GENERATIONS. The one who says that the sphere of the constellations¹⁴ completes its cycle at the end of thirty-six thousand years¹⁵ errs, for we find it moving one degree in seventy years.¹⁶ The meaning of a thousand is, endless.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁴ Which contains 360 degrees and moves one degree in 100 years.
¹⁵ After which this world ends (Krinsky). According to this interpretation God says that he will keep his oath until the end of time. This interpretation says that a generation lasts for 36 years and a thousand generations is 36,000 years.
¹⁶ Thus it completes its cycle once in 25,200 years.
¹⁷ A thousand generations means endless generations.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:10:1)


**ומשלם לשונאיו.** לכל א' משונאיו כמו וצדיקים ככפיר יבטח:


AND REPAYETH THEM THAT HATE HIM…TO DESTROY THEM.¹⁸, (literally, to destroy him). To destroy each individual that hates Him.¹⁹ Compare, *But righteous people is secure as a young lion*²⁰ (Prov. 28:1).²¹

footnotes:
¹⁸ Hebrew, le-ha’avido
¹⁹ Hence the combination of the singular with the plural. See Vol. 1, p. XVII.
²⁰ Translated literally.
²¹ Each one of the righteous people is secure as a young lion. Here too we have a combination of the singular with the plural.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:10:2)


**אל פניו.** הטעם לעצמו. וי״‎א כמו בפניו בכעסו והטעם כי ישלם לשונאיו בעבור ששנאו האמת:


TO THEIR FACE. *Be-fanav* (to their face) means to him.²² Others say that *el panav* (their face) has the meaning of *befanav*, that is, because of His anger.²³ is to be rendered His anger. See I.E. on Deut. 4:37. Its means that God will repay the wicked because they hated the truth.²⁴

footnotes:
²² To the previous person. Face is not to be taken literally.
²³ In other words, panav
²⁴ And thereby angered God.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:10:3)


**להאבידו לא יאחר לשונאו.** י״‎א כי הטעם ישלם לרשע גמול טוב שעשה בעוה״‎ז ולא יאחר לתת לו בעוה״‎ב והנכון בעיני שהוא כנגד אוהביו שהם עומדים לאלף דור שונאיו יאבדו והוסיף לא יאחר שיחשוב שיעמוד בנו או בן בנו כטעם על שלשים ועל רבעים:


TO DESTROY THEM; HE WILL NOT BE SLACK TO HIM THAT HATETH HIM. Some say that the meaning of our verse is that God rewards the wicked for his good deeds in this world and does not wait to reward him in the world to come.²⁵, 22a; Onkelos and Rashi. However, it appears to me that our verse contrasts the righteous²⁶ who will exist²⁷ for a thousand generations with the wicked²⁸ who will be destroyed. Scripture reads, *He will not be slack* because the wicked person thinks that his son or grandson shall live,²⁹ as in [*visiting the iniquity of the fathers*] *upon the children, and upon the children’s children* (Ex. 33:7).³⁰

footnotes:
²⁵ See Erubin
²⁶ Literally, ones who love God.
²⁷ By living on through their children.
²⁸ Literally, ones who hate Him.
²⁹ And receive the punishment due him.
³⁰ Hence the Torah states that the wicked themselves shall receive the punishment due them.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:11:1)


**ושמרת.** הטעם כאשר השם שומר הברית שמור אתה מצותיו:


THOU SHALT THEREFORE KEEP. It means as God keeps the covenant so shall you keep His commandments.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:12:1)


**עקב.** כמו לעולם עקב שכר באחרונה וטעם הפסוק הראשון שומר הברית ששמר ברית אברהם ואתם אם תהיו מאוהביו ישמור עוד הברית ואם היית מאוהביו ואהבך וברכך והרבך זה פירוש הברית והחסד:


BECAUSE. *Ekev* (because) has the same meaning as *ekev* (reward) in *For ever, reward*³¹ (Ps. 119:112). It means the reward which will be given at the end. The meaning of *who keepeth covenant* in the previous verse (v. 9) is that God kept the covenant with Abraham. If you will be among those that love Him, then He will continue to keep the covenant.³² in our verse is, God will continue to keep the covenant if you love him. If you will be among those who love Him, then *He will love thee, and bless thee, and multiply thee* (v. 13). This is the meaning of *the covenant and the mercy* (v. 12).

footnotes:
³¹ Translated according to I.E.
³² In other words, the meaning of that the Lord thy God shall keep with thee the covenant



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:1)


**פרי בטנך.** הבנים ובטן רמז:


THY BODY. Thy children.³³ means your children. *Bitnekha* (thy body)³⁴ is a euphemism.³⁵

footnotes:
³³ That is, the fruit of thy body
³⁴ Literally, your belly.
³⁵ For the sexual organ.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:2)


**ופרי אדמתך.** הזכיר דגן תירוש ויצהר שהם עיקר:


AND THE FRUIT OF THY LAND. Scripture mentions com, wine, and oil because they are the most important foods.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:3)


**שגר.** פירשתיו:


THE INCREASE. I have already explained the meaning of *shegar* (the increase of).³⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ I.E. renders it as flock in his short commentary on Ex. 13:12. See also his long commentary on Ex. 13:12 (Vol. 2, p. 263).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:4)


**אלפיך.** כמו אלופינו מסובלים:


THY KINE. The word *alafekha* (thy kine) is similar to the word *alufenu* (oxen) in *Whose oxen are well laden* (Ps. 144:14).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:13:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:13:5)


**ועשתרות.** כמו עדרים ומפרש גדול היה בספרד והוציא טעם למה קראו העברים הבקר בשם אלף והצאן בעשתרות וכן היה מפרש הבעלים ועשתרות בעל המזל הצומח והעשירי ועוד לא די שיברך פרי בטנך כי לא יהיה בך עקר שאין זרעו מוליד וכן בבהמתך כי עוד תהיה בריא מכל מין חולי ידוע להיות כמנהג העולם **וכל מדוי.** שהיו חוץ המנהג:


AND THE YOUNG OF. *Ashterot* (and the young of) means and the flocks of. There was a great commentator in Spain³⁷ who explained why the Hebrews referred to cattle as *alef*³⁸ (thy kine) and in *alufenu* (oxen). and to sheep and goats as *ashterot*. He similarly explained the *ba’alim* and the *ashtaroth* (Jud. 10:6) as referring to the lord of the flowering constellation³⁹, p. 202). This constellation is the first (*alef*) in the sky and its ruling planet, its lord (*ba’al*) was believed to have a powerful affect on fertility. and that of the tenth.⁴⁰, and *adar* when most of the sheep and cattle are born. Scripture goes on to say God will not only bless the fruit of your body but there will not be a barren male, that is, one whose seed is infertile, among you or among your cattle. Furthermore, you will be healthy and avoid all sorts of well-known illnesses which normally befall people. You will similarly avoid abnormal, evil diseases.⁴¹

footnotes:
³⁷ I.E. does not identify the commentator.
³⁸ As in alafekha
³⁹ The planet which rules over “the ascent, the zodiacal sign appearing on the eastern horizon at a certain moment” (I. Efros, Studies in Medieval Jewish Philosophy
⁴⁰ The tenth constellation, Capricorn, which rules during the months of tevet, shevat
⁴¹ Hence the mention of the diseases of Egypt.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:16:1

**ואכלת.** מצוה הוא לכלותם כאוכל לחם והעד לא תחוס עינך:


AND THOU SHALT CONSUME. This is a commandment to consume them as one consumes bread, as *thine eye shall not pity them* proves.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:16:2)


**כי מוקש הוא לך.** כל אחד מאלהיהם:


FOR THAT WILL BE A SNARE UNTO THEE. The reference is to each one of their gods.⁴²

footnotes:
⁴² Our verse literally reads, “Neither shalt thou serve their gods; for he will be a snare unto thee.” To be consistent, our verse should have read, neither shalt thou serve their gods; for they will be a snare unto thee. Hence I. E.’s interpretation.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:20:1)


**הצרעה.** חולי בגוף מגזרת צרעת:


THE HORNET. *Ha-tzirah* (the hornet) refers to an illness which afflicts the body. It is related to the word *tzara’at* (leprosy).

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:21:1)

THOU SHALT NOT BE AFFRIGHTED AT THEM. Only fear the Lord, who is great and awful.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:22:1)


אמר הגאון כי **פן תרבה עליך** סי' לזמן שינצחום ואין צורך וכבר פירשתיו:


LEST THE BEASTS OF THE FIELD INCREASE UPON THEE. The Gaon says that this is a sign regarding the time that they shall be victorious over them.⁴³ There is no need for the Gaon’s interpretation.⁴⁴ I have already explained it.

footnotes:
⁴³ When Israel will so increase that there will be no danger that the beasts of the field will overwhelm them, then they will know that it is time to drive out the Canaanites. See I.E. on Ex. 23:29 (Vol. 2, p. 519).
⁴⁴ For the meaning of our verse is that God will not drive out the Cananites in one year because the Land of Israel was too large for those who left Egypt to settle all of it. See I.E. on Ex. 23:29 (Vol. 2, p. 519).



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:24:1)


**השמדך.** שם הפעל ואם הוא בחירק יתכן היות כמוהו השאיר לו שריד והעד אותם שהוא הפעול:


THOU HAST DESTROYED THEM. *Hishmidekha* (thou has destroyed them) is an infinitive even though it is vocalized with a *chirik*.⁴⁵ beneath the *heh. Hishmidekha* is a *hifil*. In such cases the *heh* is normally vocalized with a *pattach* in the infinitive. Compare, *hashmid*. The word *hishir*⁴⁶ (left him remaining) in *until there was none left him remaining* (Num. 22:35) is most likely similar to it. The word *otam* (them), which is the object,⁴⁷. proves that *hishmidekha* is an infinitive.⁴⁸ is a third person (*hishmid*) plus the second person pronoun. Its meaning is, he destroyed you. However, with the word *otam* following, it is an infinitive, meaning you destroy them.

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Rather than with a pattach
⁴⁶ Which is a hifil.
⁴⁷ Of hishmidekha
⁴⁸ Hishmidekha



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:25:1)


**כסף וזהב** שעליהם או ממון תקח לעזבם:


THE SILVER OR THE GOLD THAT IS ON THEM. *Alehem* is to be rendered, that is on them. Or the meaning of our clause is, you shall not take money to leave them.⁴⁹ means for them, and the meaning of our clause is, you shall not take a bribe of money to leave them alone.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ In this case, alehem


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 7:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/7:25:2)


**תוקש.** הוי״‎ו תחת יו״‎ד יקושתי לך:


SNARED. The *vav* in *tivvakesh* (snared) is in place of a *yod*.⁵⁰ is *yod, kof, shin*. Compare, *yakoshti*⁵¹ is *yod, kof, shin*. *lakh* (I have laid a snare for thee).

footnotes:
⁵⁰ The root of tivvakesh
⁵¹ The root of yakoshti



## Chapter 8


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:1:1)


**כל המצוה.** הטעם אם רציתם לשמור המצות למען תחיו זכרו את הדרך:


ALL THE COMMANDMENT. This means, if you want to keep the commandments so that you may live then *remember all the way* (v. 2).¹ (and thou shalt remember) is to be rendered, then thou shalt remember.

¹ In other words, verse 1 and 2 are not independent statements. Furthermore, ve-zakharta



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:2:1)


**למען ענותך לנסותך לדעת.** פירשתיו:


THAT HE MIGHT AFFLICT THEE, TO PROVE THEE. I have already explained it.²

footnotes:
² How to understand “to prove thee.” The point is, since God knows the future, what point is there in testing people. I.E. explains divine tests in his comments on the “binding of Isaac.” He writes, “The reason God tested Abraham was to reward him.” See I.E. on Gen. 22:1 (Vol. 1, pp. 221-223).



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:1)


**ויענך.** בדרך:


AND HE AFFLICTED THEE. On the way.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:2)


**וירעיבך.** קודם בא המן או במן בעצמו בעבור שהיה קל על לבם וזה לא יתכן ויתכן מתאוות אחרות:


AND SUFFERED THEE TO HUNGER. Before the coming of the manna.³ Or via the manna itself, for it was light and did not satisfy their heart’s desire.⁴ However, this is not so.⁵ It most probably refers to other desires.⁶

³ Before the manna descended, Israel suffered hunger. See Ex. 16:3.
⁴ See Num. 21:5.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:3)


**ידעון.** בנו״‎ן וכן צקון לחש גם ויחנון בעבר ארנון:


KNOW. *Yade’un* (know) is spelled with a *nun*.⁷. So too the word *tzakun* (poured out)⁸. Be that as it may, the *nun* is not part of the normal form of this word. See I.E. on Is. 26:16. in *Silently they poured out a prayer* (Is. 26:16) and *va-yachanun* (and they pitched)⁹. in *and they pitched on the other side of the Arnon* (Jud. 11:18).
