⁷ The usual form of this word is yade’u
⁸ The usual form of the word is yatzeku
⁹ The usual form of the word is va-yachanu


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:4)


**הודיעך.** שם הפעל:


THAT HE MIGHT MAKE THEE KNOW. *Hodi’akha*¹⁰ plus a pronominal suffix.(make thee know) is an infinitive.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Hodi’a


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:5)


**הלחם.** שאדם תדיר בו:


BREAD. Which people always eat.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:3:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:3:6)


**מוצא פי ה'.** כמו מה שגזר כמו הדבר יצא מפי המלך והטעם כי על הלחם לבדו לא יחיה האדם רק הכח או עם הכח הבא מהעליונים במצות השם וזה פי' מוצא פי ה' והעד שלא אכלתם לחם וחייתם:


EVERY THING THAT PROCEEDETH OUT OF THE MOUTH OF THE LORD. It means that which God has decreed. Compare, *As the word went out of the king’s mouth* (Esth. 7:8). The meaning of our verse is, man does not live by bread alone but by the power,¹¹ or with the power, which comes from on high¹² refers to the power to nourish which God placed in all foodstuffs. According to the second interpretation the verse refers to divine providence. The latter was demonstrated by the manna which came from on high. by the command of God. This is the meaning of *that proceedeth out of the mouth of the Lord*. The fact that you did not eat bread and you lived is proof of this.

footnotes:
¹¹ In the nature of the food. In other words, God created the world in such a way that man can exist on all types of food. For I.E.’s use of the word “power” in the sense of nature, see I.E. on Deut. 5:26.
¹² See I.E. on Deut. 5:26: “The power and nature of all that is found beneath the heavens is determined by the upper arrangement.” According to I.E.’s first comment, everything that proceedeth out of the mouth of the Lord



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:4:1)


**שמלתך.** יש אומרי' דרך אות ואחרים אומרים כי הוציאו מלבושים רבים ממצרים ויתכן שאין בתולדת המן להוליד זיעה:


THY RAIMENT. Some say via a miracle. Others say that the Israelites brought out many garments from Egypt.¹³ However, it is most likely the nature of manna not to produce sweat.¹⁴ them.

footnotes:
¹³ The meaning of our clause is that they always had garments to wear.
¹⁴ Hence their garments waxed not old upon


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:4:2)


**לא בצקה.** מגזרת ויאפו את הבצק כי מנהג רגל האורח שהלך רגלי דרך רב שינפחו רגליו ויתכן שנתן להם השם כח או הוליכם אט:


NEITHER…SWELL. The word *batzekah* (swell) is related to the word *batzek* (dough)¹⁵ in *And they baked…the dough* (Ex. 12:39). It is usual for the feet of a wayfarer who walks great distances to swell. It is possible that God gave the Israelites strength or that he led them slowly.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁵ Dough swells.
¹⁶ Hence their feet did not swell.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:5:1)


**וידעת עם לבבך.** שידע באמונת לבו שהוא העיקר ובעבור שתשמע אל האלוה המיסר אותך ואם הרעיבך ליסרך ויענך בדרך בצמא שכרך שיביאך אל הארץ הטובה:


AND THOU SHALT CONSIDER IN THY HEART.¹⁷ You shall know by believing in your heart.¹⁸ The latter is what counts most.¹⁹, p. 91). God will reward you by bringing you into the good land, because you obeyed the God who chastens you. You obeyed God even though in order to chasten you, He afflicted you with thirst and made you suffer hunger.

footnotes:
¹⁷ Literally, you shall know in your heart. Hence I.E.’s interpretation.
¹⁸ What you claim to know should be what your heart believes.
¹⁹ True belief. I.E. says, “If belief is lacking, then the observance of the commandments is meaningless and serves no purpose” (The Secret of the Torah

SCARCENESS. *Be-miskenut* (scarceness) means in poverty and impoverishment. Compare, *misken* (poor) in *a poor and wise child* (Eccles. 4:13).



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:13:1

MULTIPLY. The *yod* in *yirbeyun* (multiply) is in place of a *heh*.²² is *resh, bet, heh*. Compare the word *yirbeh* (multiplied) later on in the verse. This is not the opinion of Rabbi Mosheh Ha-Kohen the Spaniard.²³ in *yirbeyun* is a root letter.

**ורם לבבך.** ותשכח כי עבד היית בשפלות לב ותשכח הענוי והרעב והצמא שהיה לך במדבר ואף על פי כן השם כלכל אותך:


THEN THY HEART BE LIFTED UP. And you will forget that you were a slave and your heart was downcast. You will forget the affliction and the hunger which you experienced in the wilderness and even so the Lord sustained you.²⁴

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:15:1)


**במדבר.** מושך עצמו ואחר עמו וכן הוא **במדבר הגדול.** שהוא ארוך ורחב **והנורא.** שהוא מדבר שרף:


WILDERNESS. The word *midbar* (wilderness) is to be read as if written twice, that is, through the great wilderness in length and width and [through] a dreadful wilderness of fiery serpents.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ Our verse literally reads: who led thee through the wilderness and the dreadful wherein were serpents, fiery serpents. I.E. believes that our verse should be interpreted as if written, who led thee through the wilderness, a dreadful wilderness, wherein were serpents, fiery serpents, etc.

**החלמיש.** הצור התקיף:


OF FLINT. *Tzur ha-chalamish* (the rock of flint) means the mighty rock.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 8:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/8:17:1)


**החיל הזה.** הממון וכן ועזבו לאחרים חילם. והטעם יגיעם וכחם:


THIS WEALTH. *Chayil* means wealth. Compare, the word *chelam* (their wealth) in *And leave their wealth to others* (Ps. 49:11). *Chelam* refers to that which they acquired by their efforts and strength.²⁶ means strength. Hence I.E. explains that it also refers to that which a person acquires by his efforts.

²⁶ Chayil

BUT THOU SHALT REMEMBER. This means if the thought *My power and the might of my hand hath gotten me this wealth* should enter your mind, then remember the One who gives you power.

BECAUSE. The word *ekev* (because) refers to payment at the end. It refers both to reward²⁷ and to punishment.

AS A DEVOURING FIRE. Quickly.² Scripture therefore goes on to say *quickly*³..

### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 9:11:1

AT THE END.⁸. At the end or the beginning of the fortieth day.⁹ sometimes means at the beginning and sometimes at the end. See I.E. on Num. 13:25 (Vol. 4, pp. 104,105). Look. This verse shows that the golden calf was made on the day that the tablets were given to Moses.¹⁰

THAT TIME ALSO. For he had previously prayed for Israel when they were at the sea.¹³ *Wherefore criest thou unto me* (Ex. 14:15) is proof of the latter. Furthermore, Scripture states, *and he cried unto the Lord; and the Lord showed him a tree* (Ex. 15:25).

### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 9:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/9:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/9:22:1)


**ובתבערה.** יתכן להיותו שם מקום ולא חנו שם כי אם יום א' על כן לא הזכירום באלה מסעי והוא א' משלשה מסעים שנסעו מהד סיני עד קברות התאו' כאשר פירשתי. וטעם לחבר תבערה עם מסה כי היו העם מתאוננים כמו מנסים לשם והזכיר תבערה קודם מסה כי במסה לא אירע להם כלום וי״‎א כי בא עמלק בעבור שנסו השם ועל כן הזכיר בעירת אש השם בהם וזנוב עמלק ומכת המתאוים הזכיר בתחלה הקשה וי״‎א כי תבערה היא מסה כמו מראש שניר וחרמון והנכון מה שפירשתי:


AND AT TABERAH. Taberah is probably the name of a place. Israel camped there for but one day. Therefore Scripture does not mention it¹⁵. 38. The printed texts read “them,” in place of “it.” in the Torah portion *Elleh Mase* (These Are the Stages) (Num. 33:1).¹⁶ It is one of the thirty journeys that Israel traveled from Mount Sinai until they came to Kibroth-hattaavah (Num. 33:17),¹⁷ as I explained.¹⁸ The reason Scripture connects Taberah with Massah¹⁹ is that the people *were as murmurers* (Num. 11:1);²⁰ that is, they tested God.²¹ Scripture mentions Taberah before Massah²² because nothing happened to Israel at Massah.²³ Some say that Amalek came²⁴ because Israel tested God.²⁵ Scripture therefore mentions²⁶ the burning of God’s fire among them,²⁷ Amalek’s smiting of Israel’s hindmost,²⁸ and the smiting of those who lusted.²⁹ Scripture mentions the most severe punishments first.³⁰ Some say that Taberah and Massah are one and the same place.³¹ (These Are the Stages) (Num. 33:1). It is like *From the top of Senir and Hermon* (Cant. 4:8).³² However, my interpretation is correct.

footnotes:
¹⁵ So Vat. Ebr
¹⁶ Wherein the journeys of the children of Israel in the wilderness are recorded.
¹⁷ Which is located in Kadesh-barnea.
¹⁸ That Israel journeyed from Sinai to Kadesh-barnea (Sarim). See I.E. on Deut. 1:2.
¹⁹ Israel tested God at Massah after crossing the Red Sea. See Ex. 17:7. The incident at Taberah [where Israel murmured against God and God punished them by fire (Num. 11:3)] occurred more than a year later, after Israel had journeyed from Mount Sinai.
²⁰ In Taberah.
²¹ Which is what they did at Massah. Hence Taberah and Massah are mentioned back to back.
²² Even though Israel’s camping at Massah came before Taberah.
²³ Israel was not punished for testing God in Massah. They were punished at Taberah.
²⁴ To Rephidim and attacked Israel.
²⁵ In Rephidim. Thus Israel was punished for testing God at Massah. See Ex. 17:1-7.
²⁶ That is, mentions the places where the following punishments took place.
²⁷ At Taberah.
²⁸ At Massah.
²⁹ At Kibroth-hattaavah.
³⁰ Hence Taberah comes before Massah.
³¹ Hence Taberah is not mentioned among Israel’s journeys in the Torah portion Elleh Mase
³² Senir and Hermon are one and the same place. See Deut. 3:9.

## Chapter 10


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 10:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:1:1)


**בעת ההיא.** בארבעים היום הנזכרים:


AT THAT TIME. During the forty days that are mentioned.¹

footnotes:
¹ In Deut. 9:5.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 10:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:1:2)


**ארון עץ.** אמרו המעתיקים ז״‎ל כי ארון אחר עשה משה בתחלה והעד ואעש ארון ואח״‎כ אמר ואפסול ואמר ואשים את הלוחות בארון אשר עשיתי ודעתם רחבה מדעתנו כי היה נראה לנו כי וישכון כבוד ה' דבק עם הכבוד שראו הזקנים ולא נדע מתי עלה משה כי היה נראה בדרך הפשט כי משה לא נתענה כי אם שמונים יום כי פי' אנכי עמדתי בהר כימים הראשונים הוא שנוי והטעם כאשר עמדתי בהר אותם הארבעים יום שמע ה' אלי ואמר לך למסע וזהו לך נחה את העם והנה יהי' ארון עץ בצווי והוא הארון שעשה בצלאל:


AN ARK OF WOOD. The transmitters of tradition,² of blessed memory, said that Moses first³, Ex. 1:10, and Rashi. made another ark.⁴ Proof of the latter is, *So I made an ark* (v. 3), which is followed by *and hewed two tables of stone* (v. 3) *and put the tables in the ark which I had made* (v. 5). Now their minds are superior to our minds, for it appears to us⁵ that *And the glory of the Lord abode* (Ex. 24:16) is connected with the glory which the elders saw (Ex. 24:10).⁶ cloud…*And Moses entered in the midst of the cloud, and went up into the mount; and Moses was in the mount forty days and forty nights*. According to the rabbis this verse relates to the giving of Torah (see Rashi on Ex. 24:16). Also, according to the rabbis, the forty days which this verse states Moses spent on the mountain starts with the seventh of Sivan. There are 120 days between that day and Yom Kippur when, according to Rabbinic tradition, Moses descended with the second pair of tablets. Moses thus spent 120 days on Mount Sinai. See *Pirke de-Rabbi Eliezer*, chapter 46. I.E. disagrees. He believes that the plain meaning of the text does not allow one to conclude that Moses spent 120 days on Mount Sinai. The text merely says that the elders of Israel ascended Mount Sinai and had a vision of God (Ex. 24:1-12). It does not say which day they descended. Following this vision the cloud covered the mountain for seven days, after which God called Moses to ascend the mountain for 40 days. We thus do no know what day Moses ascended the mountain, and therefore, cannot calculate 120 days between revelation and Yom Kippur. Thus Moses did not necessarily spend 120 days on Mount Sinai. We do not know when Moses ascended,⁷ for according to the plain meaning of the text it appears that Moses fasted for only eighty days,⁸ is not a repetition of Deut. 9:25 but speaks of an additional 40 days. Furthermore, *as the first time forty days and forty nights* indicates that as he fasted the first time, so did he now. for the meaning of *Now I stayed in the mount* (v. 10) repeats what was said earlier.⁹ It means when I stayed on the mountain during those forty days¹⁰ God listened to me and said, *Arise, go before the people* (v. 11), the reference being to *And now go, lead the people* (Ex. 32:34). The making of the ark of wood (v. 3) was thus by command.¹¹ is to be interpreted, I commanded that an ark of wood be made. It refers to the ark made by Bezalel.¹²

footnotes:
² The Talmudic sages.
³ Before ascending Mount Sinai to receive the second tablets. See Tanchuma
⁴ That is, other than the one later made by Bezalel. Thus the ark mentioned in our verse is not to be identified with the ark made by Bezalel which was placed in the Holy of Holies (Ex. 25:1-22; 37:1-9). The sages believed that God gave Moses the second pair of tablets at the end of 80 days and that Moses made an ark to hold them temporarily. See Rashi on our verse.
⁵ Contrary to their opinion.
⁶ Ex 24:16-18 reads: And the glory of the Lord abode upon mount Sinai, and the cloud covered it six days; and the seventh day He called unto Moses out of the midst of the
⁷ To receive the tablets.
⁸ According to the rabbis, Now I stayed in the mount, as the first time forty days and forty nights
⁹ In Deut. 9:25.
¹⁰ Mentioned in Deut. 9:25.
¹¹ So I made an ark of wood
¹² See Ex. 37:1-9.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 10:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:3:1)


**ואעש ארון.** בצווי ויהיה פי' ויקהל משה וכבר הקהיל משה וכבר הראיתיך רבים כן וכל זה בעבור שאמר ויהיו שם כאשר צוני ה' וזהו ונתת אל הארון את העדות גם יתכן להיות פירושו על ועשית לך ארון עץ והקבלה תנצח:


SO I MADE AN ARK. By command.¹³ The meaning of *And Moses assembled* (Ex. 35:1) should be interpreted, and Moses had assembled.¹⁴ as And Moses had assembled Israel; that is, Moses gathered Israel and commanded them to make the tabernacle before he ascended Mount Sinai a second time to receive the second tablets. I have already shown you many such instances.¹⁵ All of this is necessitated¹⁶ by Scripture’s statement, *and there they are, as the Lord commanded me* (v. 5).¹⁷ This¹⁸. is what *And thou shalt put into the ark the testimony* (Ex. 25:16) refers to. It is also possible that it¹⁹ (v. 5). refers to *and make thee an ark of wood*.²⁰ The tradition of the sages shall triumph.

footnotes:
¹³ By commanding Bezalel to make it.
¹⁴ Ex. 35:1 tells us that Moses gathered the people and told them to make the tabernacle and its furniture, the ark included. Ex. 35 follows the account of the giving of the second tablets, which is recorded in Ex. 34. Now, since I.E. believes that the ark spoken of in our chapter is to be identified with the ark made by Bezalel, he interprets And Moses assembled
¹⁵ Where a perfect is to be taken as a pluperfect. See Vol. 1, p. XVI.
¹⁶ I.E.’s interpretation.
¹⁷ This indicates that our verse deals with the ark in which the tablets were placed permanently; the ark in which Moses placed the tablets.
¹⁸ As the Lord commanded me
¹⁹ As the Lord commanded me
²⁰ In other words, verse 5 does not refer to the command recorded in Ex. 25:16. It thus does not speak of the ark made by Bezalel. Therefore our verse does not contradict the Rabbinic tradition.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 10:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:6:1)


**ובני ישראל נסעו מבארות בני יעקב מוסרה.** נכנס זה הפסוק במקום הזה בעבור שאמר ואתפלל גם בעד אהרן בעת ההיא ואמר כי לא מת אהרן רק בשנת הארבעים והעד שכן פירושו שאמר בעת ההיא הבדיל ה' את שבט לוי וזה היה בהר סיני כאשר פירשתי בפרשת וידבר וקדמונינו אמרו שנסעו לאחור ומה נעש' במלת שם מת אהרן ויקבר שם ור' יצחק אבן גיאת ז״‎ל הספרדי פי' שם כמו אז וכמוהו משם רוע' ואין צורך רק לפי דעתי כי מבארות בני יעקן איננו בני יעקן גם מוסרה איננו מוסרות רק קדש יקרא בארות בני יעקן ושם מדבר הר ההר מוסרה ורבים כן הלא תראה הכתוב אמר מדבר שור ובפסוק אחר מדבר איתם:


AND THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL JOURNEYED FROM BEEROTH-BENEJAAKAN TO MOSERAH; THERE AARON DIED. This verse is placed here²¹ because Moses said, *And I prayed for Aaron also the same time* (Deut. 9:20).²², Moses indicated that Aaron did not go along with those who worshipped the calf. Moses indicated that Aaron did not die until the fortieth year (Num. 33:38).²³ Moses’ statement, *At that time the Lord separated the tribe of Levi* (v. 8), which took place at Mount Sinai²⁴ as I explained in the Torah portion which opens with *And The Lord Spoke* (Num. 1:1),²⁵ is proof that this is its meaning.²⁶ The early authorities²⁷ tell us that Israel backtracked.²⁸, chapter 1, Hal. 10; *Yoma*, Chap 1, Hal. 1; *Seder Olam*, chapter 9. However, what shall we do with the clause, *there Aaron died, and there he was buried*?²⁹ Rabbi Isaac ibn Giat the Spaniard,³⁰ of blessed memory, says that the meaning of *sham* (there) is then.³¹ He says that *mi-sham* (from thence) in *misham ro’eh* (from thence, from the shepherd) (Gen. 49:24) is similar. There is no need for this interpretation. On the contrary, I believe that Beeroth-benejaakan is not to be identified with Bene-jaakkan; neither is Moserah the same as Moserot (Num. 33:31).³² In reality Kadesh³³ is called the wilderness of Beeroth-benejaakan, and the wilderness of Mount Hor is called Moserah.³⁴ There are many such examples.³⁵ Do you not see that Scripture says the wilderness of Shur (Ex. 15:22)? However, in another place Scripture refers to it as the wilderness of Etam (Num. 33:18).³⁶

footnotes:
²¹ Amidst the account of the golden calf.
²² Saying, And I prayed for Aaron also at that time
²³ And not after the incident of the golden calf, for God accepted Moses’ prayer.
²⁴ Not after the death of Aaron.
²⁵ See I.E. on Num. 3:1.
²⁶ That our verse is a parenthetical statement.
²⁷ The rabbis of the Talmud.
²⁸ According to Num. 20:22-29, Aaron died at Mount Hor, not at Moserah. Furthermore, Num. 33:31 tells us that Israel journeyed from Moseroth to Bene-jaakan. The rabbis identify Bene-jaakan with Beeroth-benejaakan and Moserah with Moseroth. Additionally, the rabbis identify Hor-haggidgad (Num. 33:32) with Gudgod (v. 7). Thus according to the rabbis, verses 6 and 7 contradict Num. 33:31,32. The rabbis solve the contradictions by saying that Israel backtracked from Mount Hor to Moserah. See Rashi: “Moses reproved Israel saying that when Aaron died on Mount Hor at the end of 40 years and the clouds of glory departed you feared war with the king of Arad, and you appointed a leader that you might return to Egypt, and you turned backwards 8 stages unto Bene-jaakan and from there to Moserah. There the sons of Levi fought with you, and they slew some of you, and you some of them, until they forced you back on the road along which you had retreated. From Moserah you returned to Gudgod, (Num. 33:33), that is identical with Hor-haggidgad (v. 8).” Also, see Yerushalmi Sotah
²⁹ Which indicates that Aaron died and was buried at Moserah.
³⁰ An eleventh-twelfth century scholar. He was a student of Rabbi Samuel Ha-Nagid and the teacher of Rabbi Moses ibn Ezra.
³¹ At that time.
³² Thus our verse does not contradict Num. 33:31.
³³ Not Bene-jaakan, for Num. 33:37 relates that Israel journeyed from Kadesh to Mount Hor.
³⁴ Hence our verse does not contradict Num. 20:22-29.
³⁵ Where a place has two names and Scripture refers to it in one place by one name and in another place by another name.
³⁶ See I.E. on Ex. 15:22 (Vol. 1, p. 309).



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 10:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:7:1)


**משם נסעו הגדגדה.** איננו חור הגדגד רק הוא שם כלל צלמונה גם פונון ואובות:


FROM THENCE THEY JOURNEYED UNTO GUDGOD. The latter is not to be identified with Hor-haggidgad (Num. 33:32).³⁷ On the contrary, it is a general term for Zalmonah and also for Punon and Oboth (Num. 33:43,44).³⁸

footnotes:
³⁷ To which Israel journeyed from Bene-jaakkan.
³⁸ Numbers tells us that Israel journeyed from Mount Hor to Zalmonah, Punon and Oboth (Num. 33:43,44).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 10:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/10:7:2)


ו**יטבתה** הוא המקום הנקרא באר כי כן כתוב ומשם בארה וזה טעם **ארץ נחלי מים** כי במשענותם כרו הנדיבים ויצאו המים וזה הפסוק שהוא ומשם נסעו הגדגדה דבק עם ויכהן אלעזר והנה פירשתי לך כי ישראל לא הקריבו זבחים אחר נסעם מהר סיני ויתכן שהקריבום בבואם אל צלמונה ופונן והיתה תחלת כהונת אלעזר בכהונה גדולה באותם המקומות בראש השנה ושמש במקום הנקרא באר ביום הכפורים והנה הפירוש כן ויכהן אלעזר כאשר נסעו אל מקום פלוני ולמה כהן אלעזר כי השם הבדיל בעת עבודת העגל את שבט הלוי לשאת את הארון לעמוד לפני השם:


[TO JOTBAH.] Jotbah is the place that is also called Beer,³⁹ (well). It is not to be identified with Jotbah in Num. 33:33 to which Israel journeyed from Hor-haggidgad. for Scripture reads, *And from thence to Beer* (Num. 21:16). This is what a *land of brooks of water* (v. 7) refers to, for the nobles of the people dug it with their staves and water came forth (Num. 21:18). This verse, namely, *From thence they journeyed unto Gudgod* is connected to *and Eleazar…ministered* (v. 6).⁴⁰ Now, I have already explained to you that Israel did not offer sacrifices after they journeyed from Mount Sinai.⁴¹ It is possible that they offered them when they came to Zalmonah⁴² is connected to *and Eleazar…* it follows that Eleazar started to serve as high priest there. and Punon and that the beginning of Eleazar’s service as high priest started in those places on Rosh Ha-Shanah.⁴³ (high priest). He ministered in a place called Beer⁴⁴ on Yom Kippur.⁴⁵. In other words, verses 6 and 7 are to be interpreted, and Eleazar ministered in Gudgod and in Jotbah. The meaning of our verses⁴⁶ thus is, And Eleazar ministered when they came to such and such a place.⁴⁷ Why did Eleazar minister? Because when Israel worshipped the calf, God separated the tribe of Levi to carry the ark and to stand before the Lord.⁴⁸ does not refer to when Israel came to Jotbah but rather to what happened at Sinai (v. 8).

WITH THREE SCORE AND TEN. I have already explained this.⁶²

footnotes:
⁶² See I.E. on Gen. 46:23 (Vol. 1, pp. 403-406). There I.E notes that Jacob is included among the 70, for if we do not count Jacob among the 70, then the number of those who descended into Egypt comes to 69. Most printed editions conclude with what we have as I.E.’s comment on 11:1.



## Chapter 11


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:1:1)


ואחר שהוא הרב' אתכם חייבים אתם לאהוב אותו והוסיף כל הימי':


THEREFORE THOU SHALT LOVE THE LORD. Since the Lord multiplied you, you are obligated to love Him. Our verse adds *always*.¹

footnotes:
¹ Thus our verse does not merely repeat Deut. 10:12.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:2:1)


**כי לא את בניכם.** התשובה היא כי עיניכם הרואות והטעם כי אלה הנוראות אינם לעתיד שיראום בניכם רק אתם ראיתם אותם:


I SPEAK NOT WITH YOUR CHILDREN THAT HAVE NOT KNOWN. The answer is², *but your eyes have seen* (v. 7).³ It⁴ (v. 7). means these wonders are not future phenomena which your children shall see. On the contrary, you yourselves have witnessed them.

footnotes:
² The question raised by our verse is, “If God did not speak with their children, to whom was He speaking?”
³ In other words, verse 7 concludes the thought raised in our verse. I.E.’s point is that our verse is to be understood, I speak not with your children…but with you whose eyes have seen the great deeds that God wrought.
⁴ …but your eyes have seen


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:2:2)


**את גדלו את ידו החזקה וזרועו הנטויה.** להוציא אתכם ממצרים:


HIS GREATNESS, HIS MIGHTY HAND, AND HIS OUTSTRETCHED ARM. In taking you out of Egypt.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:3:1)


**ואת אותותיו ואת מעשיו.** לפרעה שהוא עיקר:


AND HIS SIGNS, AND HIS WORKS. *Unto Pharaoh*, for he was most important.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Our verse mentions Pharaoh, either because he was the most important Egyptian or because he was the main source of Israel’s trouble (Weiser).



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:4:1)


**אשר הציף.** מגזרת צפו מים על ראשי:


HE MADE…TO OVERFLOW. The word *hetzif* (made to overflow) is related to the word *tzafu* (flowed)⁶. in *Waters flowed over my head* (Lam. 3:54).

footnotes:
⁶ From the root tzadi, vav, feh


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:4:2)


**ויאבדם.** הטעם כי לא קמו עוד בניהם תחתיהם להיות כמוהם:


DESTROYED THEM. Its meaning is, their children did not arise in their place to be like them.⁷. However, the Egyptians were not wiped off the face of the earth. Hence I.E.’s interpretation that the meaning of our verse is, God destroyed the type of Egyptians who enslaved and pursued the Israelites.

footnotes:
⁷ Our verse tells us that God destroyed the Egyptians unto this day



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:5:1)


**ואשר עשה לכם במדבר.** דבר המן:


AND WHAT HE DID UNTO YOU IN THE WILDERNESS. The reference is to the manna.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:6:1)


**ואת בתיהם.** הם נשיהם ובניהם:


AND THEIR HOUSEHOLDS.⁸ The reference is to their wives and to their children.

footnotes:
⁸ Literally, and their houses.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:6:2)


**ואת כל היקום.** שם כלל:


AND EVERY LIVING SUBSTANCE. *Yekum* (substance) is an all-inclusive term.⁹ usually applies to living creatures. I.E. points out that here it applies to all sorts of property.

footnotes:
⁹ Yekum


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:6:3)


**אשר ברגליהם.** בעבורם או כמשמעם ואחר שעיניכם ראו את כל אלה השמרו שתשמרו מצות השם:


THAT FOLLOWED THEM. *Be-raglehem* (that followed them) means because of them.¹⁰ literally means at their feet. Hence I.E.’s comment. On the other hand, the term may be taken literally.¹¹ Since you have seen all of this,¹² take heed so that you observe God’s commandments.¹³

footnotes:
¹⁰ Be-raglehem
¹¹ That is, those who walked in their footsteps, that is, those that followed them.
¹² The acts of God described in verses 1-6.
¹³ I.E.’s paraphrase of verses 7 and 8.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:8:1)


**וירשתם.** שבור הרי״‎ש בעבור שבר הוי״‎ו:


AND POSSESS. The *resh* in *vi-yerishtem* (and possess) is vocalized with a *chirik*¹⁴ is the word *yerashtem* plus the conversive *vav*. Thus our word should have read *vi-yerashtem*. Hence I.E.’s comment. because the *vav*¹⁵. is vocalized with a *chirik*.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Rather than with a pattach. Vi-yerishtem
¹⁵ Of vi-rishtem



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:10:1)


**כי הארץ.** הנה טעם אחר שאתם חייבים לשמור מצותיו כי הארץ איננה כארץ מצרים רק יש לה צורך אל השנה לשום השם עינו עליה והטעם בדרך משל כאדם שיראה כל מה שהיא צריכה ויתן הכל בעתו:


FOR THE LAND. Here is another reason why you are obligated to observe God’s commandments. The land is not like the land of Egypt. On the contrary, the land needs God to keep His eye on it.¹⁶ The meaning of our verse can be understood with the following parable. The Lord will act like a person who sees all that the land needs and will give everything in its time.

footnotes:
¹⁶ The land requires God’s supervision.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:12:1)


**מרשית.** בלא אל״‎ף ורבים כן ע״‎כ כ' והיה אם שמע תשמעו ונתתי מטר ארצכם וזהו עיני ה' אלהיך בה:


FROM THE BEGINNING. *Me-reshit* (from the beginning) is spelled without an *alef*.¹⁷ is usually spelled with an *alef*. There are many such instances.¹⁸ is dropped. Scripture therefore¹⁹ goes on to say, *And it shall come to pass* (v. 13); and *I will give the rain of your land in its season* (v. 14).²⁰ This is what Scripture means by the eyes of *the Lord are always upon it*.²¹

footnotes:
¹⁷ The world reshit
¹⁸ Where a silent alef
¹⁹ That is, because the land requires God’s supervision.
²⁰ That is, Scripture states that God will give rain in its season if Israel observes God’s laws, because the land requires God’s supervision. God will care for the land if Israel observes His commandments.
²¹ It means that God looks upon the land to see whether the inhabitants are observing His laws.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:14:1)


**יורה.** גשם שיורה על שנה טובה והוא בתחלת השנה:


THE FORMER RAIN. The word *yoreh* (former rain) refers to rain which indicates²² literally means to show or teach. a good year. It comes at the beginning of the year.²³

footnotes:
²² The word yoreh
²³ The early rain is an indicator of an abundance of rain.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:14:2)


**ומלקוש.** ידוע:


AND THE LATTER RAIN. The meaning of *malkosh* (the latter rain) is known.²⁴ refers to the early rain, then *malkosh* must refer to the later rain.

footnotes:
²⁴ If yoreh



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:15:1)


**ואכלת ושבעת.** שב אל דגנך ותירושך לא אל הקרוב שהוא עשב בשדך:


AND THOU SHALT EAT AND BE SATISFIED. This refers to *thy corn, and thy wine* (v. 14), not to that which comes immediately before it²⁵ namely, *grass in thy fields*.

footnotes:
²⁵ Literally, near it.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:17:1)


**יבולה.** פירשתיו:


HER FRUIT. I have previously explained the meaning of *yevulah* (her fruit).²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ See I.E. on Lev. 26:4.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:17:2)


**ואבדתם מהרה.** שיגלו ממנה כאובדים ברעב אחר שהיתה הארץ טובה על כן אתם חייבים שתשימו אלה הדברים על לבבכם:


AND YE PERISH QUICKLY. They will be exiled from the land like those who perish in hunger.²⁷ Now, since the land is good, you are therefore obligated to take these words to heart.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁷ In other words, you will be driven from the land by hunger.
²⁸ So that you are not exiled from the land.



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:18:1)


**לטוטפות.** מפורש ואם תשימון על לב לא תאבדון מעל הארץ רק תאריכו ימים כימי השמים על הארץ:


FOR FRONTLETS. Its meaning has been explained.²⁹ If you will take My words to heart, you will not be exiled from the land, and your days upon the land shall be as long as the days of the heaven above the earth.

footnotes:
²⁹ See I.E. on Deut. 6:8.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:1)


**כי אם שמור תשמרון.** הטעם זה שאמרתי לכם שתאבדון מהרה מעל הארץ הטובה דעו כי לא תכנסו שם בתחלה ולא תנצחו אם לא תשמרו מצות ה':


FOR IF YE SHALL DILIGENTLY KEEP. The meaning of our verse is: I told you that you will quickly be driven from the good land.³⁰ Know that you will not initially enter the land, and you will not be victorious if you do not keep the commandments of God.³¹

footnotes:
³⁰ If you worship idols.
³¹ The section dealing with exile from the land precedes our section. Scripture in effect says: not only will you be exiled from the land if in the future you worship idols, but if you do so now, you will not even be permitted to enter the land.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:2)


**לאהבה.** בלב:


TO LOVE. With your heart.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:22:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:3)


**ללכת בכל דרכיו.** שלא ישנה ולא יהפוך:


TO WALK IN ALL HIS WAYS. Not to change or overturn.³²

footnotes:
³² God’s laws.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:22:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:22:4)


**ולדבקה בו.** בסוף והוא סוד גדול:


AND TO CLEAVE UNTO HIM. Ultimately.³³ This is a great secret.³⁴, p. 143.

footnotes:
³³ When the soul leaves the body.
³⁴ According to I.E. cleaving to God entails leading a life wherein one strictly observes God’s commandments, masters the sciences, learns about the throne of glory, and masters the secret of the chariot seen by Ezekiel. See The Secret of the Torah



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:26:1)


**ראה.** לכל אחד ידבר:


BEHOLD. Moses speaks to each individual.³⁵ (behold) is in the singular. That which follows is in the plural. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
³⁵ The word re’eh



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:27:1)


**את הברכה אשר תשמעו.** כי בשמעכם הנה אתם מבורכים והברכה תוספת טובה והקללה מגרע' והיא מגזרת קלה:


THE BLESSING, IF YE SHALL HEARKEN. For if you hearken, behold, you are blessed.³⁶ A blessing means an addition of the good.³⁷ A curse means a diminution.³⁸ The word *kelalah* (curse) is related to the word *kallah* (light).³⁹

footnotes:
³⁶ If you observe the commandments, you will automatically be blessed.
³⁷ Such as children, wealth, strength, health, and the like. See I.E. on Gen. 2:3 (Vol. 1, p. 50).
³⁸ Of the good. See I.E. on Gen. 3:14 and the notes thereto (Vol. 1, p. 70).
³⁹ When one is cursed, the good which he has is taken away. He is therefore “lighter.”



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:29:1)


**ונתתה את הברכה.** בפה כמו ונתן אותם על ראש השעיר:


THAT THOU SHALT SET THE BLESSING. Verbally,⁴⁰ means utter the blessings. as in *and he shall put them*⁴¹ *upon the head of the goat* (Lev. 16:21).⁴²

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Set the blessing
⁴¹ The sins of Israel.
⁴² One cannot put sins on a goat. Hence it means to confess the sins over the goat.



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:1)


**בעבר הירדן.** כנגד המדבר:


BEYOND THE JORDAN. To one standing on the side of the wilderness.⁴³ generally applies to the eastern bank of the Jordan. I.E. points out that here it applies to the western bank of the Jordan. It is also called beyond the Jordan to one standing on the eastern bank, which contains the wilderness.

footnotes:
⁴³ The eastern bank of the Jordan, where Israel presently was. Beyond the Jordan


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:2)


**אחרי דרך מבוא השמש.** וטעם שתלכו אחרי דרך מערב ותמצאום:


BEHIND THE WAY OF THE GOING DOWN OF THE SUN. This means when you follow the western road you will find them.⁴⁴

footnotes:
⁴⁴ The mountains.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:3)


**בארץ הכנעני היושב בערבה.** כי יש כנעני יושב בהר:


IN THE LAND OF THE CANAANITES THAT DWELL IN THE ARABAH. For there are Canaanites who dwell in the mountain.⁴⁵

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Hence Scripture delineates which group of Canaanites it speaks of.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:30:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:4)


**מול הגלגל.** וההרים ארוכים והנה צוה באיזה מקום מההרים. **מול הגלגל** כמו וירדוף עד דן או דרך נבואה או שני שמות:


OVER AGAINST GILGAL. The mountains are long.⁴⁶ Look. Moses specified the place⁴⁷ by command. *Over against Gilgal* is similar to *and pursued as far as Dan* (Gen. 14:14).⁴⁸ It is possible that it was prophetically stated,⁴⁹ or we are dealing with two names.⁵⁰

footnotes:
⁴⁶ Hence Scripture delineates which part of the mountain is to be involved in the ritual.
⁴⁷ Of the ritual.
⁴⁸ Gilgal received its name in the days of Joshua. See Josh. 5:9. The question thus arises how could Moses employ a name which was not in existence during his lifetime. Hence I.E. points out that our verse is similar to Gen. 14:14. There, Scripture uses the term Dan with regard to an episode in the life of Abraham, even though the place could not have had this name in Abraham’s time, for Dan was named after the son of Jacob.
⁴⁹ That Moses used the name Gilgal prophetically.
⁵⁰ Two places with the same name. Gilgal in Josh. 5:9 is not the same as the Gilgal mentioned in our verse. The same would apply to Dan in Gen. 14:14.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:30:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:5)


**מול.** כמו נכח:


OVER AGAINST. The word *mul* means over against.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:30:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:30:6)


**אלוני מורה.** הוא הנזכר בימי אברהם. והברכה והקללה עוד יפרשם:


BESIDE THE TEREBINTHS OF MOREH. This is the same place as mentioned in the days of Abraham.⁵¹ Scripture will later on state the blessings and the curses.

footnotes:
⁵¹ See Gen. 12:6.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 11:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/11:31:1)

## Chapter 12


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 12:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:2:1)


**אבד תאבדון את כל המקומו**'. אמר רבי יונה המדקדק כי את אלהיהם חסר וי״‎ו והטעם ואת אלהיהם ולא אמר כלום רק הוא דבק עם מלת אשר עבדו שם:


YE SHALL SURELY DESTROY ALL THE PLACES…THEIR GODS. Rabbi Jonah the grammarian says that *et elohehem* (their gods) is lacking a *vav*, for the meaning of the phrase is, and their gods.¹ However, he contributes nothing.² is connected to *asher avedu sham* (wherein they served). *Et elohehem* is most certainly connected to *asher avedu sham* (wherein they served).³ (their gods) not *ve-et elohehem* (and their gods). I.E. feels that there is no reason to interpret *et* as if written *ve-et*.

footnotes:
¹ Rabbi Jonah maintains that our verse should be read: Ye shall surely destroy all the places wherein the nations that ye are to dispossess served, “and” their gods. He interprets this as meaning, You shall surely destroy all the places wherein the nations that you are to dispossess worshipped. You shall also destroy their gods.
² For as I.E. goes on to note, et elohehem
³ For our text reads, et elohehem


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 12:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:2:2)


**כל עץ רענן.** רענן ושאנן לעולם בשני נוני״‎ן והטעם כמו רטוב:


EVERY LEAFY TREE. The words *ra’anan* (leafy) and *sha’anan* (at peace) are always spelled with two *nuns*.⁴ is *shin, alef, nun* and that of *ra’anan is resh, ayin, nun*. Hence he points out that in *ra’anan* and *sha’anan* the final root letter is doubled. See Radak, *Sefer Ha-sharoshim*. The meaning of *ra’anan* is wet.⁵

⁴ I.E. apparently believed that the root of sha’anan
⁵ Full of sap, as in a leafy tree.

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 12:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:8:1)


ואתם **לא תעשון ככל אשר אנחנו עושים פה.** בעבור שהיו נוסעים ממסע אל מסע ויש מי שיקריב עולה במסע שירצה אחר מות אהרן ויש מי שיתן בכור צאנו ויש מי שלא יתן כי המצוה תלויה בארץ ואין זאת דעתי רק טעמו שלא היו כולם יראי השם ובעבור שאמר אנחנו הנה שם מצות רבות כעולות תלויות בארץ כי כן כתוב:


YE SHALL NOT DO AFTER ALL THAT WE DO HERE THIS DAY. Scripture states this because they were journeying from station to station.⁸ After Aaron’s death some would sacrifice a burnt offering in any one of the stations that he desired to do so.⁹ Some would present the first-born of the flock. Others did not, for the commandment¹⁰ is contingent upon the Land of Israel.¹¹ However, this is not my opinion. Its meaning,¹². rather, is that not all of them feared God. Now, Moses said “we”¹³ because, as Scripture clearly states, he made the many commandments regarding the burnt offering contingent upon the Land of Israel.¹⁴

footnotes:
⁸ On their way to the Land of Israel.
⁹ After Aaron’s death the bringing of sacrifices was optional. Hence some brought sacrifices; others did not.
¹⁰ To offer sacrifices. After Aaron’s death the bringing of sacrifices was optional. Hence some brought sacrifices others did not. Thus every man did whatsoever was right in his eyes.
¹¹ Hence Scripture tells the Israelites that once they enter the Land of Israel, they must offer the sacrifices that Scripture commands.
¹² The meaning of, Ye shall not do after all that we do here this day. Every man whatsoever is right in his own eyes
¹³ Even though Moses was not included among those who did not fear the Lord. The first part of the verse, where the term “we” is found, applies to the laws which Israel did not observe in the wilderness.
¹⁴ The burnt offering is representative of all laws in force only in the Land of Israel.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 12:9:1

**אל המנוחה** שלא יסעו הם ובניהם וזה טעם נחלה והאמת שפירוש **מנוחה** והניח לכם. **והנחלה וישבתם בטח**:


TO THE REST. So that they and their children do not have to travel.¹⁵ This is the meaning of inheritance.¹⁶) explains rest (*menuchah*). *Nachalah* refers to a place of final settlement. So does *menuchah*. However, actually the meaning of *the rest* is, *and giveth you rest* (v. 10),¹⁷ and *the inheritance* refers to *so that ye dwell in safety* (v. 10).¹⁸ refers to being in the land. According to the second interpretation it refers to living securely in the land.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 12:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:13:1)


וטעם לומר פעם שנית **בכל מקום אשר תראה.** להוצי' בשר תאוה בעבור שהשלמים הם קדש ויש כרת על טמא שאכל בשר קדש אמר על בשר תאוה הטמא והטהור:


Scripture repeats *in every place that thou seest*²¹ (v. 13) because it wants to exclude meat eaten out of desire.²² Scripture states the unclean and the clean (v. 15) with regard to meat eaten out of desire because peace offerings are sacred and one who eats sacred meat in a state of impurity incurs the penalty of excision.²³

²¹ After having stated this already in verse 5.

AS OF THE GAZELLE, AND AS OF THE HART. Which are not offered on the altar. The fat of the gazelle and also that of the hart, is permitted. Look, this is proof of my words.²⁴ However, we rely on the tradition of our fathers.²⁵

**לאכול בשעריך מעשר דגנך.** עם הלוים ידבר גם עם ישראל במעשר שני על דעת רבים:


THOU MAYEST NOT EAT WITHIN THY GATES THE TITHE OF THY CORN. Moses speaks to the Levites.²⁶ According to many he also speaks to the Israelites regarding the second tithe.²⁷

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 12:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/12:17:2)


**ובכורות בקרך וצאנך** עם הכהנים ידבר כי הלוים גם הכהנים הם בכלל ישראל כמו ואצו אתכם שידבר עם שני שבטים וחצי וכן אך פדה תפדה עם ישראל ידבר והטעם מי שהוא חייב לפדות וכן לאכול בשעריך לפני ה' אלהיך תאכלנו מי שהוא חייב לאכלו והמכחשים אמרו כי הם שנים בכורים האחד בכור פטר רחם והשני בכור עדר ואין צורך להשיב על הבליהם גם אחרים אמרו כי הבכור והמעשר יאכלם הישראל ואם היה במקום הנבחר וזה הפך הכתוב:


OR THE FIRSTLINGS OF THY HERD OR OF THY FLOCK. Moses speaks to the *kohanim*,²⁸ for the Levites and also the *kohanim* are included among the Israelites.²⁹. Compare, *And I commanded you* (Deut. 3:18) wherein Moses speaks to two and a half tribes. Similarly, Moses speaks to Israel when he says, *the first-born of man shalt thou surely redeem* (Num. 18:15), which means the one who is obligated to redeem.³⁰ Similarly, *Thou mayest not eat within thy gates*³¹ and *but thou shalt eat them before the Lord thy God* (v. 18) refer to the one who is obligated to eat.³² The heretics³³ say that there are two sorts of first-born. One is the first to break out of its mother’s womb. The second is the first-born of the flock.³⁴ There is no need to respond to their nonsense. There are also others who say that if an Israelite is in the chosen place, then he eats the first-born and the tithe. However, this is the reverse of what Scripture says.³⁵

footnotes:
²⁸ Only they may eat of the first-born.
²⁹ Hence Scripture does not specify that it is speaking to the Levites or the kohanim
³⁰ The import of the verse is, those among you who are obligated to redeem, shall redeem. Similarly, the previous verse speaks only to those Israelites to whom it applies.
³¹ In our verse.
³² Not to all Israelites.
³³ The Karaites.
³⁴ Which any Israelite may eat.
³⁵ Elsewhere Scripture states that an Israelite may not eat the first tithe or the first -born of the flock and sheep.

ואמר **כי תאוה לאכול בשר** פירוש אם תאוה נפשך לאכול בשר וכבר הזכיר זה להוסיף כי ירחק ממך המקום וגם זה הכתוב על מקום שאיננו מארץ כנען ומשפטו כמשפט שעריך:


BECAUSE THY SOUL DESIRETH TO EAT FLESH. This means, if your soul desires to eat flesh.³⁶ (because) has the meaning of if here. Moses already mentioned this.³⁷ He repeats himself in order to add, *If the place…be too far from thee* (v. 21).³⁸ Furthermore, this verse speaks of a place that is not part of the land of Canaan.³⁹ Its law is the same as the law within your gates.⁴⁰

³⁶ In other words, the term ki

## Chapter 13


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:2:1)


**כי יקום בקרבך נביא.** טעם בקרבך כי אין נביא כי אם מישראל וזאת הפרשה דבקה בעבור עכו״‎ם כי שרפת הבנים ע״‎ג. נביא שיאמר כי בהקיץ דבר השם או שלוחו עמו או בחלום ויש אומרים כי יתכן להיות הנביא ממגנבי דבר השם ופי' שאמר נביא האמת שיהי' אות כך להצדיקו ושמע השומע והגידו להיות אות לנפשו ויש אומרים כי אפילו בא האות והמופת אין להאמין בו כי הוא דבר הפך שקול הדעת ולפי דעתי שיש אות גם מופת כמו סימן והעד דברי ישעיה שאמר הנה אנכי והילדים אשר נתן לי ה' לאותות ולמופתים ואות הנביא כאשר הלך עבדי ישעיה ערום ויחף ואותות בניו שמותם על דבר המקרה שיהיה בימיהם והם עמנואל מהר שלל חש בז ושאר ישוב ובמכות והיו לך לאות ולמופת וכן רבי':


IF THERE ARISE IN THE MIDST OF THEE. Scripture reads *in the midst of thee* because there is no prophet except from among Israel.¹ This section is connected to the previous one² because they both deal with idol worship, for the burning of the children (Deut. 12:31) was an act of idol worship. Our verse deals with a prophet who claims that God, or God’s messenger, spoke to him while he was awake or in a dream. Some say that it is possible that the prophet³ was one of those who steal God’s word (Jer. 26:30). They explain that a true prophet said that a certain sign would appear to verify his word.⁴ Someone heard this and related the sign so that it should bear witness for him.⁵ Others say that even if the sign or a wonder came to pass⁶ we do not believe him,⁷ for what he says⁸ contradicts reason.⁹, Treatise III. I believe that the terms wonder¹⁰. and sign¹¹. apply also¹² (Deut. 4:34). to symbolic acts.¹³ (wonder) and *ot* (sign) usually refer to a miraculous act such as changing a rod into a serpent or water into blood. However, it may also refer to an act which illustrates what the prophet predicts will happen. According to I.E. the sign spoken of in our verse refers to the latter. Hence Scripture speaks of the sign coming to pass; i.e., the act played out by the prophet came to be. Compare, the words of the prophet Isaiah: *Behold, I and the children whom the Lord hath given me shall be for signs and for wonders* (Is. 8:18); the sign of the prophet¹⁴ in *Like as My servant Isaiah hath walked naked and barefoot* (Is. 20:3);¹⁵ the names of his children, which served as signs regarding things that would occur in their lifetime, namely, Emanuel (Is. 7:14); Maher-shalal-hash-baz (Is. 8:3); Shearjashub (Is. 7:3). Also Scripture’s statement with regard to the plagues,¹⁶ *And they shall be upon thee for a sign and for a wonder* (Deut. 28:46). There are many such instances.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹ Prophesy is found only among Israelites. I.E. considered Balaam an astrologer who claimed to be a prophet. See I.E. on Num. 22:28 (Vol. 4, p. 186). This is also the opinion of Rabbi Yehudah Ha-Levi. See Book I of the Kuzari.
² Deut. 12:29-31.
³ Spoken of in our verse.
⁴ The true prophet gave a sign to show that what he said was true.
⁵ The false prophet used the sign given by the true prophet for his own purposes.
⁶ By the prophet.
⁷ The prophet.
⁸ His call to serve other gods.
⁹ See Rabbi Saadiah Gaon, Sefer Ha-Emunot Ve-Ha-De’ot
¹⁰ Hebrew, mofet
¹¹ Hebrew, ot
¹² In addition to referring to supernatural events, as in Or hath God assayed to go and take Him a nation from the midst of another nation, by trials, by signs, and by wonders
¹³ The terms mofet
¹⁴ Isaiah gave his sons names which alluded to that which would occur in the future. Thus he named one son Immanuel (God is with us) to indicate that Judah would survive. He named another son Shear-jashub (a remnant shall return) to indicate that the remnant of Judah will repent and return to God.
¹⁵ Isaiah walked naked and barefoot in Jerusalem to indicate that the people of Judah will be led into exile barefoot and naked.
¹⁶ Which would befall Israel.
¹⁷ Where signs and wonders are used in the sense of omens.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:4:1)


וטעם **כי מנסה.** בעבור שעזבו ולא המיתו ונסיון השם להראות צדקת המנוסה:


[PUTTETH YOU TO PROOF.] Scripture says, *for the Lord…putteth you to proof*, because God left the prophet¹⁸ alone and did not kill him. The purpose of God’s test is to show the righteousness of the one who is being tried.¹⁹, 111:24. Also see I.E. on Gen. 22:1 (Vol. 1, p. 223).

footnotes:
¹⁸ Who tried to lead the Jews into idolatry.
¹⁹ So too Maimonides, Guide for the Perplexed



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:1)


**אחרי ה' אלהיכם תלכו.** שתעשו על פי יכלתכם כמעשיו ותרדפו אחרי נתיבותיו:


AFTER THE LORD YOUR GOD SHALL YE WALK. You shall do your utmost to imitate God’s deeds and pursue His paths.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:2)


**ואותו תיראו.** מלשאול למה זה:


AND HIM SHALL YE FEAR. You shall refrain from asking why this is so.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ A person must observe the commandments even if he does not understand the reasons for them.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:3)


**ואת מצותיו תשמורו.** שהם העיקר:


AND HIS COMMANDMENTS SHALL YE KEEP. The commandments which are most important.²¹. The most important “commandments are those precepts that are implanted in the heart, They are not contingent on place, time or anything else…[they] were known by reason before the Torah was given through the agency of Moses…The decalogue, with the exclusion of the Sabbath, is an example” (Strickman translation, pp. 75,76).

footnotes:
²¹ Such as belief in God. See chapter 5 of Yesod Mora


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:4)


**ובקולו תשמעו.** מה שהם זכר לעיקר:


AND UNTO HIS VOICE SHALL YE HEARKEN. This refers to precepts which recall that which is most important.²²the *tefillin…*the *tzitzit* [all serve as reminders] (*Ibid*, p. 78). The *tzitzit* (the fringes), for example, remind us of God’s commandments.

footnotes:
²² Some commandments serve as reminders of the most important precepts. For example, the Sabbath commemorates creation. Likewise, not permitting one’s slaves to work on the Sabbath recalls the exodus from Egypt. Similarly…the matzot…

**ואתו תעבודו.** במעשה:


AND HIM SHALL YE SERVE. In deed.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:5:6)

### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:6:1)


**כי דבר סרה.** תחסר מלת דבר כמו וברוב יועצים תקום:


BECAUSE HE HATH SPOKEN PERVERSION. The word *davar* (thing) has been omitted from our clause.²⁴ (for he hath spoken perversion).

footnotes:
²⁴ Our verse reads, ki dibber sarah


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:6:2)


וטעם **סרה.** שהשם צוה לסור מעבודת כוכבים או הוא מגזרת סורר ומורה והוא הנכון והיה הרי״‎ש ראוי להדגש:


*Sarah* is an adjective. Hence I.E. notes that the word which *sarah* is describing has been omitted. Our text is to be interpreted as if written, *ki dibber davar sarah*. I.E. interprets the latter as, for he has spoken words commanding us to turn away from the Lord. *But in the multitude of counselors established*²⁵ (Prov. 15:22) is similar.²⁶ Scripture reads *sarah* (perversion) because God commanded Israel to turn away (*la-sur*) from idolatry.²⁷ as meaning for He has spoken words urging us to turn away from the Lord. On the other hand, the word *sarah* might be related to the word *soreh*²⁸. (defiant) in *defiant*²⁹ *and rebellious* (Deut. 21:20). The latter interpretation is correct. The *resh* should have received a *dagesh*.³⁰. However, it doesn’t, for *resh* does not receive a *dagesh*.

footnotes:
²⁵ Translated according to I.E.
²⁶ Our text should be read as if written: But in the multitude of counselors, counsel is established.
²⁷ I.E. interprets ki dibber sarah
²⁸ From the root samekh, resh, resh
²⁹ Translated according to I.E.
³⁰ To compensate for the missing resh

**המוציא אתכם והפודך.** כפול בטעם לחזוק:


WHO BROUGHT YOU OUT OF THE LAND OF EGYPT, AND REDEEMED THEE OUT OF THE HOUSE OF BONDAGE. Scripture repeats itself for the sake of emphasis.

### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:7:1)


**כי יסיתך אחיך.** הטעם אפי' אחיך:


IF THY BROTHER…ENTICE THEE. This means even thy brother.³² is to be understood as even if your brother entices you, for the law applies to anyone who entices you.

footnotes:
³² If thy…brother entice thee


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:7:2)


וטעם **בן אמך.** שהייתם בבטן אחת והאם כגוף והאב כצורה והצורה לא יבינוה כי אם מתי מעט:


The import of *thy mother* is, you³³. This is an appellation rarely used in Scripture. The question thus arises, “Why did Scripture add *the son of thy mother*?” Furthermore, why did the Torah not describe the brother as the son of thy father? Hence I.E. comments that the matter from which a person is made comes from the mother; the father’s sperm (so I.E. believed) shapes the matter. Thus a person, biologically speaking, is closer to a brother from a mother than to a brother from a father, and probably has closer emotional ties to him. Hence Scripture states, even if your brother who is made of the same matter as you entices you, do not listen to him. were in one womb. The mother is like matter and the father is like form.³⁴ not thy father. Very few people understand the nature of form.

footnotes:
³³ And your brother were in one womb. Thus the meaning of our verse is, even if your brother who was in one womb with you entices you. Our verse speaks of thy brother, the son of thy mother
³⁴ Form gives shape to matter. Form is intangible. Different men will give different form to the matter produced by the same woman. However, all of the children will share the same matter. Hence Scripture speaks of thy mother

NEITHER SHALT THOU CONCEAL HIM. You shall not leave him alone but you shall reveal the matter until witnesses hear it³⁵ from his mouth, as is stated in Scripture.³⁶ You shall then kill him.

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:17:1)


**כליל.** כלה. לה' **אלהיך.** בעבור כבוד השם תשרפנה. באש:


EVERY WHIT. Completely to the Lord your God.⁴⁰ (every whit) is connected to *la-Adonai*. Our clause is to be read, every whit unto the Lord thy God. You shall bum it in fire because of God’s honor.⁴¹ means for the honor of the Lord thy God. By burning the contents of the city, the Israelites demonstrated that they destroyed the city because its inhabitants worshipped idols, and not for the purpose of looting.

A HEAP. The word *tel* (heap) is related to *talul* (heaped) in *upon a high mountain and heaped* (Ezek. 17:22).⁴²

THAT THE LORD MAY TURN FROM THE FIERCENESS OF HIS ANGER. And He will not destroy you because of the sins of that city.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 13:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/13:19:1)


וטעם **כי תשמע.** כן תעשה אם בקשת לעשות הישר בעיניו. ונסמכה פרשת בנים אתם לה'. בעבור המסיתים אח או בן או אב או אנשי עיר הנדחת ואסור להתאבל עליהם אף כי להתגודד כי הנה אסור הוא על כל מת:


WHEN THOU SHALT HEARKEN. The meaning of *When*⁴³. I.E. renders *ki* if. Thus our clause reads, if thou shalt hearken. Hence his interpretation. *thou shalt hearken* is, thus shall you do if you desire to do what is right in the eyes of God. The section which opens with *Ye are children of the Lord* (Deut. 14:1) is connected to this section because of the enticers,⁴⁴ namely, a brother, a sister, and a son or daughter (v. 7), or the inhabitants of the idol-worshipping city (v. 14). It is prohibited to mourn for them,⁴⁵ and it is certainly prohibited to cut oneself,⁴⁶ for behold, that is prohibited over any deceased.⁴⁷

footnotes:
⁴³ Hebrew, ki
⁴⁴ Our chapter tells us that the enticers are to be put to death.
⁴⁵ After they are put to death.
⁴⁶ To lacerate one’s flesh as a sign of mourning over their deaths.
⁴⁷ Deut. 14:1.



## Chapter 14


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 14:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:1:1)


**תתגודדו.** כטעם ושרט לנפש מגזרה על כל ידים גדודות:


YE SHALL NOT CUT YOURSELVES. *Titgodedu* (ye shall not cut yourselves) means the same as *ve-seret* (cuttings) in *cuttings…for the dead* (Lev. 19:28).¹ means you shall not cut yourselves. *Titgodedu* is related to the word *gedudot².* (cuttings) in *Upon all the hands are cuttings* (Jer. 48:37).

footnotes:
¹ In other words, lo titgodedu
² Both words come from the root gimel, dalet, dalet


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 14:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:1:2)


**ולא תשימו קרחה.** כמשפט הגוים עד היום הזה:


NOR MAKE ANY BALDNESS. As the nations do unto this very day.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 14:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:1:3)


וטעם **בנים.** אחר שתדעו שאתם בנים לשם והוא אוהב אתכם יותר מהאב לבן אל תתגודדו על כל מה שיעשה כי כל אשר יעשה לטוב הוא ואם לא תבינוהו כאשר לא יבינו הבנים הקטנים מעשה אביהם רק יסמכו עליו כן תעשו גם אתם כי עם קדוש אתה ואינך כשאר כל הגוים על כן לא תעשה כמעשיהם. וטעם הסמך פרשת לא תאכל כל תועבה כי אתה עם קדוש בלב ובפה ותהיו מובדלים מן העמים וכל רואיכם יכירו אתכם כי לא תקרחו על מת ולא תאכלו כל חי ואם אתה עם קדוש אין ראוי שתאכל טמא ותטמא הנפש:


[CHILDREN.] The meaning of *children* is, now that you know that you are children of God and that the Lord loves you more than a father loves his son, do not cut yourselves over anything that God does, for whatever the Lord does is for the good. If you do not understand God,³ then act like small children who do not understand what their father does but nevertheless rely upon him. *For thou art a holy people* (v. 2) and you are not like all the Canaanites. You shall therefore not imitate their practices. The section opening with the words *Thou shalt not eat any abominable thing* (v. 3) follows because Scripture says, as it were, you are holy in your heart and in your mouth. You shall be separated from the nations. All who see you shall recognize you, for you will not make yourself bald for the dead and you do not eat all living creatures. If you are a holy people, then it is unfitting for you to eat that which is unclean and defiles one’s soul.

footnotes:
³ If you do not understand God’s actions.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 14:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:11:1)


**כל צפור טהורה תאכלו.** הנה צפור שם כלל ותור וגוזל על השני מינין שהם תחת צפור וכן דיה ואיה תחת דאה כי עתה הוסיף לבאר:


OF ALL CLEAN BIRDS YOU MAY EAT. *Tzippor* (birds) is a general term.¹² The dove and turtledove are subcategories of the general term “birds.”¹³) divided he not.” The reference there is to the dove and the turtledove mentioned in Gen. 15:10. Scripture can refer to the dove and turtledove by the term *tzippor* because they are subcategories of *tzippor*. The *dayyah* (the kite)¹⁴ and the *ayyah*¹⁵, for *ayyah* is mentioned both here and in Lev. 11:14. (the falcon)¹⁶ are subcategories of the *da’ah* (falcon) (Lev. 11:14),¹⁷, pp. 36,37: “We find that the number of prohibited fowl listed in the Torah portion *Shemini* is 20. However, in the Torah portion *Re’eh Anokhi*, the number is 21. The answer is found in the science of logic, for *da’ah* (Lev. 11:14) is a supreme kind that includes both the *dayyah* and the *ra’ah* (Deut. 14:13).” as Scripture now elaborates.¹⁸ (the kite), the *ayyah* (the falcon), and the *ra’ah* (the glede), which are included in the category *da’ah* (falcon) in Leviticus.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 14:22:1

**עשר תעשר.** ונסמכה זאת הפרשה כי אסור לאכול כל עוף טמא ובהמה טמאה ונבלה והנה בבשר ובדגן אסור לאכול ההקדש כי אם במקום הנבחר:


THOU SHALT SURELY TITHE. This section²⁵ follows²⁶ because it is prohibited to eat any unclean winged creature and any unclean animal, nor any animal which died of natural causes.²⁷ Now it is similarly forbidden to eat sacred grain²⁸ or sacred meat in any but the chosen place.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 14:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:22:2)


**היוצא השדה שנה שנה.** אמר רבי יהודה הלוי הספרדי נ״‎ע כי הוא דבק עם זרעך והטעם הזרע שהוצאת אל השדה ולפי דעתי שהוא כפשוטו וטעמו על התבואה מה שיוצאת השדה ואם יטעון טוען כי הכתוב אמר היוצא והוא מהפעלים שאינם יוצאים לאחרים הראינו לו העיר היוצאת אלף והעד תשאיר מאה וחז״‎ל אמרו שהוא מעשר שני ודע אם תחל ממעל הנה האחד ראש ואם ממטה הנה העשירי וזה סוד הבכור והמעשר ודע כי האחד איננו בחשבון וכן העשירי כי הוא כנגד האחד כי הוא ראש למחברת השנית וסוף למחברת הראשונה וכל שרש בחשבון סמוך אליו מפניו ומאחריו ובעבור היות האחד והעשרה עיקריים והאמצעיים חמשה וששה הם הנקראים עגולים על כן אותיות הנח הם אלה הארבעה ומהם אותיות השם הנכבד והנורא:


WHICH IS BROUGHT FORTH IN THE FIELD YEAR BY YEAR. Rabbi Yehudah Ha-Levi the Spaniard, who rests in Eden, said *that which is brought forth in the field* is connected to the word *seed*, and our verse is to be understood as, you shall tithe the seed which is brought into the field for planting.²⁹ (is brought forth) is intransitive. Hence his interpretation. However, I believe that we are to interpret the verse according to its plain meaning. Our clause refers to the produce which the field brings forth.³⁰ (is brought forth) is transitive. Our verse is to be understood as follows: You shall tithe the seed which the field produces. Should some dispute this and argue that Scripture reads *ha-yotze* (which is brought forth), and *yotze* (brought forth) is an intransitive verb, then we will show him *ha-ir ha-yotzet*³¹ is the feminine form of *ha-yotze*. It is transitive. We thus see that *ha-yotze* can also be transitive. *elef* (the city that sent forth a thousand) (Amos 5:3). *Shall have a hundred left* (*Ibid.*)³² proves this.³³ is transitive, for it speaks about what is left in the city. The rabbis said that the reference is to the second tithe.³⁴ Note.³⁵ If you start from the top,³⁶ then one is the first number. If you start from below,³⁷ then, behold, ten is the first number.³⁸ This is the secret of the first-born and the tithe.³⁹ Observe. The number one does not depend on a sum.⁴⁰ The number ten is similar. It is the counterpart of one because it is the beginning of the second series of numbers.⁴¹ It is also the end of the first series.⁴² All parts of sums are connected to ten in front and in back.⁴³ The numbers one and ten are the main numbers; the middle numbers, five and six, are called the round numbers.⁴⁴ These four numbers⁴⁵ therefore⁴⁶ form the silent letters.⁴⁷ (1); *heh* (5); *vav* (6), and *yod* (10). These letters are not always sounded. They are vowel letters. One of them is present whenever a word is enunciated. Without these letters, language would be impossible. They are also the letters that form the glorious and awe-inspiring name.⁴⁸ in Ex. 3:14.

footnotes:
²⁹ That is, the produce from the seed. Rabbi Yehuudah believes that yotze
³⁰ In other words, that yotze
³¹ Ha-yotzet
³² This clause follows in the same verse.
³³ That ha-yotzet
³⁴ A tithe which is set apart after the Levite has received die first tithe. The second tithe must be eaten in Jerusalem or be redeemed and the money spent in Jerusalem.
³⁵ Ibn Ezra believed that the numbers one, five, six, and ten have special significance. See I.E. on Ex. 3:15 (Vol. 2, pp. 69-86).
³⁶ At the beginning of the numbers.
³⁷ At the bottom of the first ten numbers.
³⁸ That is, the first of the tens.
³⁹ This is the reason the first-born and the tithe are holy. The first-born corresponds to the number one, while the tithe corresponds to the number ten.
⁴⁰ Every number is made up of sums. However, the number one stands by itself. It does not consist of any sum.
⁴¹ It is the first of the tens. Hence it is similar to one.
⁴² One through nine.
⁴³ “Front” means before ten. “Back” means after ten. I.E.’s point is that the main numbers, that is numbers 1-9 end in 10. Thus 1-9 are connected to ten in front. All numbers after 10 are either 10 plus one of the main numbers or multiples of ten plus one of the main numbers.
⁴⁴ If 5 and 6 are squared, cubed or raised to the nth power, their root is always visible. For example, 5 squared is 25, 5 cubed is 125; 6 squared is 36, 6 cubed is 216, and so on (Meijler). Thus 5 and 6 are called round numbers because in effect, they orbit their root or make a circle and ultimately return to their root.
⁴⁵ One, five, six, and ten.
⁴⁶ Because of their importance.
⁴⁷ The letters alef
⁴⁸ The Tetragramatton. The latter is spelled with an alef

⁷² Not the Israelite.
⁷³ In other words, the entire chapter deals with food prohibited to Israelites.
⁷⁴ That is, the only tithe in addition to the first tithe is the one given in the third year, i.e., the poor tithe.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 14:28:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:28:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/14:28:3)


**מקצה.** תחלה וזה של שלש שנים יקרא מעשר עני ואנחנו נסמוך על קבלת אבותינו:


AT THE END.⁷⁵. At the beginning.⁷⁶ at the beginning. See I.E. on Num. 13:25 (Vol. 4, p. 104), “The word *mi-ketz* (at the end) sometimes means at the beginning and sometimes at the end.” The tithe given in the third year is called the “poor tithe.” We will rely on the tradition of our fathers.⁷⁷

⁷⁵ Hebrew, miktzeh
⁷⁶ I.E. renders miktzeh

## Chapter 15


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:1:1)


וטעם **מקץ שבע שנים תעשה שמטה.** בתחלת השנה כאשר פירשתי והעד הקהל את העם ונדבקה זאת הפרשה בעבור שאמר כי מעשר עני הוא ללוי ולגר וליתום ולאלמנה אמר וכן דבר השמטה והטעם כמו שמטוה והניחו' ותפול:


AT THE END OF¹. EVERY SEVEN YEARS.] Its meaning, as I have explained, is, at the beginning of the year.² means either at the end of or at the beginning of. Here it means at the beginning. Thus our clause reads, At the beginning of the seventh year. See I.E. on Deut. 9:11. *Assemble the people* (Deut. 31:12) is proof of this.³ (mikketz) *every seven years* (Deut. 31:10). According to I.E. the latter should be rendered: At the beginning of the seventh year. We thus see that *mikketz* can be rendered, at the beginning of. This section follows⁴ because Scripture states that the poor tithe⁵ belongs to the Levite, the stranger, the orphan, and the widow.⁶ Scripture now says that the same applies to the remission of debts (*shemittah*).⁷ The word *shemittah* is similar to *shimtuha* (throw her down) (II Kings 98:33), the meaning of which is, release her and she will fall.⁸ is, a release.

footnotes:
¹ Hebrew, mikketz
² According to I.E. the word mikketz
³ To hear the Torah read. This took place At the end of
⁴ The laws of the poor tithe (Deut. 14:28,29).
⁵ A tithe set apart for the poor.
⁶ Deut. 14:29.
⁷ The remission of debts in the seventh year is primarily for the benefit of the Levite, the stranger, the orphan, and the widow, i.e., the poor.
⁸ In other words, the meaning of shemittah



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:2:1)


**משה.** במשקל מטה והוא שם לפי דעתי ולפי דעת רבים הוא פועל ובעל כמו והחכמה תחיה בעליה את נפש בעליו יקח והטעם מי שהדבר ברשותו:


CREDITOR. The word *masheh* (loan) follows the paradigm of *matteh* (rod) (Ex. 4:4).⁹ can be taken as a verb. Hence I.E.’s comment that it is a noun and follows the vocalization of nouns like *matteh*. I believe that *masheh* is a noun.¹⁰ However, many believe that *masheh* is a verb.¹¹ The word *ba’al* (creditor) is similar to *be’aleha* (him that hath it) in *wisdom preserveth the life of him that hath it* (Eccles. 7:12) and *be’alav* (the owners thereof) in *It taketh away the life of the owners thereof* (Prov. 1:19). It means the one who has the matter¹² in his power.

footnotes:
⁹ Masheh
¹⁰ Meaning a loan.
¹¹ Meaning lending.
¹² Under discussion.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:2:2)


**ישה.** פועל יוצא והלוקח יקרא נושה בו:


THAT WHICH HE HATH LENT. *Yasheh* (that which he hath lent) is a transitive verb. The one who borrows is called a *nosheh bo* (a borrower) (Is. 24:2).¹³

footnotes:
¹³ See I.E. on Is. 24:2.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:2:3)


**יגוש.** ילחץ כמו וכל עצביכם תנגושו והנה נראה הנו״‎ן בפעלי העתיד מבעלי הנו״‎ן:


HE SHALL NOT EXACT IT. The word *yiggos* (exact) means to press. Compare, *tingosu* (exact) in *and exact all your labors* (Is. 58:3). Look, the *nun* is visible in the imperfect in a word whose stem begins with a *nun*.¹⁴ is present in *tingosu* (exact), which is an imperfect whose stem begins with a *nun*. The *nun* is usually dropped in such instances. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
¹⁴ In other words, the nun

**שמטה לה'.** לכבוד השם שנתן לו הממון וטעם הסמך המל' לשם בעבור שבת היא לה':


THE LORD’S RELEASE.¹⁵. Literally, a remission to the Lord. In honor of God,¹⁶ who has bestowed wealth upon him. The name of God is connected to the word release because the year of release is called a Sabbath unto the Lord (Lev. 25:2,4).¹⁷

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:4:1)


**אפס.** הטעם דע כי זה שצויתיך שלא תגוש את אחיך אין צורך אם היו כל ישראל או רובם שומעים בקול השם אז לא יהיה בך אביון שהוא צריך להיותך מלוה אותו רק כלכם תעבטו גוים רבים והטעם תקחו עבוטו שהוא משכון בעבור ממון שתתן לו שהוא צריך אליו לסחור בו:


HOWBEIT. This means that you shall take note that that which I commanded you, not to exact interest from your brother, will be unnecessary if all of Israel or most of them will listen to God’s voice. In this case, there will be no poor among you who need you to lend to them. On the contrary, all of you shall lend to many nations (v. 6). The meaning of *ve-ha’avateta* (and thou shalt lend) (v. 6) is, you shall take his pledge (*avot*), that is, his pawn, for the money that you shall give him, which he needs to do business.

AND THOU SHALT RULE. Furthermore, being upright you will rule over nations. However, there is a commandment stating *If there be among you a needy man* (v. 7).¹⁸ (v. 4). (stoppeth) in *kafetzah*²¹. *piha* (stoppeth her mouth) (Ps. 107:42; Job 5:16); *kafatz*²². *be-af* (shut up His compassion) (Ps. 77:10); and *yikpetzu*²³. (shall shut) in *Kings shall shut their mouths* (Is. 52:15). *Tikpotz* is a verb in the *kal*.

**האביון.** י״‎א שהוא מגזרת ולא אבה כי הוא במשקל עליון וחביון והגיון שהשרש עלה חבה הגה והטעם שהוא מתאוה לכל דבר בעבוד חסרונו:


NEEDY. Some say that the word *evyon* (needy) is related to the word *avah*²⁴. (would) in *and he would not* (Ex. 10:27), for *evyon* follows the paradigm *elyon, chevy on*, and *hegyon*, the roots of which are *ayin, lamed, heh; chet, bet, heh; heh, gimel, heh*.
