### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:8:1)


**פתח תפתח.** שם הפעל וכל מקום שאיננו כתוב הוא דרך קצרה גם הדרש יפה:


BUT THOU SHALT SURELY OPEN THY HAND. *Pato’ach* (open) is an infinitive. Whenever Scripture omits the infinitive,²⁵. it is employing an abridged style. The midrashic interpretation is also beautiful.²⁶ (thou shalt surely open) indicates that one should open his hand to the poor even one hundred times. However, I.E. believes that the form *pato’ach tiftach* is the correct way to say you shall open.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:9:1)


**בליעל.** שם וי״‎א שהיא מלה מורכבת כגון תפלה שלא יעל ולא יגאל ולא יחיה:


BASE. The word *beliya’al* (base) is a noun. Some say that *beliya’al* is a compound. It refers to a prayer that the vile person should not ascend, should not grow, and should not live.²⁸ is short for *beli ya’aleh* (without ascending). When one sees the wicked, one utters a curse to the effect that the wicked not ascend. The meaning of our verse is, may the wicked neither grow nor live.

**ורעה עינך.** כמו ושבה אל בית אביה והמלה מהשניים הנראים:


AND THINE EYE BE EVIL. *Ve-ra’ah* (and thine eye be evil) is similar to *ve-shavah* (and is returned) in *and is returned unto her father’s house* (Lev. 22:13). The word comes from a root two of whose letters are pronounced.²⁹. Its root is *resh, vav, ayin*.

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:10:1)


**בגלל.** כמו סבה לבעבור סבב כי הכל הוא בחלקי' בחלקי הכל שהוא הגוף:


THAT. *Bi-gelal* (that) means the same as *sibbah* (because of). Compare, *sabbev* (cause) in *cause to change* (II Sam. 14:20).³⁰. For the All³¹ is in the parts,³² that is, in the parts of the All, that is, the body.³³, N.Y., 1969, p. 99). In other words, the All is the nation, and the individual is a part of the nation. However, it should be noted that I.E. refers to God as the All. See I.E. on Gen. 1:26 (Vol. 1, p. 46), “God is one…He is all.” Also see I.E. on Gen. 18:21 (Vol. 1, p. 196) and Ex. 33:21 (Vol. 2, p. 695). The above-noted commentaries may have shied away from interpreting God as All here because of its pantheistic implications. It should be noted that when I.E. says that God is All, he does not intend to identify God with the world as the pantheists do. He means that God’s power and supervision hold the world together and give it continuous existence. Indeed, I.E. compares God’s relation to the world to that of speech to the speaker. As long as the speaker speaks, sounds are uttered. However, the speaker is not identified with the speech which he utters. On the other hand, I.E. was a contemporary of Rabbi Yehudah Ha-Levi and may be employing terminology similar to the one quoted above.

footnotes:
³⁰ I.E. is explaining the origin of the word sibbah
³¹ God.
³² In human beings, who are part of the whole.
³³ Thus by being kind to his fellow human being, a person is being kind to God (Weiser). Other commentaries explain that the All refers to the community of Israel and the parts to each individual Jew. Now, since all of Israel forms one body, by helping another Jew one is helping oneself (Motot and Rabbi Yosef ben Eliezer tov Elem, followed by Krinsky, Netter, and others). See Book III of the Kuzari: “Plato calls…that which is expended on behalf of the law the portion of the whole. If, however, an individual neglects ‘the portion of the whole’…of which he forms a part, he sins against the commonwealth and more against himself; for the relation of the individual to the commonwealth is as the relation of the single limb to the body…” (Isaak Heinemann translation in Three Jewish Philosophers

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:14:1)


**העניק תעניק לו.** הטעם עשה לו תפארת מגזרת וענקים לגרגרותיך או יהיה פירוש המלה כפי המקום בלא אח:


THOU SHALT FURNISH HIM LIBERALLY. *Ha’anek ta’anik* (thou shalt furnish him liberally) means treat him gloriously. *Ta’anik* (thou shalt furnish him) is related to the word *anakim* (chains)⁴¹ in *And chains about thy neck* (Prov. 1:9).⁴² (thou shalt furnish him liberally) literally means thou shalt put ornament on him. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. On the other hand, the meaning of *ta’anik* is to be derived from its context.⁴³ (thou shalt furnish him) is not related to the word *anakim* (chains) in *And chains about thy neck* (Prov. 1:9). In this case, the word has no brother.⁴⁴

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:17:1)


**באזנו ובדלת.** פירשתיו וטעם להזכיר זה כי אתה חייב לתת לו בצאתו חפשי רק אם לא ירצה לצאת תרצע אזנו:


THROUGH HIS EAR AND INTO THE DOOR. I have explained this.⁴⁵ Scripture mentions this⁴⁶ because it states⁴⁷ that you are obligated to furnish him liberally when he goes free. However, if the slave does not want to go free, then you shall pierce his ear through with an awl.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:17:2)


**ואף לאמתך תעשה כן.** דבק עם העניק תעניק לו ואם הוא רחוק כמו שלם ישלם אם אין לו ואם שלש אלה ורבים כן כי אין אשה נרצעת:


AND ALSO UNTO THY BONDWOMAN THOU SHALT DO LIKEWISE. This is connected to⁴⁸ *thou shalt furnish him liberally* (v. 14), even though it is some distance from it, for a woman does not have her ear pierced. Compare, *He shall make restitution; if he have nothing*⁴⁹. (Ex. 22:2);⁵⁰ *And if he do not these three unto her* (Ex. 21:11),⁵¹ and many other instances.

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:18:1)


**לא יקשה בעיניך.** מצאנו כתוב שלש שנים כימי שכיר וזו ראי' כי אין רשות לאדם שישכיר עצמו יותר משלש שנים ג״‎כ לשוכר אותו הנותן שכרו:


IT SHALL NOT SEEM HARD UNTO THEE. We find it written in Scripture, *three years, as the years of a hireling* (Is. 16:14). The latter proves that a person has no right to hire himself out for more than three years. The law similarly applies to the one who hires him and pays his wages.⁵²

footnotes:
⁵² One cannot hire a person for more than three years.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:18:2)


וזה טעם משנה כמו כפל וכן והיה משנה הוא משנה למלך שם משנה למלך תאר השם גם הוא מגזרת שנים כאשר פירשתי והנה זה הכתוב יפרש כי ובשביעית יצא לחפשי חנם בתחלת השנה גם יש ראיה משנת השמטה גם מיום השבת:


[DOUBLE.]⁵³. The following is the definition of *mishneh*. It means double. Similarly *mishneh* (twice) *in ve-hayah mishneh* (and it shall be twice as much) (Ex. 16:5). The word *mishneh* in the latter is a noun. However, *mishneh* (next) in *mishneh la-melekh* (next unto king) (Esth. 10:3) is an adjective.⁵⁴. 38. The *Mikra’ot Gedolot* reads: “The following is the definition of *mishneh*. It means double. Similarly *mishneh* (twice) in *ve-hayah mishneh* (and it shall be twice as much) (Ex. 16:5), the meaning of which is *mishneh lamelekh* (double unto the king). The word *mishneh* in the latter is a noun. However, *mishneh* (next) in *mishneh la-melekh* (next unto king) (Esth. 10:3) is an adjective.” The words “the meaning of which *mishneh lamelekh* (double unto the king)” appear to be a gloss. See Weiser and Krinsky. It⁵⁵ (next) in *mishneh la-melekh*. is also related, as I have explained,⁵⁶ to the word *shenayin* (two).⁵⁷, which means second to him” (Vol. 1, p. 382). Note, our verse explains that *and in the seventh he shall go out free for nothing* (Ex. 21:2) refers to the beginning of the year.⁵⁸. If the slave did not go out at the start of the seventh year he would be serving seven not six years. There is also proof of this from the year of release (*shemitttah*)⁵⁹ as well as from the Sabbath day.⁶⁰

⁵⁷ “The king is like the number one; next comes the mishneh
⁵⁸ For the clause that precedes it states, he shall serve thee six years
⁵⁹ The year of release begins at the start of the seventh year. See I.E. on Ex. 23:10,11 (Vol. 2, pp. 498,499).

**וברכך.** פועל עתיד ולא עבר כי אין הפרש ביניהם רק מטעם המקום:

### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:22:1)


**בשעריך תאכלנו.** י״‎א הכהן יאכלנו חוץ למקדש וי״‎א אחר שיש בו מום הוא לישראל וזה רחוק בעיני:


WITHIN THY GATES. Some say that this means that the *kohen* shall eat it outside of the sanctuary.⁶⁸ Others say, since the animal has a blemish it belongs to the Israelite. However, this appears far-fetched to me.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 15:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/15:22:2)


**הטמא והטהור.** שב אל תאכלנו והטעם כמו קריאה אתה הטמא או הטהור והטעם בין שיהיה טמא או טהור הוא מותר לאוכלו בעבור כי בשר קודש אם אכלו טמא הוא בכרת ובעבור שהזכיר שנה בשנה הזכיר שלש פעמים ועוד בעבור הבכור שיאכל במקדש וכן הפסח יאכל במקדש ולא חוץ ממנו:


THE UNCLEAN AND THE CLEAN. The reference is to *may eat it*.⁶⁹ prefaced to the Hebrew words for clean and unclean as a vocative *heh*. Thus *ha-tame* (the unclean) means you who are unclean, and *ha-tahor* (the clean) means you who are clean. The meaning of our clause thus is, you who are unclean and you who are clean shall eat of it. Our verse is to be understood as a call: you, the clean and the unclean. Its meaning is, one is permitted to eat it whether one is clean or unclean. Scripture states this because one who is unclean and eats holy meat is punished with *karet* (excision). Scripture reads *three times in a year* (Deut. 16:16) because it here (v. 20) states year by year.⁷⁰ Additionally,⁷¹ because the firstling is to be eaten in the sanctuary.⁷² The paschal offering⁷³ 5:8. is similarly to be eaten in the sanctuary, not outside it.

footnotes:
⁶⁹ Our verse literally reads, you shall eat it, the unclean and the clean. According to I.E. this is to be read as, the unclean and the clean you shall eat it. He interprets the heh
⁷⁰ In other words, the laws of the pilgrimage festivals follow our chapter because it ends with a law to be observed year by year, and so are the pilgrimage festivals to be observed.
⁷¹ Another reason the laws of the pilgrimage festivals follow the laws of the first-born.
⁷² The place where the Israelite makes his pilgrimage three times a year. See Ex. 23:17.
⁷³ I.E.’s interpretation contradicts Zevachim



## Chapter 16


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:1:1)


**את חדש האביב.** נקרא כן בעבור שהיה בו אביב בצאתם ממצרים והמכחישים אמרו כי לא נעשה פסח בלא אביב וכבר השיבותי על הבליהם גם אנחנו נודה כי בית דין היה מסתכל לאביב ודע כי אין לחדשים בלשון הקדש שמות כי ניסן סיון גם כסליו גם טבת גם אדד שהם נזכרים במקרא הם לשון כשדים על כן אמר הוא חדש ניסן רק מצאנו זיו ואיתני' ובול וטעם ראשון כאשר ימצא האביב הנו חדש הראשון כי משם יחל לספור החדשי' אם היתה שנה שעברה שנים עשר חדש או שלשה עשר לא נחוש על זה וזה טעם ראשון רק קדמונינו ז״‎ל העתיקו עם האביב דברים אחרים וכל דבריהם אמת:


THE MONTH OF ABIV. It is called the month of Aviv because when the Israelites went out of the land of Egypt, the first of the barley crop (*aviv*)¹ as the first of the barley crop. Thus according to I.E. *the month of Abiv* means the month when the first of the barley crop appears in the field. appeared in the fields in that month. Those who deny the Rabbinic tradition say that Passover² is not to be observed if the first of the barley crop has not appeared.³ did not appear, then the paschal lamb was not offered that year. They do not believe that an additional month was added to the year if the *aviv* had not yet arrived. (See Meijler). I have previously responded to their nonsense.⁴ We will also admit that the Beth Din⁵ took the appearance of the first barley crop into consideration.⁶. 11a,b. Note that in Hebrew the months have no names.⁷ Nisan (Esth. 3:7; Neh. 2:1), Sivan (Esth. 8:9), and also Kislev (Neh. 1:1), Tevet (Esth. 2:16), and Adar (Esth. 3:7), which are mentioned in Scripture, are Chaldean terms. Hence Scripture states, *which is the month of Nisan* (*Ibid.*).⁸. Scripture identifies the first month by its Chaldean name because the Jews did not call the month by that name in earlier times. However, we find Ziv (I Kings 6:37),⁹ Etanim (I Kings 8:2),¹⁰ and Bul (I Kings 6:38).¹¹ The meaning of first¹². is that when you find the first barley ripe, then the first month has arrived, for one starts counting the months from it We do not care about this,¹³ whether the year that passed consisted of twelve months or thirteen months.¹⁴ This is the meaning of first.¹⁵ arrives. However, the ancients of blessed memory transmitted traditions regarding many other things along with the ripe barley grain.¹⁶. 11:a,b. All of their words are true.

⁷ Hence adding a thirteenth month presented no problems.
⁸ Esth. 3:7 reads, In the first month, which is the month of Nisan
⁹ For the second month.
¹⁰ For the seventh month.
¹¹ For the eighth month. In other words, only the second, seventh, and eighth months had appellations. The other months were known only by number.
¹² Why Scripture calls Nisan the first month. See Ex: 12:2, It shall be the first month of the year to you
¹³ The declaration of the first month.
¹⁴ The arrival of Aviv determines the arrival of the first month. The number of months of the previous year is irrelevant. If the year had a set number of months their exhaustion would determine the year.

### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:2:1)


**צאן.** לחיוב הפסח:


OF THE FLOCK. To fulfill the obligation of the paschal sacrifice.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:2:2)


**ובקר.** לשלמים והעד ובקר שלשת אלפים אחר שאמר הכל לפסחים על כשבים ובני עזים וכן נתנו לפסחים חמשת אלפים ובקר חמש מאות והנה זכר הפסח קודם הבקר וכן אתה וביתך וי״‎א כי במצרים אמר כבש או עז ועתה בקר אם יוכל והראשון הוא הנכון:


AND THE HERD. For peace offerings.²⁰ The fact that Scripture says, *and three thousand bullocks* (II Chron. 35:7), after it says, *all of them for the passover-offerings*, with regard to the lambs and kids (*Ibid.*), is proof.²¹ refers to peace offerings, for Scripture implies that only the lambs and kids were offered as paschal sacrifices. II Chron. 35:7 states: *And Josiah gave to the children of the people, of the flock, lambs and kids, all of them for the passover-offerings, unto all that were present, to the number of thirty thousand*. It similarly tells us that they gave (unto the Levite) *for the passover-offerings five thousand small cattle, and five hundred oxen* (*Ibid.*, v. 9). Scripture thus mentions the Passover offerings before it mentions the cattle.²² It similarly states, *thou and thy household* (Deut. 14:26).²³. The former applies to the paschal offering and the latter to peace offerings. Some say that Scripture commanded that a lamb or a he-goat were to be offered in Egypt.²⁴ However, Scripture now tells us if one is able to, he may offer cattle. However, the first interpretation is correct.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁰ The paschal sacrifice could only be brought from the flock. See Ex. 12:5.
²¹ That And the herd
²² Which shows that the oxen were not for the Passover offerings, for if they were they would have been so mentioned.
²³ The meaning of which is, you are obligated to eat the tithe in the place of the sanctuary. Your household may do so if they want to. However, they are not obligated to do so. We thus see that Scripture juxtaposes two things that are similar but not identical in one verse. The same is true of the flock and the herd
²⁴ In Ex. 12:5, I.E. quotes this in the name of Rabbi Moses ben Amram Ha-Parsi. The later was a Karaite Bible commentator. See Vol. 2, p. 220.
²⁵ The first interpretation is that of the rabbis.

### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:7:1)


**ובשלת.** פירשתיו באש וכן ויבשלו את הפסח באש והזכיר ובשלת שלא יאכלנו חי:


AND THOU SHALT ROAST.²⁹ [literally, and you shall cook]. I have previously explained that *u-vishalta* means you shall roast in fire.³⁰. Hence I.E.’s comment. Compare, *And they roasted* (va-yevashelu)³¹ *the passover with fire* (II Chron. 35:13). Scripture reads *u-vishalta* to indicate that the Passover offering is not to be eaten raw.³² (cooked) comes from the same root as the word ripe. Thus *u-vishalta* can be taken to mean prepare (cook or roast) it to the point where it is fit to be eaten.

³² The word bashel


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:7:2)


**ופנית בבקר.** אמרו המכחישים כי ילך ביום קדש אל ביתו וארצו ואנחנו לא נסמוך על דעתנו במצות כי אם על מעתיקי התורה והנה מצאנו ביום השמיני שלח את העם והוא יום קדש וכתוב אחר וביום עשרים ושלשה לחדש השביעי שלח את העם יש אומרים כי הפירוש לאהליהם כאשר הוא וביום החול לארצם:


AND THOU SHALT TURN IN THE MORNING. Those who deny the Rabbinic tradition³³ say that the verse tells the Israelite to go to his house and land on the holy day.³⁴ We will not rely on our own minds when it comes to the commandments. We will rely only on those who transmitted the Torah.³⁵ refers to the morning of the sixteenth of Nissan. See *Hagigah* 17a,b. Look, we find Scripture stating, *On the eighth day*³⁶ *he*³⁷ *sent the people*³⁸ *away* (I Kings 8:66). The latter was a holy day.³⁹ Another verse says, *And on the three and twentieth day of the seventh month*⁴⁰ *he*⁴¹ *sent the people*⁴² *away unto their tents* (II Chron. 7:1). Some say that the meaning of *unto their tents* is, to their tents, which were close by.⁴³ They went to their lands⁴⁴ on the weekday.

footnotes:
³³ The Karaites.
³⁴ On the first day of Passover, for that is the day following the night that the Passover offering is eaten.
³⁵ According to the rabbis, and thou shalt turn in the morning
³⁶ Of Sukkot, i.e., Shemini Atzeret.
³⁷ King Solomon.
³⁸ Who came to celebrate the dedication of the Holy Temple.
³⁹ For it was Shemini Atzeret.
⁴⁰ That is, on Shemini Atzeret.
⁴¹ King Solomon.
⁴² Who came to celebrate the dedication of the Holy Temple.
⁴³ Around Jerusalem. Thus going to their tents did not entail a violation of the festival. The same is true in our verse.
⁴⁴ To their place of residence.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:7:3)


**והלכת לאהליך** סבוב לירושלים ומשם ילך אל ארצו בחול:


AND GO UNTO THY TENTS. Around Jerusalem. The Israelite would then go to his land on the weekday.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:8:1)


**ששת ימים.** אמרו הקדמונים מהחל חרמש ויתכן להיות ביום השביעי דבק עם ששת ימים והבדילו מפאה אל פאה אחרת להזהיר לא תעשה מלאכה ולא אמר כל מלאכה והטעם כל מלאכת עבודה ובמקום שאמר כל מלאכה שם כתוב אך וכאשר הזכיר הפסח שהוא חייב לבא אל מקום הכבוד הזכיר החג השני גם השלישי ולא הזכיר יום תרועה ויום העשור כי אין הצבור מתחבר במקדש:


SIX DAYS. The ancients said six days from the lifting of the sickle.⁴⁵ This took place on the second day of Passover. See Lev. 23:9-12. Scripture prohibits eating of the new crop of grain before a measure (an omer) of new grain is cut and offered on the altar. Now, the Torah commands that matzot be eaten for seven days (v. 3). This contradicts our verse which states that matzot be eaten for six days. The Talmud (*Menachot* 66a) explains that our verse refers to matzot baked from the new crop, which could not be eaten before the second day of Passover, while the verse that tells the Israelites to eat matzot for seven days speaks of matzot made from grain from the previous year. See Rashi. It is possible that *and on the seventh day* is connected to *six days*.⁴⁶ *And on the seventh day* was separated from the first half of the verse and placed in the second half of the verse because Scripture wanted to warn against doing work.⁴⁷ refers to both the first part of the verse and to the second. Our verse is to be understood as follows: *Six days thou shalt eat unleavened bread;* and [so too] on the seventh day; *and on the seventh day shall be a solemn assembly …thou shalt do no work therein*. Scripture does not say all manner of work.⁴⁸ The reason for the latter is that only servile work⁴⁹ is prohibited. Where Scripture does state all manner of work,⁵⁰ it reads, “Save” [that which every man must eat].⁵¹ When Scripture mentions that one is obligated to offer the Passover sacrifice in the glorious place, it also mentions the second festival (v. 9)⁵² and the third festival (v. 13).⁵³ It does not mention the day of *shofar* sounding⁵⁴ and the tenth day,⁵⁵ because the congregation does not gather then in the sanctuary.⁵⁶

footnotes:
⁴⁵ On the new crop. The reference is to the cutting of the “omer.”
⁴⁶ In other words, our text should read, Six days thou shalt eat unleavened bread and [so too] on the seventh day. In other words, you shall eat unleavened bread for seven days.
⁴⁷ On the seventh day. The point is and on the seventh day
⁴⁸ All manner of work is prohibited on the Sabbath and Yom Kippur. However, work necessary for the preparation of food is permitted on festivals.
⁴⁹ All work except that which is necessary for the preparation of food. See Ex. 12:16.
⁵⁰ With regard to a festival.
⁵¹ See Ex. 12:16.
⁵² The second pilgrimage festival, i.e., Shavu’ot.
⁵³ The third pilgrimage festival, i.e., Sukkot.
⁵⁴ Rosh Ha-Shanah.
⁵⁵ The tenth day of Tishri, i.e., Yom Kippur.
⁵⁶ On these days. There is no commandment to make a pilgrimage to the sanctuary on Rosh Ha-Shanah and Yom Kippur.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:10:1)


**מסת.** דגשות הסמ״‎ך להתבלע הנו״‎ן והמלה מגזרת נס כטעם הרמת יד:


AFTER THE MEASURE. The *samekh* of *missat* (after the measure) receives a *dagesh* to compensate for the missing *nun*.⁵⁷ from *the root nun, samekh, samekh*. *Missat* is related to the word *nes* (banner). It means the lifting of the hand.⁵⁸ (after the measure of the freewill-offering of thy hand) means the lifting of the freewill-offering of thy hand. Scripture uses the term lifting when it deals with an offering to the sanctuary. Compare the word *trumah* (offering), which literally means a lifting.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ Missat comes
⁵⁸ Thus missat nidvat yadekha

**נדבת ידך.** לעולה ולשלמי':


THE FREEWILL-OFFERING OF THY HAND. For burnt offerings and peace offerings.

AFTER THAT THOU HAST GATHERED IN FROM THY THRESHING-FLOOR AND FROM THY WINEPRESS. Hence this festival is called *the feast of ingathering* (Ex. 34:22).⁶⁰

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:15:1)


**תחג.** תזבח הזבח והטעם על הצבור או טעם תחוג ליחיד לעשות עולות ושלמים והעד כי יברכך והטעם תעשה זה בעבור שברך אותך השם והוסיף בכל תבואתך לבאר על ברכת התבואה שהוציאה הארץ:


SHALT THOU KEEP A FEAST. *Tachog* (shalt thou keep a feast) means you shall sacrifice the offering.⁶¹ not only means a holiday, it also refers to the sacrifice that is offered on the festival. See I.E. on Ex. 5:1 (Vol. 2, p. 115). The reference is to the congregation.⁶² offerings). On the other hand, it might refer to the individual. The Israelite is told to make burnt and peace offerings. *Shall bless thee* is proof of this. It means do this⁶³ because God blessed you. Scripture adds *all thine increase* to explain that the object of *bless thee* is the produce which the earth will bring forth.

⁶¹ According to I.E. the word chag
⁶² The injunction, you shall sacrifice the offering, is directed to the congregation, for the congregation is obligated to bring the special offerings on the festivals (the musaf
⁶³ Sacrifice burnt and peace offerings

**מעשה ידיך.** בסחורה ואומנות:


THE WORK OF THY HANDS. Trade and goods produced by craftsmen.

**והיית אך שמח.** מצוה לשמוח בחג הסוכות וי״‎א כי יברכך לעתיד ותהיה לעולם שמח:


AND THOU SHALT BE ALTOGETHER JOYFUL. [ALTOGETHER.] The meaning of altogether is, you shall always be happy.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:18:1)


אע״‎פ שכל מצוה ומצוה עומדת בפני עצמה יש כדמות דרש להדבק הפרשיות. וטעם **שופטים.** אע״‎פ שאתה חייב ללכת שלשה פעמים למקום הכהנים משרתי המקדש ושם תשאל מהחקים והמשפטים זה לא יספיק לך עד שיהיו לך שופטים בכל שעריך:


[JUDGES.] Even though each and every commandment stands by itself there is, as it were, a midrashic reason for the connection of the sections.⁶⁷ The meaning *of Judges…*is, even though you are obligated to go three times a year to the place of the *kohanim* who serve in the sanctuary and there inquire regarding the statues and laws, it will not suffice. You must have judges in all of your cities.⁶⁸

ו**שוטרים** הם המושלי' מגזרת שוטר ומושל וכן משטרו בארץ והטעם כי השופט ישפוט והשוטר יכריח המעוות:


OFFICERS. *Shoterim* (officers) means rulers. It is related to *shoter* (ruler) in *Overseer, or ruler* (Prov. 6:6) and similarly *mishtaro* (dominion thereof) in *the dominion thereof in the earth* (Job 38:33). The *shofetim* (judges) judge and the *shoterim* (officers) force the crooked [to obey the law].

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:20:1)


**צדק צדק.** עם בעלי הריב ידבר וטעם שני פעמים לדבר צדק שירויח בו או יפסיד או פעם אחר פעם כל ימי היותך או לחזוק:


JUSTICE, JUSTICE. Moses speaks to the disputants. Moses repeats the word justice to indicate that one should pursue justice whether one gains or loses. Or the word is repeated to indicate that one should pursue justice as long as one exists;⁷¹ or the word is repeated for emphasis.

⁷¹ The repetition of the word connotes continuity.

THAT THOU MAYEST LIVE. Moses speaks to those who are entering the land.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:20:3)


**וירשתה.** עם בניהם ידבר והטעם שתעמוד לך זו הירושה והדבר שיתחיל השופט לדקדק הוא על עבודת גילולים המפורסמ' בתחלה כמו לא תטע לך אשרה כי כן משפט ע״‎ג אחר אחת בתוך:


AND INHERIT THE LAND. Moses speaks to their children. It means, you⁷² will keep this inheritance.⁷³ The first thing that the judge has to be concerned with is widespread pagan practices such as the one referred to in *Thou shalt not plant thee an Asherah* (v. 21).⁷⁴ The Torah states the latter, for such is the practice of pagan worship, as *Behind one in the midst* (Is. 66:17)⁷⁵ in the midst of the garden. See I.E. on Is. 66:17. shows.

### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 16:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/16:22:1)


**ולא תקים לך מצבה.** לע״‎ג והעד אשר שנא רק מצבה שלא לע״‎ג איננה אסורה והעד הנאמן בפרשת וישלח יעקב:


NEITHER SHALT THOU SET THEE UP A PILLAR. For an idol, as *which the Lord thy God hateth* proves.⁷⁶ However, a pillar which is not set up for idol worship is permitted. The most convincing proof of this is found in the portion which opens with the words *And Jacob sent* (Gen. 32:4).⁷⁷

## Chapter 17


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:1:1)


**לא תזבח לה' אלהיך.** הנה כאשר הזכיר האשרה והמצבה הזכיר כי גם הוא אסור לזבוח לשם כל בעל מום:


THOU SHALT NOT SACRIFICE UNTO THE LORD THY GOD. Look, when Moses mentions the *asherah* and the pillar he also mentions that it is prohibited to slaughter any blemished animal to God.¹ and the pillar are prohibited, he also mentions that it is prohibited to sacrifice any blemished animal to God, for these acts are an abomination to God.

footnotes:
¹ That is, since Moses mentions that the asherah

**כל דבר רע.** פירוש מום:


EVEN ANY EVIL THING. *Any evil thing* explains any blemish.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:1:3)


**כי תועבת.** כטעם הקריבהו נא לפחתך ויש עונש לזבחו ואין עונש לאשר איננו זובח כלל חוץ מהחיוב:


FOR THAT IS AN ABOMINATION. Its meaning is similar to *Present it*² *now unto thy governor*³ (Mal. 12:8). The one who offers it is punished. However, no punishment befalls one who does not sacrifice at all, unless he is obligated to bring an offering.⁴

### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:2:1)


**כי ימצא.** בקרבך ואחר שהזכיר האשרה אצל המזבח שהיא בפרהסיא לכל שב להזהיר על היחיד על עבודת גילולים:


IF THERE BE FOUND IN THE MIDST OF THEE. After Moses mentions that one is prohibited to plant an *asherah* near an altar which is in public view, he backtracks and warns the individual not to worship idols.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ In private.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:2:2)


. **לעבור בריתו.** הפך להעמידו. דקדוק וישתחו. בספר היסוד:


IN TRANSGRESSING HIS COVENANT. *La’avor brito* (in transgressing His covenant) is the reverse of keeping His covenant. I have previously explained grammatically the word *vayishtachu* (and worshipped), in the *Sefer Ha-Yesod*.⁶

OTHER GODS. Idols, the work of man.

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:4:1)


**ודרשת היטב.** מאד מאד וכן היטב חרה לי והוא מעט רחוק מגזרת טוב:


THEN THOU SHALT INQUIRE DILIGENTLY. *Hetev* (diligently) means very much. Similarly *hetev* (greatly) in *Art thou greatly angry* (Jonah 4:9). It is a bit far-fetched to connect *hetev* to the word *tov* (good).⁷ to the word *tov* (good), the meaning of our clause is, then thou shalt inquire well.

footnotes:
⁷ If we connect hetev



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:5:1)


**וסקלתם באבנים.** זה או זאת כמו באש ישרפו אותו ואתהן ובעבור שאמר והוגד לך בסתר ושמעת הדבר בגלוי הוצרך לפרש אע״‎פ שהוגד לך ושמעת לא יומתו רק ע״‎פ שנים עדים:


AND THOU SHALT STONE THEM. The man or the woman.⁸ cannot refer to both of them, for Scripture speaks of a man or a woman. It is similar to *they shall be burnt with fire*, *both he and they* (Lev. 20:14).⁹ (ethen) can’t refer to both women, for what sin did the first woman commit? It is only the second woman taken who is put to death. If he first takes the mother, then it is the daughter who is put to death. If he first takes the daughter, then it is the mother who is put to death. The meaning of *ethen* (they) thus is, one of them. Since Scripture says, *and it be told thee* (v. 4) in secret,¹⁰ and *thou hear it* (*Ibid.*) in public,¹¹ it had to explain that even though it was told to you, and you heard, they shall not be put to death except upon the testimony of two witnesses.¹²

footnotes:
⁸ Who worshipped idols. And thou shalt stone them
⁹ The verse speaks of a man who takes a mother and daughter, or a daughter and mother. They shall be burnt with fire, both he and they
¹⁰ I.E.’s elaboration of the verse.
¹¹ I.E.’s elaboration of the verse.
¹² The law of witnesses follows the law of idolatry because the punishment of idolaters requires two witnesses to the act of idol worship.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:6:1)


**או שלשה עדים.** מקבלים עדות שנים על דעת הגאון והנה לא פירש בתורה אם תקובל עדות השאר והאשה ועדות אויב המת ויש אומרים אם היו שני עדים מכחישים שני' עדים השלישי יכריע לזכות או לחובה ויש אומרים שנים עדים מהחכמים ושלשה מהאחרים וגם אנחנו צריכים בעל כרחנו לדברי הקבלה:


OR THREE WITNESSES. Who accept the testimony of two witnesses.¹³?” So says the Gaon.¹⁴ Look, the Torah does not state whether we accept the testimony of relatives or of a woman or the testimony of an enemy of the deceased.¹⁵ Some say that if two witnesses contradict two witnesses, then a third witnesses determines innocence or guilt.¹⁶. Others say two witnesses from among the wise men and three from the others.¹⁷ We are thus forced to rely on the words of tradition.¹⁸ is, just as two witnesses form one unit, so do three witnesses form one unit, and if one of the three is disqualified the entire unit is disqualified.

footnotes:
¹³ According to the Gaon two witnesses may testify before three witnesses. The latter may then testify as to what they heard. However, two witnesses cannot testify before two other witnesses. Thus according to the Gaon our verse should be read as follows: at the mouth of two witnesses who saw the event that they are testifying to, or at the mouth of three witnesses who heard it from two witnesses, shall he that is to die be put to death. The Gaon so interpreted our verse because he wanted to avoid the question, “If two witnesses are enough to convict a person, why does Scripture go on to say or three witnesses
¹⁴ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
¹⁵ We must rely on tradition in these instances. I.E. comments thus because he will soon say that we must rely on tradition regarding the understanding of our verse.
¹⁶ Hence Scripture reads, At the mouth of two…or three witnesses shall he…be put to death
¹⁷ Non-scholars.
¹⁸ In understanding our verse, as we must with regard to the testimony of relatives, or of a woman, or the testimony of an enemy of the deceased. According to the rabbis the meaning of At the mouth of two…or three witnesses



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:7:1)


**יד העדים.** יד כל אחד מהעדים וכן וימס לבב העם ורבים כן:


THE HAND¹⁹. OF THE WITNESSES.²⁰. The hand of each one of the witnesses.²¹ is the singular. *Edim* is in the plural. We would thus expect *yede haedim*. Hence I.E.’s explanation. Similarly *and the heart of the people melted*²² (Josh. 7:6).²³ There are many other such instances.

footnotes:
¹⁹ Hebrew, yad
²⁰ Hebrew, edim
²¹ Yad
²² Heart is in the singular, people is in the plural. Thus our text should have read, the hearts of the people.
²³ Translated according to I.E.

**ובערת.** פירשתיו:


SO THOU SHALT PUT AWAY. I have previously explained the meaning of *u-vi’arta* (so thou shalt put away).



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:8:1)


**כי יפלא ממך דבר.** עם השופט ידבר בעבור היות עדות שנים להמית המת וזה פי' בין דם לדם בין דם נקי לדם חייב:


IF THERE ARISE A MATTER TOO HARD FOR THEE. Moses speaks to the judges. This follows the previous law because two witnesses are required in order to execute a person. The meaning of *between blood and blood* is, between innocent blood and guilty blood.²⁴

²⁴ In other words, the previous verse deals with executing a culprit. Our verse tells us what the judge is to do if he cannot determine whether the accused is guilty or not.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:12:1

**ומת האיש ההוא.** בית דין ימיתוהו והטעם וכל ישראל ישמעו ויראו וכאשר הזכיר השופט באר המשפטים שהוא חייב לשמרם:


EVEN THAT MAN SHALL DIE. The court shall put him to death. The reason for the latter is so that *the people shall hear, and fear* (v. 13). When Moses mentions the judge, he explains the laws that the judge must keep.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:15:2)


**אשר יבחר** ע״‎פ נביא או משפט האורים והטעם לא אשר תבחר אתה:


WHOM THE LORD THY GOD SHALL CHOOSE. Via a prophet or via the *urim* (Ex. 28:30). The point of the clause is you shall not choose him.³³

footnotes:
³³ The Lord, not you, shall choose the king.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:15:3)


**לא תוכל.** מדרך האמת:


THOU MAYEST NOT.³⁴. Literally, you cannot. From a rational point of view.³⁵ does not mean you are physically restrained. It means you are rationally restrained. It is irrational for you to place a non-Jew on the throne of Israel.

footnotes:
³⁴ Hebrew, tukhal
³⁵ Literally, from the true way. In other words, You cannot


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:15:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:15:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:15:4)


**לא אחיך** שאיננו ממשפחת ישראל ולא אדומי אע״‎פ שכתוב בו אחיך ובעבור כי הסוסים בימים ההם היו מוציאים ממצרים והעד דברי שלמה ותצא מרכבה ממצרים והנה יצטרך למען הרבות סוסיו שיחטיא העם לשוב אל מצרי' וזה דבר ברור כי השם גאלם משם ובשובם יחללו השם ואורי' הנביא בסכנת הנפש ברח לשם גם ירמיהו בעל כרחו הלך לשם:


WHO IS NOT THY BROTHER. Who is not of the family of Israel, and also one who is an Edomite even though Scripture refers to Edomites as “your brother.”³⁶ Now in those days horses were brought from Egypt. Scripture’s words concerning Solomon, *And a chariot came up and went out of Egypt* (I Kings 10:29), is proof of this. The king would thus cause Israel to sin if he wanted to multiply his horses, for he would cause the people to return to Egypt. This is clearly so:³⁷ God took Israel out of Egypt, and by going back they would desecrate God’s name. Uriah the prophet fled to Egypt because his life was in danger.³⁸ Also Jeremiah went to Egypt against his will.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁶ See Deut. 23:8.
³⁷ That is, that multiplying horses would entail going down to Egypt.
³⁸ Otherwise he would not have gone down to Egypt. See Jer. 26:21.
³⁹ See Jer. 43:5-9.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:16:1)


**לא תוסיפון.** מצוה היתה ולא נכתבה ויש אומרים מטעם לא תוסיפו לראותם עוד עד עולם וזאת דרך אחרת:


YE SHALL HENCEFORTH RETURN NO MORE. This was a commandment that was not written down.⁴⁰ [to Egypt]. The problem is that Scripture does not record such a command earlier. Hence I.E.’s comment. Some say that this law is derived from ye *shall see them again no more for ever* (Ex. 14:13). However, the latter has a different meaning.⁴¹

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Our verse reads, As the Lord hath said unto you, Ye shall henceforth return no more
⁴¹ It is a prophecy, not a command.

THAT HIS HEART TURN NOT AWAY. For he will go after his lusts.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:17:2)


**וכסף וזהב לא ירבה לו.** שלא יעניש את ישראל והנה שלמה שנתן הכסף כאבנים בירושלים מרוב אהבתו לממון התרעמו עליו ישראל ואמרו אביך הכביד את עולנו ויש אומרים בעבור הבנינים התרעמו ואין זה אמת כי הגרים בנו הבית כי כן כתוב והנה אדוניהם שהי' על המס רגמוהו במותו:


NEITHER SHALL HE GREATLY MULTIPLY TO HIMSELF SILVER AND GOLD. So that he does not punish⁴² Israel. Now Israel complained about Solomon who, because of his great love of money, *made silver to be in Jerusalem as stones* (I Kings 10:27). They said [to his son], *Thy father made our yoke grievous* (I Kings 12:10). Some say that they complained because of the buildings.⁴³ However, this is not so, for strangers built the house.⁴⁴ The latter is clearly stated in Scripture.⁴⁵ Look, following Solomon’s death Adinoram, who was over the levy,⁴⁶ was stoned to death by the Israelites (I Kings 10:18).⁴⁷

footnotes:
⁴² That is, oppress.
⁴³ That is, they complained because Solomon forced them to build his buildings.
⁴⁴ The temple and, by extension, all the other royal buildings.
⁴⁵ See I Kings 9:20-22; II Chron. 2:16,17.
⁴⁶ I.E. takes this to mean in charge of the taxes.
⁴⁷ For taxing them.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:20:1)

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 17:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/17:20:2)


**ולבלתי סור.** כי אם לא ילמד לא ידע מה המצוה ושכרו למען יאריך ימים:


**that he may not turn away** for if he did not study, he would not know the law. His reward will be **that he lengthen the days** .



## Chapter 18


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:1:1)


**לא יהיה לכהנים הלוים.** כאשר הזכיר משפטי המלך שהוא השופט הזכיר גם משפט הכהנים שהם מורי התורה ואחר שאמר שאין להם נחלה יקחו מאת הזובח הזרוע והלחיים והקיבה הזרוע מהגוף והלחיים שהלשון ביניהם מהראש והקיבה מהקרב ודרך דרש כי הכהן הוא שוחט השלמים הזרוע שכר זרועו בשחיטה והלחיים שכר הברכה והקיבה שכר הבדיקה ועל דרך הפשט כי אלה טובים משאר הבשר גם הזכיר דברי לוי כי גם הם יורו התורה בשערים והעד דברי הימים:


THE PRIESTS THE LEVITES, EVEN ALL THE TRIBE OF LEVI, SHALL HAVE NO PORTION. When Scripture mentions the laws of the king,¹ who is the judge,² it makes mention of the laws of the *kohanim*, who are the teachers of the Torah. After Scripture states that they have no inheritance,³ it states that they will take from the sacrificer⁴ the shoulder, the cheeks, and the maw:⁵ the shoulder from the body; the cheeks, which have the tongue between them, from the head; and the maw from the insides. There is a midrashic interpretation that since the *kohen* slaughters the peace offerings, he receives the shoulder as payment for the use of his shoulder in the slaughtering, the cheeks as payment for reciting the blessing,⁶ and the maw as payment for the investigation.⁷ checks the innards to see if they are healthy. If they are not, the animal is declared unfit and cannot be eaten. However, according to the plain meaning of the text, these⁸ are better than the rest of the meat.⁹ Scripture also mentions the laws regarding the Levites,¹⁰ for they too¹¹. will teach the Torah in the gates. The Book of Chronicles shows this to be the case.¹² (the Levites) *taught in Judah, having the hook of the Law of the Lord with them; and they went about throughout all the cities of Judah, and taught among the people*.

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:8:1)


**לבד ממכריו.** יש אומרים שהוא כמו מאת מכריהם בדרך רחוקה מעט ולא אבה הדקדוק רק הוא מגזר' וכי תמכרו ממכר והטעם כי אם מכר ביתו שירש מאבותיו כאשר כתוב ויצא ממכר בית ועיר אחוזתו וזה פירוש על האבות לא יאמר לו הנה יש לך לא תאכל עוד אלא חלק כחלק יאכלו וכאשר השלים להזכיר דבר השופט והכהן הזכיר דבר הנביא:


BESIDE THAT WHICH IS HIS DUE. Some say that *mimkarav* (which is his due) is similar to *makkarekhem*¹³. 38. The printed texts have *makkarehem*. (them that bestow it upon you)¹⁴ (which is his due) his relatives. They read our verse as, beside that which his relatives from his fathers’ houses bestow upon him. They interpret the latter as follows: if a member of a *kohen* family allows another *kohen* to sacrifice in his place, then his colleagues cannot say to him, “Since you are offering the sacrifice in place of your relative, you shall not in addition eat the sacred food with us.” See Rashbam. It should be noted that according to the commentaries, Levite in our verse refers to a *kohen* from the house of Levi. in *from them that bestow it upon you* (II Kings 12:8).¹⁵ However, they are a bit off the mark. The rules of grammar will not accede to it.¹⁶ comes from the root *nun, kaf, resh*, while *mimkarav* comes from the root *mem, kaf, resh*. On the contrary, *mimkarav* is related to *timkeru mimkar* (sell aught) in *and if thou sell aught* (Lev. 25:14).¹⁷. The meaning of *le-vad mimkarav al ha-avot* (beside that which is due according to the father’s house)¹⁸ is, if the *kohen* sold his house, which he inherited from his father as Scripture states, *then the house that was sold in the city of his*¹⁹. *possession, shall go out*²⁰) may sell, in a Levitical city, the house which he received as an inheritance. (Lev. 25:33). The latter explains *according to the fathers*.²¹) has inherited from his fathers. The local *kohanim* shall not say to him, Look, you have money, you shall not in addition eat the sacred food with us. On the contrary, they shall eat like portions.²² shall receive an equal share in the sacrificial meat. After Moses concludes the laws pertaining to the judge²³ and the *kohen*,²⁴ he mentions the laws regarding the prophet.²⁵

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:10:1)


**מעביר בנו.** ע״‎ג היתה כי יש גם היום בינינו גוים רבים עובדי האש:


ONE THAT MAKETH HIS SON OR HIS DAUGHTER TO PASS THROUGH THE FIRE. This was a form of idol worship, for we have among us today many gentiles who worship the fire.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ I.E. was probably alluding to the Zoroastrians.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:10:2)


**קוסם קסמים.** יתכן היותו שם כלל ופירושו חותכי משפט האומרים כי כן יהיה בלא ספק וכן גוזרין וקרוב ממנו קסם על שפתי מלך והפרט מעונן ומנחש ומכשף גם יתכן היותו שם פרט להוברי שמים וטעם קסמים שיש במשפטי הכוכבים הנקראים ראשונים ושניים:


ANYONE THAT USETH DIVINATION.²⁷. *Kosem* (one that useth divination) is most probably a general term. It refers to those who issue decrees and declare things will undoubtedly be so.²⁸ The term *gazerin* (magicians) is similar.²⁹ (Dan. 2:27) comes from *gazar* (to decree). Thus *gazerin* means those who decree. The word *kesem* (divine sentence) in *A divine sentence is in the lips of the king* (Prov. 16:10) is close in meaning to it. The particulars³⁰. are a *soothsayer* (me’onen), *or an enchanter* (menachesh), *or a sorcerer* (mechashef). It is also possible that *kosem* is a specific term for astrologers³¹. This is a phrase found in Is. 47:13. I.E. there defines it as astrologers. I.E. believed that the study of astrology was permitted. What was prohibited was making absolute predictions using astrology. It should be noted that I.E. believed that the *urim* and *tummim* entailed the use of astrology. Therefore I.E. probably believed that certain types of astrological predictions were prohibited, while others, i.e., those sanctioned by the Torah, were permitted. See I.E. on Ex. 28:5 (Vol. 2, pp. 595-598? and the notes thereto). See also Dov. Schwartz, *Astral Magic in Medieval Jewish Thought* (Heb.), Bar-Ilan University, Israel, 1999, pp. 62-91. and that *kesamim* (divinations) refers to divination that astrologers make based on the primary and secondary stars.³²

footnotes:
²⁷ Hebrew, kosem kesamim
²⁸ They predict the future in no uncertain terms.
²⁹ Gazerin
³⁰ Following the general term kesamim
³¹ The term used by I.E. is hovere shamayim
³² The primary stars are the true stars. The secondary stars are shooting stars and the like. The latter were thought to be produced by the stars themselves. Hence they are called secondary stars. See I.E. on Gen. 1:14 (Vol. 1, p. 38). See also Motot.

A SOOTHSAYER,³³. OR AN ENCHANTER.³⁴. I have explained these terms previously.³⁵

OR A CHARMER.³⁸. One who calls the demons together.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:11:2)


**אוב וידעני.** פירשתיו:


OR ONE THAT CONSULTETH A GHOST OR A FAMILIAR SPIRIT. I have explained these terms before.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁹ See I.E. on Lev. 19:31.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:11:3)


**ודורש אל המתים.** ההולכים אל בית הקברות ולוקחים עצם אדם מת ובעבור מחשבתו ושגיונו תראה לו דמות צורות בחלום גם בהקיץ וכל אלה תועבות השם כי האמת להיות לב האדם תמיד תמים עם בוראו וכאשר ישען על חכמתו לבקש האמת ומה שיהיה חוץ מהשם הנה לבו חסר כי הוא משובש והרוצה לדרוש ידרוש מהשם על פי הנביא על כן כתוב אחריו נביא מקרבך זה יהושע והעד שאמר אליו תשמעון וכתיב וישמעו אליו בני ישראל ועוד ראיה אחרת כי לא מצאנו נביא שנכנס עם ישראל לארץ כי אם יהושע גם כן יתכן להיות זה כלל לכל נביא שיקום אחר משה:


OR A NECROMANCER. Such as those who go to cemeteries and take the bone of a dead person and, because of their imagination and madness, see shapes⁴⁰ while they are awake⁴¹ or in a dream. All of these things⁴² are abominations to God. The truth is that a person must fully believe in his creator in his heart. When a person relies on his wisdom, and not on God, in seeking the truth and what will be in the future, his mind is deficient, for it is perverted. The person who wants to inquire,⁴³ let him inquire from God via a prophet. Scripture therefore afterward reads, *A prophet will the Lord…raise up…from the midst of thee* (v. 15). The latter refers to Joshua. *Unto him ye shall hearken* (*Ibid.*), which is later followed by *and the children of Israel hearkened unto him* (Deut. 34:9), is proof of this. The fact that we do not find any other prophet but Joshua entering Canaan⁴⁴ is additional proof of this.⁴⁵. The only one who fits that description is Joshua, for we do not find any other prophet but Joshua immediately following Moses. It is also possible that our verse lays down a general rule for each and every prophet who will arise after Moses.⁴⁶ applies not only to Moses’ successor, but to all prophets following Moses.

⁴³ As to what the future holds.
⁴⁴ That is, leading Israel.
⁴⁵ Verse 15 speaks of A prophet…like unto me
⁴⁶ In other words, A prophet…like unto me

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:19:1)


**אנכי אדרוש מעמו.** והנה מיתתו בידי שמים והעד האיש אשר לא שמע אל דברי הנביא שאמר הכני נא ושם כתוב בדבר ה' וכבר היה הנביא נודע:


I WILL REQUIRE IT OF HIM. His death is thus caused by God.⁴⁷ What happened to the man who did not listen to the words of the prophet who said, *Smite me, I pray thee* (I Kings 20:35) is proof of this.⁴⁸ Scripture states there that the prophet⁴⁹. spoke *by the word of the Lord*⁵⁰ (I Kings 21:35), The prophet was previously known as a prophet of God.

⁴⁷ He is not executed by a human court.
⁴⁸ The man who did not listen to the prophet was killed by a lion.
⁴⁹ Who said, Smite me, I pray thee
⁵⁰ What he said came from God, not from the prophet’s mind. Hence the addressee was obligated to follow what he said.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 18:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/18:22:1)


**אשר ידבר הנביא.** ג״‎כ דברי ירמיהו בבא דבר הנביא בזמן שיאמר ואחר שהשלים לבאר דברי המעוננים שהיו בארץ והיא דרך ע״‎ג הזהיר שלא ישפך דם נקי בארץ והטעם שתהיה הארץ טהורה מתועבת המעוננים גם לא תהיה טמאה בדם נקי:


WHEN A PROPHET SPEAKETH. The prophet Jeremiah similarly says, *when the word of the prophet shall come to pass* (Jer. 28:9) in the time predicted by the prophet.⁵¹ (Jer. 28:9). After Moses finished explaining the law relating to the soothsayers and their practices, which were practiced by the idolaters of the land, he warned⁵² that no innocent blood be shed in the land. The reason for this is that the land shall be pure from the abomination of the soothsayers, and it should also not be defiled by the shedding of innocent blood.

⁵¹ Then shall the prophet he known
⁵² In the following chapter.

HIS NEIGHBOR. *Et re’ehu* means with his neighbor. There are many such cases.⁴ has the meaning of *im*. See Ex. 1:1.

FETCHETH A STROKE. *Ve-niddechah* (fetcheth a stroke) is a *nifal. Nidche* (the dispersed of) in *He gathereth together the dispersed of Israel* (Ps. 147:2) is the *kal* form.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:5:3)


**בגרזן.** ברזל שיכרתו בו העץ מגזרת נגזרתי:


WITH THE AXE. *Garzen* (axe) refers to a metal instrument used to cut a tree down. It is related to the word *nigzarti* (I was cut off).⁵. However, I.E. believes that *nigrazti* and *nigzarti* are one and the same word, with the letters inverted. See I.E. on Ps. 31:23.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:5:4)


**ונשל הברזל.** כמו ונסע מגזרת כי ישל זיתך רק זה נשל פעל עומד ונשל גוים רבים יוצא וכן קול גדול ולא יסף עם יסף ה' יגון על דעת מדקדק גדול כי על דעתי שניהם יוצאים:


AND THE HEAD SLIPPETH FROM THE HELVE. *Venashal* (slippeth) is similar to *ve-nasa* (shall set forward) (Num. 2:17).⁶ It is related to the word *yishal* (shall drop off) in *for thine olives shall drop off* (Deut. 28:40). The word *ve-nashal* (and shall cast) in *and shall cast out many nations* (Deut. 7:1) is transitive. However, *ve-nashal* (slippeth) in our verse is intransitive. According to a great grammarian the same is true of the word *yasaf* (more) in *with a great voice, and it went on no more* (Deut. 5:19) and *yasaf* (added) in *For the Lord hath added sorrow* (Jer. 45:3).⁷ is intransitive in Deut. 5:19 and intransitive in Jer. 45:3. However, I believe that they are both transitive.

footnotes:
⁶ Both words are intransitive and are similarly vocalized.
⁷ Yasaf

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:8:1

**ואם ירחיב ה' אלהיך.** אם ישמרו כל התורה על כן כתוב כי תשמור את כל המצוה הזאת והיא שלש ערים:


AND IF THE LORD THY GOD ENLARGE THY BORDER. If Israel observes the entire Torah.⁹ Scripture therefore reads,¹⁰ *if thou shalt keep all this commandment* (v. 9), the reference being to the commandment dealing with the three cities of refuge.¹¹

Filwarg suggests reading “or the reference is (or, some say the reference is) to the commandment dealing with the three cities of refuge. See Nachmanides on this verse.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:9:1)


**וללכת בדרכיו כל הימים.** בלי הפסק ביניהם:


AND TO WALK EVER IN HIS WAYS.¹² Without any break in them.¹³

footnotes:
¹² Literally, and to walk in His ways all the days.
¹³ With out any break in the days that you walk before the Lord.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:14:1)


**לא תסיג גבול רעך.** תסיג ידוע וכן אל תסיג גבול עולם והוא קרוב מהשחית:


THOU SHALT NOT REMOVE THY NEIGHBOUR’S LANDMARKS. The meaning of *tassig* (thou shalt…remove) is known.¹⁴ Its meaning is similar to the word *tasseg* (remove) in *Remove not the ancient landmark* (Prov. 22:28). It is close in meaning to destroyed.¹⁵

¹⁴ From its context (Weiser).
¹⁵ I.E. renders our clause, thou shalt not destroy thy neighbor’s landmark. He renders Prov. 22:28, destroy not the ancient landmark.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:14:2

**ראשוני'.** שחלקו הארץ ונסמכה פרשה זו כי הסגת גבול מביא לידי ריב ומכות ורציחה על כן כתיב אחרי זאת נפש בנפש עין בעין שן בשן:


WHICH THEY OF OLD TIME HAVE SET. The ones who divided up the land.¹⁶

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:15:1)


והטוען כי חברו הסיג גבולו יביא עדים על כן **לא יקום עד אחד.** בין לרוצח בין למכה בין למסיג בין לכל דבר רק ע״‎פ שני עדים דרך קצרה כי במקום אחר ע״‎פ שנים עדים:


ONE WITNESS SHALL NOT RISE UP. This section¹⁷ follows,¹⁸ because destroying the boundary markers¹⁹ leads to quarrels, blows, and murder. Scripture therefore goes on to state, following this, *Life for life, eye for eye, tooth for tooth* (v. 21). The one who claims that his neighbor removed his landmark must bring witnesses. Hence, Scripture reads, *one witness shall not rise up*, which means that two witnesses are required in cases involving murder, assault, landmark removal, or any matter.²⁰ S*hene edim* (two witnesses) is an abridged phrase,²¹ is short for *shenaim*. for Scripture elsewhere reads, *shenayim edim* (two witnesses) (Deut. 17:6).

footnotes:
¹⁷ The section dealing with witnesses (verses 15-21).
¹⁸ It follows the section dealing with the removal of boundaries.
¹⁹ Translated according to I.E.
²⁰ It does not apply only to boundary disputes which precede it.
²¹ In other words, sheni

TO BEAR PERVERTED WITNESS AGAINST HIM. Such as accusing him of idol worship.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:18:1

TESTIFIED. *Anah* means testified. The same law applies to flogging²² and fines.²³

²² Where a false witness accused an innocent person of a crime for which the punishment is a flogging.
²³ Where a false witness accused an innocent person of a crime for which one is punished with a fine (Weiser).



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 19:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/19:21:1)


והטעם **לא תחוס עינך.** על עד שקר:


AND THINE EYE SHALL NOT PITY. This means thine eye shall not pity the false witness.²⁴ editions. *Vat. Ebr*. 38 reads, “upon one who deserves punishment.” The Pardes edition of Scriptures reads, *al sheker* (for lying). However, this appears to be an error for *al ed sheker* (upon a false witness).

## Chapter 20


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:1:1)


**כי תצא למלחמה.** דבקה עם לא תחום עינך עם מי שהוא חייב רק צאת למלחמה יש לך לחום על מארם ובונה ונוטע ורך הלבב:


WHEN THOU GOEST FORTH TO BATTLE. This is connected to *And thine eye shall not pity* (Deut. 19:21), for one is not to have pity.¹ However, when going forth to war you should pity² the man who has betrothed a woman, or built a house, or planted a vineyard, or one whose heart is faint.³

¹ On the guilty or on the one who is obligated to go into battle.
² And exempt from battle.

FEAR NOT. In your heart.

NOR BE ALARMED. And flee.

NEITHER BE YE AFFRIGHTED. In deed.⁵

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:5:1)


**חנכו.** כמו חנכת המזבח וכן חנוך לנער ומזה למדו רבותינו ז״‎ל מחנכין את הנערים והטעם כי לבו וכל תאותו לחנוך ביתו והנה לבו לביתו לא למלחמה על כן ינוס ויניס אחרים:


DEDICATED IT. *Chanakho* (dedicated it) is similar to the word *chanukkat* (dedication of) in *the dedication offer of the altar* (Num. 7:10). The word *chanokh* (train) in *Train up a child* (Prov. 22:6) is similar. Our rabbis, of blessed memory, learned from the latter that we train (*mekhannechin*) children. The reason that [one who has not dedicated his house is released from service] is that such a man’s heart and desire is to dedicate his house. Look, his mind will be upon his house, not on the battle. He will therefore flee and cause others to flee.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:6:1)


**חללו.** מגזרת חלילים כי מנהגם היה לחול בכרמים:


USED THE FRUIT THEREOF. The word *chillelo* (used the fruit thereof) is connected to the word *challilim* (flutes), for they were in the habit of dancing⁶ in the vineyards⁷ (Jud. 21:21).⁸

footnotes:
⁶ When the harvest was concluded.

### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:7:1)


**ארש אשה ולא לקחה.** בנשואין והנה זה הכתוב לאות כי יש מי שימות בבא יומו רק המת במלחמה מת בלא יומו על כן אמר דוד או במלחמה ירד ונספה:


AND WHAT MAN IS THERE THAT HATH BETROTHED A WIFE, AND HATH NOT TAKEN HER? In marriage.⁹ (betrothal) and *nesu’in* (marriage). In the first stage the woman, though married, still lived in her father’s house. In the second stage she went to live with her husband. The marriage was not consummated till the second stage. Look, this verse is a sign that there are people who die when their time comes.¹⁰ However, the one who dies in battle dies before his time. Hence David said, *or he shall go down into battle, and be swept away* (I Sam. 26:10).¹¹.

footnotes:
⁹ Brought her into his house. There were two stages to marriage in ancient Israel, erusin
¹⁰ Thus the man who has married a woman but has not consummated the marriage is exempt from the army because his time to die has probably not yet come. He will therefore be able to consummate his marriage if he does not go forth to war.
¹¹ The entire clause reads: or his day shall come to die; or he shall go down into battle, and be swept away

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:8:1)


**הירא.** להכות אחר:


THAT IS FEARFUL. To strike another person.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:8:2)


**ורך הלבב.** לסבול מכת אחר:


AND FAINT-HEARTED. To suffer someone’s blow.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:8:3)


**את לבב.** הנה את יראה נוסף רק טעמו כאשר פי' שהוא כעצם הדבר:


HIS BRETHREN’S HEART. Look, the word *et* appears superfluous.¹² (lest brethren’s heart melt). The particle *et* usually connects an active verb with the object. However, *yimmas* is in the *nifal*; it is passive. Hence the particle *et* appears to be superfluous. However, its meaning is as I have previously explained.¹³ does not always connect an active verb with the direct object. See I.E. on Ex. 10:8 (Vol. 2, pp. 187,188). It indicates the thing itself.¹⁴ (heart).

footnotes:
¹² The Hebrew reads, ve-lo yimmas et levav echav
¹³ That et
¹⁴ It is used to emphasize the word which follows in this case lev

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:9:1

SHALL BE APPOINTED. *U-fakedu* (shall be appointed) is related to the word *pekod* (appoint).

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:16:1)


**לא תחיה.** אסור שיאכילם או ישקם והכלל בימיהם היה חייב להמיתם אם יוכל ולא הזכיר הגרגשי כי הוא המעט והקטן שבהם:


THOU SHALT SAVE ALIVE NOTHING. It is forbidden to give them food or drink. The general rule is that they were to kill them if possible. The latter applied to their days. Moses does not mention the Girgashites,¹⁷ for they were the least and the smallest among them.

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:19:1)


**כי האדם עץ השדה.** כבר ביארתי בספר היסוד כי יתכן בכל לשון לקצר לאחוז דרך קצרה כמו חמור לחם רק מלת לא לא יתכן להיותה נחסרת כי הטעם יהיה להפך ומדקדק גדול ספרדי אמר כי חסר ה״‎א וכן הוא הכי האדם עץ השדה וזה הטעם איננו נכון בעיני כי מה טעם לאמר לא תשחית עץ פרי כי איננו כבני אדם שיוכל לברוח מפניך ולפי דעתי שאין לנו צורך לכל זה וזה פירושו כי ממנו תאכל ואותו לא תכרות כי האדם עץ השדה והטעם כי חיי בן אדם הוא עץ השדה וכמוהו כי נפש הוא חובל כי חיי נפש הוא חובל:


FOR IS THE TREE OF THE FIELD MAN.¹⁸. The latter literally means for man is a tree of the field. This, as I.E. goes on to say, does not make sense. Hence the interpretations which follow. I have previously explained in the *Sefer Ha-Yesod*¹⁹ that it is possible in all languages to be brief and to apply an abbreviated style, as in “an ass bread”²⁰ (I Sam. 16:20).²¹. However, it is impossible to leave out the word *lo* (no, not),²² for in this case the meaning would be reversed.²³ as if written *ki ha-adam lo etz ha-sadeh* (for the tree of the field is not a man). In other words, Onkelos claims that the word *lo* has been left out of the verse. I.E. disagrees. Hence his comment. A great Spanish grammarian²⁴ said that a *heh*²⁵. has been omitted.²⁶ in *ki ha-adam etz ha-sadeh*. Our clause should be interpreted as if written, *hakhi adam etz ha-sadeh* (for is the tree of the field man).²⁷. The latter literally means for man is a tree of the field. Hence the unnamed grammarian’s interpretation. Now this interpretation does not appear correct to me. Why would Scripture say that you shall not destroy fruit trees, because, unlike a human being, they cannot run away from you?²⁸ as, for is the tree of the field man. I believe there is no need for all this.²⁹ as if written *ha-khi adam etz ha-sadeh*. The following is the meaning of our clause. For thou mayest eat of them, but thou shalt not cut them down, for the tree of the field is man; that is, the life of man is supported by trees.³⁰ (for man) is short for *ki chayye ha-adam* (for the life of man). Compare, *For he taketh a life to pledge* (Deut. 24:6), which is short for, for he taketh that which sustains man’s life to pledge.

footnotes:
¹⁸ Hebrew, ki ha-adam etz ha-sadeh
¹⁹ A Hebrew grammar composed by I.E. The book survives only in fragments.
²⁰ Translated literally.
²¹ An ass bread is short for an ass laden with bread
²² If one wants to employ an abbreviated style.
²³ Onkelos renders ki ha-adam etz ha-sadeh
²⁴ I.E. does not identify the grammarian.
²⁵ An interrogative heh
²⁶ From the word ki
²⁷ Our verse reads, ki ha-adam etz ha-sadeh
²⁸ This is the meaning of our verse if we render ha-khi adam etz ha-sadeh
²⁹ To interpret ki ha-adam etz ha-sadeh
³⁰ In other words, ki ha-adam


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:19:2)


**ואותו לא תכרות.** דבק עם לבא מפניך במצור הנה לא תשחית עץ פרי שהוא חיים לבן אדם רק מותר שתאכל ממנו ואסור לך להשחיתו כדי שתבא העיר מפניך במצור והעד על זה הפירוש שהוא נכון שאמר וכרת ובנית מצור:


BUT THOU SHALT NOT CUT THEM DOWN. This is connected to *that it should he besieged of thee*.³¹ Look, do not destroy a fruit tree, which is a source of sustenance for human life. Be that as it may, you are permitted to eat of it but you are prohibited from destroying it so that the city will be besieged because of you. The fact that Scripture then states *thou mayest destroy and cut down*,³² *that thou mayest build bulwarks* (v. 20), is proof of this.

footnotes:
³¹ In other words, you shall not cut down the fruit trees to use their wood to build siege works.
³² The trees that do not produce fruit.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 20:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/20:20:1)


**עד רדתה.** כי לולי שהיתה נשגבה לא אמר הכתוב ימים רבים:


UNTIL IT FALL. Scripture would not have said a *long time* (v. 19) if the city were not mighty.³³

³³ And required siege works to take it.

IF ONE BE FOUND SLAIN. As Scripture mentions the war against enemies,¹ it goes on to say what the law is if one fights against his fellow² and a corpse is found in the land of Israel, and the one who slew him is unknown.³

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:3:1)

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:3:2)


**ולא משכה בעול.** להביא אבנים כמנהג העגלות:


AND WHICH HATH NOT DRAWN IN THE YOKE. To bring stones, as is the practice with heifers.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ They are used for pulling wagons.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:4:1)


**איתן.** תקיף:


ROUGH. The word *etan* (rough) means mighty.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:4:2)


**וערפו שם.** יסירו עורף העגלה:


AND SHALL BREAK…THE NECK. *Ve-arefu* (and they shall break) means they shall remove the back of the neck of the heifer.⁶ means back of the neck. *Ve-arefu* literally means they shall neck it. Hence I.E.’s interpretation.

### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:6:1)

AND ALL THE ELDERS OF THAT CITY. The elders referred to in *then thy elders…shall come forth* (v. 2).



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:7:1)


ה״‎א **שפכה.** תחת סימן הרבים כמו שפכה אשורי ועל דרך הדרש שישוב לאחת כי היד לשון נקבה ברוב:


[SHED.] The *heh* in *shafekhu* (shed) indicates the plural.⁹ is usually used to indicate the third person plural perfect. In our verse a *heh* is used in place of the *vav*. It is like the *heh* in *shafekhu ashurai* (my steps slipped) (Ps. 73:2).¹⁰ is spelled with a *heh* in place of a *vav*. However, interpreting it midrashically, the *heh* refers to each one of the hands, for the word *yad* (hand) is in most cases feminine.¹¹ is the third person perfect feminine. In this case our verse reads: *yadenu shafekhah* (our hands it has spilled). According to I.E. a combination of the plural with the singular refers to each one of the plural. See I.E. on Gen. 49:22 (Vol. 1, p. 441).

footnotes:
⁹ A vav
¹⁰ Here too the word shafekhu
¹¹ If we ignore the vowels, then our word reads shafekhah. Shafekhah


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:7:2)


**ועינינו לא ראו.** ויתכן שהשם צוה לעשות כן העיר הקרובה כי לולי שעשו עבירה כדומה לה לא נזדמן להם שיהרג אדם קרוב מהם ומחשבות השם עמקו וגבהו לאין קץ אצלנו:


NEITHER HAVE OUR EYES SEEN IT. It is possible that God commanded the city closest to the slain to perform this¹² because if the city had not committed a similar deed, then the murder of a person near their city would not have occurred. God’s thoughts are deep and infinitely beyond our comprehension.¹³

footnotes:
¹² Ritual.
¹³ Hence it is very difficult to understand the details of the ritual which they must perform.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:7:3)


**וענו ואמרו.** כמו וענית ואמרת ושם אפרשנו:


AND THEY SHALL SPEAK AND SAY. *Ve-anu ve-ameru* (and they shall speak and say) is similar to *ve-anita ve-amarta* (and thou shalt speak and say) (Deut. 26:5). I will explain it there.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ See I.E. on Deut. 26:5.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:8:1)


**כפר לעמך.** ששגגו ולא שמרו הדרכים המסוכנים:


FORGIVE, O LORD, THY PEOPLE. Who erred and did not guard the dangerous roads.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ And are thus indirectly responsible for the death.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:8:2)


**ונכפר.** חסר תי״‎ו כמו ונוסרו כל הנשים והם שנים בנינים בנין התפעל עם בנין נפעל:


SHALL BE FORGIVEN. A *tav* has been omitted in the word *ve-nikapper*¹⁶. (shall be forgiven). It is similar to the word *venivasseru*¹⁷. (may be taught) in *that all women may be taught* (Ezek. 23:48). They¹⁸ and *ve-nivasseru*. are a combination of two forms, the *nifal* and the *hitpa’el*.

footnotes:
¹⁶ The word should have read ve-nitkapper
¹⁷ The word should have read ue-nitvassseru

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:8:3)


וטעם זכר **אשר פדית.** כאשר פדיתם ממצרים למען חסדיך כן פדם מזה הדם ועונשו:


Scripture mentions *whom thou hast redeemed*¹⁹ to indicate that as You redeemed Israel from Egypt as a manifestation of Your great mercies, similarly redeem them from this blood and the punishment for its shedding.

footnotes:
¹⁹ The question is, why add this appellation to Israel? The Bible does not usually describe Israel in this manner.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:9:1)


**ואתה תבער הדם הנקי.** פירושו עונש הדם הנקי או יהיה הדם דם הנקי ויש אומרים אתה חייב לבער שפיכות דמים והנכון בעיני הוא אשר הזכרתי כי לא ישפך דם נקי בארצך אם תעשה הישר בעיני ה' כסוד שכר עבירה עבירה ושכר מצוה מצוה:


SO SHALT THOU PUT AWAY THE INNOCENT BLOOD. The meaning of *the innocent blood* is, the punishment for the shedding of the innocent blood.²⁰ (ha-dam hanaki) *from the midst of thee*. The question arises, “What does this clause mean?” The innocent blood has already been shed, i.e., put away. Hence I.E. points out that *ha-dam ha-naki* is short for *onesh ha-dam ha-naki* (the punishment for the innocent blood), and our verse teaches that by performing this ritual you remove from your midst the punishment for the shedding of innocent blood. See Meijler. On the other hand, the word *ha-dam ha-naki* (the innocent blood) is short for *ha-dam, dam ha-naki* (the blood, the innocent blood).²¹, the spiller of innocent blood. That is, if after the ritual is performed the murderer is discovered, you shall put away the shedder of blood, of innocent blood. Some say that the meaning of *so shalt thou put away the innocent blood* is, you must eradicate the shedding of blood. However, in my opinion, and I have mentioned this before, it means no innocent blood will be shed in your land if you do what is right in the eyes of God. The latter is in keeping with the secret that the reward of sin is sin, and the reward of a commandment is the commandment.²² 2:1. If innocent blood was shed near the city it was because the people of the city committed a similar crime. Thus the wages of sin is more sin.

footnotes:
²⁰ Our verse reads, So shalt thou put away the innocent blood
²¹ According to Meijler our verse is interpreted as if written shofekh hadam, dam ha-naki
²² Avot



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:10:1)


**כי תצא.** זו הפרשה דבקה עם כי תצור אל עיר ונכנסה ביניהם פרשת עגלה ערופה בעבור החלל הנמצא בא״‎י. ולא כן חוצה לארץ. ואף כי במלחמה:


WHEN THOU GOEST FORTH TO BATTLE. This section is connected with *When thou shalt besiege a city* (Deut. 20:19).²³ The section dealing with the decapitated heifer²⁴ was inserted between them because it deals with a slain person found in the Land of Israel.²⁵ This law does not apply outside of the Land of Israel²⁶. and certainly not in time of war.²⁷

footnotes:
²³ Both deal with war.
²⁴ Verses 1-9.
²⁵ When one goes to war he encounters slain people. There is thus a thematic connection between the sections.
²⁶ For verse 1 explicitly states, in the land which the Lord thy God giveth thee
²⁷ In the Land of Israel. Thus Scripture contrasts the law regarding finding a slain person in time of peace and finding one in time of war.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:11:1)


**אשת.** כבר דרשוהו חז״‎ל:


A WOMAN. The rabbis have already explained this.²⁸ on this verse.

²⁸ The rabbis tell us that the law also applies to married women. See Sifre

OF GOODLY FORM. In his own eyes.²⁹ on this verse.
