²⁹ In other words, the law applies even in cases where most people would consider the captive woman unattractive. See Sifre


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:11:3)


**וחשקת בה.** שתחזקנה לקחתה לאשה כי אחר כן יאמר ובעלתה וכמוהו ואקח אותה לי לאשה:


AND THOU HAST A DESIRE UNTO HER. You will desire to take her for a wife,³⁰). “Wouldest take her to thee for a woman” might mean that the Israelite merely wants to have sex with her. Hence I.E.’s comment that the intention is to marry her. for after this Scripture states, *and be her husband. So I took her to be my wife* (le-ishah) (Gen. 12:19) is similar.³¹

footnotes:
³⁰ Our verse literally reads: and thou hast a desire unto her, and wouldest take her to thee for a woman (le-ishah
³¹ That I took her to be my woman means I took her to be my wife.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:12:1)


**והבאתה אל תוך ביתך וגלחה.** י״‎א שמא בעבור שערה חשק בה על כן תגלח:


THEN THOU SHALT BRING HER HOME TO THY HOUSE; AND SHE SHALL SHAVE HER HEAD. Some say that she shaves her head as he may have desired her because of her hair.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:12:2)

PARE. *Ve-asetah et tzipparneha* (and pare her nails) means she shall let her nails grow long so that she will appear disgusting in his eyes.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:1)

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:2)

AND BEWAIL. So that he is no longer happy with her.³²

footnotes:
³² So that he no longer enjoys her company, for she is morose.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:3)


**ירח ימים.** אולי יקל חשקו וי״‎א **וגלחה** בעבור היותה טמאה וכן משפט המצורע:


A FULL MONTH. Perhaps his desire will cool. Others say:³³ SHE SHALL SHAVE HER HEAD. Because she is unclean.³⁴ The law regarding the leper is similar.³⁵

footnotes:
³³ What follows is an alternate interpretation of verses 12 and 13. It may be a later insertion into the commentary by I.E., for he repeats the verse. It should be noted that a similar phenomenon appears at the end of his comments on Ex. 20:14 and in his comments on Ex. 34:6.
³⁴ Ritually unclean.
³⁵ The leper shaves his head when undergoing purification. See Lev. 14:8,9.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:4)


**ועשתה.** תיקון. ותקונה לכרות העודף וכן לא עשה שפמו ותחת מעשה מקשה והתיקון עיקר כל מעשה במקרא חוץ אם בא אחריו דבר מגונה גם יהיה רע שלם:


AND PARE. *Ve-asetah* (and pare)³⁶ means and fix. Its fixing consists in cutting off the excess.³⁷ Compare, *asah* (trimmed)³⁸ in *nor trimmed his heard* (II Sam. 19:25) and in *And instead of curled hair* (ma’aseh³⁹ miksheh) *baldness* (Is. 3:24). The main meaning of the word *ma’aseh* (work) in Scripture is fixing,⁴⁰ except for instances when something unseemly follows.⁴¹ (in doing that which was evil) (Deut. 9:18). However, even in the latter instance the reference might be to complete an evil deed.

footnotes:
³⁶ Literally, and do.
³⁷ The part of the nail that is longer than usual.
³⁸ Literally, did.
³⁹ Literally, the work of.
⁴⁰ See I.E. on Gen. 2:4 and the notes thereto (Vol. 1, p. 51).
⁴¹ Compare, la’asot ha-ra


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:5)


**והסירה את שמלת.** כי היא מטונפת:


AND SHE SHALL PUT THE RAIMENT OF HER CAPTIVITY FROM OFF HER. For it is filthy.⁴²

footnotes:
⁴² Her garments are most probably soiled. This interpretation, unlike the previous one, does not believe that he removes her garments of captivity because they were the cause of her captor’s desire for her.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:6)


**וישבה בביתך ובכתה.** והטעם תשב בביתך ירח ימים לבכות:


AND SHALL REMAIN IN THY HOUSE, AND BE WAIL…A FULL MONTH. This means she shall remain in your house a month to cry.⁴³

footnotes:
⁴³ For her father and her mother.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:7)


**את אביה ואת אמה.** אם קרוב י״‎א כי זה הבכי על אביה ועל אמה שלא התיהדו ולפי דעתי שחייב כל אדם בשקול הדעת לכבד את אביו ואת אמו בחיים ובמות:


HER FATHER AND HER MOTHER. If close by.⁴⁴. 38 reads, if he (her captor) killed them. Some say that she bewails her father and mother because they did not convert to Judaism.⁴⁵ However, I believe that reason teaches us that every person is obligated to honor his father and his mother while they live and also after their deaths.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ If her parents were recently killed, that is, if they were killed in the war. Weiser believes that the reference is to her parents’ residence. If the place of their residence is close by, then the captive woman can ascertain the fate of her parents. Vat. Ebr
⁴⁵ In this case she bewails her parents even they are still alive.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:8)


וטעם **ובכתה.** שתתאבל עליהם כמשפט ישראלית כי היא מתייהדת אם נהרגו כאשר נשבת ואין צריך להזכיר תרחץ כי היא צריכה למי נדה כי כבר נכתב אתם ושביכם:


The meaning of *and bewail…* is, she shall mourn her parents if they were killed while she was taken captive in the same manner that an Israelite women mourns, for she is converting to Judaism. Scripture had no need to mention that she shall bathe,⁴⁶, a pool of water which purifies the impure. All converts have to immerse themselves in a *mikveh*. for it is necessary for her to be purified by the waters of sprinkling,⁴⁷. According to Rabbinic interpretation the reference is to a *mikveh*. as Scripture earlier states, *ye and your captives* (Num. 31:19).⁴⁸

footnotes:
⁴⁶ In a mikveh
⁴⁷ Hebrew, me niddah
⁴⁸ Shall purify yourselves in the “waters of sprinkling.”


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:9)


וחז״‎ל אמרו עד שלשה חדשים ואע״‎פ שאין צורך לחזוק הנה העד ויהי כמשלש חדשים והוא הזמן שיחל הולד להתנועע ואסור לשכב עם אשה בספק אם היא הרה בעבור הולד כי יש מי שיולד בחסרון שנים חדשים כמנהג ג״‎כ ביתרון רק הוא המעט:


[AND AFTER THAT THOU MAYEST GO INTO HER, AND BE HER HUSBAND.] Our rabbis, of blessed memory, said that he has to wait three months.⁴⁹ on this verse. Even though the words of our sages need no support⁵⁰ I offer the following *as* proof, *And it came to pass about three months after* (Gen. 38:24).⁵¹ The latter is the time when the fetus begins to move. It is prohibited to have sexual relations with a women when one is in doubt as to whether she is pregnant or not, because of the uncertain paternity of the fetus; for some women give birth two months short of the normal term.⁵² Others give birth later than the normal term.⁵³ However, the latter rarely occurs.⁵⁴

footnotes:
⁴⁹ Before marrying the captive woman, in order to ascertain the paternity of the child that she might be carrying. See Sifre
⁵⁰ That it takes three months before one is certain as to whether or not a woman is pregnant.
⁵¹ Three months after Tamar had intercourse with Judah her pregnancy first became evident.
⁵² Thus if a woman gives birth after nine months and had intercourse with one man nine months ago, and intercourse with another man seven months ago, we do not know whether she carried for nine months and the first man is the father, or she had the child prematurely and the second man is the father.
⁵³ At 11 months it is thus possible for the captor to wait three months before taking the captive woman and for the latter to have a child 8 months later without the captor being the husband. This being the case, why did the rabbis say that the captor has to wait three months? They should have said that he should wait four months.
⁵⁴ The rabbis were not concerned with what occurs rarely.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:13:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:13:10)


**ירח ימים.** כמו חדש ימים וכבר פירשתיו ונקרא החדש ירח כי עקרו הירח:


A FULL MONTH. *Yerach yamim* (a full month) is similar to *chodesh yamim* (a whole month) (Num. 11:21).⁵⁵ I have explained this previously.⁵⁶. See I.E. on Gen. 4:4 (Vol. 1, p. 81). According to I.E., *chodesh yamim* refers to a complete cycle of the month. A month is called a *yerach*⁵⁷ means a moon. because the moon is its basis.

footnotes:
⁵⁵ Both terms mean a full month.
⁵⁶ The term chodesh yamim
⁵⁷ Yerach



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:14:1)


**ושלחתה לנפשה.** תעשה רצונה:


THEN THOU SHALT LET HER GO.⁵⁸. You shall do to her as she desires.⁵⁹ to mean her desire. He interprets *veshilachta le-nafshah* as you shall allow her to do whatever she wants.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ Hebrew, ve-shilachta le-nafshah
⁵⁹ I.E. takes the word nafshah


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:14:2)


**ומכור לא תמכרנה בכסף.** דבר הכ' במנהג הרב והטעם בשום דבר:


BUT THOU SHALT NOT SELL HER AT ALL FOR MONEY. Scripture speaks of what is widely practiced.⁶⁰ Its true meaning is, do not sell her.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Most take money when they transact a sale.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:14:3)


**לא תתעמר בה.** כמו והתעמר בו ומכרו ואין להם שלישי ופירושו לפי מקומו בטעם רמאות:


THOU SHALT NOT DEAL WITH HER AS WITH A SLAVE. The word *titammer* (deal as with a slave) is similar to the word *ve-hitammer* (and he deal as with a slave) in *and he deal with him as a slave* (Deut. 24:7). The word is not found a third time.⁶¹ The meaning of the word has to be ascertained from its context. It means deceitfully.⁶² deceitfully.

footnotes:
⁶¹ It is found only twice in Scripture.
⁶² According to I.E. our clause reads, thou shalt not deal with her deceitfully, and Deut. 24:7 as, and he deal with him

THAT WAS HATED. *Seni’ah* (hated) is an adjective. This section follows⁶⁴ because the preceding section⁶⁵ reads, *and thou hast a desire unto her* (v. 11)⁶⁶ *and…if thou have no delight in her* (v. 14).⁶⁷

IN THE DAY THAT HE CAUSETH HIS SONS TO INHERIT. When he is in good health; or on the day of his demise when he is deathly ill;⁶⁸ or the law courts cause his sons to inherit.⁶⁹

MAKE…FIRST-BORN. In word⁷⁰ and in status.

[THAT HE MAY NOT.] *Lo yukhal* (that he may not) is to be understood like its counterparts.⁷¹

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:17:1)


**יכיר.** יעשה לו הכרה או שיודה לפני אחרים מי שהוא בכור אם היה במקום שלא יודעו או הוא חייב מבתחלה שיכיר מי שהוא הבכור מבניו אם היו תאומים:


HE SHALL ACKNOWLEDGE. He shall acknowledge him by an act,⁷² or he shall admit before others who the firstborn is, if the first-born is in a place where he is unknown, or he is obligated right at the beginning to acknowledge which one of his sons is the first-born should they be twins.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:17:2)


**לתת לו פי שנים.** שיקח שני חלקים אם היו שלשה שיחשבו שהם ארבעה ויקח שני חלקים ואם שני בנים יחשבו שלשה וכן הכל ולפי דעתי שכן פירוש ויהי גא פי שנים ברוחך אלי כאשר פירשתי במקומו:


BY GIVING HIM A DOUBLE PORTION. The first-born shall take two parts. If there are three sons let them imagine that there are four and the first-born shall take two parts. If there are two sons let them imagine that there are three, and so in all cases. In my opinion this too is the meaning of *I pray thee, let a double portion of thy spirit be upon me* (II Kings 2:9), as I explained in its place.⁷³

⁷³ We no longer have I.E.’s commentary on Kings.

**כי הוא ראשית אונו.** הנודע עם הישר ידבר וכל ישראל בחזקת ישרים:


FOR HE IS THE FIRST-FRUITS OF HIS STRENGTH. The one known as first-born. Scripture speaks of the upright.⁷⁴ All of Israel are presumed to be upright.⁷⁵

STUBBORN. The word *soreh* (stubborn) is similar to the word *sorre’ah* (stubborn) in *like a stubborn heifer* (Hos. 4:16). He does not keep the positive commandments.

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:20:1)


**זולל.** מפורש והוא זולל בשר רק הוא שם כלל לנותן בכל מה שיתאוה כל מה שיבוקש ממנו:


HE IS A GLUTTON. The meaning of *zolel* (glutton) is explained.⁷⁸. It refers to one who gluttonously devours meat. However, the term refers to anyone who commits himself to all that he lusts for, to all that his desires ask of him.⁷⁹

footnotes:
⁷⁸ Prov. 23:20 speaks of zolele basar
⁷⁹ Even though Prov. 23:20 speaks of being gluttonous in regard to meat.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:20:2)


**וסובא.** מרבה לשתות והוא המשתכר והנה זה כמו אפיקורוס כי לא יבקש חיי העוה״‎ז כי אם להתענג בכל מיני מאכל ומשתה. ונסמכה זו הפרשה בעבור אשת יפת תאר והעד ושם אמו. והרמז שרמזתי בבני אהרן:


AND A DRUNKARD. *Sove* refers to one who drinks a lot. It refers to a drunkard. This individual is essentially a heretic.⁸⁰. He desires life in this world only for eating and drinking. This portion⁸¹ was placed here because of the *woman of goodly form*.⁸² *And his*⁸³ *mother’s name was Shelomith*⁸⁴ (Lev. 24:11) and the hint which I gave with reference to the sons of Aaron⁸⁵ are proof of this.

footnotes:
⁸⁰ The term used by I.E. is apikores
⁸¹ The portion dealing with the rebellious son.
⁸² The captive woman.
⁸³ The man who cursed God.
⁸⁴ Shelomith fornicated with an Egyptian. A woman of such questionable morals gave birth to a son who cursed God.
⁸⁵ Aaron married Elishva, who was a righteous woman. He therefore had righteous children.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:22:1)


**ותלית אותו.** כבר קבלנו מחז״‎ל ענין הנתלים:


AND THOU HANG HIM. We have received from the rabbis, of blessed memory, traditions regarding those who are hanged.⁸⁶

footnotes:
⁸⁶ Which culprits are to be hanged. Rabbi Eliezer believes that all those who are executed by stoning are then hanged for display. The sages limit this punishment only to those who are hanged for cursing God.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 21:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/21:23:1)


**כי קללת אלהים תלוי.** בעבור שברך השם ועל דרך הפשט כי אלהים פועל והקללה תבוא לכל מקום קרוב מהתלוי ויש לו סוד דבק בנפש על כן ולא תטמא את אדמתך ונסמכה זו הפרשה בעבור המלחמה כי בתחלה כי תצא והנה גבעון יוכיח כי משפט לא תלין איננו לכבוד המת רק לכבוד הארץ כי משפט הערלים והישראלים [איננו] (בס' מ״‎ח מ״‎ז) שוה:


FOR HE THAT IS HANGED IS A REPROACH UNTO GOD.⁸⁷. For he cursed God.⁸⁸ as, he is hanged (*taluy*) for cursing God (*ki killelat Elohim*). When people see his body they are reminded that God was cursed. The latter is a reproach to God. However, according to the plain meaning of the text, God is the subject.⁸⁹ The curse comes to all places close to the hanged.⁹⁰ as for God curses the hanged. He interprets this as meaning God curses those who are near a hanged person. It⁹¹ has a secret connected to the soul.⁹² Scripture therefore goes on to say, *that thou defile not thy land*.⁹³ This section follows because of the war,⁹⁴ for Scripture first states, *When thou goest forth to battle* (Deut. 20:1). Now the incident at Gibeon⁹⁵ shows that the purpose of the law which says that his body shall not remain upon the tree all night is not out of respect for the deceased but rather out of respect for the land,⁹⁶ for the law of the Canaanites and the law of the Israelites differ.⁹⁷. 38 and other manuscripts. Some printed editions read: for the law of the uncircumcised and the law of the Israelites are the same. According to this reading Israel did not allow non-Jews to remain hanging on the tree because the Israelites respected Canaanite practice. The *Vat. Ebr*. 38 version is to be preferred for many reasons.

footnotes:
⁸⁷ Hebrew, ki killelat Elohim taluy
⁸⁸ This intrerpretation renders ki killelat Elohim taluy
⁸⁹ And he that is hanged the object.
⁹⁰ I.E. renders ki killelat taluy
⁹¹ This law.
⁹² The area infects the soul. I.E. probably meant that the sight of a hanged person and its putrefying smell affect a person’s psyche and prevent him from concentrating on philosophical and religious studies.
⁹³ By leaving a putrefying body exposed.
⁹⁴ Dealt with in verses 10-15.
⁹⁵ In the war at Gibeon Joshua captured five Canaanite kings and hanged them. He removed their bodies before nightfall. He did not do this out of respect for the deceased, for according to Canaanite practice there was nothing wrong in allowing the bodies to rot in public. Hence he did this out of respect for the land. See Josh. 10:26,27.
⁹⁶ That it not be defiled.
⁹⁷ The Gibeonites did not allow burial of the sons of Saul whom they hanged. Their bodies remained on the tree for months. See II Sam. 21:1-14. So Vat. Ebr



## Chapter 22


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:1:1)


**לא תראה את שור.** אפי' אתה יוצא למלחמה וזה דרך דרש:


THOU SHALT NOT SEE THY BROTHER’S OX. Even when you go out to war.¹ to teach us that this law applies even in time of war. This is midrashic.

footnotes:
¹ The law concerning a lost object was placed in the Torah portion When Thou Goest Forth to Battle


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:1:2)


**והתעלמת.** דבק עם לא תראה והזכיר שור ושה וכבר הזכיר החמור וכן משפט כל בהמה:


AND HIDE THYSELF. This is connected to *Thou shalt not* see.⁵. Scripture mentions the ox and the sheep (v. 1). The ass was previously mentioned (Ex. 23:4).⁶ The same law applies to all beasts.

footnotes:
⁵ The meaning of our verse is, thou shalt not see and hide thyself
⁶ Where Scripture first mentions the law dealing with a lost animal. I.E. was disturbed by the omission of ass in verse 1. Hence his Exodus citation.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:2:1)


**ואספתו.** אם היית שב או בצווי שתפקדנו ביד נאמן:


THEN THOU SHALT BRING IT HOME.² If you are coming back³ or you command someone; that is, you give it to an honest person for safekeeping. ⁴

² This superscription from verse 2 was probably inadvertently misplaced by a copyist because it seems to belong after I.E.’s comment’s on verse 1.
³ If you find the lost animal on your way back from the war.
⁴ When you go off to war you ask someone else to bring the animal home.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:3:1)


**לא תוכל.** כחביריו:


THOU MAYEST NOT. *Lo tukhal* (thou mayest not)⁷ is to be understood like its counterparts.⁸), like its counterparts, is to be rendered, you are not permitted (thou mayest not). See I.E. on Deut. 17:15; 21:16.

footnotes:
⁷ Literally, you will not be able.
⁸ It (lo tukhal



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:4:1)


**נופלים.** כמו רובץ. ומלת נופלים כלל:


FALLEN DOWN. *Nofelim* (fallen down) means crouching. The word *nofelim* is a general term.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ It applies to all sorts of non-standing positions.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:4:2)


**הקם תקים עמו.** ולא לבדך כמו עזוב תעזוב עמו:


THOU SHALT SURELY HELP HIM TO LIFT THEM UP AGAIN. Not by yourself.¹⁰ Compare, *thou shalt surely release it with him* (Ex. 23:5).

footnotes:
¹⁰ You do not have to do the lifting by yourself. You are obligated to help the owner lift the animal. However, this obligation does not apply in instances where the owner stands by and does not assist you.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:5:1)


**לא יהיה כלי גבר.** נסמכה בעבור צאת למלחמה כי האשה לא נבראת כי אם להקים הזרע ואם היא תצא עם אנשים למלחמה תבא בדרך לידי זנות וכן לא ילבש גבר והטעם זכר שלא נתמלא זקנו יתערב עם הנשים וינאף הוא והן ואינו יודע וזה יורה כי מנהג ישראל היה וכן ברובי המלכיות להיות מלבוש האנשים איננו כמלבוש הנשים רק הפרש ביניהם וי״‎א על חוץ לדרך ואין צורך רק השם יתעב מי שישנה מעשה השם:


A WOMAN SHALL NOT WEAR THAT WHICH PERTAINETH UNTO A MAN. This law was stated here because Scripture deals with going out to war. Women were created only to have children.¹¹ If a woman joins men in going to war she will come to engage in illicit sexual relations on the way.¹² The law that states *neither shall a man put on a woman’s garment* is similar.¹³ The reason for this law is as follows. If a male who does not have a full beard¹⁴ mixes with women, then he and they will have sexual relations without anyone knowing.¹⁵, as in *Vat. Ebr*. 38. A male dressing as a female has access to women. Rashi has a similar explanation. This law indicates that the custom of Israel was for the garments of men to be unlike the garments of women. There was a definite difference between the garments.¹⁶ This is also the practice in most countries. Some say that our verse speaks of unnatural intercourse.¹⁷ However, there is no need for this interpretation.¹⁸ The fact is that God abominates the one who changes the works of the Lord.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹¹ Legitimate children.
¹² Women who march off to war with men will have relations with them and produce illegitimate children. I.E. most probably inserted “on the way” because he wanted to explain why this law follows the previous one. It does so because both deal with something happening on the way. See I.E.’s comments on verse 6 and the notes thereto.
¹³ The purpose of the law is to prevent illicit sexual intercourse.
¹⁴ A full beard is not to be taken literally. It means enough facial hair to indicate that the person is a male.
¹⁵ Reading ve-en yode’a
¹⁶ Of males and females.
¹⁷ The reference is to homosexuality.
¹⁸ Our verse should be taken in accordance with its plain meaning.
¹⁹ Which cross dressing does, for one is then unable to distinguish between male and female.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:6:1)


**כי יקרא.** נסמכה זאת הפרשה בעבור בדרך:


IF A BIRD’S NEST CHANCE TO BE BEFORE THEE IN THE WAY. This section²⁰ follows because Scripture earlier mentioned *by the way* (v. 4).²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ Verses 6 and 7.
²¹ Both sections thus have a common theme.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:6:2)


**יקרא.** כמו נקרא נקראתי והם מגזרת מקרה וקרוב מפגע:


CHANCE. The word *yikkare* (chance) is similar to *nikro nikreti* (As I happened by chance) (II Sam. 1:6). These words have the meaning of chance.²² and of *nikro nikreti* is *kof, resh, alef*. This root means to call. However, this meaning would be out of place here and in II Sam. 1:6. Hence I.E. points out that our root is a variant of *kof, resh, heh*, which means to meet or to chance upon. It is close in meaning to “meet.”

footnotes:
²² The root of yikkare


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:6:3)


אל״‎ף **אפרוחים** נוסף כמו אל״‎ף ובאזרועך והטעם כי אכזריות הוא בלב להיות אם על בנים רוטשה וכן שור או שה:


[YOUNG ONES.] The *alef* in *efrochim* (young ones) is superfluous. It is like the *alef* of *u-ve-ezro’akha* (and with your arm) (Jer. 32:21).²³. I.E. probably quoted from memory. It is very cruel to crush a mother with her children. Hence the reason for this law. The same applies to an ox ²⁴ or a sheep.²⁵. Lev. 22:28 prohibits the killing of a lamb or an ox and its mother in the same day.

footnotes:
²³ There is no such word in Scripture. I.E. probably has Jer. 32:21 in mind. However, the reading there is u-ve-ezro’a
²⁴ Lev. 22:28.
²⁵ Ibid

THAT IT MAY BE WELL WITH THEE. For God will pity you and do good things for you.

AND THAT THOU MAYEST PROLONG THY DAYS. As you did not exterminate all of the nest but left its root.²⁶

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:8:1)


**כי תבנה בית חדש.** נדבקה זאת הפרשה אחר שנלחמו והם בארץ החל במצות הבית:


WHEN THOU BUILDEST A NEW HOUSE. This section²⁷ is placed here because it deals with Israel in its land after they wage war. Scripture opens with the law dealing with a house.

footnotes:
²⁷ Verses 8 and 9.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:8:2)


**מעקה.** טעמו ידוע מהקום (ס״‎א מהמקום) ואין ריע לו ומדקדק הוציאו מגזירת עקת רשע והביא עצמו בעקה:


A PARAPET. The meaning of *ma’akeh* (parapet) is known from its context. It has no neighbor.²⁸ A grammarian connected it to the word *akat* (oppression of) in *the oppression of the wicked*²⁹ (Ps. 55:4).³⁰ However, he oppressed himself.³¹ comes from the root *ayin, kof, heh*, and *akat* from *ayin, vav, kof*.

footnotes:
²⁸ That is, it is not found again in Scripture.
²⁹ The grammarian reads this as the enclosure of the wicked; i.e., the wicked surround him with trouble.
³⁰ The roof is enclosed by the fence.
³¹ By offering an unacceptable interpretation. For ma’akeh


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:8:3)


**הנופל.** נקרא על שם סופו וכן יומת המת וכן ובגדי ערומים תפשיט ויתרוצצו הבנים ומלות רבות לאין מספר נקראו על שם סופם ולא נדע אם כן דרך הלשון. או הוא דברי נבואה כמו וירדוף עד דן עד נחל אשכול על כן יעזב איש את אביו ואת אמו ואחר מצות הבית מצות הזרע והנטע כי אחר שהוא בארץ ובנה בית וזרע:


IF ANY MAN FALL.³² Scripture speaks of the end result.³³ The following are similar: *The dead shall be put to death* (Deut. 17:6);³⁴ *And stripped the naked of their garments* (Job 22:6);³⁵ *And the children*³⁶ *struggled together* (Gen. 25:22). There are an innumerable number of words that are named because of the end result. We do not know if this is linguistic style or prophetic statements, as in *and pursued as far as Dan*³⁷ (Gen. 14:14); *unto the valley of Eshcol*³⁸ (Num. 13:23); and *Therefore shall a man leave his father and his mother*³⁹ (Gen. 2:24). After laying down the law dealing with the house, Scripture goes on to deal with the laws of seeds and plants, for after entering the land the Israelite would build a house and then plant.

footnotes:
³² Literally, for the one who falls will fall.
³³ Hence Scripture refers to the person who will fall as the one who falls, even though he had not yet fallen.
³⁴ Translated literally.
³⁵ The naked cannot be stripped. Thus Scripture refers to those who are about to be stripped as stripped.
³⁶ The reference is to the fetuses that Rebekah was carrying. Scripture refers to them as children because they soon would be so.
³⁷ Scripture speaks of Abraham’s pursuit of the Mesopotamian kings who invaded Canaan. Dan, the person after whom the city of Dan was named, was not yet born. Hence the name is used prophetically. See I.E. on Gen. 14:14.
³⁸ The Torah relates that the spies sent out by Moses came to a place called the valley of Eshcol. However, it was so named after the spies came there.
³⁹ This was said prophetically by Adam. When Adam made the statement, there were as yet no children or fathers and mothers.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:9:1)


**לא תזרע כלאים.** פירוש פן תקדש כבר פירשו מנחם בן סרוק הספרדי במחברתו והוא מגזרת קדש בעבור שיתערב זה עם זה:


THOU SHALT NOT SOW THY VINEYARD. The following is the meaning of *pen tikdash* (lest the fullness): Rabbi Menachem ben Saruk the Sephardi has already explained in his notebook that the word *tikdash* (fullness) is related to the word *kodesh* (holy).⁴⁰ can also be taken to mean profaned. See Rashi. [The product of two seeds sown together becomes holy] because one mixes with the other.⁴¹

footnotes:
⁴⁰ The word kodesh
⁴¹ I.E. does not explain why. He most likely believed that producing a species which God did not create was emulating God’s work. The product was thus a godlike creation and holy.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:10:1)


**לא תחרוש בשור ובחמור.** דבק עם הזריעה והשם חמל על כל מעשיו כי אין כח החמור ככח השור ובעבור הזריעה אמר שעטנז וכבר הזכירו על דרך כלל ובכאן פרט שהוא הצמר והפשתים לא אחרים:


THOU SHALT NOT PLOW WITH AN OX AND AN ASS. This law is connected to the one dealing with sowing two kinds of seed. God had mercy on his creatures. An ass is not as strong as an ox. The law prohibiting the mixture of linen and wool⁴². follows⁴³ because Scripture earlier mentioned the prohibition of mixing seeds. This law was previously mentioned in general terms.⁴⁴ However, Scripture is specific here. It limits the prohibition to wool and linen, and not to any other kinds of material.

footnotes:
⁴² Shatnez
⁴³ In verse 10.
⁴⁴ In Lev. 19:19. There, Scripture forbids the wearing of a garment from of a mixture. However, it does not say what sort of mixture is prohibited. It does so here.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:12:1)


**גדלים תעשה לך.** אמרו המכחישים שהוא מצוה בפני עצמה בלילה כמו הציצית ביום על כן כתוב אשר תכסה והמעתיקי' העתיקו כי זאת מצות הציצית ביום ופירוש גדלים כמו בבנינה גדלים מעשה שרשרות. והמכחישים הוציאוה מגזרת גדולה ואנחנו מאמינים בדברי קדמונינו לבדם והזכיר זה הכתוב לבאר על ארבע כנפות ונסמך לשעטנז בעבור הציצית שהוא מותר:


THOU SHALT MAKE TWISTED CORDS. The deniers⁴⁵ say that this is a separate commandment⁴⁶ pertaining to nighttime. It is similar to the fringes that are to be worn during the day. Scripture therefore reads, *wherewith thou coverest thyself*.⁴⁷ However, the transmitters of tradition say that our verse speaks of the fringes that are to be worn during the day.⁴⁸ 43a. The meaning of *gedilim* (twisted cords) is similar to the word *gedilim* (wreaths) which is employed with regard to the building of the temple. Compare, *wreaths* (gedilim) *of chainwork* (I Kings 7:17).⁴⁹ means twisted threads, for chain-work means twisted. The deniers connect the word *gedilim* to the word *gedolah* (large).⁵⁰ means large threads. We believe only in the words of our ancestors. Scripture mentions this law a second time⁵¹ in order to add *upon the four corners*.⁵² The law mandating fringes is connected to the law prohibiting a mixture of linen and wool because the latter does not apply to fringes.

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Those who deny the Rabbinic tradition. The reference is to the Karaites.
⁴⁶ It is not to be identified with a similar commandment in Num. 15:37-41 requiring the wearing of fringes, for the latter pertains to the day, whereas our verse deals with the night.
⁴⁷ To indicate that it deals with fringes that are to be worn at night.
⁴⁸ See Menachot
⁴⁹ In other words, gedilim
⁵⁰ They saw that gedilim
⁵¹ That is, in addition to mentioning it in Num. 15:37-41, it also mentions it here.
⁵² Numbers mentions corners, it does not mention four corners.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:13:1)


**כי יקח איש אשה.** נסמכה הפרשה לדעת המכחישים בעבור תכסה בה והאמת על דרך דרש שנסמכה בעבור הבית ואחר כן הבגד ואחר כן בעלת הבית:


IF ANY MAN TAKE A WIFE. According to the deniers⁵³ this section was connected to the law of fringes because the latter contain the phrase *wherewith thou coverest thyself*.⁵⁴ (v. 12) to refer to a blanket. Scripture alludes to marriage as placing a covering over a woman. Cf. Ruth 3:9. The allusion is to sharing one blanket. Its true reason can be explained midrashically. The laws are placed one next to the other because first comes the house, next a garment,⁵⁵ and then the mistress of the house.⁵⁶

footnotes:
⁵³ The Karaites.
⁵⁴ They take coverest thyself
⁵⁵ The reference must be to new garments, for one does not build a house if he does not have a garment to wear.
⁵⁶ A wife. A person first builds a house, then buys new garments, and then marries.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:14:1)

WANTON. The word *alilot* (wanton) means a cause.⁵⁷ to the word *illah* (cause). He renders *alilot devarim* as “a cause of words.” In other words, he states the reason he wants to divorce her.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:15:1

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:19:1)


וטעם **כל ימיו.** כי לעולם תהיה ברשותו עד יום מותו:


[HE MAY NOT PUT HER AWAY ALL HIS DAYS.] The meaning of *all his days* is that she shall always be under his care. She shall be so until the day of his death.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:21:1)


**וסקלוה.** שיתכן שאחר שארסה שכב איש אותה כי המארס לא ארם אותה רק היא אומרת שהיא בתולה ומעתיקי הדת בארו הדבר באר היטב ואלה הבתולים ימצאו עד זמן קצוב וכפי תולדת הנערות ובעבור וסקלוה נסמכה פרשת הבעולה והמאורסה:


SHALL STONE HER. Someone most likely slept with her after he betrothed her, for the one who betrothed her would not have done so unless she told him that she was a virgin. The transmitters of the law explained the matter very clearly.⁵⁹ The signs of virginity are found until a fixed time, according to the nature of young ladies.⁶⁰ 59a, “And he (the High Priest) shall take a wife in her virginity (Lev. 21:13) excludes one who is a *bogeret* (a female above the age of 12 1/2) for her virginity has ended.” I.E. took this statement literally. However, other rabbis interpret this to mean that older women bleed less than those younger than 12 1/2. See *Tosafot, Yevamot* 59a. Also see *Ketuvot* 9a,b and Rashi and *Tosafot* comments there. Also see Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Ishut* 11:12,13. The law dealing with a married women was connected to the law of the betrothed damsel because Scripture states, *and the men of the city shall stone her*.⁶¹

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Witnesses are required to the act of adultery if the damsel is to be executed. She is not executed if she is merely found not to be a virgin.
⁶⁰ Thus past a certain age the husband cannot claim that his wife was not a virgin. According to the Talmud, the virginity of a girl above the age of 12 1/2 dissipates. See Yevamot
⁶¹ The same penalty is exacted from a woman living in the house of her husband (v. 24).



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 22:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/22:22:1)


**גם** פירושו זה כמו זה והנה פירוש זה עוד:


BOTH OF THEM. The meaning of *gam*⁶² is also. The literal meaning of *u-metu gam shenehem* (then they shall both of them die) thus is, they shall also die both of them. The word *gam* seems out of place. Hence I.E. renders *gam* as this one like that one. The meaning of *u-metu gam shenehem* thus is, the two of them shall die, this one like that one. is this one like the other one. Now Scripture goes on to further elucidate this topic.

⁶² The literal meaning of gam

## Chapter 23


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:1:1)


**לא יקח איש את אשת אביו.** האנוסה על כן נסמכה זאת הפרשה:


A MAN SHALL NOT TAKE HIS FATHER’S WIFE. The woman whom his father raped.¹ is not in keeping with this ruling. See *Yevamot* 97a. This section therefore follows.²

footnotes:
¹ This is the opinion of Rabbi Judah in the Talmud. However, the halakhah
² For the previous section deals with rape.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:2:1)


**פצוע דכה.** ביצים בלא אבר [ס״‎א חסר] ואמרו המדקדקים כי הטעם בשרם שהוא דכא וזה רחוק:


HE THAT IS CRUSHED.³. *Dakkah* (crushed) refers to the testicles.⁴ as one who is wounded (*petzu’a*) in the testicles (*dakkah*). The word⁵. does not appear again in Scripture.⁶. 38, which says, *betzim belo chaver*. The *Mikra’ot Gedolot* edition of I.E. reads: *betzim belo ever* (testicles without a penis). This reading appears wrong in view of the fact that Scripture goes on to say that one missing a penis shall not enter the congregation of God. The grammarians say that it refers to the flesh,⁷ which is soft.⁸ (testicles) means soft. The grammarians explain that the testicles are referred to by the term *dakkah* because the flesh of the testicles is soft. However, this is far-fetched.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:2:3

**בקהל ה'.** הטעם שלא יחשב כאחד מקהל ה' שיקח בת ישראל וכן עמוני ולא עמונית מואבי ולא מואבית והעד הנאמן רות והכתוב שאמר לא תבאו בהם על נשי שבעת הגוים. גם הכתוב בעזרא ויבדילו כל ערב הם (הנזכרים) ושם פירשתיו:


INTO THE ASSEMBLY OF THE LORD. It means that they shall not be considered as part of God’s assembly with regard to taking a daughter of Israel. Similarly, an Ammonite male to the exclusion of an Ammonite female, and a Moabite male to the exclusion of a Moabite female.⁹ Ruth is the true witness to the latter.¹⁰ The verse that says *neither shall they come among you* (I Kings 11:2)¹¹, Ammonite and Moabite women fall under Scripture’s marriage ban. speaks of the women of the seven nations.¹² refers to the Zidonians and Hittites, who were Canaanites, and not to the Moabites and the Ammonites. Furthermore, the verse in the Book of Ezra¹³ which states, *that they separated from Israel all the alien mixture* (Neh. 13:3),¹⁴ refers to the males. I have explained it there.¹⁵

### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:3:1)


**ממזר.** אחז״‎ל הבא מן העריות והשני ממי״‎ן נוספין כממי״‎ן נהרסו ממגורות ואחרים אמרו כי הוא שם גוי וישב ממזר באשדוד כי רחוק הוא שיקרא שם ישראל ואף כי בעת הישועה בשם גנאי והמתרגם תפש דרך קצרה ודרש:


Our rabbis, of blessed memory, said that a *mamzer* (bastard) is a child that results from a sexual act which is deemed an *uncovering of the nakedness*.¹⁶ The two *mems* of *mamzer* are superfluous.¹⁷ is a variant of *zar*, (a stranger). A *mamzer* is a child born to people who must remain strangers to each other with regard to sexual relations. They are like the *mems* in *mamgurot* (barns) in *the barns are broken down* (Joel 1:17). Others say that *mamzer* is the name of a people. Compare, *And a mamzer shall dwell in Ashdod* (Zech. 9:6), for it is far-fetched for Israel to be given a shameful name.¹⁸. It is even more unlikely for this to happen in the time of the salvation.¹⁹ refers to Israel then the prophet is saying that the bastards (Israel) shall dwell in Ashdod when the messiah comes. Therefore what the prophet says is that in the messianic era a nation called *mamzer* will dwell in Ashdod. The Aramaic translator of the Bible was brief and midrashic.²⁰ to Zech. 9:6 renders *And a mamzer shall dwell in Ashdod*, and the children of Israel shall dwell in Ashdod where they were strangers. Thus the *Targum* renders *mamzer* in Zechariah as strangers. *Vat. Ebr*. 38 says, the Aramaic translator of the Bible interpreted it midrashically. The latter reading appears correct, for the *Targum* elaborates on the text.

¹⁸ Mamzer
¹⁹ Zechariah speaks of the messianic era. If mamzer
²⁰ Targum Jonathan

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:10:1)


**כי תצא מחנה.** זהו המחנה שיצא אל האויב ושם הארון והוא מחנה קטן ואיננו מחנה הגדול כי שם מחיצות עשויות ובצאתם אל המלחמה הם קרובים סביבות הארון וברור כי טמא מקרה משחית ונסמכה זו הפרשה בעבור מלחמת מדין שהזכיר דבר בלעם כי צוררים הם בעבור אחותם והארון היה עם המחנה שיצא על מדין:


WHEN THOU GOEST FORTH IN CAMP. The reference is to a camp which goes out to fight the enemy accompanied by the ark. Scripture speaks of a small camp. The Torah does not speak of the great camp,²¹ for this camp had built partitions.²² When Israel went to war they were close to and around the ark. It is clear that a person who is unclean because of a nocturnal event²³ destroys his seed.²⁴ This section follows because of the Midianite war, for Scripture mentioned the topic of Balaam.²⁵ The Midianites assailed Israel because of the affair of their sister.²⁶ The ark was with the camp that went out to fight the Midianites.²⁷

²⁴ Any emission of seed renders a person unclean. See Lev. 15:16-18.
²⁵ In verses 5 and 6. In other words, this section follows because it is connected to the Midianite war which came about because of Balaam.
²⁶ Their sister Cozbi slept with Zimri, a prince of the tribe of Simeon. I.E. does not refer only to Cozbi. He is referring to all of the Midianite women who enticed Israel to worship idols. See Num. 25:18; 31:15-18.
²⁷ See Num. 31:6.

CHANCETH. The *mem* of *mi-kereh* (chanceth) is a preposition.³⁰ (chance). *Mi-kereh* means from a chance. *Mi-kerah laylah* (that which chanceth him by night) thus means from a chance of the night *Mi-kereh* follows the paradigm of *mi-sedeh* (part of the field) in *part of the field of his possession* (Lev. 27:16).

### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:12:1)


**לפנות ערב.** כטעם כי פנה היום גם חזוק הוא זה לדברי רז״‎ל והמכחישי' אמרו כי לפנות כמו לפני ולא אמרו כלום וי״‎א כי טעמו כאשר יחל הערב לפנות והנה אחר שאמר הכתוב לפנות ערב אמר וכבוא השמש הנה קודם הערב וכן לפנות בקר קודם זרוח השמש:


WHEN EVENING COMETH ON. *Li-fenot erev* (when evening cometh) is similar in meaning to *ki fanah ha-yom* (for the day declineth) (Jer. 6:4).³¹. In other words, *li-fenot erev* means when the day declines, when the shadows of evening come. According to Krinsky and others the reference is to the afternoon when the sun begins its decline. Filwarg believes the reference is to about an hour and a third before the onset of night. See I.E. on Gen. 1:18 (Vol. 1, p. 40). “Know that evening begins at sunset and lasts for one and a third hours, during which time a light-like appearance is seen before sunrise.” Also see Rashi: “He should immerse himself close before the setting of the sun” (Rosenbaun and Silberman translation). This also strengthens the opinion of the rabbis of blessed memory.³² The deniers³³ say that *li-fenot* (cometh on) means before.³⁴ refers to night. They interpret *li-fenot erev* as before night comes, i.e., after sunset. They say nothing of value. Some say³⁵ that the meaning of *li-fenot erev* (when evening cometh) is, when the evening begins to come.³⁶ Now, after Scripture employs the phrase *li-fenot erev*, it says, *and when the sun is down. Li-fenot erev* thus means before the evening.³⁷ *Li-fenot boker* (when the morning appeared) is similarly before sunrise.³⁸

**ויד.** מקום כמו יד הירדן גם זו ראיה כי ממחנ' ישראל לא יצא בעל קרי ולא כל אדם בשעת הצורך ואף כי התינוקות והעד שאמר הכתוב וישלחו מן המחנה כל צרוע וכל זב וכל טמא לנפש והטעם כל טמא מטמא ולא טמא יום:

Proof of this is the verse which states, *that they put out of the camp every leper, and every one that hath an issue, and whosoever is unclean by the dead* (Num. 5:2),⁴² which refers to uncleanliness that is passed on⁴³ and not to one who is unclean for a day.⁴⁴

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:18:1

ונסמכה פרשת קדשה בעבור העבד ומעתיקי הדת אמרו כי הקדש הוא הנשכב והעד מבנ״‎י. ואמר אליהוא וחיתם בקדשים כי נפשם כנגד חיתם והנה קדשים כנגד' נועד וכתוב וגם קדש היה בארץ בתי הקדשים ושם כתוב אשר הנשים אורגות שם ואני לא אבין טעם קדשה וכאשר נחפשה היטב טעם המלה אז היא ידועה שהיא מזומנת לכל עובר ושב והעד איה הקדשה א״‎כ יהיה קדש כמעשה המצרים והמבין יבין ובעבור קדשה נסמכה פרשת אתנן זונה:


[THERE SHALL BE NO HARLOT] The law of the *kedeshah* (harlot) follows because the previous section deals with a bondman.⁵⁴ The transmitters of tradition say that the term *kadesh* (sodomite) refers to the one who is slept with.⁵⁵ The phrase *of the sons of Israel* is proof of this.⁵⁶ Elihu said, [*Their soul perisheth in youth] And their life as that of the depraved* (kedeshim) (Job 36:14); *their soul* corresponds to *their life* and *kedeshim* corresponds to *in youth*.⁵⁷ Scripture also states, *And there were also sodomites* (kadesh) *in the land* (I Kings 14:24); *the house of the sodomites* (kedeshim) II Kings 23:7). Scripture there continues, *where the women*⁵⁸ *wove coverings* (*Ibid.*). I do not understand the meaning of the word *kedeshah*. (harlot). When we investigate carefully the meaning of the word we find that it refers to a woman who is known to be prepared to offer herself to all who come and go. *Where is the harlot* (kedeshah) (Gen. 38:21) is proof of this. If this is so,⁵⁹ refers to a female engaged in heterosexual intercourse, then so must the term *kadesh*, for *kadesh* is the masculine form of the noun. then the term *kadesh* refers to an Egyptian-like practice.⁶⁰. See I. E. on Haggai 2:12. The intelligent will understand.⁶¹ The law dealing with a harlot’s hire follows because our section deals with a harlot.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:20:1

**לא תשיך לאחיך.** בעבור שהזכיר העבד הנכרי אע״‎פ שכתוב בו לא תוננו יש לך רשות שתשיכנו:


THOU SHALT NOT LEND UPON INTEREST TO THY BROTHER. This law is mentioned here because Scripture earlier mentions the non-Jewish bondman (v. 17). Scripture states that even though it is prohibited to wrong the latter,⁶⁸ it is permitted to take interest from him.

footnotes:
⁶⁸ The foreign bondman who has found refuge in Israel.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:20:2)


**לא תשיך לאחיך.** יש הפרש בין תשך לתשיך והטעם שלא תקבל מאחיך שיתן לך רבית גם הוא עובר על מצות רבית והנה המלה יוצאה לשנים פעולים ורבים אמרו כי תשיך כמו תשך אין הפרש ביניהם וכמו כן פעולים רבים במקרא:


THOU SHALT NOT LEND UPON INTEREST TO THY BROTHER. There is a difference between *tishokh* (you shall take interest)⁶⁹. and *tashikh* (Thou shalt lend upon interest).⁷⁰. The meaning of *lo tashikh* (Thou shalt not lend upon interest) is, you shall not accept interest from your brother when he offers it;⁷¹ is you shall not cause interest to be given. your brother also violates the law regarding interest.⁷² Notice, *tashikh* is a word governing two acts.⁷³ and refers to both you and your brother. You shall not cause your brother to give you interest, and your brother shall not give you interest. Many say that *tashikh* is similar to *tishokh*.⁷⁴ is a *hifil*, it has the meaning of a *kal*. It means you shall not take interest. There is no difference between them. There are many such verbs in Scripture.⁷⁵ with the meaning of the *kal*.

footnotes:
⁶⁹ Which is a kal
⁷⁰ Which is a hifil
⁷¹ The meaning of lo tashikh
⁷² When he gives it.
⁷³ It is a hifil
⁷⁴ In other words, even though tashikh
⁷⁵ Verbs in the hifil



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:22:1

WHEN THOU SHALT VOW A VOW. This law follows because Scripture mentioned *for any vow* when it spoke of *the hire of a harlot* (v. 19).

FOR THE LORD THY GOD WILL SURELY REQUIRE IT. This is a metaphoric statement. God will act like a person.⁷⁶ He will demand what you vowed.

AND IT WILL BE SIN IN THEE. *Chet* (sin) means punishment.⁷⁷

THAT WHICH IS GONE OUT OF THY LIPS. Regarding anything where God’s name is mentioned.⁷⁸

⁷⁸ Any sort of oath wherein God’s name is mentioned.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:24:2)


**נדבה.** כי כל נדר נדבה ואין כל נדבה נדר וזה הדבר ברור:


FREELY.⁷⁹. The reference is to an offering which is freely given. For every vow offering⁸⁰. is a free-will offering.⁸¹ (freely). According to I.E. comments on Lev. 7:15 a *neder* refers to a vow made in a time of stress, a *nedavah* refers to an offering brought whenever the spirit moves a person to do so. For the talmudic point of view see Megillah 1:6. However, not every free-will offering is a vow offering.⁸² The aforementioned is clear.⁸³

footnotes:
⁷⁹ Hebrew, nedavah
⁸⁰ Hebrew, neder

**אשר דברת בפיך.** אחר שיצא הדבור לא תוכל להשיבו:


WHICH THOU HAST PROMISED WITH THY MOUTH. After the vow has come out of your mouth you cannot take it back.

**כי תבא בכרם רעך.** לבצור מותר שתאכל ונסמכה זאת הפרשה כי משלך אתה חייב לתת בנדר ושל אחרים לא תקח לך:


WHEN THOU COMEST INTO THY NEIGHBOR’S VINEYARD. To harvest grapes⁸⁴ then you are permitted to eat of them. This section follows to indicate that you are obligated to set aside from your own when taking a vow, but you must not take to yourself that which belongs to another.⁸⁵

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:26:3

EARS. The meaning of *melilot* (ears) is known from its context.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 23:26:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:26:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/23:26:4)


**חרמש.** מלה מרובעת ובעבור תניף אמרו המכחישים כי כן לעוברי דרכים:


A SICKLE. *Chermesh* (sickle) is a quadriliteral.⁸⁸. Most Hebrew words are triliteral. Hence I.E.’s comment. The deniers⁸⁹ say that this law applies to those who pass by the field,⁹⁰ for Scripture says, *thou shalt not move a sickle*.⁹¹, for this is the very task of a farm laborer.

⁸⁸ Its root is chet, resh, mem, shin

## Chapter 24


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:1:1)


**כי יקח איש אשה.** גם הוא חשובה כשרה. [ס״‎א כשדה]:


WHEN A MAN TAKETH A WIFE. She is also considered fit.¹. However, she is disliked by her husband. I.E. takes issue with Beth Shamai, which interprets *some unseemly thing* as a sexual transgression. See *Mishnah, Gitten* 9:10. Other editions read, she is also considered as a field (*ke-sadeh*). A woman, like a field, is acquired by a money transaction. See *Kedushin* 2a. Hence the law regarding a woman follows that of the field. Filwarg believes that I.E. compares a woman to a field in that both are seeded and produce fruit.

footnotes:
¹ Hebrew, kesherah


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:1:2)


**חן בעיניו.** שהיא הפך תולדתו:


FAVOR IN HIS EYES. For her nature is the reverse of his.²

footnotes:
² The reason that she did not find favor in her husband’s eyes was not due to a moral imperfection.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:1:3)


**ומצא בה.** ולא יוכל לגלות כי אין לו עדים והטעם או מצא בה כמו ומכה אביו ואמו:


AND HE HATH FOUND.³. I.E.’s reading is *And he had found*. The problem is that the verse reads *ki matza* (because he has found). Weiser suggests that *u-matza* is I.E.’s paraphrase of *ki matza*. However, I.E. goes on to prove that the *vav* prefixed to a word sometimes has the meaning of “or.” It is a bit farfetched to say that I.E. intends to say that *ki matza* should be interpreted as *u-matza*, and that *u-matza* should be interpreted as *or has found*, because a *vav* sometimes has the meaning of or. It thus appears either that I.E.’s copy of Deuteronomy had *u-matza* or that I.E. quoted from memory and committed an egregious error. And he cannot reveal it,⁴ for he has no witnesses.⁵ The meaning of *and he hath found* is, or he hath found, as in *And he that smiteth his father, or his mother* (Ex. 21:15).

footnotes:
³ Hebrew, u-matza
⁴ The moral imperfection found in her.
⁵ To the immoral act that he accuses her of.

**ויצאה.** שלא תשוב שם. ונודע הדבר שיצאה מהיותה בביתו:

AND BECOMETH. She becomes another man’s wife.

[ANOTHER.] The words *acher* (another) and *acharon* (latter) (v. 3) are adjectives.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:3:1)

OF DIVORCEMENT. A bill of divorce is called a *sefer keritut*⁷ because their marriage has been severed forever.

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:4:1)


**הטמאה.** כנגד בעל ראשון והנה טעמו טמא' היא לו בעבו' שידע' איש אחר ודגשות הטי״‎ת בחסרון תי״‎ו התפעל וכן הכבד את הנגע:


AFTER THAT SHE IS DEFILED. With regard to the first husband. Its meaning is defiled to him because another man knew her. The *tet* of *huttamma’ah* (she is defiled) receives a *dagesh* to compensate for the missing *tav* of the *hitpa’el*.⁸. Similarly the word *hukkabbes* (is washed) in *after that the plague is washed* (Lev. 13:55).⁹ with the *tav* of the form omitted. The latter is compensated by placing a *dagesh* in the *caf*.

footnotes:
⁸ Which should have followed the heh
⁹ It too is a hitpa’el

**ולא תחטיא.** צווי לישראל:


AND THOU SHALT NOT CAUSE THE LAND TO SIN. This is a command to Israel.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:1)


**אשה חדשה.** י״‎א בתולה ולדעת רבים חדשה כנגדו כי יוכל להשיב אשה גרושה אם לא נטמאה ועל כן חדשה:


A NEW WIFE. Some say a virgin.¹⁰ According to many its meaning is new to him,¹¹ for it is possible for a man to remarry the woman whom he has divorced if she has not been defiled. Scripture therefore reads a new wife.

footnotes:
¹⁰ In other words, new means a woman who was never married.
¹¹ Even a widow or divorcee.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:2)


**נקי יהיה לביתו.** שלא יהיה עליו עול מלכות:


HE SHALL BE FREE FOR HIS HOUSE. The yoke of the king shall not be upon him.¹²

footnotes:
¹² He shall not be conscripted into doing any of the king’s service.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:3)


**נקי** כמו אי נקי:


FREE. *Naki* (free) is similar to *naki* (innocent)¹³ in *him that is innocent* (Job 22:30).

footnotes:
¹³ That is, free from guilt.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:5:4)


למ״‎ד **לכל דבר.** נוסף כמו והשלישי לאבשלום:


[WITH ANY BUSINESS.] The *lamed* placed before *le-khol davar* (with any business) is superfluous.¹⁴ should be read *kol davar. Le-khol davar* literally means to any business. *Khol davar* means any business. It is like the *lamed* in *le-avshalom* (Absalom) in *the third, Absalom* (I Chron. 3:2).¹⁵. Literally, the third, to Absalom. However, the context does not permit this interpretation. Hence the *lamed* of *le-avshalom* is superfluous, and *le-avshalom* is to be read as if written *avshalom*.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Le-khol davar
¹⁵ Hebrew, ha-shelishi le-avshalom



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:1)


**לא יחבול רחים ורכב.** אמרו המכחישים כי נדבקה זאת הפרשה עם ושמח את אשתו כי רמז למשכב כי אסור שימנע מן המשכב וזה הבל וריק והביאו ראיה תטחן לאחר אשתי וכבר פירשתיו והאמת שהוא כמשמעו שאסור לאדם שיחבול רחים ותחסר מלת חובל כמו ויאמר ליוסף אשר ילדה אותה ללוי ורבים כן כי טעמם ויאמר לו האומר ילדה לו היולדת:


NO MAN SHALL TAKE THE MILL OR THE UPPER MILLSTONE TO PLEDGE. The deniers¹⁶ say that this section is connected to *and shall cheer his wife whom he hath taken* (v. 5),¹⁷. This can be rendered, he shall not harm (Krinsky). Thus the meaning of our verse is, one shall not harm his partner by refraining from taking the upper position (the upper millstone) or the lower position (the mill). for our verse alludes to coitus, namely, that it is forbidden to abstain from sexual intercourse. However, this is sheer nonsense. They offer as proof, *Then let my wife grind unto another* (Job 31:10). I have previously explained the latter.¹⁸ The truth is that our verse is to be taken at face value,¹⁹ that a person is prohibited from taking a mill to pledge. The word *chovel* (one who takes a pledge) is missing from our verse.²⁰. This literally means he shall not take the mill or the upper millstone to pledge. The question is: To whom does “he” refer? Hence I.E. points out that the word *chovel* (anyone that takes a pledge) has been omitted. Our verse should be read as if written, *lo yachavol chovel rechayim va-rechev* (any one that takes a pledge shall not take the mill or the upper millstone to pledge). It is similar to *and he said to Joseph* (Gen. 48:1) and to *whom she bore to Levi* (Num. 26:29), for the meaning of the former is, someone said to Joseph, and of the latter, the one who bore, bore to Levi.²¹ There are many such cases.²²

footnotes:
¹⁶ The Karaites.
¹⁷ The reference is to the Karaite Bible commentator Levi ben Yefet. According to Levi ben Yefet the meaning of our clause is that a man (the upper millstone) or a woman (the mill) shall not abstain from sexual relations with each other. The Hebrew word for take to pledge is lo yachavol
¹⁸ That intercourse is metaphorically described by the term “grinding.” See I.E. on Job 31:10.
¹⁹ Despite the fact that “grind” is used as a metaphor for sexual intercourse in Job.
²⁰ Our verse reads, lo yachavol rechayim va-rechev
²¹ See I.E. Gen. 48:1 (Vol. 1, p. 412).
²² Where Scripture omits the subject.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:2)


**רחים.** שם שנים ולא יתכן להיותם נפרדים:


THE MILL. The word *rechayim* (mill) is in the dual form. It does not come in the singular.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:3)


**ורכב.** היא ההוה במים וי״‎א העץ שהוא על הרחים וי״‎א כי רחים התחתונ' ורכב העליונה:


THE UPPER MILLSTONE. *Rechev* (upper millstone) refers to a water mill. Others say that it refers to the wood which is placed on the mill.²³ Still others say that the *rechayim* (mill) refers to the lower millstone and *rechev* to the upper millstone.

footnotes:
²³ The reference is probably to a wooden handle which was used to turn the upper stone.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:6:4)


**כי נפש הוא חובל.** כי בו מחית נפשות והסומכים על הסמך הפרשיות איננה טענה כי כל מצו' עומדת בפני עצמה והסמיכות כדרך דרש וזאת הפרש' דבקה כי הזכיר שלא יגזול ענבים לתת אל כליו וחרמש בקמה ולקחת אשה והיתה לו גם לאחר ויצא מרשותו והלוקח אשה שישמח בה לא יהיה עשוק שיצא בצבא וחביל' הרחים עושק וגם גונב נפש וכן נגע הצרעת שלא ישמרו משפטיה תזיק הקרובים וכן לא תעשוק העני בעבוטו והשכיר והבנים הקטנים שלא ימותו בעון אביהם והטיית משפט גר יתום וחבול בגד אלמנה ושכחה בשדה כי היא לעני וכן לא תעולל וכן פן יוסיף ונקלה וחסימת השור עשק כי יגע ולא יאכל והממאן ליבם כדמות עשק והמחזקת במבושים עושקת אבן ואבן איפה ואיפה עשק ממש גם עמלק עשק לישראל:


FOR HE TAKETH A MAN’S LIFE TO PLEDGE. For life is sustained by these instruments. Those who rely on the juxtaposition of the sections have no argument,²⁴ for each commandment stands by itself.²⁵ The explanation of their juxtapositions is midrashic.²⁶ This section²⁷ is connected to the previous ones in the following way:²⁸ the Torah mentions that one is not to steal grapes by putting them in his pots (Deut. 23:25), or to lift up a sickle upon his neighbor’s grain (Deut. 23:26); or to take a woman after she has been his and then left his house and married another (v. 1). It also states that one has to cheer the woman whom he marries (v. 5); he is not to be oppressed by going into the army (*Ibid.*). The taking of a millstone in pledge is a form of oppression.²⁹ So too is kidnapping a person. Similarly the plague of leprosy, for if he does not observe its laws then the plague will injure those in proximity to it.³⁰ Scripture similarly states, do not oppress a poor person by taking a pledge from him (v. 10). It similarly states do not oppress a hired person (v. 14) and small children should not be put to death for the sins of the fathers (v. 16). It likewise warns against perverting the justice due a stranger or the fatherless (v. 17). It prohibits the taking of a widow’s garment as a pledge (v. 17). It prohibits one from retrieving a sheaf which was forgotten in the field, for it belongs to the poor (v. 19). Similarly *Thou shalt not glean* (v. 21). Likewise *lest if he should exceed…Then thy brother should be dishonored* (Deut. 25:3).³¹ The muzzling of an ox (v. 4) is an act of oppression, for the animal toils but cannot eat of its toil. Similarly, refusing to perform levirate marriage (Deut. 25:5-10) is a form of oppression. The woman who grabs a man’s testicles (Deut. 25:11) commits an act of oppression. Employing diverse weights and diverse measures (Deut. 25:13,14) is oppression in its purest form. Furthermore, Amalek oppressed Israel (Deut. 25:17-19).³²

footnotes:
²⁴ That our verse speaks metaphorically of sexual intercourse.
²⁵ The commandments are not necessarily tied together. Each commandment is an independent unit.
²⁶ It therefore cannot be used as a reason to interpret a law metaphorically when the plain meaning of the text is acceptable.
²⁷ The law regarding taking a mill to pledge.
²⁸ That is, if we want to connect this section midrashically to what comes before, we can so in the following manner.
²⁹ Hence the positioning of our law.
³⁰ By infecting them.
³¹ Deut. 25:3 prohibits the infliction of more that 40 stripes to one being punished by lashes. Exceeding this number of stripes is a form of oppression.
³² Thus the laws in Deut. 23:25-25:19 are thematically related. Hence the law prohibiting the taking of a mill was included in this section.

ANY OF HIS BRETHREN. Scripture adds *of the children of Israel* because the Edomites are called brothers.³³

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:9:1)


**למרים.** הנה ראיה על דרש אל תקרי מצורע אלא מוציא שם רע:


UNTO MIRIAM. Here is proof of the midrashic statement that we should not read the word *metzora* as *metzora* (a leper) but as an acronym for *motzi shem ra* (one who invents libel).³⁶ on the Torah portion *Metzora* 2. According to the rabbis, leprosy is a punishment for speaking ill of one’s neighbor. Miriam was punished with leprosy for speaking ill of Moses (see Num. 12). According to I.E., Scripture’s telling us that we are to recall Miriam’s punishment is another way of saying, remember the punishment for speaking ill of your neighbor.

³⁶ See Tanchumah

DOST LEND. The word *tasheh* (thou…dost lend) comes from the root *nun, shin, heh*.

ה״‎א **מאומה.** נוסף כה״‎א לילה:


The *heh* of the word *me’umah* (any manner of loan) is superfluous.³⁷. Compare, Job 31:7; Dan. 1:4. It is like the *heh* of *laylah* (night).³⁸. Compare, Is. 16:3. See I.E. on Gen. 1:5 (Vol. 1, p. 33).

### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:13:1)


**תהיה צדקה.** פירוש שתשקול חבירך במאזני צדק עמך שישכב בעבוטו ועל דעת המעתיקים כי יש הפרש במקומות בין צדק לצדקה:


AND IT SHALL BE RIGHTEOUSNESS⁴⁰. UNTO THEE. You shall weigh your neighbor in the righteous scales⁴¹ which you possess⁴² means and you shall act righteously. so that he sleeps with his pledge.⁴³ According to the transmitters of tradition there are places wherein there is a difference between the word *tzedek* (righteousness) and *tzedakah* (righteousness).⁴⁴ means righteousness and *tzedakah* charity. In other words, *tzedek* is a general term, while *tzedakah* is a particular term, as in “collectors of charity (tzedakah)” (*Sabbath* 118b) and “deeds of love are worth more than almsgiving” (*tzedakah*) (*Sukkah* 49b).

⁴² In other words, and it shall be righteousness unto thee
⁴³ That is, you permit him to sleep in his garment.
⁴⁴ According to the rabbis, tzedek

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:16:1)


ותועי רוח שאלו איך אמר הכתוב **לא יומתו אבות** ובמקום אחר אומר פוקד עון אבות ושאלתם תהו כי לא יומתו אבות על בנים מצוה על ישראל ופוקד עון אבות על בנים הוא הפוקד וכבר פירשתיו במקומו:


[THE FATHERS SHALL NOT BE PUT TO DEATH.] Those whose spirits wander ask: “How could Scripture say: *The fathers shall not he put to death…*when it says in another place, *visiting the iniquity of the fathers upon the children* (Ex. 20:5; Deut. 5:9)?” Their question is nonsense, for *the fathers shall not he put to death for the children* is a commandment directed to Israel.⁴⁷ However, God is the one who metes out punishment in *visiting the iniquity of the fathers upon the children*. I have earlier explained this in its place.⁴⁸

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 24:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:20:1)


**כי תחבוט.** כמו חובט חטים והוא פעל יחבט קצח בנין נפעל וכן כל אותיות אחעה״‎ר כאשר יבנה מהם בנין נפעל ואלה הם לא יאצר ולא יחסן. ולא יהרג. יעבד בו. יראה כל זכורך כי כן המשפט:


WHEN THOU BEATEST. *Tachbot* (thou beatist) is similar to *chovet* (beating) in *beating out wheat* (Jud. 6:11). It is in the *pa’al*.⁵⁶. The word *yechavet* (is beaten) in *But the black cummin is beaten* (Is. 28:27) is a *nifal*. All letters whose first root letter is an *alef, chet, heh, ayin*, or *resh* are vocalized like *yechavet* when conjugated in the *nifal*. The following are examples of each one of the aforementioned: *ye’atzer*⁵⁷. (treasured) and *yechasen*⁵⁸. (laid up) in *it shall not be treasured nor laid up* (Is. 23:18); *yehareg*⁵⁹. (killed) as in *ve-lo yehareg* (and will not be killed); *ye’aved*⁶⁰. (plowed) in *neither be plowed* (Deut. 21:4); *yera’eh*⁶¹, *alef*, *heh*. (appear) in *and they shall not appear* (Deut,. 16:16), for this is the rule.⁶², *chet*, *heh*, *ayin*, or *resh* is vocalized with a *tzere* rather than with a *chirik*, which is the usual vocalization.

footnotes:
⁵⁶ That is, a kal
⁵⁷ From the root alef, tzdai, resh
⁵⁸ From the root chet, samekh, nun
⁵⁹ From the root heh, resh, gimel
⁶⁰ From the root ayin, bet, dalet
⁶¹ From the root resh
⁶² The letter prefixed to all words whose first root letter is an alef

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/24:20:2)


**לא תפאר.** לא תחפש הפארות והם הסעיפים וכן ותעש בדים ותשלח פארות. גם הוא כתוב באל״‎ף. ואם נקרא בוי״‎ו וכן נאדות היין:


THOU SHALT NOT GO OVER THE BOUGHS AGAIN. *Lo tefa’er* (Thou shalt not go over the boughs) means you shall not seek after its “glory,” that is, after its branches. Compare, *pore’ot* (sprigs) in *And shot forth sprigs* (Ezek. 17:6). It too⁶³. is spelled with an *alef* even though it is read as if written with a *vav*.⁶⁴ is pronounced like a *cholam*. Compare, *nodot*⁶⁵ in *nodot* is pronounced like a *cholam*. (wineskins) in *and wineskins, worn* (Josh. 9:4).

⁶³ Like tefa’er
⁶⁴ The alef
⁶⁵ The alef
