## Chapter 25


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:1:1)


**ושפטום.** השופטים כמו לא יחבול רחים:


AND…JUDGE THEM. *U-shefatum*¹ literally means and they will judge them. The subject is omitted. Our verse literally reads, *if there be a controversy between men and they go unto judgment, and they judge them* (u-shefatum). Hence I.E. explains that “and they judge them” is short for: and the judges judge them. is to be rendered, and the judges judge them. Our verse is similar to *No man shall take the mill or the upper millstone to pledge* (Deut. 24:6).²

footnotes:
¹ U-shefatum
² Here too the subject is omitted, for the verse literally reads, He shall not take the mill to pledge. See I.E. on Deut. 24:6 and the notes thereto.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:2:1)


**בן הכות.** בחיר״‎ק כמו בן נון (ס״‎א בן) יקה והטעם כמו בן בליעל. וכן שבן לילה היה. והוא חייב. להכותו לפי דעתי שהכה חברו:


DESERVE TO BE BEATEN. The word *bin* (deserve) is vocalized with a *chirik*.³, i.e., *ben*. Compare, *bin nun* (son of Nun) (Josh. 1:1) and *bin yakeh* (son of Jakeh) (Prov. 30:1). *Bin hakkot* (deserve to be beaten) is similar to *ben bliyya’al* (a base fellow) (I Sam. 25:17).⁴ usually means son of. However, in *bin hakkot* and *ben bliyya’al, ben* signifies that the subject belongs to a certain group. *Bin laylah* (in a night) in *which came up in a night* (Jonah 4:10) is similar. The judge is obligated to beat him.⁵ means that the wicked falls into the category of those whom the judge is obligated to have beaten. I believe that Scripture calls the culprit wicked⁶. because he struck his neighbor.

footnotes:
³ It is usually vocalized with a segol
⁴ The word ben
⁵ Bin hakkot
⁶ Hebrew, rasha


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:2:2)


**והפילו השופט.** בציווי וכן והכהו והעד לפניו:


THAT THE JUDGE SHALL CAUSE HIM TO LIE DOWN. By command.⁷ The same applies to *and to be beaten*, as *before his*⁸ *face* proves.

footnotes:
⁷ The judge shall ask officers of the court to place him in a prone position.
⁸ The judge’s.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:2:3)


**כדי רשעתו במספר.** המכות. היה נראה לנו שיש עון שיוכה עליו עשר גם עשרים ופחות ויותר והעד כדי רשעתו רק לא יוסיף על ארבעים לולי דברי הקבלה והוא לבדו האמת וי״‎א כי טעם רשעתו במכות גדולות או קטנות וכלם במספר ארבעים:


ACCORDING TO THE MEASURE OF HIS WICKEDNESS, BY NUMBER. Of blows. It would have appeared to us, were it not for the words of tradition,⁹ that there are some sins that a person is punished for with ten blows. Others are punished with twenty blows. Other sins are punished with a lesser or greater number. The clause *according to the measure of his wickedness* is proof of this. In any case,¹⁰ the blows shall not exceed forty. However, the words of tradition alone are true.¹¹ Others say that *his wickedness*¹². refers to greater or lesser blows,¹³ all of which number forty.¹⁴

footnotes:
⁹ Which tells us that whenever the punishment of flogging is administered, thirty-nine (39) lashes are inflicted.
¹⁰ Regardless of the sin committed.
¹¹ That all those who are punished by flogging receive 39 lashes.
¹² That is, according to the measure of his wickedness
¹³ It refers to the intensity of the blows.
¹⁴ In other words, all those who are punished by flogging receive 40 lashes. However, their intensity varies according to the type of sin committed.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:3:1)


וטעם **ונקלה אחיך.** שהוא דבר ידוע וגזירה מהשם להיות ארבעים לסבול הסובל כדין שמים ולא בחפץ השופט:


THEN THY BROTHER SHOULD BE DISHONORED. For the thing¹⁵ is well known. It is a decree from the Almighty that the sufferer suffer forty blows. This is a decree from heaven so that the number of blows not depend on the will of the judge.

footnotes:
¹⁵ That this particular individual is flogged.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:4:1)


בי״‎ת **בדישו.** אמרו המכחישים שהוא קודם דישה ואין צורך:


[WHEN HE TREADETH.] The deniers¹⁶ say that the *bet* in *be-disho* (when he treadeth down) indicates prior to treading.¹⁷, before he treads. The muzzle is not put on the animal while he treads. It is put on the animal before the treading begins. Hence their interpretation. However, this is unnecessary.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁶ The Karaites.
¹⁷ The deniers render be-disho
¹⁸ For the meaning of Scripture is, do not put a muzzle on the animal so that it is muzzled while it treads the grain.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:5:1)


**כי ישבו אחים יחדו.** גם הם אמרו כי אינם אחים ממש כי אם קרובים והביאו ראיה מבועז ולא אמרו כלום כי אין שם זכר יבום כי אם גאול' ומה טעם להזכיר יחדו ואם היו במדינ' אחת או בחצר אחד או שהיו אוהבים זה את זה. ואמרו כי הנה הכתוב אמר ויבמה וזה אות כי איננה יבמה כי אם בשם כמו שבה יבמתך והנה נואלו ונסכלו כי הנה מפורש בבני יהודה ויבם אותה והוא יבמה גם יבמתך בעבור היותן לשני אחים וכל איש דעת ידע כי המצות שנתנו למשה שהם מצות לא תעשה לא היו אסורות קודם לכן ואילו היה אדם מונע עצמו מהן וקדם משה לא היה הדבר רע בעיני השם אף כי שכתב כי את כל התועבות האל עשו אנשי הארץ אשר לפניכם ולא מצאנו נביא שהזהירם והנה הכתוב אמר על אונן שלא נתן זרע לאחיו וירע בעיני ה' על כן אנחנו נסמוך על הקבל' שהם אחים ממש:


IF BRETHREN¹⁹ (brothers). DWELL TOGETHER. The deniers also say that these brethren²⁰ are not actual brothers,²¹ but are relatives. They brought proof from Boaz.²² (Ruth 4:5). However, they contribute nothing, for there is no mention of levirate marriage,²³ only of redemption.²⁴ Why mention the word *together*?²⁵ Does it matter if they were in one state or in one courtyard, or if they loved each other?²⁶ They say,²⁷. 38. *Mikra’ot Gedolot* reads: He says. look, the verse says *ve-yibbemah* (and shall perform the duty of a husband’s brother unto her).²⁸ means a brother-in-law. This shows that he is her brother-in-law in name only.²⁹ as, and be a brother-in-law to her. Now if the true brother-in-law was being addressed Scripture would not say, and be a brother-in-law to her. Hence we must interpret *ve-yibbemah* as meaning “act as a brother-in-law to her.” The upshot of all this is that a true brother-in-law is not being addressed. It is like *thy sister-in-law* (yevimtekh)³⁰ is the word *yevamah* (sister-in-law) plus the second person personal pronoun. Orpah was the wife of Ruth’s brother-in-law (*yavam*). She is referred to as a *yevamah* because she took her husband’s place. We thus see that the term *yavam* in its various forms can refer to one who acts like one’s brother-in-law. *is gone back* (Ruth 1:15). Now observe that they have become foolish and stupid, for Scripture states, with regard to the sons of Judah, *and perform the duty of a husband’s brother* (ve-yabbem) *unto her*³¹ (Gen. 38:8).³² your sister-in-law Tamar. Now he³³ was her³⁴ brother-in-law.³⁵ Also, the term *yevimtekh* (thy sister-in-law) (Ruth 1:15) was used because they were married to two brothers.³⁶ was not employed figuratively in Ruth. All intelligent people know that the negative commandments given to Moses³⁷ were not previously prohibited.³⁸ However, it would not have been evil in the eyes of God if someone abstained from them³⁹ before the law was given to Moses.⁴⁰ The fact that Scripture states, *for all these abominations have the men of the land done, that were before you* (Lev. 18:27), shows that this is certainly the case. We do not find a prophet who warned them.⁴¹ 5: “The fundamental commandments are those precepts that are implanted in the heart. They…were known by reason before the Torah was given through the agency of Moses” (Strickman translation, p. 75). Look, Scripture says of Onan, who did not give seed to his brother, *the thing which he did was evil in the sight of God* (Gen. 38:10).⁴² We will therefore rely on the tradition that they are actual brothers.

footnotes:
¹⁹ Hebrew, achim
²⁰ The brothers spoken of in our verse.
²¹ For Scripture explicitly prohibits a man from marrying his sister-inlaw. See Lev. 18:6.
²² Boaz married Ruth, who was the wife of his deceased relative, to raise up the name of the dead upon his inheritance
²³ In the Book of Ruth.
²⁴ Boaz married Ruth so that her husband’s field would not pass into the hands of a stranger. He thus “redeemed” the said field. See Ruth 4:1-6.
²⁵ In our verse.
²⁶ According to the Rabbinic tradition the word brethren means brothers and together means alive at the same time. However, if we accept the Karaitic interpretation then we do not know what Scripture means by the word together.
²⁷ So Vat. Ebr
²⁸ The word yavam
²⁹ The Karaites interpret ve-yibbemah
³⁰ Yevimtekh
³¹ Tamar, the widow of Er the son of Judah.
³² Judah told his son Onan to go and perform the duty of a husband’s brother unto
³³ Onan the brother of Er.
³⁴ Tamar’s.
³⁵ It is thus clear that levirate marriage was performed by the actual brother-in-law.
³⁶ In other words, the term yevamah
³⁷ Excluding the Noahide laws, which were given to Adam and Noah (see Meijler).
³⁸ There was thus no prohibition before the revelation at Sinai against a man to marrying his sister-in-law.
³⁹ That is, from those negative commandments from which reason tells us to abstain.
⁴⁰ In other words, God would have been pleased with a person who abstained from practices which He would later prohibit. If the law prohibiting intercourse with a sister-in-law was based on reason, i.e., it was an abomination, then Scripture would not have condemned Onan for not wanting to impregnate his sister-in-law.
⁴¹ From engaging in the practices that Lev. 18:27 refers to, for Scripture believes that one ought to know not to engage in these practices by employing one’s rationality. See Yesod Mora
⁴² God thus wants a brother to marry the childless widow of his biological brother.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:6:2)

THAT HIS NAME BE NOT BLOTTED OUT. Scripture speaks of his inheritance.⁴⁸ I have previously explained the meaning of *that she beareth* in my comments on *unto a foreign people* (Ex. 21:8).⁴⁹refers to the deceased’s first-born brother (*Yevamot* 24a). ### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:7:1)


**יבמי.** האמת כי היו״‎ד סימן הפעול:


MY HUSBAND’S BROTHER. The *yod* of *yevami* (my husband’s brother) is in fact the sign of the object.⁵⁰ is not the noun *yavam* (brother-in-law) plus the *yod* suffix meaning “my” but a combination of the verb *yabem* plus the *yo*d suffix meaning me. According to this interpretation *yevami* means to act as a brother-in-law to me.

footnotes:
⁵⁰ In other words, the word yevami

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:9:1)

AND LOOSE. *Ve-chaletzah* (and loose) is similar to *chaletzu* (draw out) in *draw out the breast* (Lam. 3:4). Its meaning is to remove.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:9:2)


**בפניו.** מפורש בדברי הקבל' שהוא כמו לפניו ודרך הפשט לפני הנעל כמו והיא כתובה פנים ואחור:


IN HIS FACE. The transmitters of tradition explain that the word *be-fanav* (in his face) has the meaning of before his face.⁵² 106b. According to its plain meaning, *be-fanav* means before the shoe, for *panav* here is similar to *panim* (in front) in *front and on the back*⁵³ (Ezek. 2:9).⁵⁴ means before it, i.e., before the shoe.

### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:11:1)


**כי ינצו.** הפך כי ישבו אחים יחדו ואלה האנשים זרים או אחים:


WHEN MEN STRIVE. The reverse of *If brethren dwell together* (v. 5).⁵⁵ The men spoken of are strangers or brothers.

footnotes:
⁵⁵ There is thus a thematic connection between this law and the one that precedes it. Hence its juxtaposition.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:11:2)


**במבושיו.** ביציו מגזרת בושה והוא עזות מצח לגלות דבר הנסתר שהוא ערוה גם הוא מקום מסוכן:


BY THE SECRETS. *Mevushav* (the secrets) means his testicles. It is related to the word *bushah* (shame).⁵⁶. The literal meaning of *mevushav* is, his shameful things. Hence I.E.’s interpretation that *mevushav* (the secrets) means his testicles. It is an act of audacity to uncover that which is hidden, namely, a person’s nakedness. It is also a vital organ.⁵⁷

footnotes:
⁵⁶ Its root is bet, vav, shin
⁵⁷ Literally, a dangerous place. Hence the severe punishment.

### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:13:1)


**בכיסך.** מקום למאזנים או יוציא מן הכיס בדרך רמאות והראשון הוא נכון והעד כיס אחד יהיה לכלנו משקל אחד. ומגזרת תכוסו על השה ואם הוא משורש אחר ורבים פירשו כמו חריט ואין צורך:


IN THY BAG.⁶⁰. Where you place the scales.⁶¹ (bag) is the name of a receptacle where the scales are kept. Or, do not deceitfully take the weights from the bag.⁶² The first interpretation is correct. The proof text is, *Let us all have one hag* (kis)⁶³ (Prov. 1:14), the meaning of which is, one scale.⁶⁴ (Prov. 1:14) is, let us all have one scale, for let us all have one bag makes no sense in this context. The word *kis* (bag) is related to the word *tachossu* (ye shall make your count)⁶⁵ means you shall apportion. A person uses scales to measure the weight of portions. See I.E. on Ex. 12:4 (Vol. 2, p. 219). in *ye shall make your count for the lamb* (Ex. 12:4) even though *tachossu* comes from a different root.⁶⁶ comes from the root *caf, vav, samekh. Tachossu* comes from the root *caf, samekh, samekh*. Many explained the word *kis* to mean a pouch.⁶⁷ (a cloth into which an object is placed). In this case *kis* refers not only to a receptacle for scales but to any bag or purse. There is no need for this interpretation.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Hebrew, be-khisekha
⁶¹ Kis
⁶² False weights. In this case the reference is not to the bag where the scales are kept but to a separate bag where the weights are kept.
⁶³ Translated according to I.E.
⁶⁴ The meaning of Let us all have one bag
⁶⁵ According to I.E. tachossu
⁶⁶ Than kis. Kis
⁶⁷ Hebrew, cheret

THAT THY DAYS MAY BE LONG. For it is well known that all righteous kingdoms will endure, for righteousness is like a building. However, unrighteousness⁷⁰ is like a ruin. Its wall collapses in a moment

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:16:1)


**כל עושה אלה.** איפה ואיפה אבן ואבן והכלל כל עושה עול בפה ובמעשה בסתר או בגלוי:


FOR ALL THAT DO SUCH THINGS. Those who possess diverse measures and diverse weights. This is followed by a general rule, namely, *even all that do unrighteously* verbally and by deed, secretly or publicly.



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:1)


**אשר קרך.** מגזרת כל הקורות אותם:


HOW HE MET THEE. *Karekha* (met thee) is related to the word *korot* (befallen) in *all that had befallen them* (Gen. 42:29).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:2)


**ויזנב בך.** כרת זנבך והם הנחשלי' אחריך שלא היה בם כח ללכת ויתכן להיות מלת נחשלים כמו נחלשי' וכן כבש כשב:


AND SMOTE THE HINDMOST OF THEE. *Va-yezanev bekhah* (and smote the hindmost of thee) means,⁷¹ means a tail. Thus *va-yezanev* means and he tailed you. Hence I.E.’s comments. and cut off your tail (*zanav*), namely, *all that were enfeebled in thy rear*,⁷² means and he cut off all those in your rear. that is, those who did not have the strength to walk. It is possible that the word *necheshalim* (enfeebled)⁷³. is similar to *nechelashim* (enfeebled).⁷⁴. Similarly *keves* (lamb) and *kesev* (lamb).⁷⁵

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:3)


**ואתה עיף.** כי ברפידים בא והעם צמא למים כי כן כתוב:


WHEN THOU WAS FAINT. For Amalek came to Rephidim where the people thirsted for water. The latter is clearly stated.⁷⁶.

⁷⁶ See Ex. 17:1, And…the children of Israel…encamped in Rephidim; and there was no water for the people to drink


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:18:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:4)


**ויגע.** מן הדרך:


AND WEARY. From the way.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:18:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:18:5)


**ולא ירא.** שב אל עמלק והוא פעל עבר כמו כי כבד ממך כי זקן יצחק כאשר אהב:


AND HE FEARED NOT. This refers to Amalek. *Yare* (feared) is a verb in the perfect.⁷⁷ in the *kal*. However, *yare* is vocalized *kamatz, tzere*. Hence I.E.’s comment. It is like the word *kaved* (heavy) in *for the thing is too heavy for thee* (Ex. 18:8); *zaken* (old) in *that when Isaac was old* (Gen. 27:1), and *ahev* (loveth) in *such as he loveth* (*Ibid.*, 8).⁷⁸ in the third person perfect.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ Words in the third person past are usually vocalized kamatz, pattach
⁷⁸ All of these words are vocalized kamatz, tzere



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:1)


**בהניח ה' אלהיך.** הטעם כי זאת המצוה תלוי' אחר שירשו את הארץ ושקט' הארץ ממלחמה מכל סביבותיה כי כל זמן שיתעסקו עם מלחמות הקרובים אליהם אינם חייבים להלחם בעמלק:


WHEN THE LORD THY GOD HATH GIVEN THEE REST. It means that this commandment takes effect after Israel inherits the land and the land is quiet from all the wars with the surrounding nations, for as long as they are involved in waging war with the nations close to them they are not obligated to fight Amalek.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 25:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:2)


**תמחה את זכר עמלק.** זהו והמתה מאיש ועד אשה מעולל ועד יונק משור ועד שה שלא יאמר זה השור היה משלל עמלק ודברי התורה כלל ודברי שמואל הנביא פרט:


THAT THOU SHALT BLOT OUT THE REMEMBRANCE. Namely, *slay both man and woman, infant and suckling, ox and sheep, camel and ass* (I Sam 15:3), so that no one says this ox is from the spoil taken from Amalek. The words of the Torah⁷⁹, is a general statement. are a general statement. The words of the prophet Samuel⁸⁰. go into detail.

footnotes:

⁸⁰ But slay both man and woman, infant and suckling, ox and sheep, camel and ass

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:3)


**לא תשכח.** אחר זכור לחזוק וכן משפט מצות רבות בתורה:


THOU SHALT NOT FORGET. Scripture says this after having already stated *Remember…*(v. 17) for emphasis. The same is true with many other commandments in the Torah.⁸¹

## Chapter 26


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:1:1)


**והיה כי תבא.** בעבור שכתוב בהניח ה' אמר השם כי יש מצות הן קודם בהניח ה' רק כאשר תבא והם הבכורים והמעשר וכתיבת התורה על האבנים ובנות מזבח והברכה והקלל' על שני ההרים:


AND IT SHALL BE, WHEN THOU ART COME. Since Scripture wrote, *Therefore it shall be, when the Lord thy God hath given thee rest* (Deut. 25:19),¹ God now says that there are commandments that must be observed before He gives you rest. In fact, these commandments take effect as soon as you enter the land. They are: the first fruits,² the tithes,³ writing the Torah on the stones,⁴ building the altar,⁵, 4-6. and the blessing and the curse on the two mountains.⁶

footnotes:
¹ Then you shall go to war against Amalek and destroy him.
² Verses 2-11.
³ Verses 12-15.
⁴ Deut. 27:1-3.
⁵ Ibid.
⁶ Mount Grezim and Mount Ebal (Deut. 27:11-26).



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:2:1)


**בטנא.** כדמות סל וחברו טנאך וזאת המצוה על הקרובים אל מקום המקדש:


IN A BASKET. *Tene* (basket) is a basket-like receptacle. The word *tanakha* (thy basket) (Deut 28:5) is its companion.⁷ This commandment⁸ 3:4, “When they reached the Temple mount even King Agrippa would take his basket on his shoulder” (Krinsky). Or, bringing fresh fruits (Weiser). See Mishnah *Bikkurim* 3:3, “They that were near to Jerusalem brought fresh figs and grapes, and they that were far off brought dried figs and raisins.” is incumbent upon those who are close to the place of the sanctuary.

footnotes:
⁷ The other place the word is found in Scripture.
⁸ Placing the first fruits in baskets. See Mishnah Bikkurim

וטעם **בימים ההם.** שזה חיוב כל זמן ששם כהן גדול:


[THAT SHALL BE IN THOSE DAYS.] *In those days* means that this law is in force as long as there is a *kohen gadol* there.⁹

**הגדתי.** הטעם כדי שישמעו הקטנים או הוא כדמות הודיה שהשם השלים שבועתו והעד הפרי:


I PROFESS. The reason for this utterance is so that the children will hear. Or it is, like a statement of thanks to God, for He has fulfilled his oath. The fruit¹⁰ is proof of this.¹¹

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:1)


**וענית.** יתכן שישאלו הכהנים לאמר מה זה שהבאת על כן וענית או כדמות תחלה וכן ויען איוב הראשון:


AND THOU SHALT SPEAK. It is possible that the *kohanim* will ask,¹² “What is this that you brought?” Hence Scripture reads *ve-anita*¹³ (and thou shalt speak). On the other hand, *ve-anita* may mean, and you shall begin speaking. Compare, *And Job spoke* (va-ya’an)¹⁴ and *ve-anita* come from the same root. *and said* (Job 3:2).¹⁵ asked the worshipper, “What is this that you brought?”

footnotes:
¹² The celebrant.
¹³ Literally, and you shall answer. Hence I.E.’s comment.
¹⁴ Va-ya’an
¹⁵ In this case we do not have to assume that the kohanim


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:2)


**אובד אבי.** מלת אובד מהפעלים שאינם יוצאים ואילו היה ארמי על לבן היה הכתוב אומר מאביד או מאבד ועוד מה טעם לאמר לבן בקש להאביד אבי וירד מצרימה ולבן לא סבב לרדת אל מצרים והקרוב שארמי הוא יעקב כאלו אמר הכ' כאשר היה אבי בארם היה אובד והטעם עני בלא ממון וכן תנו שכר לאובד והעד ישתה וישכח רישו והנה הוא ארמי אובד היה אבי והטעם כי לא ירשתי הארץ מאבי כי עני היה כאשר בא אל ארם גם גר היה במצרים והוא היה במתי מעט ואחר כן שב לגוי גדול ואתה ה' הוצאתנו מעבדות ותתן לנו ארץ טובה ואל יטעון טוען איך יקרא יעקב ארמי והנה כמוהו יתרא הישמעאלי והוא ישראלי כי כן כתוב:


A WANDERING ARAMEAN. The word *oved* (wandering) is an intransitive verb. If Aramean referred to Laban¹⁶ (a wandering Aramean was my father) is to be rendered an Aramean (Laban) sought to destroy my father (Jacob). See *Sifre*, the Passover Haggadah, and Rashi. then Scripture should have read *mavid or me’abbed*.¹⁷ Furthermore, what reason would Scripture have had for stating, *Laban sought to destroy my father and he*¹⁸ *went down to Egypt*? Laban did not cause Jacob to descend to Egypt. It thus appears to me that the term Aramean refers to Jacob. Scripture, as it were, reads, when my father was in Aram he was “perishing.” That is, he was poor, he had no money. Similarly *oved* (perish) in *Give strong drink unto him that is ready to perish* (Prov. 31:6). *Let him drink, and forget his poverty* (Prov. 31:7) is proof.¹⁹ The meaning of our phrase thus is, a perishing Aramean was my father. Its import is, I did not inherit the land from my father, for my father was poor when he came to Aram.²⁰ He was also a stranger in Egypt. He was few in number.²¹ He then became a large nation.²² You O Lord took us out of slavery and gave us a good land. Let the one who disputes²³. not argue, “How could Jacob be called an Aramean?”²⁴ Look, we find the same with Ithra the Ishmaelite²⁵ who, as Scripture clearly states,²⁶ was an Israelite.²⁷

footnotes:
¹⁶ According to the midrash, arami oved avi
¹⁷ For these are the transitive forms of the verb.
¹⁸ My father.
¹⁹ That “perish” in the previous verse refers to poverty.
²⁰ When he fled from his brother Esau.
²¹ When he came down to Egypt.
²² In Egypt.
²³ Against I.E.’s interpretation of arami oved avi
²⁴ Jacob was a Hebrew.
²⁵ See Chron. 2:17.
²⁶ In II Sam. 17:25.
²⁷ Scripture refers to him as an Ishmaelite because he lived in the land of the Ismaelites. So too Jacob, who was called an Aramean because he lived there.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:3)


**במתי.** סמוך כי לא נמצא לשון יחיד. ומתים לשון רבים ורבי יונה דקדק אותה כולה באר היטב בערך מתה:


FEW IN NUMBER.²⁸. Literally, with men of few, i.e., few men. The word *mete* (men of) is in the construct,²⁹ has been omitted from our text. Our verse should be read as if written, *mete am me’at* (Krinsky). for it is not found in the singular.³⁰ cannot be in the construct with *me’at*, which is a singular. If *mete* was in the construct with *me’at* then our text would read, *mete me’atim*. The word *metim* is a plural. Rabbi Jonah the grammarian explained it very well under the entry *mem, tav, heh*.

²⁸ Hebrew, mete me’at
²⁹ With the word am. However, the word am
³⁰ Hence the form mete

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:14:1)


**לא אכלתי באוני.** כמו בן אוני ולחם אונים והטעם רמזתי על לשון הקודש וטעם אוני כמו אבלי כי אם הייתי אבל לא אכלתי (קודם) [ס״‎א קודש] המעשר:


I HAVE NOT EATEN THEREOF IN MY MOURNING. The word *oni* (my mourning) is similar to the word *oni* (sorrow) in *ben-oni* (the son of my sorrow) (Gen. 35:18) and *onim* (mourners) in *bread of mourners* (Hosea 9:4). I alluded⁴⁰ to its meaning⁴¹ in Hebrew.⁴². 38. Some emend our verse to read, “in my book on the Hebrew language (*Moznayim*).” See Weiser. The meaning of *oni* is, my mourning. If I was in mourning then I did not eat of it before setting aside the tithe.⁴³. Some editions read *lo akhalti kodesh ha-ma’aser*, I did not eat the holy, the tithe. The latter is forbidden to a mourner in the time between the death and burial of a close relative.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:14:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:14:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:14:4)


**למת.** לצרכי מת וי״‎א לעבודת כוכבים והטעם באלה הדברים כי אסור הוא שיוציא מהדגן לשום דבר עד תת המעשר שהוא קדש כי אם יתן מהמותר הוא דרך בזיון. ואף כי אם נתן בטמא וכאשר נשמרתי אני ושמרתי הדגן בעבור להוציא הקדש כן שמור אלהינו ארצנו וזה טעם ואת האדמה:


FOR THE DEAD. For the need of the dead. Others say the reference is to idol worship.⁴⁶ on Ps. 106:28. These things⁴⁷ are enumerated⁴⁸ because it is prohibited to use the grain for any purpose before giving of the tithe which is holy.⁴⁹ If he gives from the remainder he is acting disrespectfully.⁵⁰ This is certainly so if he exchanges it for something unclean. As I kept myself and I kept the grain, that is, I removed that which is holy from my house, so may our God watch over our country.⁵¹ This is the meaning of *and the land* (v. 15).

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:1)


**היום הזה.** אלה דברי משה:


THIS DAY. These are the words of Moses.⁵³

footnotes:
⁵³ Up to now Scripture reports the declaration of the one who brought the first fruits. What now follows are Moses’ words to the people of Israel.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:2)


**ושמרת.** בלבך:


THOU SHALT THEREFORE OBSERVE. In your heart.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:3)


**ועשית אותם.** בארץ:


AND DO THEM. In the land.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:1)


**האמרת.** מלשון גדולה וקרוב מגזרת בראש אמיר ויאמר רבי יהודה הלוי הספרדי נ״‎ע כי המלה מגזרת ויאמר והטעם כי עשית הישר עד שיאמר שהוא יהי' אלהיך גם הוא עוש' לך עד שאמרת שתהי' לו לעם סגולה ויפה פירש והנה תהיה מלת האמרת פעל יוצא לשנים פעולים:


THOU HAST AVOUCHED. *He’emarta* (avouched) means exalted. It is close in meaning to *amir* (uppermost bough) in *In the top of the uppermost bough* (Is. 17:6). Rabbi Yehudah Ha-Levi, who rests in Eden, says that the word is related to *va-yomer* (said). Its meaning is, you were so righteous that the Lord said He is your God. God likewise acted very righteously towards you that you said that you would be His own treasure. He explained well. Now the word *he’emarta* is a *hifil*.⁵⁴

footnotes:
⁵⁴ Meaning you caused [Him] to say. According to Rabbi Yehudah Ha-Levi our verse reads: Thou hast caused the Lord to declare this day to be thy God…



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 26:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:1)


**ולתתך עליון.** זה הוא שכר האמיר':


AND TO MAKE THEE HIGH. This is the reward for extolling God.



## Chapter 27


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:1)


**ויצו משה.** בצווי השם:


AND MOSES…COMMANDED. By the dictate of God.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:2)


**וזקני ישראל.** אמר כן להקל וכן רבים וכבר הזכרתים:


AND THE ELDERS OF ISRAEL. He¹ said this to lighten his load.² There are many such instances.³ I have previously noted them.⁴

footnotes:
¹ Moses.
² Moses directed the elders to deliver the message to Israel in order to lighten his own load.
³ Where Moses asked the elders in assisting him in delivering his message to Israel.
⁴ See I.E. on Deut. 12:21.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:3)


**שמור את כל המצוה.** ומה היא והקמות לך אבנים גדולו' להכיל התורה ויאמר הגאון ז״‎ל כי כתוב עליהם מספר המצות כמו הכתובות בהלכות גדולות בענין אזהרות ויפה אמר:


KEEP ALL THE COMMANDMENTS. Namely, *that thou shalt set thee up great stones* to contain the entire Torah. The Gaon,⁵ of blessed memory, said that the commandments⁶ were listed upon the stones, as in the *Halakhot Gedolot*.⁷ was the first to enumerate the 613 commandments. Rabbi Saadiah Gaon uses the *Halakhot Gedolot* as an illustration of a list of the 613 commandments, for it was the first work to provide such a list. Rabbi Saadiah Gaon interprets thus because he does not believe that the stones were large enough for the entire five books of the Torah to have been written upon. They were listed in a manner similar to the *azharot*.⁸ (exhortation) is 613. He explained well.

footnotes:
⁵ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁶ The 613 commandments of the Torah.
⁷ A Geonic compendium by Rabbi Simon Kayyra (ninth century) of Talmudic law arranged according to the order of the tractates of the Talmud. The Halakhot Gedolot
⁸ Liturgical poems devoted to the enumeration of the 613 commandments. The numerical value of the Hebrew letters of azharot



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:2:1)


וטעם **בשיד.** שיעמדו:


[WITH PLASTER] Plaster was employed so that it⁹ would last.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁹ The monument of stones.
¹⁰ SeeRabbi Joseph Bekhor Shor on this verse. He explains that the Torah was written on a number of stones held together by plaster. Without the plaster the stones would fall apart. I.E. apparently interpreted similarly.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:3:1)


**למען.** כי השם יעזרך אם החלות לשמור מצותיו וזאת המצוה הראשונה לביאתם לבנות להם מזבח חדש להודות לשם שהחלו להיותם בארץ:


THAT THOU MAYEST. For God will aid you¹¹ if you begin to observe His commandments. This first precept upon entering the Land of Israel, namely, the command to build a new altar, was to give thanks to God for their starting to inherit the land.

footnotes:
¹¹ In conquering the land.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:8:1)


**באר היטב.** הכתיבה:


VERY PLAINLY. The reference is to the writing.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:1)


**וידבר משה והכהנים הלוי.** כאשר כתוב וזקני ישראל והעיקר משה:


AND MOSES AND THE PRIESTS THE LEVITES. This is in keeping with Scripture’s statement, *and the elders of Israel* (v. 1).¹² and the Levites assisted Moses. Moses was the main one.¹³ and the Levites.

footnotes:
¹² The kohanim
¹³ He was the one who received the revelation from God and related it to the kohanim


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:2)


**הסכת.** פירושו לפי מקומו כי אין ריע לו:


KEEP SILENCE. The meaning of the word *hasket* (keep silence) can only be known from its context, for it has no neighbor.¹⁴

¹⁴ The word is not found again in Scripture.

THIS DAY THOU ART BECOME A PEOPLE. For God will make a covenant with you.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:10:1)


**ושמעת.** היום בקולו:


THOU SHALT THEREFORE HEARKEN. Today, to His voice.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:10:2)

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:14:1)


וטעם **הכהנים הלוים.** כי הם האומרים ארור האיש. על הברכה בני הגבירות ועל הקללה בני השפחות ואם יחסרו שנים ובני לאה רבים ונלקחו הגדול והקטן:


Scripture mentions *the priests and the Levites* (v. 9), for they are the ones who said, *Cursed be the man* (v. 15). The children of the main wives¹⁵ stood over the blessings,¹⁶ while the children of the handmaids¹⁷ stood over the curse.¹⁸ Now if the latter lacked two,¹⁹ the sons of Leah were numerous,²⁰ and her oldest²¹ and youngest²² were taken.²³

footnotes:
¹⁵ The tribes descended from Rachel and Leah. See verse 12.
¹⁶ On Mount Gerizim (v. 12).
¹⁷ The tribes descended from Bilhah and Zilpah. See verse 13.
¹⁸ On Mount Ebal (v. 13).
¹⁹ Tribes. Only four tribes were descended from the handmaids. However, six tribes were commanded to stand over the blessing and six over the curse.
²⁰ Leah had six sons.
²¹ Reuben.
²² Zebulan.
²³ From the tribes that stood on Mount Gerizim and added to the ones on Mount Ebal.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:14:2)


**וענו.** כמו וענית ואמרת וחז״‎ל אמרו כי טעם לברך ברוך האיש אשר לא יעשה פסל ומסכה וכן הכל ועל דרך הפשט הברכה היא ברוך אתה בעיר והקללה הפך זה. והעד בספר יהושע:


AND THE LEVITES SHALL SPEAK. *Ve-anu* (shall speak) is similar to *ve-anita* (and thou shalt speak) in *And thou shalt speak and say* (Deut. 26:5).²⁴ Our rabbis, of blessed memory, said that the meaning of *to bless*²⁵ 31a: “They turned their faces towards Mount Gerizim and opened with the blessing: *blessed is the man who does not make a graven or molten image*, and both parties respond amen. They then turned their faces towards Mount Ebal and opened with the curse: *Cursed is the man who maketh a graven or molten image*, and both parties respond amen.” is to say, *blessed is the man who does not make a graven or molten image*, and similarly all of them.²⁶ However, according to the plain meaning of the text, the blessing refers to *Blessed shalt thou be in the city* (Deut. 28:3).²⁷, that is, Deut. 28:3-13. The curse is the reverse of this.²⁸ Proof of this is found in the Book of Joshua.²⁹ are not mentioned in the Torah.

footnotes:
²⁴ See I.E. on Deut. 26:5 and the notes thereto.
²⁵ Scripture lists only curses in verses 16-21 Blessings are not mentioned. However, the rabbis say that each one of the curses had its blessing counterpart. See Sotah
²⁶ All of the curses have their blessing counterpart.
²⁷ That is, the blessing beginning with Blessed shalt thou be in the city
²⁸ The curse recorded in Deut. 28:16-68 is the reverse of the blessing recorded in Deut. 28:3-13.
²⁹ See Josh. 3:35. Joshua speaks of the blessings and curses recorded in the Torah. These can only refer to chapter 28, for the blessings spoken of by the rabbis in Sotah



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:15:1)


וטעם להזכיר אלה י״‎א עבירות בעבור שיוכל לעשותם בסתר והעד ושם בסתר כי אם יעשה בגלוי יומת וכן מכה רעהו בסתר והחל מהעיקר שהוא בינו ובין בוראו ואחר כן בינו ובין אבותיו כי מי ידע אם יקלה אותם:


Scripture mentions these eleven sins³⁰ because an individual can commit them in secret. *And setteth it up in secret* (v. 15) is proof, for if one commits this crime³¹ in the open he is put to death.³² *He that smiteth his neighbor in secret* (v. 24) is similar. Scripture begins with the most important, namely, that which concerns a person and his creator.³³ It then speaks of a person and his parents,³⁴ for who knows if he dishonors them.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁰ Mentioned in verses 15-25.
³¹ Making a graven or molten image.
³² By a court of law.
³³ Making a graven or molten image.
³⁴ Dishonoring one’s parents.
³⁵ In the privacy of their home.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:16:1)


**מקלה.** לשון קלון פועל יוצא:


DISHONORETH. The word *makleh* (dishonoreth) means to dishonor. It is a transitive verb.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:17:1)


**מסיג גבול רעהו.** כי הוא דבר נסתר ו**משגה עור** ג״‎כ לא יוכל לפרסם מי שהשגהו:


CURSED BE HE THAT REMOVETH HIS NEIGHBOR’S LANDMARK. For it is done in secret. [MAKETH THE BLIND TO GO ASTRAY.] Likewise it is not possible for the blind person to identify the one who led him astray.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:19:1)


**מטה משפט גר יתום.** כי אין להם עוזר והנה גם הוא בסתר והזכיר הגר גם היתום והאלמנה כי אם יטה הדיין משפט אחרים יערערו עליו ויפרסמוהו והגר והיתום והאלמנה אין להם כח. ושוכב עם אשת אב ואחות וחותנת אינו נחשד להתייחד עמה על כן הוא דבר נסתר ואין כן שאר העבירות:


CURSED BE HE THAT PERVERTETH THE JUSTICE DUE TO THE STRANGER, FATHERLESS. For they have no one to help them. Look, this too is done in secret.³⁶ Scripture mentions the stranger as well as the orphan and the widow because if the judge perverts the justice due others, they will accuse and publicize him. The stranger, the orphan, and the widow are powerless. Sleeping with one’s father’s wife, with a sister, or with a mother-in-law is an act that is committed in secret, for one is not suspected when he is alone with any one of them. It is not so with other sins.³⁷

footnotes:
³⁶ No one knows why a judge ruled the way he did.
³⁷ Hence only these eleven sins are specifically mentioned in Scripture.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:21:1)


**עם כל בהמה.** זכר או נקבה כי אין לה פה שתצעק ותערער כמו הזכור על כן לא הזכירו:


CURSED BE HE THAT LIETH WITH ANY MANNER OF BEAST.³⁸ is feminine. Our verse could thus conceivably be translated, cursed be he that lieth with any manner of female beast. Hence I.E.’s comments. Male or female, for neither have a mouth with which to cry out and protest. Thus, a female animal is like a male animal;³⁹ therefore Scripture does not mention the male.

footnotes:
³⁸ Hebrew, behemah. Behamah
³⁹ As neither can cry out and protest verbally.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:25:1)


**לוקח שוחד.** לפי דעת רבים שהוא הדיין ולפי דעתי שהוא עד שקר:


HE THAT TAKETH A BRIBE. According to many the reference is to the judge. According to my opinion the reference is to a false witness.



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 27:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:26:1)


**אשר לא יקים.** י״‎א על כל התורה וי״‎א על אלה הנזכרים ולא אמרו כלום בעבור שכתוב לעשות אותם והנכון בעיני כי קלל על מצות לא תעשה הנזכרים וקלל מי שלא ישמור גם בסתר מצות עשה על כן אמר לעשות אותם:


THAT CONFIRMETH NOT. Some say that the reference is to the entire Torah. Others say that it refers to the specifically mentioned sins.⁴⁰ However, what they say is meaningless, for Scripture says, *to do them*.⁴¹ However, it appears to me that Scripture cursed the violators of the precepts which are mentioned.⁴² It also cursed the one who secretly does not observe the positive commandments. Scripture therefore reads, *to do them*.

footnotes:
⁴⁰ The sins mention in verse 15-25.
⁴¹ The verse curses those who fail to observe positive practices, not only those who violate certain laws.
⁴² In verses 15-25.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:4:2)

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:5:1)


**טנאך.** מקום הלחם והוא מוקדם ומאוחר עם משארותיך. או מקום שיושם שם הקמח:


THY BASKET. The place of bread. *Thy basket* precedes *and thy kneading trough* but should come after it.⁸ On the other hand, *thy basket* may refer to the place where the flour is placed.⁹.

footnotes:
⁸ For the dough has to be kneaded before it is made into bread.
⁹ Hence Thy basket precedes and thy kneading trough



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:6:1)


**בצאתך.** לחוג ולסחור ולשלול או בכל ביאה ויציא' בכל יום ויום והטעם בכל אשר תעשה תצליח וההפך כתוב:


WHEN THOU GOEST OUT. To celebrate festivals,¹⁰ to engage in business,¹¹ and to take spoil,¹² or in all your daily comings and goings.¹³ Its meaning is, you shall be successful in all that you do. The reverse is written [in the curses].¹⁴, etc., is the reverse of our verse.

footnotes:
¹⁰ The three pilgrimage festivals.
¹¹ When you travel a great distance on business.
¹² When you go out to war.
¹³ In your mundane affairs.
¹⁴ Verse 19, which states, Cursed shalt thou be when thou comest in



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:7:1)


ואחר שאמר שתהיה עליון על כל הגוים אמר כי אם יקשרו אויביך על כן אמר הקמים עליך:


[THAT RISE UP AGAINST THEE.] Since Scripture earlier stated that God will set Israel on high above the nations (v. 1), it now goes on to say that should the nations revolt against Israel [the Lord will cause thine enemies] *that rise up against thee* [to be smitten before thee].


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:7:2)


**ובשבעה.** לשון רבים בעבור שוב ימי השבוע כי הוא תלוי בעליונים:


AND…SEVEN. This means many.¹⁵ The number seven is employed because of the continuous cycle of seven days of the week.¹⁶ It¹⁷ has a set number of days because is contingent on the bodies which are on high.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁵ Seven is not to be taken literally.
¹⁶ It is the maximum number of days in the week. Scripture did not use a month because there are not the same number of days in each month.
¹⁷ The week.
¹⁸ Each day of the week is connected to one of the seven moving heavenly bodies, namely, the sun, the moon, and the five visible planets.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:8:1)


**באסמיך.** באוצרותיך מטעם המקום:


IN THY BARNS. *Ba-asamekha* (in thy bams) is in your storehouses. Its meaning is determined by its context.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:1)


**לעם קדוש.** והקדוש' לשמור המצות:


FOR A HOLY PEOPLE. Holy by observing God’s commandments.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:2)


וטעם **יקימך.** יעמידך:


The meaning of *yekimekha* is, will establish thee.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:3)


**תשמור.** בלב:


SHALT KEEP. In your heart.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:9:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:9:4)


**והלכת.** במעשה:


AND WALK. Practice.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:11:1)


וטעם **והותירך.** אם תהיה רעה בארץ ורעב מארה אתה תשאר לטובה:


AND…WILL MAKE THEE OVERABUNDANT. The meaning of *and will make thee overabundant*¹⁹. I.E. takes this to mean will cause you to be left over. is, if evil comes to the world, if hunger or blight comes, you will be set aside for good.

footnotes:
¹⁹ Hebrew, ve-hotirekha



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:12:1)


**אוצרו.** שהוא בכחו לצאת וכן אוצרות שלג על כן אחר הותירך אמר דבר הגשם כי העוצר סבת הרעב:


HIS…TREASURE. Which is potentially ready in heaven to go out.²⁰ Compare, *the treasuries of the snow* (Job 38:22).²¹ Scripture speaks of rain after stating *will make thee overabundant*,²² for the withholding of rain is the cause of famine.

footnotes:
²⁰ Hence rain is referred to as a treasure.
²¹ The reference is to heaven.
²² The meaning of which, according to I.E., is, will allow to survive.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:12:2)


**והלוית גוים רבים.** כי ירעבו ולא יבא זמן שתלוה אתה והכלל שתהיה לעולם לראש:


AND THOU SHALT LEND UNTO MANY NATIONS. For they shall suffer famine. However, no time shall come where you shall borrow. The sum of the matter is, you will always be on top.²³

footnotes:
²³ Literally, the head.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:13:1)


**כי תשמע.** בלב לשמור ולעשו' והכלל שלא תסו' מאחרי השם לעבוד עבודת כוכבים כי הוא עיקר כל העבירות לכפור בעיקר והפך זה יחוד השם לשמור ולעשות כי עיקר השמיר' המעש':


IF THOU SHALT HEARKEN. In your heart to observe and to do. The sum of the matter is, do not turn aside from God to serve idols, for the root of all sins is to deny God.²⁴ The reverse of the aforementioned is to affirm that the Lord is one and to hearken and to observe, for the most important aspect of hearkening is observing.

footnotes:
²⁴ Literally, the root. If you worship another god you are in effect denying the Lord.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:20:1)


**המארה.** המגרע':


CURSING. Diminution.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ Of that which you require and that which is good. According to I.E. a blessing refers to an increase and a curse to a diminution. See I.E. on Gen. 2:3 (Vol. 1, p. 50) and Gen. 3:14 (Vol. 1, p. 71) and the notes thereto.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:20:2)


**המהומה.** שלא יוכלל להשלים המעש':


DISCOMFITURE. A state wherein one can not finish what he has started.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:20:3)


**המגערת.** שלא ישמח במעשיו רק ידאג בו אחר יגיעתו:


REBUKE. A state in which one cannot enjoy what he has worked for, but is concerned for it²⁶ after he has toiled.

footnotes:
²⁶ Lest it be stolen or the like.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:1)


**בשחפת.** י״‎א שהם מיני חלאים:


CONSUMPTION. Some say that *shachefet* (consumption) refers to various types of illnesses.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:2)


**בקדחת.** בכל יום. מגזרת כי אש קדח' באפי. ומהומה ומגערת ושחפת וקדחת ודלקת שם התואר:


WITH FEVER. Each day. *Kaddachat* (fever) is related to *kadechah* (kindled) in *For a fire is kindled* (Deut. 32:22). The words *mehumah* (discomfiture), *migeret* (rebuke), *shachefet* (consumption), *kaddachat* (fever) and *dalleket* (inflammation) are adjectives.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:22:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:3)


וטעם **ובדלקת.** שלישית או רביעית:


*Dalleket* refers to a fever which comes every third or fourth day.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:22:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:4)


**ובחרחור.** מגזרת וחרה נחושת':


AND WITH FIERY HEAT. The word *charchur* (fiery heat) is related to the word *charah* (bum) in *and the bottom thereof may burn* (Ezek. 24:11).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:22:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:5)


**ובחרב.** מגזרת חורב:


AND WITH DROUGHT. The word *cherev* (drought) is related to the word *chorev* (dry).²⁷ is a sword.

footnotes:
²⁷ The usual meaning of cherev


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:22:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:6)


**ובשדפון.** נפיחות וכן שדופות קדים וי״‎א הפוך והטעם דקוק:


AND WITH BLASTING. *Shiddafon* (blasting) means bloating. Compare, *shedufot kadim* (blasted with the east wind)²⁸ as bloated by the east wind. (Gen. 41:23). Others say the reverse is true. Its meaning is then thin.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁸ This interpretation renders shedufot kadim
²⁹ Withered, shriveled.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:22:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:22:7)


**ובירקון.** כמשמעו בלשון קדר וי״‎א כי אלה חליים בזרע ובשדפון כמו שדופות קדים והעד הכיתי אתכם בשדפון ובירקון. על כן אחריו והיו שמיך וכן בפרשת אם בחקותי:


AND WITH MILDEW. *Yerakon* (mildew) is to be rendered according to its meaning in Arabic.³⁰ Some say that these³¹ (blasting) and *yerakon* (mildew). refer to diseases of the seed.³² *U-vashiddafon* (blasting) is similar to *shidufot* (blasted) in *blasted with the east wind* (Gen. 41:23). *I have smitten you with blasting* (shiddafon)³³ here refers to a disease of the seed, for Amos goes on to speak of gardens, vineyards, and olive trees. See Amos 4:9. *and mildew* (Amos 4:9) is proof. Therefore Scripture afterwards reads, *and thy heaven that is over thy head shall be brass*. We find the same in the Torah portion *If Ye Walk in My Statutes* (Lev. 26:3).³⁴

footnotes:
³⁰ It is a disease in which the skin develops a greenish color.
³¹ Shiddafon
³² Unlike the illnesses mentioned up to this point which attack people, these attack plants.
³³ Shiddafon
³⁴ Scripture first speaks of illnesses that strike people and then of plagues that fall upon the plants.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:23:1)

BRASS. Like brass. The same applies to iron.³⁵

### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:24:1)


**אבק ועפר.** ברוב החורב וכח הרוחות:


POWDER AND DUST. Because of the great dryness and the power of the winds.³⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ The drought and the winds will turn the soil to dust and powder.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:24:2)


**עד השמדך.** מלה זרה כמו ביום הבראך:


UNTIL THOU BE DESTROYED. *Hishamedakh* (thou be destroyed) is irregular.³⁷ rather than with a *sheva* beneath the *mem* and a *kamatz* beneath the *kaf*; i.e., compare the word *hishamedkha* in verse 20. Compare, *hibbare’akh*³⁸ (in the day that thou wast created) (Ezek. 28:15).³⁹ (thou be destroyed) and *hibbare’akh* are similarly vocalized. Some editions read, *be-yom hibbare’akh* (from the day that thou wast created) (Ezek. 28:15).

footnotes:
³⁷ It should have been vocalized with a segol
³⁸ Its vocalization is also irregular.
³⁹ Hishamedakh



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:25:1)


**לזעוה.** הפוך כמו כבש כשב:


A HORROR. Some of the letters of *za’avah* (horror) are reversed.⁴⁰ is a variant of *zeva’ah*. Compare, *keves* (lamb) and *kesev* (lamb).⁴¹

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Za’avah
⁴¹ There too some of the letters reverse their positions.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:26:1

AND THERE SHALL BE NONE TO FRIGHTEN THEM AWAY. To frighten away the birds and beasts.



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:1)


והנשארים יוכו בשחין מצרים:


[THE BOIL OF EGYPT.] Those who remain will be struck with the boil of Egypt.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:2)


**ובטחורים.** הם היוצאים במקום הנסתר על כן כתוב ובעפולים והוא מגזרת ויבא אל העופל:


AND WITH THE EMERODS. They appear in the hidden places.⁴² Scripture therefore reads *u-vafolim*⁴³. The *keri* reads *u-vatchorim*. (and with the emerods). The latter is related to the world *ofel* (hill) in *And when he came to the hill* (II Kings 5:24).⁴⁴ because they are hill-like; i.e., they consist of swellings. According to Weiser its meaning is, hemorrhoids are called *ofalim* because hills are used for hiding and hemorrhoids appear in hidden places.

footnotes:
⁴² They are swellings that appear in hidden places.
⁴³ This is the ketib
⁴⁴ Hemorrhoids are called ofalim


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:27:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:3)


**ובגרב.** כן בלשון ישמעאל:


AND WITH THE SCAB.⁴⁵. The same is true in Arabic.⁴⁶.

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Hebrew, garav
⁴⁶ The Arabic word for scab is jarabu


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:27:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:27:4)


**ובחרס.** י״‎א שיצטרך אל חרס כאיוב ואם הוא בסמ״‎ך וי״‎א במכת השמש. והנכון שהוא שם חולי כנזכרי' ואין לו חבר:


AND WITH THE ITCH.⁴⁷. Some say that he will need a potsherd⁴⁸. He will need a potsherd (*cheres*) to scratch himself to relieve his itching. as Job did.⁴⁹ This is so⁵⁰ because one employs a potsherd for relief if struck by this illness. even though the word *cheres* (itch) is spelled with a *samekh*.⁵¹. Others say *u-ve-chares* (and with the itch) means: and with a blow from the sun.⁵² is the sun. See Jud. 14:18. Hence this interpretation renders *u-ver-chares*, and with sunstroke. However, the correct interpretation is that *cheres* (itch) refers to the name of a disease like the ones mentioned previously. The word has no neighbor.⁵³

footnotes:
⁴⁷ Hebrew, cheres
⁴⁸ Hebrew, cheres
⁴⁹ Job used a potsherd to relief his itching. See Job 2:8.
⁵⁰ The disease is called cheres
⁵¹ While the word for potsherd is spelled with a sin
⁵² One of the meanings of the word cheres
⁵³ It is not found elsewhere in Scripture. Hence we do not know what sort of disease it refers to.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:28:1)


והשגעון והעורון והתמהון שלשתן בלב:


MADNESS…] Madness, blindness, and astonishment are afflictions of the heart.⁵⁴

### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:29:1)


**והיית ממשש.** פירוש בעורון:


AND THOU SHALT GROPE. In blindness.⁵⁵

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:29:2)


**תצליח.** פעל יוצא:


PROSPEROUS. *Tatzli’ach* (prosperous) is a transitive verb.⁵⁶

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:29:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:29:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:29:3)


**עשוק וגזול.** מאויב ואין מושיע והחל לפרש איך יהיה עשוק:


OPPRESSED AND ROBBED. By the enemy, and there shall be none to save thee. Scripture commences to explain in what manner Israel shall be oppressed.



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:30:1)


**ישגלנה.** כנוי לשכיבה וכן שגל המוכנת למטה:


SHALL LIE WITH HER. *Yishgalennah* (lie with her) is a euphemism for sexual intercourse. The same applies to the word *shegal* (the queen) (Ps. 45:10). It refers to a woman prepared for bed.⁵⁷

footnotes:
⁵⁷ For sexual intercourse. See I.E. on Ps. 45:10.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:30:2)


**תחללנו.** מגזרת חליל':


USE THE FRUIT THEREFOF. *Techallennu* (use the fruit thereof) is related to the word *chalil* (flute).⁵⁸. 38. The printed editions read *chalilah*. See I.E. on Deut. 28:6: “They were in the habit of dancing [to the tune of flutes] in the vineyards.” Hence the term *techallennu*.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ So Vat. Ebr



### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:32:1)


**וכלות.** כמו נכספה וגם כלת' וכן ותכל דוד ואינו חסר נפש כאשר פירש חסר הדעת כי ותכל פועל יוצא ולא נזכר הפועל כמו אשר ילדה אותה:


AND FAIL WITH LONGING. The word *kalot* (fail with longing) is similar to the word *kaletah* (pineth) in *My soul yearneth, yea, even pineth* (Ps. 84:3) and to *va-tekhal* (failed with longing) in *va-tekhal david* (and caused David to fail with longing)⁵⁹ (II Sam. 13:39). The latter is not lacking the word “soul” as the one lacking in knowledge says,⁶⁰ is to be rendered, And David’s soul failed with longing. It should be noted that this interpretation was offered by Rashi, Radak, and the *Targum*. for the word *vatekhal* is transitive. The subject⁶¹, i.e., the one who caused David to fail in longing. is omitted,⁶² as it is in “whom she bore”⁶³ (Num. 26:59).⁶⁴

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Translated according to I.E.
⁶⁰ The “one lacking in knowledge” says, va-tekhal david
⁶¹ Of va-tekhal
⁶² According to I.E our text is to be interpreted, and caused David to fail in longing. See Radak on II Sam. 13:29. Radak quotes an interpretation which renders the verse, And the wife of David caused him to fail in longing. This apparently is the way I.E. interprets our verse (Krinsky).
⁶³ Translated according to I.E.
⁶⁴ Scripture omits the person who did the bearing.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:33:1)


**ורצוץ.** בחזקה:


AND CRUSHED. Powerfully so.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:34:1

ופי' **בשגעון.** שיראה גופו מלא שחין:


MAD. This explains *with madness* (v. 28.). He will see his body covered with boils (v. 35).⁶⁵

footnotes:
⁶⁵ And this will drive him mad.



### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:35:1)


**על הברכים ועל השוקים.** כי בהיות השחין שם קשה לסור:


IN THE KNEES, AND IN THE LEGS. For when the boils are upon the knees and the legs, [it is hard to remove them].


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:35:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:35:2)


**אשר לא תוכל להרפא.** בעבור היותם מחוץ לגוף:


WHEREOF THOU CANST NOT BE HEALED. Because they are on the surface of the body.⁶⁶

footnotes:
⁶⁶ According to I.E. a physician is permitted to heal only external wounds and not internal ones. Hence I.E. explains that Scripture here states that Israel will be struck by wounds that a physician will not be able to heal even though they are on the surface and may be dealt with by a physician (Krinsky).



### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:36:1)


**ועבדת שם.** ולא יועיל לך רק תהיה לשמה כל רואיך ישומו:


AND THERE SHALT THOU SERVE. And it shall not avail you but you shall become an astonishment. That is, all who see you shall be astonished.⁶⁷

footnotes:
⁶⁷ All who see Israel will be shocked at what happened to them. Rashi interprets similarly.



### Verse 37

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:37:1)


**למשל.** באוזן:


A PROVERB. In the ear.⁶⁸

footnotes:
⁶⁸ You will serve as an example illustrating a point.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:37:2)


**ולשנינה.** בפה מגזר' ושננתם:


AND A BYWORD. In the mouth.⁶⁹ *Sheninah* (byword) is related to the word *ve-shinnantam* (and thou shalt teach them diligently) (Deut. 6:7).

footnotes:
⁶⁹ People will speak about you.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:37:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:37:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:37:3)


**ינהגך ה'.** בעונך וקודם זה יבא עליך שנת רעב ואתה עודך בארצך:


SHALL LEAD THEE. Because of your sins. However, before this, a year of hunger shall come upon you when you are still in your land.⁷⁰

footnotes:
⁷⁰ In other words, verse 38 which follows does not speak of the exile but rather of what will precede the exile.



### Verse 38

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:38:1)


**יחסלנו.** יכריתנו והוא מן חסיל מין ממיני הארבה:


SHALL CONSUME THEM. *Yachselennu* (shall consume them) means shall destroy them. *Yachselennu* comes from *chasil*, which is the name of a type of grasshopper.⁷¹ means shall be “grasshoppered.”

footnotes:
⁷¹ Thus yachselennu



### Verse 39

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:39:1)


**תטע.** תיגע בנטיע' ובעבוד':


PLANT. You shall toil in planting and in the vineyards.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:39:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:39:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:39:2)


**ולא תשת'.** בשעת הבציר:


BUT THOU SHALT NEITHER DRINK OF THE WINE. At the time of the harvest.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:39:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:39:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:39:3)


**ולא תאגר.** תקבץ והוא פעל כמו אגרה בקציר:


NOR GATHER THE GRAPES. *Te’egor* (gather the grapes) means you will gather. It is a verb. *Te’egor* is similar to *agerah* (gathereth) in *And gathereth her food in the harvest* (Prov. 6:8).



### Verse 40

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:40:1)


**לא תסוך.** כמשפט וסוך לא סכתי. וזה הפך ושמן על ראשך:


BUT THOU SHALT NOT ANNOINT THYSELF WITH THE OIL. As is the practice.⁷² Compare, *neither did I anoint myself at all* (Dan. 10:3). This is the reverse of *And let thy head lack no oil* (Eccles. 9:8).⁷³

footnotes:
⁷² It was the practice of people to anoint themselves.
⁷³ Eccles. 9:8 records the normal practice. Dan. 10:3 records the abnormal practice.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:40:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:40:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:40:2)


**כי ישל.** מבנין נפעל:


SHALL DROP OFF. *Yishal* (drop off) is a *nifal*.⁷⁴.

footnotes:
⁷⁴ From the root nun, shin, lamed



### Verse 42

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:42:1)


**יירש.** מהכבד הדגוש והטעם כמו להוריש גוים:


SHALL…POSSESS. *Yeyaresh* (shall possess) is a *pi’el. Yeyaresh* is similar to *le-horish* (to drive out) in *to drive out nations* (Num. 4:38).⁷⁵

footnotes:
⁷⁵ In other words, it means to drive out or to destroy. The word might be confused with a similar word meaning to inherit. Hence I.E.’s comment.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:42:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:42:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:42:2)


**צלצל.** מלה מרובע' אין לו ריע ופירושו כפי מקומו:


THE LOCUST. *Tzelatzal* (locust) is a quadriliteral. It has no neighbor.⁷⁶ Its meaning is according to its place.⁷⁷

footnotes:
⁷⁶ The word does not appear again in Scripture.
⁷⁷ Its meaning can be ascertained from its context.



### Verse 43

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:43:1)


**מעלה מעלה.** פעמים שאין למעלה ממנו או תמיד וכן מטה מטה הטעם מאד מאד:


HIGHER AND HIGHER. The word is repeated to indicate a height over which no one else towers. On the other hand it may indicate permanency.⁷⁸ *Lower and lower* is similar. Its meaning is very low.

footnotes:
⁷⁸ The stranger shall be permanently over you.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:44:1)


**הוא יהיה לראש.** כטעם ועבד לוה לאיש מלוה:


HE SHALL BE THE HEAD. As in *And the borrower is servant to the lender* (Prov. 22:7).

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:46:1)


**לאות.** מהשם שתזכור כי מרדת בו ולא בך לבדך כי גם בזרעך כטעם אבותינו חטאו ואינם וכבר פירשתיו:


FOR A SIGN. From God, so that you remember that you rebelled against Him. It will not only be a sign in you but also in your offspring, as in *Our fathers have sinned, and are not* (Lam. 5:7).⁷⁹. I have previously explained this.⁸⁰

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:47:1)

BY REASON OF THE ABUNDANCE OF ALL THINGS. Which you desired,⁸¹ or all your needs.⁸²

⁸¹ You had everything that you desired.

### Verse 49

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:49:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:49:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:49:1)


**ידאה.** יעוף וכן וידא על כנפי רוח:


SWOOPETH DOWN. *Yideh* (swoopeth down) means flies. Compare, *va-yede* (he did swoop down) in *Yea, He did swoop down upon the wings of the wind* (Ps. 18:11).



### Verse 53

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:53:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:53:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:53:1)


**ואכלת פרי בטנך.** במצור:


AND THOU SHALT EAT THE FRUIT OF THINE OWN BODY. During the siege.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:53:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:53:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:53:2)


**יציק לך.** כל אחד מאויבך או הוא כן בעבור היותו בסוף:


SHALL STRAITEN THEE. Each one of your enemies.⁸³ (shall straiten) is in the singular. *Oyevekha* (thine enemies) is in the plural. According to I.E. when a verb in the singular governs a noun in the plural, the verb refers to each one within the plural. Or the word *oyevekha* is so vocalized⁸⁴ rather than *oyivkha*. because it comes at the end of a verse.⁸⁵ is really a singular. Our text should thus be rendered, wherewith thine enemy shall straiten thee.

footnotes:
⁸³ Yatzik
⁸⁴ The word reads oyevekha
⁸⁵ In other words, oyevekha



### Verse 54

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:54:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:54:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:54:1)


**אשר** יותיר. האויב או יותירם בהחבא וכל זה בעבור המצור:


WHOM HE HATH REMAINING. This refers to the enemy.⁸⁶ literally means he will leave over. According to this interpretation the verse reads, which the enemy will leave alive. Or it means which he will leave over by hiding them.⁸⁷ refers to the man mentioned in the opening of the verse. Our verse should be interpreted, which he will save by hiding them. All of this will he due to the siege.

footnotes:
⁸⁶ Yotir
⁸⁷ According to this interpretation yotir



### Verse 55

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:55:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:55:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:55:1)


**בכל שעריך.** בכל מקום:


IN ALL THY GATES. In any place.⁸⁸ means in any place in your country.

### Verse 57

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:57:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:57:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:57:1)
