AND AGAINST HER AFTERBIRTH. *Shilyah* (afterbirth) refers to the place where the child dwells while in the mother’s belly.⁸⁹

footnotes:
⁸⁹ It refers to the placenta.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:57:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:57:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:57:2)


**מבין רגליה.** כנוי כמו ולא עשה את רגליו:


FROM BETWEEN HER FEET. This is a euphemism.⁹⁰ Compare, *and he had neither dressed his feet* (II Sam. 19:25).⁹¹ is, he did not shave his pubic hair. Also see Talmud, *Yevamot* 103a.

footnotes:
⁹⁰ For the vagina.
⁹¹ Feet refers to the genital area. According to Rashi on II Sam. 19:25, the meaning of and he had neither dressed his feet



### Verse 59

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:59:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:59:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:59:1)


**מכתך.** לשון יחיד עם רבים מורכבת וכן ועדותי זו אלמדם:


THY PLAGUES. *Makkotekha* (thy plagues) is a compound of a singular⁹² and the plural.⁹³ is a plural. We would thus expect a *yod* to follow the *tav* and the *tav* to be vocalized with a *segol* in *makkotekha*. However, the *tav* in *makkotekha* is vocalized with a *sheva*, and *makkotekha* has no *yod* in it. The latter is the spelling and vocalization of the singular. Compare, the word *edoti* (my testimony)⁹⁴ is a plural. Thus the word should read *edotai*, for “i” is a singular suffix. Thus *edoti* is a compound of a plural with a singular pronominal suffix. in *And My testimony that I shall teach them* (Ps. 132:12).

footnotes:
⁹² Suffix.
⁹³ A noun in the plural. Makkot
⁹⁴ Edot


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:59:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:59:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:59:2)


טעם **והפלא.** להיות דבר מופלא שלא נראה ולא נשמע כמוהו:


The meaning of *ve-hifla* (will make wonderful) is, it will be an extraordinary thing, for nothing like it was ever seen or heard of.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:59:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:59:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:59:3)


**ונאמנים.** שלא יסורו:


OF LONG CONTINUANCE.⁹⁵. Literally, and faithful. They will not be removed.⁹⁶

footnotes:
⁹⁵ Hebrew, ve-ne’emanim
⁹⁶ In other words, “faithful” means they will do what they are supposed to do.



### Verse 61

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:61:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:61:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:61:1)


**יעלם ה'.** מגזרת העלה ואם הוא חטוף וי״‎א כמו יביאם לשון ארמי:


WILL THE LORD BRING UPON. *Yalem* (will bring) is related to the word *he’elah* (brought up)⁹⁷, and means bring up. (Jer. 16:15) even though it⁹⁸ is vocalized with a *sheva. He’elah* is vocalized with a *chataf segol*. Since both words are in the *hifil* we would expect them to be vocalized similarly. Hence I.E.’s comment. is vocalized with a *sheva*. Others say that *yalem* means will bring.⁹⁹ It is an Aramaic word.¹⁰⁰ in Aramaic means to enter. See Dan. 2:16.

footnotes:
⁹⁷ It comes from the root ayin, lamed, heh
⁹⁸ Yalem
⁹⁹ Rather than bring up. There is not much difference in practical meaning between the two interpretations. They only differ as to the etymology of the word.
¹⁰⁰ The word al



### Verse 63

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:63:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:63:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:63:1)


וטעם **כאשר שש.** שלא תחשוב כי יזיק לשם או יתאבל כטעם אם צדקת מה תתן לו:


AS THE LORD REJOICED. You should not think that this will harm God or that God will mourn.¹⁰¹ Its meaning is similar to¹⁰² *If thou he righteous, what givest thou to him* (Job 35:7).

footnotes:
¹⁰¹ For what happened to you.
¹⁰² The import of our verse is similar to Job 35:7.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:63:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:63:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:63:2)


**ונסחתם.** מבנין נפעל והוא חסר נו״‎ן. מגזרת יסח ה':


AND YE SHALL BE PLUCKED. *Ve-nissachtem* (and ye shall be plucked) is a *nifal*. It is lacking a *nun*.¹⁰³ is dropped. Thus the word reads *nissachtem* rather than *ninsachtem*. It is related to the word *yissach* (will pluck) in *The Lord will pluck up* (Prov. 15:25).

footnotes:
¹⁰³ The root nun



### Verse 66

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:66:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:66:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:66:1)


**תלואים.** באל״‎ף תחת ה״‎ח השורש:


SHALL HANG. The word *telu’im* (shall hang) has an *alef* in place of the stem *heh*.¹⁰⁴ comes from the root *tav, lamed, heh*. However, *telu’im* is spelled with an *alef*. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
¹⁰⁴ Telu’im



### Verse 68

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 28:68:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:68:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:68:1)


**והתמכרתם.** אינו כמו ונמכרתם רק פירושו אתם תבקשו למכור עצמכם אנשים ונשים ואין קונה והנה זאת הברית במצות השם בערבות מואב:


AND…YE SHALL SELL YOURSELVES. *Ve-hitmakkartem* (and ye shall sell yourselves) does not mean and you will be sold.¹⁰⁵, not in the *nifal*. (Cf. *nimkartem* in Is. 50:1.) Furthermore, Scripture goes on to say, *and no man shall buy you*. It means you, both men and women, will seek to sell yourselves and no man shall buy you. Observe, this covenant was made by the command of God in the plains of Moab.¹⁰⁶

footnotes:
¹⁰⁵ By others, for the word is in the hitpa’el
¹⁰⁶ I.E.’s paraphrase of verse 69.



## Chapter 29


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:1:1)


ו**יקרא משה אל כל ישראל.** לכרות הברית על כן אחריה אתם נצבים היום:


AND MOSES CALLED UNTO ALL ISRAEL. To establish a covenant. Scripture therefore reads afterwards, *Ye are standing this day* (v. 9).¹ (v. 11).

footnotes:
¹ And goes on to say, that thou shouldest enter into the covenant of the Lord


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:1:2)


וטעם **אתם ראיתם.** ואחר כך ואולך אתכם ואחר כן ויצא עוג כי יש מהם שראו האותות שעשה השם במצרים והם והבאים אחריהם ראו מופת המן שלא אכלו לחם זולתו ואלה הבנים ראו כי השם עזרם והכו שני המלכים:


The reason Scripture reads *Ye have seen* and then *And I have led* (v. 4) and then *And Og…came out* (v. 6) is that some of the Israelites saw the signs that God showed in Egypt,²was directed to this group. and they and those who came after them³ saw the miracle of the manna,⁴…was directed to this group. for they ate no food aside from the manna. And these sons⁵ saw that God helped them and they smote two kings (v. 6).

footnotes:
² You have seen…
³ The first generation born in the wilderness.
⁴ And I have led
⁵ The second generation born in the wilderness.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:3:1)


וטעם **לב לדעת.** בעבור שנסו השם עשר פעמים ולא הזכירו האותות שראו:


[BUT THE LORD HATH NOT GIVEN YOU A HEART TO KNOW.] Scripture reads a *heart to know* because they tested God ten times⁶ and did not make mention of the signs which they witnessed.

footnotes:
⁶ See Num. 14:22.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:3:2)


וטעם **נתן.** כי מאתו העלילה הראשונה ועוד כי ראו שלא הוצרכו ללחם ויין:


Scripture reads *given* because God is the first cause.⁷. Hence I.E. points out that Scripture ascribes man’s achievements to God because God is the first cause and in that sense can be said to give man *a heart to know*. Furthermore,⁸ they saw that they had no need for bread or wine.⁹

footnotes:
⁷ Man has free will and thus must develop a “knowing heart” on his own. However, our verse implies that it is God who gives man a heart to know
⁸ Israel is obligated to observe the commandments.
⁹ In addition to the miracles which Israel witnessed in Egypt, they experienced the miracle of living in the wilderness for forty years without bread or wine (Meijler). In other words, our verse (and the one before it) continues the theme of verse 1.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:4:1)


וטעם **לא בלו.** פירשתיו. ומכת סיחון ועוג שהיה קרוב על כן אתם חייבים לשמור הברית:


[ARE NOT WAXEN OLD.] I have previously explained this.¹⁰ Sihon and Og were recently¹¹ defeated.¹² You are therefore¹³ obligated to keep the covenant.

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:8:1)


**למען תשכילו.** כמו תצליחו והוא פעל יוצא לב' פעולים והעד תעשון כמו ויהי דוד לכל דרכיו משכיל:


THAT YE MAY…PROSPER. *Taskilu* means prosper. It is a *hifil*¹⁴ as the word *ta’asun*¹⁵ is the object of *taskilu*. Hence the meaning of *le-ma’an taskilu et kol asher ta’asun* is that *ye may make to prosper all that ye do*. (all that ye do) proves. Compare, *maskil* (great success) in *And David had great success in all his ways* (I Sam. 18:14).¹⁶ means to prosper. The root of *maskil* (*sin, kaf, lamed*) means intelligence. Hence I.E.’s comments.

¹⁵ Ta’asun
¹⁶ We thus see that the word maskil



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:9:1)


**אתם נצבים לפני ה' אלהיכם.** סביבו' הארון:


YE ARE STANDING THIS DAY. Around the ark.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:9:2)


**ראשיכם.** כמו האלה' שרה אמו והנבוא' עודד הנביא וכן הוא ראשיכ' שהם ראשי שבטיכ' כמו ונירם. והטעם כי הנשיאי' סמוכים ואחריה' הזקני' ואחריה' השוטרים ואחריה' כל איש ישראל הזכרי' ואחריה' הטף בזכרי' ואחריה' הנשים ואחריה' הגרים:


YOUR HEADS. *Roshekhem* (your heads) is similar *to ha**ohelah* (the tent) in *into the tent Sarah his mother* (Gen. 24:67)¹⁷ is short for into the tent, the tent of Sarah his mother. Similarly, *your heads* is short for your heads the heads of. See I.E. on Gen. 24:67 (Vol. 1, p. 243). and *even the prophecy, Odad the prophet*¹⁸ (II Chron. 15:8).¹⁹ is short for even the prophecy, the prophecy of Odad the prophet. See I.E. on Gen. 24:67 (Vol. 1, p. 243). *Roshekhem shivtekhem* (your heads, your tribes) is to be interpreted as your heads, who are the heads of your tribes.²⁰ is short for *roshekhem roshe shivtekhem*. *Roshekhem* (your heads) is like the word *va-niram* (we have shot at them) (Num. 21:30).²¹ (we have shot at them, Heshbon is destroyed). According to I.E. this is to be interpreted as And their king, the king of Heshbon was destroyed. See I.E. on Num. 21:30 (Vol. 4, p. 175). The word is to be so interpreted for the princes²² were closest to the ark.²³ After them came the elders, followed by the officers, after whom came all the men of Israel, that is, the males, followed by the male little ones, after whom came the women, followed by the strangers.

footnotes:
¹⁷ Translated literally. According to I. E. into the tent Sarah his mother
¹⁸ Translated literally.
¹⁹ According to I.E. even the prophecy, Odad the prophet
²⁰ Roshekhem shivtekhem
²¹ Num. 21:30 reads, va-niram avad cheshbon
²² The heads of the tribes.
²³ According to I.E. our verse records the hierarchical arrangement of the Israelites before the ark, when the covenant was made. If we take our verse literally the word “tribes” breaks up the arrangement. Hence I.E. insists that our verse reads, your heads, the heads of your tribes.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:11:1)


**לעברך.** שם הפועל. כטעם ויעברו בין בתריו. והנה מלת עבר בחסרון בי״‎ת להפך:


THAT THOU SHOULDEST ENTER INTO THE COVENANT. *Le-ovrekha* (that thou shouldest enter) is an infinitive.²⁴ It is like *va-ya’avru* (and passed) in *and passed between the parts thereof* (Jer. 34:18).²⁵ (that thou shouldest enter) means that you should pass, i.e., that you should enter the covenant, for they used to cut an animal in half when making a covenant and walk between the halves. Observe, the word *avar* (passed) minus the *bet*²⁶ has the opposite meaning.²⁷ preceding *brit* means to violate the covenant. Hence our verse reads, *le-ovrekha bi-verit*. Had our verse read, *le-ovrekha berit*, it would have meant that you should violate the covenant.

footnotes:
²⁴ With the pronominal suffix.
²⁵ In other words, le-ovrekha
²⁶ Following it.
²⁷ The word avar



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:13:1)


**ולא אתכם לבדכם.** רק עמכם ועם הבאים אחריכם שהם בניכם ובני בניכם:


NEITHER WITH YOU ONLY. But with you and with those who come after you, namely, your sons and your sons’ sons.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:14:1)


נו״‎ן **ישנו.** נוסף:


[BUT WITH HIM THAT STANDETH HERE WITH US THIS DAY.] The nun of *yeshno* (that standth here) is superfluous.²⁸ is the word *yesh* plus the third person pronominal suffix. That suffix is a *vav*. Thus we would expect *yesho* rather than *yeshno*. Hence I.E.’s comment (Krinsky).

footnotes:
²⁸ Yeshno


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:14:2)


**ואת אשר איננו.** עם אשר איננו רק יבא אחרינו ואינו כטעם האומרים כי רוחות הבאים היו שם:


AND ALSO WITH HIM THAT IS NOT HERE WITH US. *Ve’et asher ennnu* (and also with him that is not) means with him that is not here with us²⁹ means and with. This is not the usual meaning of *et*. Hence I.E.’s comment. See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 27): *“Et* means the very thing…However, sometimes…*et* is used in place of with.” but will come after us. It is not to be interpreted as those who say that the spirits of the coming generations were there.³⁰. See *Tanchuma, Nitzavim: The souls were there, but the bodies* [these souls were to enter] *had not yet been created*.

²⁹ Here with us today. In other words, ve’et
³⁰ This is the opinion of some midrashim

**כי אתם ידעתם.** יש מכם שרא' תועבת מצרים והטעם אלהיהם וכלכם ראיתם תועבו' הגוים:


FOR YE KNOW. There are among you some who witnessed the abominations of Egypt, namely, their gods. Additionally, all of you have seen the abominations of the nations.

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:17:1)


וטעם **פרה ראש ולענה.** כי הראש מזיק השלמים והוא מהחליי' המדביקים:


[A ROOT THAT BEARETH GALL AND WORMWOOD.] For gall injures the healthy.³¹ It is a disease that is infectious.³²

³¹ Hence it symbolizes idolatry. Even a spiritually healthy person is injured by idolatry.

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:1)


וטעם **את האלה הזאת.** ארור האיש אשר יעשה פסל ומסכה:


THIS CURSE. This curse refers to *Cursed be the man that maketh a graven or molten image* (Deut. 27:15).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:2)


**והתברך בלבבו.** אמר רבי יהודה הלוי ז״‎ל כי והתברך הוא כמשמעו כאשר ישמע הקלל' יתברך בלבבו והוא אומר שלום יהיה לי. כאומר בשמעו החרם חוץ ממני:


THAT HE BLESS HIMSELF IN HIS HEART. Rabbi Judah Ha-Levi, of blessed memory, says *that he bless himself* is to be taken according to its plain meaning. When he hears the curse he will bless himself in his heart and say I shall have peace. In other words, when he hears the ban³³ he will say it will not fall on me but upon someone else.

footnotes:
³³ The curse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:3)


**למען ספות.** אמר רבי יהודה הלוי נ״‎ע ל' כריתה כמו האף תספ'. כלומר שיחשוב המתברך לבטל דברי הצדיק שהוא כנוי במלת רוה עם דבר הצמא' והנה כנה הצדיק ברוה. כמו כעץ שתול על מים. והרשע בצמאה כמו כערער בערבה. ויחשוב כי גם ברכתו בלבו תפסיק הקללה ויפה פיר' לולא שמלת רוה תואר השם והיה ראוי להיות רווי את הצמא. ויאמר רבי יונה המדקדק כי טעמו בעבור שיראה שיכלה התם עם הרשע על כן יחשוב בלבו רע ואין זה נכון בעבור שלום יהיה לי. ולפי דעתי שמלת ספות לשון תוספת כמו ספות חטאת. והטעם שלום יהיה לי אע״‎פ שאלך בשרירות לבי כי בצדקת הצדיקים אחיה. כי הם רבים ואני יחיד חוטא. על כן כתוב אחריו לא יאבה ה' סלוח לו כי אז והטעם מיד יכרת גם זה הפסוק לעד על פירושי במלת ונסלח לו. והנה ספות כי הרוה תוסיף על הצמאה ומשל הצדיק ברוה כטעם כעץ שתול על פלגי מים והרשע בצמאה כטעם כערער בערבה והעד שכתוב בתחלה שרש פורה ראש ולענה והוא במקום צמא:


THAT…BE SWEPT AWAY. Rabbi Judah Ha-Levi, who rests in paradise, says that the word *sefot* (swept away) means to extirpate. Compare, *tispeh*³⁴. [thou (wilt) sweep away] in *Wilt Thou indeed sweep away* (Gen. 18:23). In other words, the one who blesses himself³⁵ wants to nullify the words of the righteous,³⁶ who are metaphorically called “the watered,” with the words of “the dry.” Look, the righteous are called watered. Compare, *For he shall be like a tree planted by the streams of water* (Jer. 17:8). The wicked are called dry. Compare, *For he shall be like a tamarisk in the desert* (*Ibid.*, v. 6). The wicked person thinks that the blessing which he utters in his heart will also stop the curse. He explained well were it not that the word *ravah* (well watered) is an adjective.³⁷ (dry). The point is that Rabbi Judah’s interpretation requires that nouns, not adjectives, be employed in our verse, for according to his interpretation the verse speaks of people. Filwarg argues that Rabbi Judah’s interpretation requires a noun because the dry and the watered are metaphors for curses and blessing, which are nouns. He interprets in this manner because later on I.E. himself interprets the dry and the watered as referring to people. The problem with Filwarg’s interpretation is that I.E. quotes Rabbi Judah as saying, “The righteous are called watered…The wicked are called dry.” Our text should have read, *ravi*³⁸ *et ha-tzama*³⁹ (the well watered…with the dry). Rabbi Jonah the grammarian⁴⁰ says that it means⁴¹. because the corrupt sees the righteous⁴² destroyed along with the wicked⁴³ he therefore thinks evil in his heart.⁴⁴. The wicked person says I might as well be wicked, for both the righteous and the wicked suffer one fate. They are both swept away. However, this is incorrect because Scripture reads, *I shall have peace*.⁴⁵ implies that he says something positive to himself. However, there is nothing positive in saying the righteous and I will be swept away. In my opinion the word *sefot* (swept) means to add. Compare, *sefot chattat* (add sin) (Is. 30:1). The added sin is the wicked person’s statement, I shall have peace even though I walk in the stubbornness of my heart. I will live because of the righteousness of the righteous, for the pious are many and I am an individual sinner.⁴⁶. A nation is judged by the majority of its people. The wicked believe that the merit of the majority, who are righteous, will protect the sinners, who are a minority. Scripture therefore afterwards reads, *The Lord will not be willing to pardon him*⁴⁷. *but then…* (v. 19). The meaning of the latter is he shall immediately be destroyed. This verse is also proof of my interpretation of *and he shall be forgiven*⁴⁸. According to I.E. the word *salach* means to delay punishment. See I.E. on Num. 14:19, “Selach refers to being slow to anger.” (Num. 15:28). The meaning of *sefot* is that the well watered will add to the dry. Scripture compares the righteous to the well watered, as in *And he shall be like a tree planted by streams of water* (Ps. 1:3). It compares the wicked to the dry. Compare, *For he shall be like a tamarisk in the desert* (Jer. 17: 6). The fact that Scripture at first reads, *a root that beareth gall and wormwood* (v. 17), is proof of this.⁴⁹ It is in a dry place.⁵⁰ grows in parched land. Hence the wicked person is called the dry (Meijler, Krinsky). I.E.’s interpretation presents a problem. He interprets “the watered and the dry” as referring to people. However, earlier I.E. rejects Rabbi Judah Ha-Levi’s interpretation for this very reason. As noted above, Filwarg attempts to justify I.E.’s position. However, his interpretation is forced.

³⁵ The wicked.
³⁶ Moses.
³⁷ As is tzeme’ah
³⁸ A noun.
³⁹ A noun.
⁴⁰ Rabbi Jonah ibn Janach.
⁴¹ The meaning of that he blesses himself in his heart…that the watered are swept away with the dry
⁴² The watered.
⁴³ The dry.
⁴⁴ Rabbi Jonah’s interpretation of that he bless himself in his heart
⁴⁵ That he blesses himself in his heart
⁴⁶ I.E.’s interpretation of the watered be swept away with the dry
⁴⁷ Hebrew, selo’ach lo
⁴⁸ Hebrew, ve-nislach lo
⁴⁹ That dry refers to the wicked.
⁵⁰ The root that beareth gall and wormwood



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:19:1)


**יעשן אף.** ישרפנו אש האף עד שיראה העשן והנה אם הוא יחיד ימות מיד ואם היא משפחה ורבצה בו כל האלה והטעם שתרבץ האלה במשפחה עד שימח' שמה ואם הוא שבט והבדילו ה' לרעה וכמוהו והיה לה' והעלתיהו עולה כאשר פירשתיו:


THE ANGER OF THE LORD…SHALL BE KINDLED.⁵¹ (literally, shall smoke). Our clause literally reads, the anger of the Lord…shall smoke. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. The fire of God’s anger shall bum him to the point where the smoke will be visible. Observe, if the sinner be an individual he will die immediately, and if it is a family *all the curse…shall lie upon him*. This means that the curse shall lie upon the family until its name is eradicated. If the sinner be a tribe, *the Lord shall separate him unto evil* (v. 20).⁵² refers to an individual; *and all the curse…shall lie upon him* refers to a family; *and the Lord shall separate him unto evil* refers to a tribe. Similarly *And I will offer it up for a burnt-offering* (Jud. 11:31), as I have explained.⁵³ there has the meaning of or. It appears that I.E. interpreted similarly.

footnotes:
⁵¹ Hebrew, yeshan
⁵² Thus The anger of the Lord…shall be kindled against that man
⁵³ We no longer have I.E.’s commentary on Judges. However, Radak in his comments on Judges notes that the vav



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:21:1)


**ואמר הדור האחרון.** שהם בניכם גם הנכרים הרואים את מכת הארץ:


AND THE GENERATION TO COME. Your children. Also the foreigners who see the plagues of the land.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:22:1)


**גפרית.** תפלה לשם שתהי' כל ארצה שרופה בגפרית ומלח כמהפכת המקומות הנזכרים ויש מפרשים אומרים כי ואמרו כל הגוים שנוי בעבור שארכו הדברים כמשפט הלשון ואמרו הקדמונים:


BRIMSTONE. A prayer to God that all the land should be burnt in brimstone and salt like the overthrow of the places that are mentioned.⁵⁴ Some explain that *even all the nations shall say* (v. 23) repeats what was stated earlier⁵⁵ because Scripture went off on a tangent,⁵⁶ as is the style of the language.⁵⁷ [24.] THEN MEN SHALL SAY. The earlier inhabitants.⁵⁸

footnotes:
⁵⁴ Our verse records the prayer that will be offered by coming generations and by the foreigner. When they see what God did to the land, they too will curse it.
⁵⁵ In verse 21. Our verse repeats parts of verse 21 because it picks up its trend of thought.
⁵⁶ In verse 22. In other words, verse 23 is a continuation of verse 21.
⁵⁷ To go off on a tangent and then return to the original point.
⁵⁸ The earlier inhabitants shall respond to their children and to the foreigner.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:22:2)


וטעם **אלהי אבותם** שעזבו שידעו הם ואבותם ועבדו אשר לא ידעו:


[THE GOD OF THEIR FATHERS.] They forsook the God that they and their fathers knew and they served gods whom they did not know.

AND THAT HE HAD NOT ALLOTTED UNTO THEM. I have previously explained this.⁵⁹ (Deut. 4:19).

### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:27:1)


**ויתשם.** בחסרון נו״‎ן לנתוש רק הוא מובלע כמשפט:


AND THE LORD ROOTED THEM OUT. *Va-yitteshem* (and rooted them out) is missing a nun.⁶⁰. Compare li-netosh (to root out) (Jer. 1:10). The *nun* is swallowed by the *dagesh*, as is the rule.⁶¹.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Its root is nun, tet, shin
⁶¹ For words whose first root letter is a nun

[AS IS THIS DAY.] These are the words of the respondents.⁶²

### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:1)


**הנסתרות.** הטעם מי שיעבוד עבודת כוכבים בסתר:


THE SECRET THINGS. The reference is to one who serves idols in secret.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 29:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:2)


**לה' אלהינו** והטעם כי משפטו ביד השם והוא יפרע ממנו ואם היתה בגלוי חיוב לנו ולבנינו לעשות ככתוב בתורה. וי״‎א כי הנסתרות והנגלות הם לה' ואין לנו כי אם לעשות דברי התורה וזה הפי' בלא ראש ורגל ומה טעם יש להזכיר כן במקום הזה ובעבור שאמר וישליכם אל ארץ אחרת אמר כי אם תשוב אל ה' אע״‎פ שאתה חוצה לארץ השם ישיב שבותך ועל דעת רבי יהודה המדקדק הראשון כי הוא יניח. והטעם ימצא מנוח' לנשבים ויתן להם רחמים ואחר כן ישוב ויקבץ:


BELONG UNTO THE LORD OUR GOD. Its meaning is that his punishment is in the hands of God, and the Lord will exact punishment from him. However, if the sin was committed in public there is an obligation on us and our children to do in accordance with that which is written in the Torah. Others say⁶³ that the secret and the revealed things belong to God. It is incumbent upon us only to observe the words of the Torah. Now this interpretation has no head or foot.⁶⁴ What reason is there to mention this here?⁶⁵ Scripture states that if you *return unto the Lord* (Deut. 30:2), *God will turn thy captivity* (*Ibid.*, v. 3) even though you are outside of the land because it stated earlier, *and cast them into another land* (v. 27). According to Rabbi Judah⁶⁶ the first grammarian, the meaning of *ve-shav* (then shall turn) (Deut. 30:3) means God will give rest;⁶⁷ (*Ibid.*, v. 3) as God will give rest to your captivity. that is, he will give rest to the captives and be merciful to them, and after this He will return and gather.⁶⁸ in 30:3 as will He give rest, and the second *ve-shav* as and will He return.

footnotes:
⁶³ That the meaning of our verse is.
⁶⁴ It makes no sense and has no legs to stand on.
⁶⁵ It does not continue the thought of the previous verse.
⁶⁶ Rabbi Judah Chayuj.
⁶⁷ See I.E. on Num. 10:36 (Vol. 4, p. 75). Rabbi Judah renders God will turn thy captivity
⁶⁸ Your captivity. It should be noted that Rabbi Judah renders the first ve-shav



## Chapter 30


### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 30:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:4:1)


**בקצה השמים.** כנגד ארץ ישראל:


IN THE UTTERMOST PARTS OF HEAVEN. With regard to the Land of Israel.¹

footnotes:
¹ For a person living in the Land of Israel, the place of Israel’s exile appears to be at the uttermost parts of the heavens. However, in truth the heavens have no uttermost part for they are round.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 30:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:6:1)


**ומל ה' אלהיך.** כי לו נתכנו העלילות הראשונות. ואחר שאמר תשוב לשמוע בקולו הוא יעזרך וימול לבבך ולבב זרעך לא כמילת הבשר שאתה חייב למול אותו:


AND THE LORD THY GOD WILL CIRCUMCISE. For He is the first cause of all actions.² seems to indicate that man has no free will, for it implies that God will change man’s nature so that he will no longer sin. Hence I.E.’s comment. After saying, you shall return unto the Lord and listen to His voice,³ Moses says that unlike the circumcision of the flesh, which you are obligated to execute, God will help you and will circumcise your heart and the heart of your seed.

footnotes:
² I.E.’s paraphrase of I Sam. 2:3. In other words, God is the first cause who gave man the power to circumcise his heart. Since all things can be traced back to God, God can be said to “circumcise” man’s heart. And the Lord thy God will circumcise thy heart
³ I.E.’s paraphrase of the opening of verse 6.

וטעם **והותירך.** בעבור שיתן הקללות על השונאים:


[AND THE LORD THY GOD WILL MAKE THEE OVERABUNDANT.] Scripture states *ve-hotirekha*⁴ as He will permit you to remain. (make thee overabundant) because God will place curses upon the enemies [of Israel]⁵

⁵ Which shall destroy them. However, you shall survive.

**כאשר שש על אבותיך.** שנחלו הארץ כי עם הגולים ממנה ידבר:


AS HE REJOICED OVER THY FATHERS. Who inherited the land, for Scripture addresses those who were exiled from it.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 30:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:10:1)


**הכתובה בספר התורה הזה.** כי עיקר כל המצות שם ואם הם צריכים פירוש קבלה ולא תהיה שמורה עד שיהיה הלב שלם על כן בכל לבבך כי מצות השמירה עם הלב:


WHICH ARE WRITTEN IN THIS BOOK OF THE LAW. For the main part of all the commandments is there,⁶ although they need the explanation provided by tradition.⁷ They⁸ cannot be kept unless the heart is complete.⁹ Scripture therefore states, *with all thy heart*, for the commandment to keep means to keep with one’s heart.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁶ Hence Scripture mentions the written law and omits the oral law.
⁷ The oral tradition elaborates the laws which are found in the Torah.
⁸ The commandments.
⁹ Has total belief in God.
¹⁰ Belief must accompany observance. See I.E. on Ex. 31:18 (Vol. 2, p. 653).

TOO HARD. The word *niflet* (too hard) means concealed.¹¹ Or its meaning is, wondrous, that is, something that will cause you to grow weary while trying to accomplish it.¹²

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:13:1)


**מעבר לים.** פירוש רחוקה ויתכן שהוא הים הגדול שלא יוכל אדם להכנס ברחבו מפני חשכת המים:


BEYOND THE SEA. This explains *neither is it far off* (v. 11). It is possible that the reference is to the great sea¹³ whose width a person cannot enter¹⁴ because of the darkness of the water.¹⁵

¹⁴ That is, one cannot cross it.

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 30:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:14:1)


**בפיך ובלבבך.** שכל המצות עיקרם הלב ויש מהם זכר בפה לחזוק הלב ויש מעשה כדי שיזכור בפה:


IN THY MOUTH, AND IN THY HEART. For the heart is the core of the commandments.¹⁶ Some commandments require the uttering of statements which serve to reinforce the heart.¹⁷ to reinforce our belief. Others consist of deeds¹⁸. so that a person will utter the required statements.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁶ The commandments that pertain to the heart are the most important ones. Among the latter are the commandments to believe in God, to love God, etc.
¹⁷ We thus recite the shema
¹⁸ Such as donning tefilin
¹⁹ So that a person will remember to recall verbally the commandment tied to the ritual. However, not all commandments observed by deeds have this as their purpose. See I.E. on Ex. 31:18 (Vol. 2, p. 410) where he divides the positive commandments into three categories: those to be observed by the heart, those to be observed by speech, and those to be observed by action.

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 30:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:1)


**כי הוא חייך.** על דעת המפרשים שהוא שב אל קול השם ולפי דעתי שהוא השם כאשר אמר המשורר ה' ישעי ואלהי חסדי ומגיני וחרבי:


FOR THAT IS THY LIFE.²⁹. According to the commentaries this refers to the voice of God. I believe that the reference is to God,³⁰, which reads, for he is your life. Hence this interpretation. as the poet says,³¹ the Lord is my salvation;³². *The God of my mercy* (Ps. 59:11); *My shield* (Ps. 18:3); and my sword.³³.

footnotes:
²⁹ Hebrew, ki hu chayyyekha
³⁰ Literally, ki hu chayyyekha
³¹ We should not be surprised that the Torah refers to God by the term life. For Scripture terms God my sword, my shield, my mercy, my salvation.
³² Compare Ps. 18:47: The God of my salvation
³³ Compare, Deut. 33:29: The Lord is the sword of thy excellency



## Chapter 31


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:1:1)


**וילך.** הלך אל כל שבט ושבט להודיע שהוא מת שלא יפחדו וחזק לבם בדברי יהושע על כן כתוב אחריו ואתה תנחילנה אותם ולפי דעתי כי אז ברך השבטים ואם ברכותיהם מאוחרות במכתב:


AND MOSES WENT. Moses went to each tribe and tribe to inform them that he was about to die and that they should not fear.¹ He strengthened them² with his words to Joshua.³, etc.,(vs. 7,8). Scripture therefore afterwards reads, *and thou shalt cause them to inherit it* (v. 7). I believe that he then⁴ (v. 7). blessed the tribes.⁵ This is so even though the blessings are recorded later.⁶

footnotes:
¹ For they would not be left leaderless.
² He encouraged them.
³ Be strong and of good courage; for thou shalt go with this people into the land
⁴ After charging Joshua in the sight of all of Israel
⁵ While they were assembled together.
⁶ In Deut. 33.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:2:1

**לצאת ולבוא.** במלחמה והטעם כי אילו לא הייתי מת עתה אין בי יכולת להלחם ואין לכם צורך למי שיעזור אתכם כי השם ישמיד הגוים גם יהושע והעד מה שראיתם בעיניכם במלחמת סיחון ועוג:


GO OUT AND COME IN. In war.⁷ Even if I did not die now, I would not have the ability to wage war. You have no need for any person to help you, for God will destroy the nations.⁸ Joshua will do the same.⁹ (v. 3). What you saw with your eyes in the war of Sihon and Og is proof.¹⁰

footnotes:

⁸ I.E.’s paraphrase of the first part of verse 3.

AND UNTO ALL THE ELDERS OF ISRAEL. The Sanhedrin.¹⁴ Or its meaning is like *And Moses and the elders of Israel commanded* (Deut. 27:1).¹⁵ in teaching the Torah.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:10:1

AT THE END OF EVERY SEVEN YEARS. At the beginning of the year.¹⁶ (at the end) sometimes means at the beginning. See I.E. on Deut. 15:1. I.E. interprets thus because the seventh year was to be a year of Torah study. ### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:15:1)


**וירא ה' באוהל.** הידוע הטעם כי עמוד הענן היה על הפתח והשם היה נראה באהל וזהו הכבוד ודבור ואצונו זהו ויצו את יהושע בן נון במצו' השם יתברך על כן אמר אשר נשבעתי להם ואח״‎כ פרשת הנך שוכב עם אבותיך ואין מוקדם ומאוחר בתורה וכן פ' ויהי ככלות משה לכתוב דבקה עם ויכתוב משה את התורה הזאת:


AND THE LORD APPEARED IN THE TENT. The specified tent.²³ (tent) has a *heh ha-yedi’ah* prefixed to it. The meaning of our verse is that the pillar of cloud was at the door of the tent and God was seen inside the tent. This²⁴ is what the glory²⁵ refers to.²⁶ The statement *that I may give him a charge* (v. 14) refers to *And he*²⁷ *gave Joshua the son of Nun a charge* (v. 23) by the command of God, may He be blessed. Scripture therefore reads, *which I swore unto them* (*Ibid.*).²⁸ After this came the passage *Behold, thou art about to sleep with thy fathers* (v. 16).²⁹ There is no chronological order in the Torah. The passage *And it came to pass, when Moses had made an end of writing the words of this law* (v. 24) is connected to³⁰ *And Moses wrote this law* (v. 9).

footnotes:
²³ The tent of meeting mentioned in the previous verse. Hence the word ohel
²⁴ The pillar of cloud.
²⁵ The glory of God.
²⁶ When Scripture speaks of the glory of God appearing in the tent of meeting, the reference is to the pillar of cloud. See Ex. 16:10.
²⁷ Moses.
²⁸ Moses uses the term “I” because he was speaking for God by God’s command.
²⁹ According to I.E. the sequence of events was as follows: Verses 14,15; 23; 16-22; 9. The reason for the aforementioned is purely exegetical. It allows for a logical, sequential unfolding of events.
³⁰ It follows.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:1)


**וקם העם הזה.** לא יתכן היותו דבק עם אשר לפניו כי מה טעם העם הזה וזנה:


AND THIS PEOPLE WILL RISE UP. It is not possible for this to be connected to what precedes it, for what reason is there to say,³¹ *and this people will rise up, and go astray*.³²

footnotes:
³¹ At this point.
³² Our verse does not logically follow God’s telling Moses that He is going to charge Joshua. Hence verses 16-22 follow what is recorded in verse 23.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:2)


וטעם **וזנה.** לצאת במחשבתו מתחת רשותו:


The meaning of *ve-zanah* (and go astray) is, to leave God’s authority³³ in one’s thought.

footnotes:
³³ Literally, to leave God’s domain, to believe in another god.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:3)


**אחרי אלהי נכר הארץ.** ידענו כי השם אחד והשנוי יבוא מהמקבלים והשם לא ישנה מעשיו כי כולם בחכמה. ומעבודת השם לשמור כח הקבול כפי המקום על כן כתוב את משפט אלהי הארץ על כן אמר יעקב הסירו את אלהי הנכר והפך המקום הדבק בעריות שהם שאר. והמשכיל יבין:


AFTER THE FOREIGN GODS OF THE LAND. We know that God is one.³⁴ Change comes from the recepients.³⁵ God does not change His works,³⁶ for they are executed in wisdom. It is part of the service of God to harness one’s power to receive spiritual inspiration in accordance with the place.³⁷ Scripture therefore reads, *the manner of the God of the land* (II Kings 17:26).³⁸ Jacob therefore said,³⁹ *Put away the strange gods* (Gen. 35:2).⁴⁰ The place is unlike other places with regard to engaging in sexual intercourse with women whom Scripture deems as being of close flesh.⁴¹ The intelligent will understand.⁴²

footnotes:
³⁴ God is unchangeable. God remains the same at all times.
³⁵ Of Divine beneficence. According to I.E. praying to God does not change God’s mind. It changes the petitioner. The person changes his fate because he becomes a different person. Thus God’s decrees remain unchangeable.
³⁶ The laws of nature, or any of His actions.
³⁷ The Land of Israel. According to I.E. if a person follows a certain way of life, i.e., following God’s commandments, certain things will follow automatically. He also believed that God’s influence varies in accordance with the place. Hence certain laws have to be followed in the Land of Israel if the land is to maintain its sanctity and receive the amount of divine influence allotted to it. See I.E. on Gen. 4:14; 28:16. Also see Ex. 15:17.
³⁸ The reference is to the people whom the king of Assyria brought to Samaria. They wanted to learn the manner of the God of the land because they knew that if they wanted to remain in the Land of Israel, they would have to alter their ways to fit the religious requirements of the land.
³⁹ To his sons.
⁴⁰ According to I.E., Jacob told his sons, “Now that we are in the Land of Israel put away the images which you held on to while you were outside of the Land of Israel.”
⁴¹ A person is not allowed to marry a variety of close relatives. Among these are two sisters and an aunt. However, Jacob married two sisters and Amram married his aunt. According to I.E. they did so because they were outside of the Land of Israel. See I.E. on Lev. 18:26.
⁴² That the Land of Israel has special spiritual significance.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:16:4)


וטעם **בקרבו.** שב אל נכר כאילו אמר וזנה אחרי אלהי עם נכר שהם אלהי הארץ. והקרוב כי בקרבו שב אל הארץ כי נמצא לשון זכר כמו ולא נשא אותם הארץ. נעתם הארץ:


[AMONG THEM.] *Be-kirbo* (among them) refers to *foreign*.⁴³. I.E. does not believe that Scripture would use the term “among them” when referring to foreign gods, for such gods do not exist, and it is impossible to come among them. Hence his interpretation. Scripture, as it were, states, and go astray after the gods of a foreign people,⁴⁴ who are the gods of the land.⁴⁵ is short for “the foreign people of the gods of the land.” However, it appears that *be-kirbo* refers to the word *ha-aretz* (of the land),⁴⁶ should be translated as “in it” or “into the midst of it” and our verse rendered, and go astray after the foreign gods of the land into the midst of which they enter. for the word *eretz* is found in the masculine.⁴⁷ which is masculine, can refer to *eretz. Eretz* is usually feminine. Hence I.E.’s comment. Compare, *And the land* (ha-aretz) *was not able to bear* (nasa)⁴⁸ is masculine. *them* (Gen. 13:6); *the land* [is] *burnt up* (netam⁴⁹ is masculine. aretz) (Is. 9:18).

footnotes:
⁴³ That is, foreign people. Our verse reads, and go astray after the foreign gods of the land, whither they go to he among them
⁴⁴ The Canaanite nations.
⁴⁵ In other words, the foreign gods of the land
⁴⁶ In this case be-kirbo
⁴⁷ Hence the word be-kirbo
⁴⁸ Nasa
⁴⁹ Netam

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:18:1)


וטעם **הסתר אסתיר.** שאם יקראו אלי לא אענם והמשל כאדם שלא ירא' ולא ידע מה יעש'. וכן מנהג לשון לדבר בכפל ובעלי הדקדוק יבינו זה:


[AND I WILL SURELY HIDE.] The meaning of *And I will surely hide* is, if they call Me, I will not answer. Scripture compares God to a person who does not see or know what is happening. It is the custom of Scripture to employ two verbs.⁵² (I will surely hide). Literally, hide, I will hide. Those who know grammar understand this.⁵³

footnotes:
⁵² Our verse reads, haster astir
⁵³ That it is biblical style to employ an infinitive followed by a conjugated verb.

IN THAT THEY ARE TURNED. This,⁵⁴ as I previously explained,⁵⁵ is the great evil. There is no evil which tops it.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:19:4)


ומלת ולמדה גם שימה זרה כי המשפט להיות ה״‎א ולמדה קמוץ ותחת הדל״‎ת פת״‎ח קטן:


The words *ve-lammedah* (and teach thou it) and also *simah* (put it) are irregular, for grammatically the *heh* of *ve-lammedah* should have been vocalized with a *kamatz* and the *lamed* with a *segol*.⁵⁸ rather than *ve-lammedah*. Similarly *simah* should have read *simeha*.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ Our word should have read ve-lamdeha


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:19:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:19:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:19:5)


**לעד בבני ישראל.** דבק בטעם עם כי אביאנו. גם יתכן להיותו מובדל בעבור וענתה כטעם לא תענה ברעך כאילו השיר' עונה לאשר יאמרו למה מצאתנו כל זאת:


A WITNESS…AGAINST THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL. This meaning of our verse is tied to *For when I shall have brought them* (v. 20).⁵⁹ (v. 19), *For when I shall have brought them into the land…* The song that is being discussed in these verses is the song in the following chapter. It is also possible that our verse is independent,⁶⁰ because Scripture states, *ve-anetah* (that…shall testify) (v. 21).⁶¹(v. 20) *that this song shall testify before them* (v. 21). *Ve-anetah* (that…shall testify) is similar to *ta’aneh* (bear)⁶² does not mean will answer but rather will testify. in *thou shalt not bear…against thy neighbor* (Ex. 20: 13). The song, as it were, will testify before those who ask, “Why did all this happen to us?”⁶³

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Our verse and the next one form one unit. The verses are to be read as follows: Now…write ye this song…that this song may be a witness for Me against the children of Israel
⁶⁰ In other words, our verse is not tied to the verse that follows.
⁶¹ In this case, verse 20 opens a new thought and is tied to verse 21. The verses are to be read: For when I shall have brought them into the land…
⁶² That is, testify. In other words, ve-anetah
⁶³ The song will explain to all why what occurred came to pass.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:21:1)


**לפניו.** כמו נגדו והטעם שלא יזוז העד ממנו רק עמו יהיה תמיד:


BEFORE THEM. *Le-fanav* means before them. It means that the witness will not be removed from before them but shall always be with them.⁶⁴

footnotes:
⁶⁴ Israel shall always have a copy of the song with them.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:21:2)


**כי ידעתי את יצרו.** כי אילו לא הייתי יודע את העתידות כבר ידעתי מה שעשו עד היום הזה:


FOR I KNOW THEIR IMAGINATION. For even if I did not know the future,⁶⁵ I know what they have done until this day.⁶⁶

footnotes:
⁶⁵ Supernaturally.
⁶⁶ And on this basis can surmise how they will act in the future.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:22:1)


**ביום ההוא.** שלא איחר הדבר ויתכן שהיה יום מותו אחר מכתב דברי התורה כי השירה כתב משה עמה:


THE SAME DAY. Moses did not tarry in doing this thing.⁶⁷ It is possible that this took place on the day he died, which was after writing the words of the Torah, for Moses wrote the song with it.

footnotes:
⁶⁷ In committing the song to writing.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:24:1)


**עד תומם.** דבק עם דברי:


UNTIL THEY WERE FINISHED. This is connected to *the words*.⁶⁸

### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:25:1)


**ויצו משה את הלוים.** דרך קצרה כאילו אמר הלוים הנזכרים שהם הכהנים ולא כל הלוים כי אינם נוגעים בארון:


THAT MOSES COMMANDED THE LEVITES. Scripture is being brief.⁶⁹. Our verse, as it were, says, the earlier-mentioned Levites who are *kohanim*, but not all the Levites, for they do not touch the ark.⁷⁰.

⁶⁹ The Levites is short for the kohanim, the Levites
⁷⁰ I.E.’s interpretation of that bore the ark of the covenant of the Lord


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:25:2)


וטעם **נושאי ארון ברית ה'.** בצווי וכן רבים:


[THAT BORE THE ARK OF THE CONVENT OF THE LORD.] By command. There are many such instances.



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:26:1)


**והיה שם בך לעד.** עם כל ישראל ידבר והכהנים בכללם כאשר דבר לישראל. וטעמו הראובני והגדי לבדם כאשר פירשתי:


IT MAY BE THERE FOR A WITNESS AGAINST THEE. Moses speaks to all of Israel. The *kohanim* are included when he speaks to Israel. However, when Moses addressed the Reubenites and the Gadites he spoke to them alone, as I previously explained.⁷¹

footnotes:
⁷¹ See Deut. 3:18.



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:27:1)


**ואף כי אחרי מותי.** גם כי ידעתי אחרי מותי כי כן אמר לי השם וקם העם הזה אחר שתשכב עם אבותיך וזהו אחרי מותי:


AND HOW MUCH MORE AFTER MY DEATH. In addition, I know that after my death…⁷² (and how much more after my death) means, and how much more after my death will you rebel. for God told me, *and this people will rise up, and go astray* (v. 16) after you sleep with your fathers. This is what *after my death* refers to.⁷³

footnotes:
⁷² You will rebel. According to I.E., ve-af ki achare moti
⁷³ After my death you will rebel.



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 31:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:29:1)


וטעם **וקראת.** כמו וחטאת עמך ושם מפורש:


[BEFALL YOU.] The meaning of *ve-karata* (and…will befall you) is similar to *ve-chatat* (but the fault is) in *but the fault is in thine people* (Ex. 5:15).⁷⁴. It is explained there.⁷⁵

## Chapter 32


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:1:1)


**האזינו.** כבר פירשתי בספרי כי מלת האזינו מגזרת אזן כאילו אמר הטו אזן:


GIVE EAR. I have previously explained in my book¹ (Weiser). that the word *ha’azinu* (give ear) is related to the word *ozen* (ear). Scripture, as it were, says incline your ear.²

footnotes:
¹ The Moznayim
² I.e., pay attention.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:1:2)


וכבר הודעתיך שהגאון אמר כי טעם **השמים.** המלאכים וטעם **הארץ.** אנשי הארץ. או עדות הגשם היורד מן השמים והארץ תתן יבולה והנכון בעיני כי העיקר בעבור היותם עומדים לעולם וכן שמעו הרים גם אבן יהושע ושם כתוב כי היא שמעה וכבר רמזתי לך על נשמת האדם שהיא אמצעות בין הגבוהי' לשפלי' והיא תדמה הכל על צורת היכלה. ואף כי להבין גם היושבת בהיכל. על כן תגביה השפלי' ותשפיל הגבוהים:


I have previously informed you³ that the Gaon⁴ says that the meaning of *heavens* is the angels and the meaning of the *earth* is the people of the earth.⁵ On the other hand, its meaning might be that the rain that comes down from heaven and the fruit which the earth produces are called to bear witness.⁶. I believe that the correct interpretation is that Moses called heaven and earth to witness because they last forever.⁷ Similarly *Hear, O ye mountains* (Micah 6:2) and also Joshua’s stone.⁸ Scripture there writes that the stone heard. I have hinted to you that man’s soul is intermediary between the upper and lower worlds.⁹ The soul compares all things to its body.¹⁰ It certainly does so when speaking to other human beings.¹¹ It therefore elevates the low and lowers the high.¹² (Num. 13:29).” Also see I.E. on Ex. 19:20 (Vol. 2, pp. 388,389).

footnotes:
³ See I.E. on Deut. 3:24. However, I.E. does not there mention Rabbi Saadiah Gaon by name.
⁴ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁵ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon renders our verse: Give ear, O inhabitants of heaven, and I will speak, And let the people of the earth hear the words of my mouth.
⁶ See Rashi: “If Israel will act worthily, the witnesses will come and give them their reward…the ground will give its increase, the heavens will bestow its dew.” Rashi’s interpretation is based on the Sifre
⁷ In other words, heaven and earth do not refer to angels and people, or rain and the produce of the earth.
⁸ See Josh. 24:27: And Joshua said…‘Behold, this stone shall be a witness…for it hath heard all the words of the Lord…’
⁹ Man’s soul comes from the middle world. See I.E. on Ex. 19:20 (Vol. 2, p. 338). For the upper, middle, and lower worlds, see I.E. on Ex. 3:15 (Vol. 2, pp. 86-90).
¹⁰ Literally, its palace. The body serves as a “palace” for the soul. The mind explains reality by employing figures of speech based on the body. For example, the earth opened her mouth, etc.
¹¹ Literally, other inhabitants of the palace.
¹² See I.E. on Gen. 1:26 (Vol. 1, p. 45). “Now a human being cannot speak of things above or below without employing human terminology. Hence Scripture uses such terms as the mouth of the earth (Num. 16:30), the hand of the Jordan



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:1)


**יערף.** מגזרת יערף מזבחותם כטעם ירד:


SHALL DROP. The word *ya’arof* (shall drop) is related to the word *ya’arof* (he will break down) in *He will break down their altars* (Hos. 10:2). The meaning of *ya’arof* (shall drop) is will come down.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:2)


**לקחי.** מה שיקח אחד ממנו כטעם למוד וכן כי לקח טוב:


MY DOCTRINE. *Likchi* (My doctrine) refers to that which one will take away¹³ means took. The noun *lekech* thus means a taking. from him.¹⁴ It means a lesson. Compare, *lekech* (doctrine) in *For I give you a good doctrine* (Prov. 4:2).

footnotes:
¹³ The word lakach
¹⁴ Learn from Moses.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:3)


**כשעירם.** הנכון בעיני שהם דקים:


AS THE SMALL RAIN. I believe that *se’irim* (small rain) refers to light rain.¹⁵ (showers), which refers to a heavy rain.

footnotes:
¹⁵ For it stands in contrast to revivim


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:2:4)


**וכרביבים.** מגזרת רב ושניהם שמות התאר והעד כי עשב חזק מדשא. וי״‎א כי שעירים מגזרת שער ואחרים אמרו מגזרת סערת ה' ואם הוא בשי״‎ן וכן וישתער עליו כמו ויסתער עליו גם יתכן שאין לו אח במקרא והנה הטעם כפול כדרך כל הנבואות לחזוק ועיקר הטעם שהתפלל משה שיהיו דבריו כטל וכמטר שלא ישובו ריקם כי אם הרוו את הארץ כי כן כתוב והטע' שיכנסו דבריו בלבו' השומעי' כמעשה הגשם על הארץ להולידה ולהצמיחה:


AND AS THE SHOWERS. The word *revivim* (showers) is related to the word *rav* (numerous). They¹⁶ (small rain) and *revivim* (showers). are adjectives. The fact that *esev* (herb) is stronger than *deshe* (tender grass) is proof of this.¹⁷ (small rain) refers to light rain and *revivim* (showers) to a heavy rain, for Scripture speaks of *se’irim* falling on *deshe* and *revivim* on *esev*. Some say that *se’irim* (small rain) is related to *sa’ar* (hair).¹⁸ Others say that it is related to the word *sa’arat* (storm of)¹⁹ (small rain) means a rainstorm. in *storm of God* (Jer. 30:23) even though *se’irim* is spelled with a *sin*.²⁰ (storm) with a *samekh*. Similarly, the phrase *ve-yista’er alav* (shall come against him like a whirlwind) (Dan. 11:40), which is spelled with a *sin*, means the same as *ve-yista’er alav* spelled with a *samekh*.²¹. It is also possible that the word *se’irim* has no brother in Scripture.²² Our verse repeats itself²³…repeats *And the showers…* for emphasis, as is the style in all prophecies. The essence of our verse is that Moses prayed that his words be like dew and rain which, as it is written, do not return unfulfilled²⁴ but water the earth.²⁵ The meaning of the latter is that Moses’ words should enter the hearts of those who hear them in the same way that the rain affects the earth and fructifies it and causes it to produce plants.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Se’irim
¹⁷ That se’irim
¹⁸ It refers to raindrops as thin as hair.
¹⁹ In this case, se’irim
²⁰ And sa’arah
²¹ The word for storm is usually spelled with a samekh
²² Its meaning is to be ascertained from its context.
²³ My speech…repeats My doctrine…; As the small rain
²⁴ See Is. 55:11.
²⁵ Is. 55:10



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:3:1)


**הבו גודל.** שב אל השמים והארץ כמו שאמר השמים מספרים כבוד אל כאשר פירשתיו וכן יהללוהו שמים וארץ:


ASCRIBE YE GREATNESS. This is addressed to the heavens and the earth, as Scripture states, *The heavens declare the glory of God* (Ps. 19:2), which is to be understood in accordance with my interpretation.²⁶ Similarly *Let the heavens and earth praise Him* (Ps. 69:35).

footnotes:
²⁶ Those who study astronomy realize how great God is. It is in the latter sense that the heavens declare God’s glory. See I.E. on Ps. 19:2.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:1)


והחל **הצור תמים פעלו.** להודיע כי כל מה שאירע לישראל בעבור מעשיהם הרעים היה:


[THE ROCK, HIS WORK IS PERFECT.] Moses opens with this phrase to make it known that all that befell Israel did so because of their evil deeds.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:2)


וטעם **הצור.** שהוא עומד תמיד כצור. ואל תתמה. כי כן יאמר הכתוב כארי' ישאג כי לא יוכל המדבר לדמות מעשי השם ית' כי אם אל מעשיו. כי הכל מעשיו. וכן צור לבבי כמו משען ותוקף ועמידה. וי״‎א מלשון צורה וכן וצורם לבלות שאול. ועיקר שאין לו עצם מקבל רק הוא צור קדמון והראשון נכון בעיני:


The meaning of *The Rock* is that God, like a rock, is everlasting. Do not be amazed at this,²⁷ for Scripture similarly states, *The Lord…shall roar like a lion* (Hos. 11:10).²⁸ Scripture does this because one who speaks cannot compare God’s work to anything but His works, for everything is His work. Rock, in *But God is the rock of my heart* (Ps. 73:26), similarly means a support, might, and permanence. Others say that *tzur* (rock) is related to the word *tzurah* (image). Compare, *tzuram* (their form) in *And their form shall be for the nether-world* (Ps. 49:15). Its basic meaning is that God does not consist of matter,²⁹. which can be molded into a form.³⁰ On the contrary, He is Primary form.³¹ The first interpretation appears correct to me.

footnotes:
²⁷ That Scripture compares God to a rock.
²⁸ We thus see that Scripture compares God to mundane objects.
²⁹ Hebrew, etzem
³⁰ According to medieval philosophy everything on earth consists of matter and form.
³¹ He does not consist of matter. According to this interpretation our clause should be rendered, The Primary form, for all His ways are justice.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:3)


**תמים פעלו.** כי כל מפעל פועל חסר בעבור הצטרכו לאשר הוא למעלה ממנו לבד מפעל השם הנכבד והכל (שנים) (בם' מ״‎ח גרס שונים) חוץ מיוצר הכל:


HIS WORK IS PERFECT. All the works of an artisan are incomplete³² because the artisan needs the assistance of one greater than he.³³ The work of God alone is perfect. Everything is a duality³⁴ except for the creator of all.³⁵

footnotes:
³² A human being’s work is not perfect.
³³ The work of an artisan or an author can always be improved upon. Furthermore, most artisans need assistance. Hence what they alone produce can never be perfect.
³⁴ Matter and form. Some versions read, everything is in flux.
³⁵ God does not consist of matter and form.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:4)


**כי כל דרכיו משפט.** שלא ישתנו רק הם על משפט אחד וזו היא התהלה (התחלה) וכל הנבראים ישתנו מעשיהם כפי צרכיהם ומעשי השם (לכבודו) (בם' הנ״‎ל גרס לבדו) בגבורה כפי החכמה. ואין לטעון מעמידת השמש כי סודו ברור במלת וירח בעמק אילון כאשר פירשתי במקומו:


FOR ALL HIS WAYS ARE JUSTICE. His ways do not change.³⁶ On the contrary, they follow one law.³⁷ This is the praise of God.³⁸ (v. 3). All those who are created change their deeds in accordance with their needs.³⁹ God’s works⁴⁰. were done for His glory,⁴¹. with might⁴². and wisdom.⁴³. Some editions read: God’s works alone were done in might in accordance with wisdom. The standing still of the sun (Josh. 10:12)⁴⁴ is no argument to the contrary because, as I explained in its place,⁴⁵ its secret is clear, from the clause *And thou, Moon in the valley of Aijalon* (*Ibid.*).⁴⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ The laws of nature are unchangeable.
³⁷ They are eternal.
³⁸ That God’s works are unchangeable. This is what Moses means by ascribe ye greatness unto our God
³⁹ People change their behavior in accordance with their needs. However, God is unchangeable.
⁴⁰ The working of the universe. See Chapter 12 of the Yesod Mora
⁴¹ The world reflects God’s glory. See Ps. 19:1, The heavens declare the glory of God
⁴² Some editions read: God’s works alone were done in might. Creation was a mighty act. Compare the blessing recited upon hearing thunder: “Whose might and power fill the world.” Also see Ps. 29:1, Ascribe unto the Lord glory and strength
⁴³ Hence they are unchanging. See Job 12:13, With Him is wisdom and might
⁴⁴ Which appears to have entailed a suspension of the laws of nature.
⁴⁵ I.E.’s commentary on the Book of Joshua is no longer extant.
⁴⁶ See Ralbag on Josh. 10:12. Ralbag was bothered by the issue raised by I.E. According to Ralbag the meaning of Josh. 10:12 is, may Israel defeat its enemies while the sun is still over Gibeon and the moon over the valley of Aijalon. Perhaps I.E. interpreted similarly.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:5)


**אל אמונה ואין עול.** הוא לבדו יש לו גבורת אמת כי כל גבורה בגבורים הוא מקרה מיד אחר. והטעם אחר שהוא אל אמונה לא יתכן שיעשה עול ויתכן להיות ואין עול בדרכיו. או מלת אל מושכת עצמה ואחרת עמה:


A GOD OF FAITHFULNESS AND WITHOUT INIQUITY. God alone has true might,⁴⁷. *El* means mighty. (See I.E. on Ex. 3:15). Hence I.E.’s comment on God’s might. for all the might of the mighty is an “accident;”⁴⁸ that is, it depends on outside sources.⁴⁹ The meaning of our clause is, it is not possible that God does iniquity because He is a God of faithfulness.⁵⁰ (and without iniquity). The literal meaning *of ve-en avel* is, and no iniquity. According to this interpretation, and no iniquity means and God does no iniquity. It is possible that *ve-en avel* (and without iniquity) means and without iniquity in His ways.⁵¹ On the other hand, it is possible that the word *El* (God) is to be read as if written twice.⁵² is to be interpreted as if written, *ve-en El avel*.

footnotes:
⁴⁷ The term used for God in our clause is El
⁴⁸ Accident in Aristotelian philosophy refers to a quality which is not part of the essence of a being. For example, color is said to be an accident, for the color of an object is not part of its essence.
⁴⁹ No king or ruler is autonomous. His power is dependent on the will of his underlings.
⁵⁰ Our verse reads ve-en avel
⁵¹ In other words, and no iniquity is short for, and no iniquity in his ways.
⁵² Our clause is to be read: A God of faithfulness, not a God of iniquity. According to this interpretation, ve-en avel


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:4:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:4:6)


**צדיק וישר הוא.** בעבור שאמר שחת לו אמר כי צדקתו שחתו עם נפתל והנה הוא לבדו ישר ויושר הישר לתקן המעות על כן טעם לו בעבור שהיו לו בתחלה:


JUST AND RIGHT IS HE. Since Scripture states *shichet lo* (is corruption His) (v. 5),⁵³ literally means he destroyed. I.E. renders *shichet lo* as He destroyed that which belonged to Him, that is Israel. it says a perverse people corrupted God’s righteousness.⁵⁴ Look, God alone is right.⁵⁵ The rightness of the right is to fix that which is crooked.⁵⁶ The word *lo* (v. 5) therefore refers to what He had at first.⁵⁷ means He destroyed what was His, that is, Israel. According to I. E. the word *lo*, which usually means him, is to be interpreted as *shelo* (his).

footnotes:
⁵³ Shichet
⁵⁴ Israel corrupted God’s righteous ways by sinning..
⁵⁵ God destroyed the perverse. Hence his act of destruction was not unjust.
⁵⁶ By punishing the wicked. Hence what God did to Israel was not unjust.
⁵⁷ In other words, shichet lo



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:5:1)


**לא בניו.** אלה שאמרו שאינם בניו וכן בנים לא אמון בם. והטעם שלא יודו שהוא בראם ואם יודו בפה לא יודו בלב והעד שלא יראו מהשם. ולמ״‎ד לו משרת עצמו ואחר עמו והוא למומם והטעם בעבור מומם שהוא דור עקש ופתלתל:


NO; HIS CHILDREN’S IS THE BLEMISH. *Lo banav* (no, his children’s) means those who say that they are not his children. Scripture similarly states, *Children in whom is no faithfulness* (v. 20). Its meaning is that they do not admit that God created them. Although they might profess their belief verbally, they do not believe it in their hearts. The fact that they do not fear God⁵⁸ is proof of their lack of belief. The *lamed* of *lo* (his) serves itself and another word along with it. It is to be prefixed to the word *mumam* (the blemish).⁵⁹ is to be read *le-mumam*. The entire clause is to be interpreted as if written, *lo banav le-mumam*. The meaning of *lemumam* is, because of their blemish, their blemish consisting in being *A generation crooked and perverse*.⁶⁰

footnotes:
⁵⁸ For Scripture declares them to be a crooked and perverse generation.
⁵⁹ Mumam
⁶⁰ According to this opinion our verse should be interpreted: God destroyed what originally belonged to Him, that is, those who, because of their blemish (which consists in their being a generation perverse and crooked), say that they are not His children.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:5:2)


**ופתלתל.** מתחזק ומתנצח בעקשותו וכן נפתולי אלהים. י״‎א כי טעם שחת לו כמו שחתו להם והטעם לנפש' ואל תתמה בעבור שהמלה לשון יחיד כי כן משפט הל' הלא תראה זכור ימות עולם בינו שנות דור ודור והנכון בעיני שהוא כן שחת לשם והטעם השחתת דרכי השם הנטועים והחקים שצוה כאילו אמר שחת לו דור עקש שהם לא בניו וזהו מומם והעד כי כן פירושו הבא אחריו והוא הלה' תגמלו זאת וכבר פירשתי טעם הה״‎א בספר מאזנים:


AND PERVERSE. The meaning of *u-fetaltol* (and perverse) is he grows strong and triumphant in his crookedness.⁶¹ (a crooked and perverse) as crooked and triumphant. Hence his interpretation. Compare the word *naftule* (wrestlings)⁶² (perverse) and *naftule* (wrestlings) come from the same root, *nun, peh, lamed*. in *with mighty wrestlings* (Gen. 30:8). Some say that the meaning of *shichet lo* (is corruption His) is, they corrupted themselves; that is, they corrupted their souls. Do not wonder that the singular *lo* is employed,⁶³ for this is the style of the language. Can you not see that Scripture reads, *Remember* (zekhor)⁶⁴ is singular. *the days of old, Consider* (binu)⁶⁵ is singular. *the years of many generations* (v. 7). I believe that *shichet lo* (is corruption His) means they corrupted God;⁶⁶ is, they corrupted to him. that is, they corrupted the ways of God, which are implanted in man’s mind,⁶⁷ as well as the statutes which He commanded. Scripture, as it were, says the crooked generation that is not His children corrupted Him.⁶⁸ This is their blemish. The verse that follows, namely, *Do you thus requite the Lord*, is proof that this is its interpretation.⁶⁹ I have explained the meaning of the *heh*⁷⁰. The next verse opens with an interrogative *heh*. Hence I.E.’s comment. in the book entitled *Moznayim*.

footnotes:
⁶¹ I.E. renders ikkesh u-fetaltol
⁶² Petaltol
⁶³ Whereas the rest of the verse refers to Israel in the plural.
⁶⁴ Zekhor
⁶⁵ Binu
⁶⁶ The literal meaning of shichet lo
⁶⁷ Those laws of the Torah which are based on reason. See I.E. on Ex. 20:1 (Vol. 2, p. 407).
⁶⁸ His ways.
⁶⁹ For the verse speaks of harming God, as it were.
⁷⁰ The rules of the interrogative heh

**נבל.** במעשיו:


FOOLISH. In their deeds.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:2)


**ולא חכם.** בלב והנה כלו חסר במחשבה ובמעשה:


AND UNWISE. In heart. Look, the entire people are totally deficient in thought and deed.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:3)


**אביך.** בעבור שאמר שאינם בניו:


THY FATHER. Scripture states this because it earlier stated that they are not His children.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:4)


**קנך.** כטעם ה' קנני:


THAT HATH GOTTEN THEE. *Kanekha* (gotten thee) is similar to *kanani* (made me)⁷¹ in *The Lord made me* (Prov. 8:22).

footnotes:
⁷¹ Or gotten me.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:6:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:5)


**הוא עשך.** תקנך:


HE…MADE THEE. Fully developed you.⁷². According to I.E. the word may have the meaning of fixing in final form. See I.E. on Gen. 2:4 (Vol. 1, p. 51). According to I.E. this probably means that God saw to it that you reached maturity.

footnotes:
⁷² Hebrew, tikken


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:6:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:6:6)


**ויכוננך.** לקבל התיקון:


AND ESTABLISHED THEE.⁷³. I.E. renders this, and prepared you. For full development.⁷⁴

footnotes:
⁷³ Hebrew, va-yekhoneka
⁷⁴ God nurtured Israel so that they would reach the stage of maturity. I.E. renders our verse: Is not He your father that acquired you. He saw to it that you developed and prepared you for development.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:1)


**ימות.** עם ימי שתי לשונות וכן שנות עם שני:


THE DAYS OF. *Yemot* (days of) and *yeme* (days of) are two forms.⁷⁵ (day). The same is the case with *shenot* (years of) and *shene* (years of).⁷⁶ (year).

footnotes:
⁷⁵ Two forms in the plural construct of the word yom
⁷⁶ Two forms in the plural construct of the word shanah


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:2)


**עולם.**זמן שעבר:


OF OLD. *Olam* (of old) means the time that has past.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:3)


**שאל אביך.** בעבור שאמר זכור אם שכחת מה שספרו לך אבותיך שאל אותם:


ASK THY FATHER. Scripture states this because it earlier⁷⁷ said *Remember*. If you forgot what your fathers told you, ask them.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ At the opening of the verse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:7:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:4)


**ויגדך.** כמו ויגד לך. וכן כי ארץ הנגב נתתני. בני יצאוני:


AND HE WILL DECLARE UNTO THEE. *Ve-yaggedekha* means and he will declare unto thee.⁷⁸ means unto thee. The *kha* ending usually indicates the accusative (you or thee). Thus *ve-yagaddekha* would appear to mean, and He will declare you. Hence I.E. points out that here it means will declare to you. Compare, *netattani* (thou hast given to me)⁷⁹ is usually the sign of the accusative. Compare *channani* (Gen. 33:11). Thus we would ordinarily render, *netattani*, you have given me. Hence I.E. points out that here it has the meaning of you have given to me. We thus see that at times accusative suffixes are not to be rendered literally. in *for that thou hast given to me in the Southland*⁸⁰ (Josh. 15:19). And *yetzauni* (are gone forth of me) in *My children are gone forth of me* (Jer. 10:20).⁸¹ is to be rendered as gone forth of me, not gone forth me.

footnotes:
⁷⁸ The suffix kha
⁷⁹ The suffix ni
⁸⁰ Translated according to I.E.
⁸¹ Here too an accusative ending is not to be rendered as an accusative, for yetzauni


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:7:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:7:5)


**זקניך.** הטעם כפל כי כן דרך צחות:


THINE ELDERS. Scripture repeats itself, for it is elegant to do so.⁸²…repeats the idea of *Ask thy father…*
