⁴⁰⁷ A connective vav
⁴⁰⁸ From the word ammo
⁴⁰⁹ Ketuvot
⁴¹⁰ According to this interpretation ve-khipper
⁴¹¹ The Land of Israel will atone for Israel’s sins. See I.E. on Gen. 23:19 (Vol. 1, p. 232): “Scripture tells of the purchase of the field of Machpelah to teach us of the superiority of the Land of Israel over all the countries, both for the living and the dead.”
⁴¹² The verse serves as a means by which to transmit the idea. See I.E. on Gen. 1:26 (Vol. 1, p. 47).
⁴¹³ Ve-khipper
⁴¹⁴ At the end of the word adamah. Adamah
⁴¹⁵ In admato
⁴¹⁶ Of adamah
⁴¹⁷ A feminine. If ve-khipper
⁴¹⁸ In other words, ve-khipper
⁴¹⁹ Avanim shachaku mayim
⁴²⁰ Our verse reads, ve-khipper admato
⁴²¹ Ve-khippartahu
⁴²² Which speaks of executing justice upon the nations.
⁴²³ Waged by Gog against Israel.
⁴²⁴ From the impurities of the nations (Krinsky). Or purify it by avenging the innocent blood shed by the heathens (Cohen).



### Verse 44

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:44:1)


**ויבא משה.** להורות כי אחר שהלך אל כל שבט ושבט וציום בבואו אל אהל מועד ושם נקהלו אליו כל בני ישראל אז דבר השירה הזאת:


AND MOSES CAME. Our verse teaches that after Moses went to each one of the tribes and charged them,⁴²⁵ when he came to the tent of meeting and all of the children of Israel were gathered unto him, he spoke the words of this song.⁴²⁶

footnotes:
⁴²⁵ See Deut. 31:1.
⁴²⁶ In other words, and Moses came means and Moses came to the tent of meeting where all of Israel were gathered. This took place after he had visited each tribe separately and told them that he was about to die.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 32:44:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:44:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/32:44:2)


**הוא והושע בן נון.** כמו אז ישיר משה ובני ישראל גם זאת להגדיל מעלת יהושע לעיני הכל. וקראו הכתוב הושע בשם שהיו כל ישראל יודעין אותו כי מי ידע שכנה שמו משה יהושע כי אם קריאי מועד לבדם ודרך הדרש ידועה:


HE, AND HOSHEA THE SON OF NUN. This is similar to *Then sang Moses and the children of Israel* (Ex. 15:1).⁴²⁷ Also, this⁴²⁸ was done to exalt the status of Joshua in the eyes of all. Scripture refers to Joshua by the name of Hoshea because it was the name by which he was known to all of Israel, for who aside from the elect of the assembly knew that Moses referred to Hoshea by the name of Joshua. The approach of the midrash⁴²⁹. is known.⁴³⁰

### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:2:1)


**מסיני בא.** ויאמר הגאון ז״‎ל כי הר סיני ושעיר ופארן קרובים וזה הכתוב הוא על מעמד הר סיני ואמר סיני כי מסיני כמו בסיני וכן משעיר:


CAME FROM SINAI. The Gaon,³ of blessed memory, says that Mount Sinai, Seir, and Paran are close to each other.⁴ 3:8. “These (Sinai, Seir, Paran) are three names for Mount Sinai.” The Gaon claims that the different names refer to different sides of the mountain. This verse speaks of the revelation at Sinai. Scripture reads *mi-sinai* (from Sinai), for *mi-sinai* has the same meaning as *be-sinai* (on Sinai).⁵ (the Lord came from Sinai) is, God came to Sinai. The same applies to the term *mise’ir* (from Seir).⁶ 3:8. Also see Mark Cohen, *Under Crescent and Cross*, p. 156, Princeton University Press, 1994.

footnotes:
³ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁴ See Sefer Ha-Emunot Ve-Ha-De’ot
⁵ That is, to Sinai. Thus the meaning of Adonai mi-sinai ba
⁶ It means to Seir. Saadiah Gaon is reacting to an interpretation offered by Jewish heretics or apostates. The apostates interpreted our verse as follows: God first revealed himself to Israel at Sinai. He then revealed himself to the Christians (Seir). He then revealed himself to the Arabs (Paran). This heretical interpretation is based on a midrash which identifies Seir with Christianity and Paran with the Arabs. See Sefer Ha-Emunot Ve-Ha-De’ot


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:2:2)


**מרבבות קדש.** הם המלאכים והטעם על השכינה שירדה:


FROM THE MYRIADS HOLY. The reference is to the angels. Scripture speaks of the Divine presence, which descended.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ On Mount Sinai.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:2:3)


**אש דת למו.** התורה שנתנה באש וברק ואמר כי טעם ה' מסיני בא דרך תפלה כאומר אתה שבאת בסיני והנחלת תורה יחי ראובן וזה רחוק כי היה ראוי להיות החיה ואל תמית. וחסרי אמונ' אמרו כי טעם משעיר על דת אדום. ופארן על דת ישמעאל ואלה תועים הלא ראו כי לא החל בתחלה כי אם לברך ישראל לבדם וכן כתוב אשר ברך משה איש האלהים את בני ישראל ואמר וזרח משעיר למו שפירושו להם כמו ואין למו מכשול. והנכון בעיני שהפרשה היא ברכה כלל לכל ישראל ואח״‎כ פרט לכל שבט ושבט ואחר כן כללם במקום אין כאל ישורון. והנה מצאנו שאמרה דבורה ה' בצאתך משעיר בצעדך משדה אדום והנה אין שדה אדום הר כאשר אמר הגאון כי שלשה הרים הם סמוכים או לנכח ובס' תהלות אלהים בצאתך לפני עמך בצעדך בישימון סלה ואחריו כתוב ארץ רעשה ובצעד השכינה בישימון לא היה יום מתן תורה. כי אחר מדבר פארן באו בישימון ואמר חבקוק אלוה מתימן יבוא שהוא מבני אדום תימן אומר כטעם משדה אדום. והנה הכל במלחמות ידבר והעד יקום אלהים יפוצו אויביו וכאשר יושיע השם את עבדיו ידמה הכתוב רדת עזר מהשם והארץ תמוג וההרי' ימסו והשמים ירעשו והעד בשירו' דוד ותגעש ותרעש הארץ. וטעם זה סיני פירשתיו במקומו. וכן הוא הפירוש השם בא והטעם הכבוד שנכנס בישראל ותחלת הכנסו מסיני וזרח משעיר והטעם כל השנים שהיו ישראל במדבר לא הראה השם גבורה בעמים עד בא ישראל אל שדה אדום וכן כתוב בהררם שעיר עד איל פארן והנה הם קרובים שעיר ופארן והטעם מפורש כי ממקום שעיר כאשר באו לשעיר אז זרח הכבוד לישראל והופיע מהר פארן והטעם כפול כי פה הראה יפעתו:


A FIERY LAW UNTO THEM. The Torah, which was given through fire and lightning.⁸ He⁹ says that the *Lord came…*is a prayer. It means, You who came to Sinai and bequeathed the Torah,¹⁰ *Let Reuben live* (v. 6). However, this interpretation is far-fetched, for Scripture should read, keep Reuben alive and do not put him to death.¹¹, the import of which is, may Reuben live, and not die. If Saadiah Gaon’s interpretation was correct the verse would read *hachayeh et re’uven ve-al tamito* (keep Reuben alive and do not put him to death). In other words, Scripture would have employed the imperative *hachayeh* (keep him alive) rather than the third person *yechi* (let him live). It similarly would have employed the second person *tamito* (you will put him to death) in place of the third person *yamut* (he will die). Those who lack faith say that *from Seir* refers to the religion of the Edomites,¹² and Paran refers to the faith of the Ishmaelites.¹³ However, these err. Do they not see that Moses commenced at first to bless Israel alone. Scripture thus reads, *wherewith Moses the man of God blessed the children of Israel* (v. 1). Scripture goes on to say *ve-zarach mi-se’ir lamo*, the meaning of which is, And rose from Seir unto them.¹⁴ Compare, *lamo* (for them) in *And there is no stumbling for them* (Ps. 119:165).¹⁵ means unto them. It appears to me that this chapter is a general blessing to all of Israel.¹⁶ Moses then goes on to address each tribe and tribe individually.¹⁷ Then Moses includes them all in the verse which reads, *There is none unto God, O Jeshurun* (v. 26). Look,¹⁸ we find that Deborah said, *Lord, when Thou didst go forth out of Seir, When Thou didst march out of the field of Edom, The earth trembled* (Jud. 5:4). Clearly the field of Edom is not a mountain as the Gaon says,¹⁹) is parallel to Seir. If *sede edom* does not refer to a mountain neither does Seir. for the Gaon maintains that Sinai, Seir, and Paran are three mountains close to each other or facing each other. Also, the Book of Psalms states, *O God, when Thou wentest forth before thy people, When Thou didst march through the wilderness* (yeshimon); *Selah* (Ps. 68:8). Scripture then states, *The earth trembled* (Ps. 68:9).²⁰ not at Sinai. I.E. comments thus because someone might still insist on identifying the field of Edom with Mount Sinai. It should be noted that I.E. insists that the clause “the earth trembled” in Jud. 5:4 and the similar clause in Ps. 68:9 refer to the same event. See I.E. on Ps. 68:8. Now the Divine presence did not march through the wilderness (*yeshimon*) on the day that the Torah was given, for Israel came to the wilderness (*yeshimon*) after they had passed through the wilderness (*midbar*) of Paran.²¹ Furthermore, Habakkuk says, *God cometh from Teman* (Hab. 3:3). Teman was one of the sons of Edom, for we read, *Teman, Omar* (Gen. 36:11).²² *From Teman* means the same as *from the field of Edom*. Look, all the verses²³ speak of war. *Let God arise, let His enemies be scattered* (Ps. 68:2) is proof of this. Scripture describes God’s saving of His servants in terms of help coming down from heaven, the earth melting, the mountains dissolving, and the heavens quaking. *Then the earth did shake and quake* (Ps. 18:8) in David’s song is proof of this. I have explained the meaning of *even yon Sinai* (Ps. 68:9) in its place.²⁴) did. In other words, the phrase *zeh sinai* does not imply that what comes before (the earth trembled…) refers to the revelation at Sinai. The following is the explanation of our verse. *The Lord came* refers to the glory that entered into Israel. It first entered Israel at Sinai. *And rose from Seir* means that God did not show His might among the nations all the years that Israel was in the wilderness until Israel came to the field of Edom. Scripture similarly states, *in their mount Seir, unto El-paran* (Gen. 14:6). Seir and Paran are thus close to each other. The meaning of *And rose from Se’ir* is thus explained, for *mi-seir* means from the place of Seir; that is, when Israel came to Seir, God’s glory rose to Israel. *He shined forth from mount Paran* repeats the idea expressed by *and rose from Seir*. It means that God here²⁵ showed His shining.

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:3:1)

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:3:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:3:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:3:9)


**תכו.** בלא אח וי״‎א מל' מתוך כאילו הולכים בתוך רגלך ואחרים אומרים כי התי״‎ו במקום ה״‎א כתי״‎ו תרגלתי לאפרים כטעם אפילו שיוכו בשבילך לא יעזבו דבריך וזה רחוק. והנכון שהוא כמו נמשכו והטעם שילכו אל המקום ששם הארון שהוא הדום רגלי השם וזאת היא הברכ' שהשם יהיה חומת אש סביב ישראל ויהיה סגולתו והלוים יורו התורה תמיד:


AND THEY SIT DOWN. *Tukku* (sit down) has no brother.³⁸ Some say³⁹ that it is related to the word *mi-tokh* (from the midst of).⁴⁰. They, as it were, walk in the midst of Your feet. Others say that the *tav*⁴¹. is in place of a *heh*.⁴² is a variant of *hukku* (were beaten). It is like the *tav* of *tirgalti* (I taught)⁴³ is a variant of *hirgalti*. in *I taught Ephraim* (Hos. 11:3). The meaning of our verse is, even if they are beaten on Your account, they will not forsake Your words. However, this is farfetched. Actually it means are drawn;⁴⁴ is, they are drawn to your feet. they go to the place of the ark which serves as the footstool for God’s feet. This is the blessing:⁴⁵ May God be a wall of fire round about Israel.⁴⁶ May Israel be God’s treasure.⁴⁷ May the Levites always teach the Torah.⁴⁸

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:4:1)


**תורה צוה לנו משה.** זהו שישא ויאמר דור לדור כך העתקנו מפי משה ירושה בידינו ויהיה למ״‎ד לנו מושך עצמו ואחר עמו. וכן היא מורשה לקהלת יעקב כמו והנבואה עודד הנביא ויהי מורשה מורשה קהלת יעקב:


MOSES COMMANDED US A LAW. This is what they shall bear upon their lips and say from generation to generation: thus did we copy with our hands an inheritance from the mouth of Moses.⁴⁹ The *lamed* of *lanu* (us) applies to itself and also to another word.⁵⁰. *Morashah kehillat ya’akov* (an inheritance congregation of Jacob)⁵¹ literally means an inheritance congregation of Jacob. Hence I.E.’s comment. should be read as if written *morashah le-kehillat ya’akov* (an inheritance of the congregation of Jacob). It is similar to *ve-hanevu’ah oded ha-navi* (even the prophecy Oded the prophet)⁵² (II Chronicles 15:8).⁵³ [even the prophecy (they), prophecy of Oded the prophet]. See I.E. on Gen. 2:9 (Vol. 1, p. 54). Or⁵⁴ *morashah* (an inheritance) should be read as if written twice. Our clause should be read as if written an inheritance, an inheritance of the congregation of Jacob.⁵⁵ (Moses commanded us a law, an inheritance, an inheritance of the congregation of Jacob).

footnotes:
⁴⁹ In other words, Moses dictated to us and we transcribed the Torah.
⁵⁰ It is to be placed before the word kehillat
⁵¹ Morashah kehillat ya’akov
⁵² Translated literally
⁵³ Which is to be read as if written ve-hanevu’ah nevu’at oded ha-navi
⁵⁴ I.E. is obviously offering another interpretation.
⁵⁵ Our verse should be read as if written torah tzivvah lanu mosheh morashah, morashah kehillat ya’akov



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:5:1)


**מלך.** הוא משה ששמעו ראשי עם התורה מפורשת מפיו והטעם כי הוא היה כמלך והתאספו אליו ראשי השבטים ויחזיק זה הפי' שאמר הכתוב כי התורה נתנה לכהנים שהם לפי דעתי כל קדושיו בידיך ואל כל זקני ישראל זהו בהתאסף ראשי עם גם שם כתוב הקהילו אלי כל ראשי שבטיכם. ויאמר רבי יהודה הלוי ז״‎ל כי המלך רמז לתורה וכן היה מפרש אין מלך בישראל:


A KING. The reference is to Moses, from whose mouth the heads of the people clearly heard the words of the Torah. The meaning of our verse is that Moses was the equivalent of a king, and the heads of the tribes were gathered unto him.⁵⁶ This interpretation is supported by Scripture’s statement that the Torah was given to the *kohanim*,⁵⁷ who, in my opinion, are meant by *All His holy ones-they are in Thy hand* (v. 3), and to *all the elders of Israel* (Deut. 31:9). This is what *When the heads of the people were gathered* refers to. Scripture also writes there, *Assemble unto me all the elders of your tribes* (Deut. 31:28). Rabbi Judah Ha-Levi, of blessed memory, says that the *king* alludes to the Torah. He similarly used to explain *there was no king in Israel* (Jud. 18:l).⁵⁸

footnotes:
⁵⁶ To study Torah.
⁵⁷ Deut. 31:9.
⁵⁸ He renders the verse, there was no Torah in Israel. That is, there was no rule of law.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:1)


**יחי ראובן.** הנה החל מהבכור כי כן המשפט ולא הזכיר שמעון בעבור בעל פעור כי העובדים היו שמעונים ומספרם לעד גם נשיאם נהרג או הלך על דרך יעקב שלא ברך שמעון ולוי ובעבור כבוד אהרן נתלה השבט ממנו ולא היה בשבט שמעון גדול כמותו. ואם יטעון טוען למה בירך ראובן. התשובה כי יעקב ברכו והברכ' היא אל תותר שיהיה כאחד מאחיו. ואחר ראובן הזכיר יהוד' שהוא בעל דגל והוא עולה בתחלה למלחמה ואחר כן לוי ואחריו בנימין בעבור היות הלוים דרים בירושלים שהיתה בין יהודה ובין בנימין כן כתוב. ואחר כן שבט יוסף שלא יאחרנו וכבר הקדים אחיו הקטן ואחר כן זבולון בעבור שהוא נלחם ואחר כן יששכר. וכאשר תמו בני הגבירות החל מגד שהוא עם דגל בני הגבירו' ואחר כן דן שהוא בעל דגל ואחר כן נפתלי כי הוא גדול מאשר:


LET REUBEN LIVE. Moses starts with the first-born, for this is the proper practice. He does not mention Simeon because of the incident of Baal-peor,⁵⁹ for those who served Baalpeor were from the tribe of Simeon.⁶⁰ Their number⁶¹ proves this.⁶² Also, their prince was killed.⁶³ It is also possible that Moses followed in the footsteps of Jacob,⁶⁴ who did not bless Simeon and Levi. The tribe of Levi was blessed out of respect for Aaron. Simeon had no one as great as Aaron.⁶⁵ Should one who disputes argue, “Why did Moses bless Reuben?”⁶⁶ The answer is that Jacob blessed Reuben. The blessing was *have not thou the excellency* (Gen. 49:4).⁶⁷ The meaning of the latter is that Reuben shall be like one of his brothers. Moses mentions Judah after Reuben⁶⁸ because he was in charge of a standard⁶⁹ and he was the first to lead in battle. Next Levi and then Benjamin are mentioned⁷⁰ because the Levites dwelled in Jerusalem, which was situated between Judah and Benjamin. Scripture clearly states this.⁷¹ Moses then mentions the tribe of Joseph so as not to delay naming him. He had already mentioned his younger brother.⁷² He next mentioned Zebulun because he waged war.⁷³ (Jud. 5:18). He then mentioned Issachar.⁷⁴ After Moses finished mentioning the children of the wives, he commenced with Gad,⁷⁵ who camped among the standard of the children of the wives.⁷⁶ Moses then mentions Dan, who was in charge of a standard.⁷⁷ He then mentioned Naphtali because he was older than Asher.⁷⁸

footnotes:
⁵⁹ See Num. 25:1-9.
⁶⁰ See Num. 25:14.
⁶¹ Their drop in number.
⁶² When the tribe of Simeon was counted in the second year after the Exodus, they numbered 59,300. (Num. 1:23). When they were counted following the incident of Baal-peor, they numbered 22,200 (Num. 26:14). In other words, the number of the tribe was diminished by 37,100.
⁶³ Num. 25:14.
⁶⁴ On his deathbed. See Gen. 49:5-7.
⁶⁵ Hence they were not blessed.
⁶⁶ When Jacob apparently did not bless him. See Gen. 49:3,4.
⁶⁷ Of dignity and power.
⁶⁸ Even though Levi was born before Judah.
⁶⁹ For committing harlotry with a Midianite princess. The point is that the leader of the tribe of Simeon as well as a large proportion of the sinners at Baal-peor were Simeonites. Hence Moses did not bless them. See Num. 2:3-9.
⁷⁰ After Judah.
⁷¹ See Josh. 15:8.
⁷² Benjamin.
⁷³ Zebulun is a people that jeoparded their lives unto death
⁷⁴ Who, like Zebulun, was a son of Leah.
⁷⁵ That is, he commenced the listing of the children of the handmaids with Gad.
⁷⁶ He camped under the banner of Reuben. See Num. 2:14.
⁷⁷ See Num. 2:25.
⁷⁸ See Gen. 30:8-13.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:2)


**יחי ראובן.** תפלה:


LET REUBEN LIVE. A prayer.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:3)


**ואל ימות** שיתכן שיהיה כבן אדם לחיות שנים קצובות וימות:


AND NOT DIE. For it is possible that he will be as a person who lives a certain number of years and then dies.⁷⁹

footnotes:
⁷⁹ In other words, Moses prayed that the tribe of Reuben exist forever and not disappear with the death of the present generation.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:6:4)


**ויהי מתיו מספר.** ואל יהי מתיו מספר כמו ולא למדתי חכמה ובי״‎ת באל שדי כאשר פירשתי רבים כי יתכן שיחיה לעולם ויהיו מעטים וכל דבר שיספר הוא מעט. וכן ואני מתי מספר:


IN THAT HIS MEN BECOME FEW.⁸⁰ *Ve-yehi metav mispar*⁸¹ literally means and let his men be numbered (few). is to be rendered, and let his men not be numbered (few).⁸² (not) which precedes *yamot* is to be inserted after the *vav* of *ve-yehi*. Our clause is to be read as if written *va-al yehi metav mispar*. It is similar to *And I have not learned wisdom* (Prov. 30:3)⁸³ (not, no) is to read as if written twice. Prov. 30:3 literally reads: I have not learned wisdom and I have knowledge of the Holy One. The verse should be read as if written: I have not learned wisdom and I have no knowledge of the Holy One. and to the *bet* of *be-el shaddai* (as God Almighty) (Ex. 6:3)⁸⁴. According to I.E. this is be interpreted as if written *u-veshemi*, for the *bet* of *be-El* also applies to the word *u-shemi*. See I.E. on Ex. 6:3 (Vol. 2, pp. 131,132.) as I have explained many times. Moses said this⁸⁵ because it is possible for the tribe of Reuben to live forever but consist of few people. All things that are numbered are few. Compare, *And I am men of number(Gen*. 34:30).⁸⁶

footnotes:
⁸⁰ Literally, and let his men be numbered.
⁸¹ Ve-yehi metav mispar
⁸² For the word al
⁸³ Wherein the word lo
⁸⁴ Ex. 6:3 reads u-shemi
⁸⁵ That Reuben’s men not be few.
⁸⁶ Translated literally.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:7:1)


**וזאת ליהודה.** וכן אמר ליהודה שיחיה ולא ימות והוסיף עוד שמע ה' קול יהודה בבואו למלחמה כי הוא יעלה בתחלה:


AND THIS FOR JUDAH. He similarly said to Judah,⁸⁷ means he similarly said this to Judah. “Let Judah live and not die.” He also added, “Hear, Lord, the voice of Judah” when he goes to war, because Judah would go into battle first.⁸⁸

footnotes:
⁸⁷ And this for Judah
⁸⁸ When Israel conquered Canaan. See Jud. 1:1,2; 20:18.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:7:2)


**ואל עמו תביאנו.** אל המחנה אשר בגלגל או תפלה על היוצאים להלחם על ארץ אויב:


AND BRING HIM IN UNTO HIS PEOPLE. To the camp, which was in Gilgal.⁸⁹ On the other hand, it might be a prayer regarding those who go to do battle in the land of the enemy.⁹⁰

footnotes:
⁸⁹ Israel’s main camp was at Gilgal during the period of the conquest. See Chapters 9 and 10 of Joshua.
⁹⁰ During the period of the monarchy. See Rashi.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:7:3)


**רב לו.** יספיקו לו ידיו ואין לו צורך למי שיעזור אותו כי אם עזרת השם לבדו וי״‎א מל' מריבה והטעם ידיו יריבו בעדו ואחרים אמרו כמו וברקים רב מגזרת ורבו:


HIS HAND SHALL CONTEND FOR HIM. *Rav lo* (contend for him) means shall be sufficient for him⁹¹ means abundant. Hence *rav lo* literally means shall be abundant for him, that is, shall be more than enough for him to do what he has to do. so that he has no need for anyone to help him but God’s help alone. Others say that the word *rav* is related to the word *merivah* (conflict). The meaning of *yadav rav lo* is that his hands shall fight for him. Others say that the word *rav* is like the word *rav* (shot forth) in *and He shot forth lightnings* (Ps. 18:15). It is related to *va-robbu* (and shot at him) (Gen. 49:23).⁹².

footnotes:
⁹¹ The word rav
⁹² According to this interpretation our clause reads: his hands shall shoot for him; that is, he will be able to hold a bow and shoot at his enemies. See Ps. 18:35, so that mine arms do bend a bow of brass



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:8:1)


**וללוי אמר.** בעבור לוי אמר לשם כמו אמרי לי אחי הוא:


AND OF LEVI. *U-le-levi amar* (and of Levi he said) is to be interpreted, And of Levi⁹³ is to be rendered, and of Levi, not and to Levi. he said to God.⁹⁴, which is all the text provides, is short for he said to God. Compare, *say of me* (li):⁹⁵ is to be rendered of me, not to me. See I.E. on Gen. 20:13 (Vol. 1, p. 213). *He is my brother* (Gen. 20:13).

⁹³ U-le-levi
⁹⁴ He said
⁹⁵ Here too the word li

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:8:2)


**לאיש חסידך.** לאיש שהוא חסידך:


WITH THY HOLY ONE. *Le-ish chasidekha* (with Thy holy one) means to the man who is Your holy one.⁹⁶ (with Thy holy one) literally means to the man Your holy one. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁹⁶ Le-ish chasidekha


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:8:3)


**אשר נסיתו במסה.** שם כמו בנסיון והטעם שהיה חסיד ולא נמצא בו דופי חוץ מדבר מריבה שכבר הענשתו עליו גם זה יחזיק פירושי בדבר העגל כי לכבוד השם עשהו:


WHOM THOU DIDST PROVE AT MASSAH. Massah is a noun. It means a test.⁹⁷ The meaning of our clause is: Aaron was pious. Aside from the incident at Meribah,⁹⁸ for which You already punished him,⁹⁹. no unseemly thing was found in him.¹⁰⁰ Also, this verse¹⁰¹ supports my interpretation regarding the incident of the calf, that is, that Aaron made the calf for God’s glory.¹⁰². See I.E. on Ex. 31:18 (Vol. 2, pp. 653-663).

¹⁰⁰ Otherwise they would be mentioned here.
¹⁰¹ In additions to other proofs.
¹⁰² Our verse implies that the only sin that Aaron committed was the one at Meribah. Had Aaron sinned in making the golden calf, then that sin would have been mentioned. Furthermore, if Aaron had made an idol Moses would not have referred to him as ish chasidekha

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:11:4

THAT THEY RISE NOT AGAIN. *Min yekumun*¹¹⁴ literally means of them will arise against them. (that they rise not again) means of those that will rise against them.¹¹⁵ is short for *min asher yekumun alehem*, that is, of them that will arise against them. *Yekumun* is a variation of *yakum* (they shall arise). Compare, *la-yam mekhassim* (cover the sea) (Is. 11:9).¹¹⁶ is to be read as if written *asher la-yam mekhassim*, i.e., that cover the sea.

¹¹⁵ In other words, min yekumun
¹¹⁶ La-yam mekhassim



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:12:1)


**לבנימן אמר.** בעבור בנימין שהוא ידיד השם ישכון בנימין לבטח על השם והשם שוכן בין כתיפיו:


OF BENJAMIN HE SAID. *Le-vinyamin* means of Benjamin.¹¹⁷ usually means to. Hence I.E.’s comment. Moses said of Benjamin, who is the beloved of the Lord,¹¹⁸ should be interpreted, *le-vinyamin yedid Adonai amar*. Benjamin shall dwell in safety by God, and God shall dwell between his shoulders.

footnotes:
¹¹⁷ Not to Benjamin. The lamed
¹¹⁸ In other words, le-vinyamin amar yedid Adonai


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:12:2)


**חופף עליו.** על הכבוד שהיה דר בירושלם בחלק בנימין כי כן כתוב:


HE COVERETH HIM. The reference is to God’s glory which, as Scripture writes,¹¹⁹ dwelled in Benjamin’s portion, in Jerusalem.

footnotes:
¹¹⁹ Josh. 18:28.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:12:3)


וטעם **בין כתפיו.** באמצע נחלתו או שהיה נראה גם להיות דבק עם חופף:


The meaning of *between his shoulders* is, in the center of his inheritance. Or *between his shoulders* means that God’s glory was visible.¹²⁰ Furthermore, *between his shoulders* is also connected to *covereth*.¹²¹ is to be interpreted, He covereth him, and dwelleth between his shoulders all the day (Krinsky).

footnotes:
¹²⁰ Over the temple. See Rashi: The temple was built on the highest spot of Benjamin’s land.
¹²¹ He covereth him all the day, And He dwelleth beween his shoulders



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:13:1)


**ממגד.** שיתנו השמים:


FOR THE PRECIOUS THINGS. Which the heavens will give.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:14:1)


**תבואות שמש.** כי תבואות הזרע והכרם תלויה בשמש:


THE FRUITS OF THE SUN. For the fruits of the seed and the vineyard are dependent upon the sun.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:14:2)


**גרש.** מגזרת ויגרשו מימיו והוא שיעשה האויר בלחותו כי אין חומו ככח השמש. ואמר ירחים בעבור שיתחדשו והן רבים בשנה אחת:


THE YIELD. *Geresh* (yield) is related to the word *vayigreshu* (cast up) in *And its waters cast up* (Is. 57:20). The moon does this¹²² via the moisture which is in the air,¹²³ for its heat is unlike the power of the sun.¹²⁴ Scripture reads moons¹²⁵ because they renew themselves,¹²⁶ and there are many of them in the year.¹²⁷

footnotes:
¹²² Produces its yield.
¹²³ The moon affects the moisture in the air. This results in “the yield of the moon.”
¹²⁴ Which directly influences the growth of plants. The sun’s rays directly influence plant life. The moon has no rays. I.E. believed that the moon influences plant growth by its effect on the moisture in the air.
¹²⁵ There is one moon. Hence I.E.’s comment.
¹²⁶ The moon reappears each month.
¹²⁷ There is thus a new moon each month. Hence one may speaks of “moons.”



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:15:1)


**ומראש.** הטעם וממגד ראש הררי קדם:


AND FOR THE TOPS. This means and for the precious things that grow on the tops of the ancient hills.¹²⁸ from the previous verse also applies here. Thus *u-merosh harere kedem* (and for the tops of the ancient mountains) is to be read as if written *u-mi-meged rosh ha-rere kedem* (and for the precious things that grow on the tops of the ancient mountains).

footnotes:
¹²⁸ In other words, the word meged


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:15:2)


**גבעות עולם.** כפול:


THE EVERLASTING HILLS. Scripture repeats itself.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:1)


**וממגד ארץ.** כפול ארץ המישור:


AND FOR THE PRECIOUS THINGS OF THE EARTH. Scripture repeats itself. *The earth* refers to flat land.¹²⁹ is in contrast to the mountains and hills mentioned in the previous verse.

footnotes:
¹²⁹ For the earth


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:2)


**ומלואה.** מאשר היא מלאה:


AND THE FULNESS THEREOF. From what it is full of.¹³⁰

footnotes:
¹³⁰ Not only the produce of the land but of all things that the earth is full of, i.e., gold, silver, etc. (Krinsky).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:3)


**ורצון שכני סנה.** הוא השמים:


AND THE GOOD WILL OF HIM THAT DWELT IN THE BUSH. The reference is to heaven.¹³¹ (bush) as meaning heaven. However, he rejects this interpretation in Ex. 3:2 (Vol. 2, pp. 52,53).

footnotes:
¹³¹ I.E. interprets the word seneh


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:16:4)


**תבואתה.** הברכה הזאת והתחברו שנים סימנין התי״‎ו והה״‎א כמו עזרתה ישועתה בצרתה ובעבור שהזכיר התבואה דמה השבט לשור ואמר בכור שורו כי יש לו כח רב כי הוא ראשית און:


LET THE BLESSING COME. *Tavotah* (let the blessing come)¹³² is the elongated or poetic form of the word *tavo*. refers to this blessing.¹³³ Two signs,¹³⁴ the *tav* and the *heh*, are joined together.¹³⁵ should have read *tavo’ah* or *tavo’at. Tavotah* is a combination of the two. Compare, *ezratah* (help) (Ps. 44:27);¹³⁶ is a combination of the two feminine forms *ezrat* and *ezrah*. *yeshu’atah* (salvation) (Ps. 3:3);¹³⁷ (salvation) is a combination of the two feminine forms *yeshu’ah* and *yeshu’at*. *ba-tzaratah* (in…distress) (Ps. 120:1).¹³⁸ is a combination of *tzarah* and *tzarat*. Moses compared the tribe of Joseph to a bullock because he earlier spoke of produce.¹³⁹ Moses referred to Joseph as *His firstling bullock* (v. 17) because a first-born bullock has great strength as it is the first of its father’s might.

footnotes:
¹³² Tavotah
¹³³ The blessing mentioned in verses 13-16.
¹³⁴ Two letters which are signs of the feminine.
¹³⁵ The elongated form of tavo
¹³⁶ Ezratah
¹³⁷ Yeshu’atah
¹³⁸ Tzaratah
¹³⁹ Bulls are used in plowing and for pulling carts loaded with produce.

**הדר לו.** גוף גדול והטעם מספר רב:


MAJESTY IS HIS. His¹⁴⁰ body is large. It means that the tribe of Joseph is large in number.

¹⁴⁰ The bullock’s.

THE HORNS OF THE WILD-OX. In power.

THE TEN THOUSANDS OF EPHRAIM. As in the words of Jacob.¹⁴¹

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:18:1

**שמח זבולון בצאתך.** למלחמה וכן זבולן עם חרף נפשו למות:


REJOICE, ZEBULUN, IN THY GOING OUT. To war. Similarly *Zebulun is a people that jeoparded their lives unto the death* (Jud. 5:18).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:18:2)

IN THY TENTS. Compare, *For he saw a resting-place that it was good* (Gen. 49:15).¹⁴²

footnotes:
¹⁴² Gen. 49:15 refers to the tribe of Issachar.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:19:1)


**עמים הר יקראו.** בעבור שזבולן דר לחוף אניות יהיה עשרו רב וזהו כי שפע ימים יינקו והוא מגזרת שפעת גמלים:


THEY SHALL CALL PEOPLES UNTO THE MOUNTAIN. Zebulun shall possess great wealth because he dwells where ships dock. This is the meaning of *they shall suck the abundance of the seas*. The word *shefa* (abundance) is related to the word *shifat* (caravan) in *The caravan of camels shall cover thee* (Is. 60:6).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:19:2)


**ושפוני טמוני חול.** אין לו אח וטעמו כמו טמוני וכן אדמת עפר והטעם שיהא להם עושר רב עד שיטמנהו בחול ובעבור עשרם יבואו רובם אל הר השם לזבוח זבחי תודה לשם:


AND THE HIDDEN TREASURES OF THE SAND. The word *sefune* (hidden) has no brother.¹⁴³ It has the same meaning as *temune* (treasures).¹⁴⁴. I.E. believes that Scripture employs two words back to back that have one meaning. It means hidden hidden [of the earth]. Similarly *admat afar* (dust).¹⁴⁵ The meaning of the *hidden treasures of the sand* is that Zebulun will have so much wealth that they [the tribe] will hide it in the sand. And because of their wealth, they will come in a multitude to the mountain of God to offer sacrifices of thanksgiving to the Lord.

footnotes:
¹⁴³ It is not found elsewhere in Scripture.
¹⁴⁴ Our verse reads sefune temune
¹⁴⁵ Dan. 12:2. Both words mean the same. Our text literally reads dust dust.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:20:1)


**מרחיב גד.** דמהו לאריה כל רואיו יברחו ממנו ואין מי יצר לו:


ENLARGETH GAD. Moses compares Gad to a lion. All who see him will flee from him. No one shall torment him.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:1)


**וירא ראשית לו.** הטעם שראה לנפשו ויבקש לו נחלה קודם לישראל:


AND HE CHOSE A FIRST PART FOR HIMSELF.¹⁴⁶) for himself. According to I.E. *he saw* is short for he sought an inheritance. *First* means before the other Israelites, and *himself* means he sought it for himself. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. This means he looked out for himself and sought an inheritance before the other Israelites.¹⁴⁷

footnotes:
¹⁴⁶ Literally, he saw a first (reshit
¹⁴⁷ Received their inheritance. See Num. 32.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:2)


**כי שם חלקת.** כמו חלק:


FOR THERE A PORTION OF A RULER WAS RESERVED. The word *chelkat* (portion of) means a portion.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:3)


ו**מחוקק** הוא הגדול והשליט כמו לבי לחוקקי ישראל:


[A RULER.] *Mechokek* (ruler) refers to someone who is great and a ruler. Compare, *My heart is toward the governors* (le-chokeke) *of Israel* (Jud. 5:9).


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:21:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:4)


ו**ספון** כמו וספון בארז (בתוכם ספונים) [צ״‎ל בבתיכ' ספוני'] שטעמו שהם ספונים וכן קירות הספון והטעם כי ראה ארמונים ספונים שהיו חלק גדולי האמורי:


[RESERVED.] The word *safun* (reserved) is similar to *safun* (ceiled) in *and it is ceiled*¹⁴⁸ *with cedar* (Jer. 22:14); and *sefunim* (ceiled) in *be-vattekhem sefunim* (Haggai 1:4), which means your houses which are ceiled. The import of *ki sham chelkak mechokek safun* (for there a portion of a ruler was preserved)¹⁴⁹ is that Gad saw the paneled palaces that were the portion of the nobles of the Amorites.

footnotes:
¹⁴⁸ Ceilinged or paneled.
¹⁴⁹ Which, according to I.E., means for there is the ceiled palace of the ruler.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:21:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:5)


**ויתא.** חסר אל״‎ף והטעם שבאו ראשי גד עם ישראל חלוצי צבא ומשה ראה זה בנבואה:


CAME. *Va-yeta* (came)¹⁵⁰. is missing an *alef*.¹⁵¹, for its root is *alef, tav, alef*. The meaning of our clause is that the heads of Gad came armed with the children of Israel.¹⁵² Moses prophetically saw this.

footnotes:
¹⁵⁰ Spelled vav, yod, tav, alef
¹⁵¹ It should be spelled vav, yod, alef, tav, alef
¹⁵² To conquer Canaan. See Josh. 1:12-18; 22:1-9.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:21:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:21:6)


**צדקת ה' עשה.** שמלא בידו את אשר דבר בפיו שנדרו בני גד שיצאו עם ישראל להלחם עם אויביהם ולא הזכיר ראובן ויתכן כי זה בעבור שהיו בני גד גבורים יותר מבני ראובן והנה דברי הימים לעד שאמר על בני גד ופני אריה פניהם ולא תמצא כן בבני ראובן:


HE EXECUTED THE RIGHTEOUSNESS OF THE LORD. Gad fulfilled with his hands¹⁵³ that which he spoke with his mouth, for the children of Gad vowed that they would go out with Israel to wage battle against their enemies.¹⁵⁴ Moses did not mention Reuben.¹⁵⁵ It is possible that he omitted Reuben because the tribe of Gad were greater warriors than the tribe of Reuben. The words of Chronicles, which states regarding the tribe of Gad, *whose faces were like the faces of lions* (I Chron. 12:9), is proof of this. We do not find this stated regarding the tribe of Reuben.

footnotes:
¹⁵³ That is, Gad did in practice.
¹⁵⁴ Num. 32:16,17.
¹⁵⁵ When he mentioned Gad’s keeping the oath which they made to join Israel in the conquest of Canaan. The same oath was made and observed by the tribe of Reuben. See Num. 32:16,17 and Josh. 1:12-18; 22:1-9.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:22:1)


**יזנק.** אין לו חבר במקרא והטעם דמהו בגבורתו לגור אריה כאשר ידלג מהר בשן:


LEAPETH. The word *yezannek* (leapeth) has no companion in Scripture. Our clause is to be understood as follows: Moses compared Dan’s might to that of a lion’s whelp leaping from Mount Bashan.¹⁵⁶ means leap. According to I.E. our verse should be understood as follows: Dan is as powerful as a young lion leaping forth from Mount Bashan. Bashan is a district in Transjordan.

footnotes:
¹⁵⁶ In other words, yezannek

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:23:1)

SATISFIED WITH FAVOR. *Seva ratzon* (satisfied) is an adjective. It means he will be sated with favor.¹⁵⁷ is not to be taken as an imperative with our verse meaning Naphtali be satisfied with God’s favor.

THE SEA AND SOUTH. *Yam*¹⁵⁸ (sea) as west. *ve-darom* (the sea and south) means that Naphtali’s inheritance shall be in the southwest.¹⁵⁹

¹⁵⁸ I.E. renders yam
¹⁵⁹ Of the tribe of Dan (Weiser).

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:24:1)

THE FAVORED. *Ratzuy* (favored) means the beloved.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:24:2)

AND LET HIM DIP HIS FOOT IN OIL. Because of the abundance of oil. It is similar to *He washeth his garments in wine* (Gen. 49:11). Jacob similarly said,¹⁶⁰ *his bread shall be fat* (Gen. 49:20).

### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:25:1)


**ברזל ונחשת מנעליך.** כי בחלקו יהיה הרי ברזל ונחשת:


IRON AND BRASS SHALL BE THY BARS.¹⁶¹. I.E. renders this, the place where you walk upon with your shoes (*na’alkha*). Hence I.E.’s interpretation. For mountains of iron and brass shall be in his portion.¹⁶²

footnotes:
¹⁶¹ Hebrew, minalekha
¹⁶² Hence Asher will step upon iron and brass.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:25:2)


**וכימיך דבאך.** אין לו ריע וטעם המתרגם ידוע כי תוקף יהיה לך כל ימיך ויש אומרים שהאל״‎ף תחת אות הכפל כמו אשר בזאו נהרים ארצו והטעם מגזרת דובב שפתי ישנים. וזה הטעם בלי טעם:


AND AS THY DAYS, SO SHALL THY STRENGTH BE. The word *dovekha* (thy strength) has no neighbor.¹⁶³ The interpretation of the Aramaic translation¹⁶⁴ (and as thy days, so shall thy strength be), and as thy youth so shall your strength be [all the days of your life]. is known, namely, that you will be strong all your days. Some say that the *alef* of *dovekha* is in place of the doubled letter.¹⁶⁵ comes from the root *dalet, bet, bet*. It is like the word *baze’u* (divide)¹⁶⁶, for the *alef* in *baze’u* takes the place of a *zayin*. in *Whose land the rivers divide* (Is. 18:2). They believe that *dovekha* is related to the word *dovev* (moving gently) in *Moving gently the lips of those that are asleep* (Cant. 7:10).¹⁶⁷ to mean: as in your youth so shall you speak [all the days of your life]; i.e., you shall always speak happily as young people are wont to do (Weiser). This interpretation is not reasonable.¹⁶⁸

¹⁶⁵ In other words, dovekha
¹⁶⁶ Which is a variant of bazezu
¹⁶⁷ They interpret u-kheyamekha dovekha
¹⁶⁸ It does not fit the context.

THERE IS NONE LIKE UNTO GOD, O JESHURUN. Moses goes back to bless all of Israel.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:26:2)


**רוכב שמים.** פירוש שהוא תקיף והעד שהוא רוכב שמים:


WHO RIDETH UPON THE HEAVEN. That is, who is mighty. The fact that He *rideth upon the heaven* is proof of this.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:26:3)


**ובגאותו שחקים** הטעם בגאותו הם עומדים:


[AND IN HIS EXCELLENCY ON THE SKIES.]¹⁶⁹ It means, the heavens stand by God’s might.¹⁷⁰ (in His excellency) by His might. Therefore *And in His might the skies* is short for and by His might the heavens stand. I.E. renders our verse as follows: He who rides upon the heavens is your help, and He [so too is] by whose might the heavens stand.

### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:27:1)


**מעונה אלהי קדם.** דע כי מעון כמו סוכה והעד ויהי בשלם סוכו ומעונתו בציון ורבים כן והטע' כי מעונה שלך הוא אלהי קדם כמו עליון שמת מעונך. מעין אתה היית לנו. ולעול' המעון למעלה על החוסה בו והמקום למטה:


THE ETERNAL GOD IS A DWELLING-PLACE. Note that the word *ma’on* (dwelling-place) means a covering (i.e., a *sukkah*).¹⁷¹ is so called because it has a covering over it. *In Salem also is set His covering*¹⁷² (sukko), *And His dwelling-place* (me’onato) *in Zion*¹⁷³ is parallel to *me’onato*. (Ps. 76:3) is proof of this. There are many such examples.¹⁷⁴ is used in the sense of protection. The meaning of our verse is: The eternal God is your covering.¹⁷⁵ Compare, *Even the Most High, thy habitation* (me’onekha)¹⁷⁶ (Ps. 91:9) and *Thou hast been our dwelling-place* (ma’on) (Ps. 90:1). The *ma’on* (dwelling place) is always above the one who takes refuge in it.¹⁷⁷ means a protective covering. God serves as a protective covering above us. The “place”¹⁷⁸).” is below.¹⁷⁹ refers to “up above” and *makom* to below. God is referred to as “The Place” because He is present in all places on the earth. See I.E.’s introduction to Esther, “God is not referred to as ‘The Place (*makom*)’ in any of the books of Scripture. On the contrary, He is referred to as ‘dwelling place (*ma’on*),’ for the *ma’on* which is always on high. However, the ancients, of blessed memory, called God ‘The Place’ because all places are full of His glory.”

¹⁷⁹ Either beneath the protective covering or beneath the person being protected. In other words, ma’on

**ומתחת זרועות.** שיתמכו אותך:


AND UNDERNEATH ARE THE EVERLASTING ARMS. Which will support you.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 33:27:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:27:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/33:27:3)


וטעם **עולם.** שכן יהיו נצח ולא תחלשנה והמעון שלך לא יסור כי הוא אלהי קדם והטעם קדמון שאין לו ראשית ואחרית. וטעם ראשית ואחרית כנגד הנבראים והגאון אמר כי ומתחת פירוש ומתחת השם ולא פירש כלום:


Scripture reads *everlasting* to teach that it shall always be so. The hand will not weaken, and your covering (*ma’on*) will not be removed, for He is the eternal God. The meaning of *kedem* (everlasting) is that God has no beginning and no end. Scripture speaks in human terms when it speaks of beginning and end.¹⁸⁰ The Gaon says that *and underneath* means and underneath God. He did not explain anything.

¹⁸⁰ In reference to God.

THE FOUNTAIN OF JACOB. All those who came forth out of the fountain of Jacob.¹⁸¹ is, All those who came forth out of the fountain of Jacob, i.e., all those descended from Jacob. Similarly *And are come forth out of the fountain of Judah* (Is. 48:1).

IN A LAND. *El eretz* is to be rendered, in a land.¹⁸² as in *Vat. Ebr*. 38. It is similar to *if they pray in this place*¹⁸³ (el ha-makom ha-zeh)¹⁸⁴ (to this place) is to be interpreted as if written *bamakom ha-zeh*. (I Kings 8:35).

**אף שמיו.** שמי הארץ או ישראל והטעם כי תולדות הארץ להוציא דגן ותירוש אף השמים יתנו תמיד טלם ואין צורך להשקות:


YEA, HIS HEAVENS. The heavens of the earth or of Israel.¹⁸⁵ The meaning of our verse is, it is the nature of the land¹⁸⁶ to produce corn and oil. Also, the heavens shall always give their dew and there will be no need to water the ground.¹⁸⁷

And they came near, and put their feet upon the necks of them*.

## Chapter 34


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 34:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:1:1)


**ויעל משה.** לפי דעתי כי מזה הפסוק כתב יהושע כי אחר שעלה משה לא כתב ובדרך נבואה כתבו והעד ויראהו ה' גם ויאמר ה' אליו גם ויקבור:


AND MOSES WENT UP. I believe that Joshua wrote from this verse onward,¹ for Moses did not write² after he ascended.³ Joshua wrote prophetically.⁴ *And the Lord showed him; And the Lord said unto him* (v. 4); and also, *And he was buried* (v. 6) are proof.⁵

footnotes:
¹ In other words, Joshua composed Chapter 34.
² Anything.
³ Mount Nebo. Hence he could not have composed this chapter.
⁴ Chapter 34 was written under Divine inspiration. Hence it could be appended to the Torah.
⁵ That Chapter 34 was written prophetically, for otherwise how would Joshua know these things.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 34:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:5:1)


**וימת שם משה עבד ה'.** שאפי' במותו עשה מה שצוהו כעבד:


SO MOSES THE SERVANT OF THE LORD DIED THERE. Even in his death Moses, like a servant, did what God commanded him.


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 34:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:5:2)


**על פי ה'.** כי הוא אמר לו עלה ומות וכן ' ' נכתב על אהרן על' פי ה' וכן על פי ה' יחנו:


ACCORDING TO THE WORD OF THE LORD.⁶ (literally, by God’s mouth). Hence I.E.’s comment. For God told him, *Get thee up* (Deut. 32:49) *and die* (Deut. 32:50). Scripture similarly writes with regard to the death of Aaron, *at the commandment of the Lord*⁷. (Num. 33:38). It similarly states, *At the commandment of the Lord*⁸. We thus see that *al pi Adonai* is not to be taken literally but is to be interpreted according to God’s command. I.E. comments thus because there is a midrash which takes *al pi Adonai* literally and tells us that Moses died by God’s kiss. See *Mo’ed Katan* 28a; *Bava Batra* 17a. Also see Rashi. *they encamped* (Num. 9:23).

footnotes:
⁶ Hebrew, al pi Adonai
⁷ Hebrew, al pi Adonai
⁸ Hebrew, al pi Adonai



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 34:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:6:1)


**ויקבר אותו.** הוא קבר עצמו שנכנס במערה בגיא וכן וירעו הרועים אותם ויראו שוטרי בני ישראל אותם ודע כי הר העברים שהוא הר נבו שהוא צלם ככב הוא בעצמו הגיא שהוא ראש הפסג' שהוא מול בית פעור והעד שאמר הכתוב כי נסע ישראל מבמו' אל הגיא אשר בשדה מואב ראש הפסגה וכתיב ויעל משה מערבות מואב אל הר נבו ראש הפסגה ושם מת ושם קבורתו וכתיב ונשב בגיא מול בית פעור והנה אחר שישבו ישראל בגיא אמר הכתוב ויסעו בני ישראל ויחנו בערבות מואב וכתוב אחר ויחנו בהרי העברים לפני נבו ויסעו מהרי העברים ויחנו בערבות מואב והנה דבר ברור כי במקום שמת משה שם קבורתו:


AND HE WAS BURIED.⁹ (literally, and he buried him). Moses buried himself.¹⁰ (and he buried him) is to be rendered, and he buried himself. I.E.’s point is that the pronoun *oto* (him) or *otam* (them) sometimes has the meaning of himself or themselves. He entered a cave in the valley. The same is true of *but the shepherds fed them* (otam),¹¹ (them) is to be rendered themselves. Our clause thus reads, but the shepherds fed themselves. and *fed not My sheep* (Ezek. 34:8) and, *And the officers of the children of Israel did see them*¹² (otam)¹³). (Ex. 5:19). Note,¹⁴); the valley (*gai*) which is over against Beth-peor (Deut. 34:6). I.E. goes on to explain that all of these terms refer to one and the same place. Mount Abarim is also known as Mount Nebo.¹⁵ Nebo is in the image of Mercury.¹⁶. According to I.E. the idol Nebo stood and was worshipped on Mount Abarim. Hence Mount Abarim was also known as Mount Nebo. Mount Abarim is also known as “the valley which is the top of Pisgah which is over against Beth-peor.”¹⁷ The fact that Scripture tells us that Israel journeyed *from Bamoth to the valley that is in the field of Moab, by the top of Pisgah* (Num. 21:20) is proof of this.¹⁸) is to be identified with the top of Pisgah. Furthermore, Scripture writes, *And Moses went up from the plains of Moab unto mount Nebo, to the top of Pisgah* (v. 1).¹⁹ Moses died there²⁰ and his grave is there.²¹ Scripture states,²² *So we abode in the valley over against Beth-peor (Deut. 3:29). Now, Scripture tells us that after Israel dwelt in the valley…the children of Israel journeyed, and pitched in the plains of Moab (Num. 22:1). Another verse states, And [Israel] pitched in the mountains of Abarim, in front of Nebo. And they journeyed from the mountains of Abarim, and pitched in the plains of Moab (Num. 33:47,48). Look, it is clear that Moses’ burial place was in the place where he died.²³ and Sotah* 13b.

HIS NATURAL FORCE. The *heh* of *lechoh* (his natural force) is in place of a *vav*.²⁶. Here it is indicated by a *heh*. Hence I.E.’s comment. ### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 34:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:9:1)


**מלא** **רוח חכמה.** היא דוח ה' והעד כי סמך משה את ידיו עליו כי לא ימצא כמותו בישראל שישען עליו:


WAS FULL OF THE SPIRIT OF WISDOM. The reference is to the spirit of the Lord.³³ (Is. 11:2). It then goes on to explain that the spirit of the Lord is the spirit of wisdom and understanding (*Ibid.*). See I.E. on Ex. 31:3 (Vol. 2, pp. 645,646). *For Moses had laid his hands upon him* is proof of this,³⁴. for there was not found in Israel one such as Joshua for Moses to lean on.³⁵ to mean relied on him, i.e., took him for a disciple. Be that as it may, according to I.E, *For Moses had laid his hands upon him* does not mean that the placing of Moses’ hand on Joshua caused Joshua to be full of the spirit of wisdom but rather that Moses placed his hands on Joshua because Joshua was full of the spirit of God.

footnotes:
³³ Scripture states, And the spirit of the Lord shall rest upon him
³⁴ That Joshua was full of the spirit of wisdom
³⁵ Another term for “place his hands on him.” It is possible that I.E. interprets Moses had laid his hands upon him


###### Ibn Ezra on Deuteronomy 34:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/34:9:2)


**ויעשו כאשר צוה ה' את משה.** שישמעו אל יהושע:


AND DID AS THE LORD COMMANDED MOSES. That they hearken unto Joshua.
